  - Tests: in the packages the exact request sequences of relation changes on both versions with the fake HTTP adapter (removes before adds, nothing sent when the server already matches, 4008 and 4009 tolerated, the wrapped 4002 of an add, a temporary id refused), duplicate, the subscription ports with 12002 and 403, `RelationChange` and the ported indentation cases; in the app the payload JSON, the local apply with the inverse rows, temporary ids at both ends (an edge queued on an unsent child that names an unsent parent, sent with both server ids after both remaps, the local rows rewritten), the wait when the other create is behind, the failure classes, the overlay, duplicate (refused with queued ops, the copy upserted, a failure propagated), the subscription repository, the quick-add tree (two and three levels, the inherited project, the edges queued after the creates), and widget tests for every behaviour of the sheet and the project overflow at 200 % text scale. Contract tests on both API versions: the write and read clients (relations added and removed twice without a failure, the inverse on the other task, a cycle, a missing other task as the wrapped failure, duplicate with `copiedfrom`, subscribe and unsubscribe on a task and a project with the inherited opt-out and the creator's automatic subscription) and the pipeline (an offline quick add of a parent with two indented children that reaches the server as a parent with two subtasks while the local rows carry the server ids, a relation added and removed through the outbox, duplicate, subscribe and unsubscribe).

## D42: App icon: the illustrated llama with a check badge, adaptive, themed and iOS 18 variants derived by script

*Accepted 2026-10-13, by the owner. Replaces the "checkbox ears" icon of [D15](#d15-app-id-devcoderoftimevikunja); the name and the ids of D15 are unchanged.*

- **Context:**
  - The owner supplied a new logo, a 2000 × 2000 px raster illustration (a white llama bust on a blue-to-teal gradient with an orange check badge), and asked for adaptive icons that look like a themed reference: a dark llama on a light tint in light mode, a light llama on a dark tint in dark mode.
  - Android draws adaptive icons from a background and a foreground layer on a 108 dp canvas, masks them to the launcher's shape (72 dp visible, a 66 dp safe zone) and, from Android 13, tints a monochrome layer for themed icons. iOS 18 adds dark (transparent foreground, system background) and tinted (grayscale, system tint) icons.
  - There is no vector source of the illustration.
- **Decision:**
  - The illustration is the iOS icon and, as a rounded square, the in-app logo.
  - `branding/build-icons.sh` derives everything else from it with ImageMagick: the background modelled as a four-corner gradient, the llama cut out against it with soft, un-mixed edges, the badge as an anti-aliased disk; the adaptive foreground with the head inside the safe zone (no mask clips the ears), the neck running past the bottom and the badge on the lower right; the monochrome glyph as the silhouette with the eyes, the muzzle (keeping nose and mouth) and the inner ears cut out and the check cut out of the badge, used for the themed icon and, as the head alone, for the status bar icon; the Android 7 legacy icon as the visible 72 dp of the adaptive layers; the iOS 18 dark icon as the llama and badge on transparency, from which flutter_launcher_icons desaturates the tinted one.
  - `branding/preview.sh` renders every variant into one sheet for review.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Tracing the illustration into a vector by hand:** a faithful vector of a shaded illustration is designer work; the raster derivation is reproducible and good enough up to 1024 px. A commissioned vector stays an open point before the store release.
  - **The illustration as the adaptive foreground unchanged:** its ears and badge sit outside the 66 dp safe zone, so round masks would clip them, and the flat bottom of the bust would show inside the mask.
  - **A desaturated colour layer as the themed icon:** Android uses only the alpha of the monochrome layer, so shading is lost; the glyph needs explicit cut-outs to keep a face.
- **Consequences:**
  - Every icon changes on Android and iOS, including the themed and iOS 18 variants, the status bar icon and the logo on the connect, first-sync and notification setup screens.
  - An illustrated front-facing llama is closer to Vikunja's own artwork and to other llama logos than the checkbox-ears icon was; D15's check with the Vikunja maintainers before the store release covers it.
  - Small sizes come from a raster, so 24 px status bar icons and 48 px launcher icons are slightly softer than a vector would give.

## D43: Task lists from the local database like the web's, a view snapshot for positions and server-only filters, the Overview's saved filter downloaded by the sync, Upcoming with the web presets and a pinned overdue part

*Accepted 2026-10-14, step 9. Builds on [D29](#d29-ui-reads-through-repository-interfaces-the-online-implementation-is-replaced-by-drift-in-step-6-without-ui-changes) (the lists read through repositories) and [D33](#d33-incremental-sync-watermark-on-updated-with-a-120-s-overlap-keyset-over-updated-id-delete-detection-by-per-project-count-check-and-id-reconcile-daily-full-resync) (the sync that fills them); the online-era paging (`watchProjectTasks`, `loadMoreProjectTasks`, `PagedList`) is removed.*

- **Context:**
  - Since step 6 every task of the account is in the local database, but the lists were the step 5 screens: the Overview grouped every open task by due date, archived projects included; a project showed its tasks newest first with a "Show done tasks" switch; subtasks appeared as ordinary rows; there was no Upcoming. The task row showed the title, the identifier, the due date, a priority chip from high up and the labels.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0, both API versions, probed on 2026-10-13; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **Views** come with `GET /projects` (stored since step 6). A new project gets List (filter `done = false`), Gantt, Table and Kanban. A view's tasks endpoint ANDs the request filter with the view filter, so a `done = false` view never returns done tasks, and the web has no "show done" any more. Without `expand` it returns every matching task; the web's List view sends `expand=subtasks` and gets the roots (matching tasks whose parent does not match) plus every descendant of them, transitively and unfiltered, so a done subtask of an open parent shows nested and an open subtask of a done parent shows at the top level.
    - **Positions are per view** and only readable through the view's tasks endpoint; every other endpoint answers `position: 0`. New tasks go to the top of every view. A move does not bump `updated`, so the incremental sync never sees it.
    - **The web's List view** nests by `useTaskListFiltering` (a task is hidden at the top level when a parent is in the result) with the children in the parent's relation order, unbounded and without collapse, and sorts by one key out of 17 (default: manual, by position).
    - **The web's Home** shows every open task (`GET /tasks`, which leaves out archived projects) sorted by due date, undated last, newest first; when `frontend_settings.filter_id_used_on_overview` names a saved filter that still exists, it shows that filter's open tasks instead (`GET /projects/{pseudo}/tasks`, `done = false`). The setting holds the saved filter's pseudo project id (`-(filterId) - 1`), and every user has the saved filter "My Open Tasks" from registration on.
    - **The web's Upcoming** asks for `done = false && due_date < to && due_date > from` (strict), drops the lower bound for "Show overdue tasks" and adds the undated tasks for "Show tasks without date"; its default range is now to now + 7 days, and its 14 presets are datemath strings the server evaluates, with `now/w` rounding to Monday whatever the user's week start.
    - **The web's row** shows the priority only when the task is open and at least `minimum_priority` (medium when unset), the labels by title, every assignee's avatar, the due date (red only when overdue), icons for a description, a repeat, comments and attachments, a checklist summary counted from `data-checked` in the description, and a bar for percent done. Avatars are authenticated blobs, and the initials and marble providers answer SVG.
- **Decision:**
  - **Matching set on the device where the filter allows it.** `classifyViewFilter` (`vikunja_logic`) sorts a view's filter into none, `done = false`, `done = true` or other. The first three are evaluated on the local tasks, so a change made here shows at once, offline too. For any other filter the list is the server's answer from the view's last download, with tasks deleted here hidden; a task changed here stays until the next download, as on the web until it refetches, and a task created here appears after it. Step 24's filter engine replaces this.
  - **The web's tree.** `buildTaskTree` keeps the matching tasks that have no parent among them and follows each with all its descendants from the local database, in any project and done or not, in the parent's relation order (the DAO hydrates relations in the order they were stored, which is the server's), recursively, under both parents when a child has two, with a guard against cycles. Rows are indented per level, visually capped at four levels.
  - **Show done** stays as an app addition: offered on `done = false` views only, saved per view, off by default. On, the view is built like one without a filter, and the done top-level tasks follow the open ones in a Done part; a view without a filter always has that part, a `done = true` view and a server-only view have one part. Done subtasks of open parents stay nested either way, as on the web.
  - **Order.** The top-level rows follow the view's sort, saved per view (the web's 17, "Manually" first). Manual order uses the positions of the view's last download: open tasks the download did not have (created here, or on the server since) first, newest first, then by position and id, as the server puts new tasks on top; in the Done part done-at, newest first, except in `done = true` and server-only views, which keep the view's positions. Other sorts use their key with missing values last in both directions and fall back to the manual order on ties; priority and percent done count 0 as a value, as the server does, and "Updated" uses the created time for a task that was never sent.
  - **View snapshots.** `refreshProjectList` runs the sync and downloads the view's tasks sorted by position over all pages (`filter_timezone` from the user's timezone setting when set), storing the task ids, their positions and the time as the view's snapshot (`list_snapshots`, `list_snapshot_tasks`). The project screen downloads quietly when it opens a view whose snapshot is missing or older than five minutes, and on pull-to-refresh with the usual failure messages; a server-only view that was never downloaded says so (downloading, offline, or failed). The sync never downloads views.
  - **The Overview.** `watchOverviewTasks` shows, when the setting names a saved filter the last project sync saw, that filter's open tasks from the snapshot `overview:<pseudoId>` with tasks done or deleted here hidden, under a notice with the filter's name ("My Open Tasks" translated) and the time its current content was downloaded (an unchanged download is not rewritten, so the lists do not reload); otherwise every open task outside archived projects and their subprojects (`watchActiveOpenTasks`, which also ends the old leak of archived tasks). The grouping by due date stays. The sync records the saved filters it sees in the project list (`sync_state` `saved_filters`) and, after the tasks phase of every run, the background runs included, downloads the filter's open tasks; a failure there is logged and does not fail the run.
  - **Upcoming** is a fourth tab between Overview and Projects. `selectUpcoming` evaluates the range on the device in its own time zone with the server's datemath (`evaluateDatemath`: weeks from Monday, days and months keeping the wall clock across DST): a preset or a custom date range (default the next 7 days), strict bounds, one section per day, and two switches saved per account. **Show overdue tasks** is on by default and puts every overdue open task into a pinned Overdue part, never repeated below, where the web drops the lower bound instead. **Show tasks without date** is off by default and adds a No due date part.
  - **Preferences per account** (`list_preferences` in the account database, deleted with it, kept by a full resync): the sort and show-done per view, the last list view per project, the Upcoming range and switches. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the last list view per project is no longer kept: a project opens on its default view each time.*
  - **The row** follows the web: the priority chip from `minimum_priority` and only on open tasks (low, medium and high up in three colours), the task's colour, the labels de-duplicated and by title, up to three avatars and "+N" (authenticated through `AvatarsPort`, cached per account, SVG and missing ones as initials, other failures retried after two minutes), the due date also on done tasks, icons for a description and a repeat, the checklist ring with "2 of 3 tasks", and the percent bar. The identifier, the project and the pending marker stay. The row is one screen-reader node that also names the parent of a subtask. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the identifier goes, shown and spoken; the project follows a folder in its colour, muted without one.*
  - **Schema v6** adds the three tables. Its migration also clears the time of the last full sync: before v6 a child's pull could move its edge to the end of the parent's relations, and the full run that follows the upgrade restores the server's order everywhere.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **A filter engine now.** Evaluating every view filter and saved filter on the device is step 24's work (the whole filter language, date math and include-nulls). The three filter shapes the server creates by default cover almost every list, and the snapshot gives the server's answer for the rest with the same code that reads positions.
  - **Ignoring per-view positions.** Without them "Manually" would be newest first, which differs from the web as soon as someone drags a task there. A download per view is cheap, happens only where the list is looked at, and needs no sync change.
  - **Downloading every view in the sync.** One request or more per list view on every run, mostly for positions that rarely change, while the screen can download what it shows.
  - **The web's Upcoming semantics for overdue.** Dropping the lower bound mixes overdue tasks with today's into one list ordered by due date; a pinned part shows what is late at a glance, which matters more on a phone, and the switch keeps the web's filtering otherwise.
  - **Hiding done subtasks of open parents.** The web shows them, struck through, and they tell how far a parent has come.
- **Consequences:**
  - The lists work offline as the web shows them online: the same rows, the same nesting and the same order for views with the default filters, and for other views the server's answer as of the last download.
  - A project screen costs one view download every five minutes while it is open (one request per 50 tasks), and an Overview with a saved filter one request per sync run.
  - Tasks created here show at the top of a manually sorted list until the next view download places them; in a server-only view and in the Overview's saved filter they show after the next download.
  - Upcoming can differ from the web when the account's timezone setting differs from the phone's zone, because the phone evaluates in its own zone.
  - Not built: Favorites and saved filters as lists of their own (step 8's smart lists, step 24), Table, Gantt and Kanban views (steps 20 and 29; the switcher lists list views only), drag to reorder (step 22), comment and attachment icons (steps 27 and 26), `date_display`, `time_format` and `default_view` (step 8), a collapsible tree.
  - Tests: the ported web vectors (`useTaskListFiltering`, `ShowTasks`, `checklistFromText`), every preset at several dates including a DST change, all 17 sorts, the filter classes, the snapshot and preference stores, the v5 to v6 migration, the Overview and project list sources, the sync's saved filters and overview download, and widget tests for Upcoming, the Overview notice, the project screen and the row at 200 % text in dark mode. Contract tests on both API versions: avatars, view positions, the saved filter's tasks and the two settings, and in the app the local tree against the server's List view (roots in order, the same tasks), the Overview's saved filter against the server and Upcoming against the server's filter strings.

## D44: Task detail complete, rich text from an own parser of the web editor's schema, details and comments online on demand, deep links on the app scheme

*Accepted 2026-10-19, step 10. Carries out the native read-only rendering that [D18](#d18-tiptap-description-editor-in-a-webview) leaves to step 10. Changes [D23](#d23-browser-sign-in-through-flutter_web_auth_2-client-id-and-redirect-uri) in two places: the sign-in callback filter on Android is narrowed to its host and path, and iOS now declares a URL type for the scheme. Extends the account switch of [D37](#d37-account-switcher-a-sheet-from-an-avatar-button-and-an-accounts-section-a-switch-resets-the-navigation-the-most-recently-active-account-follows-a-removal-inactive-outboxes-drain-in-the-foreground) to links.*

- **Context:**
  - The detail sheet showed the synced row: the fields, the relations of step 14 and the follow bell, with the description as plain text from a regex stripper. Attachments, comments, reactions, the creator, the colour and the project path were missing; there was no way into a task from outside the app.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0, both API versions, probed on 2026-10-19; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **The server stores description, comment and title HTML unsanitized.** The web renders descriptions and comments through TipTap 3 on ProseMirror, whose schema (20 node types, 8 marks) is the effective allowlist; DOMPurify is only used for project descriptions. The client is the only defence.
    - **One single read carries the rest:** `GET /tasks/{id}` with repeated `expand=reactions`, `comment_count` and `is_unread` adds the reactions (a map from value to users), the comment count and the unread flag (present only when true); `subscription` exists only on single reads. Attachments and the cover id come on single and list reads.
    - **Comments** are a separate paged list (50 at most, sorted by `created` with second resolution only), each with its reactions; **attachments** download only with the bearer, `preview_size=sm|md|lg|xl` gives a PNG of that width for decodable images and the original for anything else, SVG included. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): 2.7.1 ignores `preview_size` on v2 and has a v2 route `…/preview?size=` instead; v1's `preview_size` stays and answers a JPEG (a PNG for an image with transparency), never wider than the image, and 422 code 4038 for anything it cannot preview, SVG and text included. The app asks for previews over v1 wherever the server offers it.*
    - **Unread** is set only by another user's comment and cleared by v2 `PUT`, v1 `POST /tasks/{id}/read`, idempotent even for foreign or missing tasks; the web clears it on open when the read said unread.
    - **By-index:** `GET /projects/{id or identifier}/tasks/by-index/{n}`; v2 answers an index the task had before it moved with a 307 to a root-relative, hard-coded `/api/v2` Location, v1 with 404. Identifiers are unique per instance, upper-cased by Go (which keeps `ß`) and may contain any character; an all-digit one cannot be reached by identifier.
    - **Deep linking:** Flutter 3.47 hands platform links to the router by default; go_router replaces its initial location with a platform route unless told not to; flutter_web_auth_2's Android callback filter matched every URI of the app's scheme.
- **Decision:**
  - **Own parser and renderer.** `parseRichText` (`vikunja_logic`, on `package:html`) ports ProseMirror's HTML parser with the web editor's rules into a typed tree (`RichDocument`: paragraphs, headings, lists, task lists with their source offsets, quotes with the replied comment, code, rules, tables with valid cell colours, images classified as this server's attachments or external, text runs with marks and allowed links, hard breaks, mentions, task links). It matches the web's ProseMirror JSON on all 123 oracle vectors, 118 exactly and 5 with listed reasons (no autolink-on-load, an empty `<p><br></p>` counts as empty, `math` and form controls dropped with their text, a `noscript` quirk of `package:html`, images that are neither attachments nor absolute http(s) dropped), and a seeded fuzz suite and hostile vectors pin that only schema nodes and allowed hrefs come out. Markup deeper than 96 elements is reduced to text. The app renders the tree with material_ui and cupertino_ui widgets: headings with header semantics, read-only checkboxes with checked semantics, code and wide tables in horizontal scrollers (tables beyond 50 columns or 5,000 cells row by row), mention pills, task-link pills from the local task, links in the theme's colour, all inside a `SelectionArea`. Not flutter_widget_from_html_core (a probe showed it applying inline styles, showing iframe text, loading every image through an unauthenticated `NetworkImage`, making `javascript:` links tappable and colouring links from the legacy theme), not flutter_html (does not compile), not a WebView (D18).
  - **Links and images.** A task link, or an http(s) href that `parseTaskIdFromUrl` accepts against the account's server root and `frontend_url`, opens the task in the app in the sheet's account, as a sheet over the current one; other http(s) links open in the browser, relative ones resolved against the frontend URL; `mailto`, `tel`, `sms` and the web's other link schemes go to the system through `ExternalLinkOpener`'s allowlist. Attachment images (`data-src`, else `src`) load through the authenticated `AttachmentImages` cache at the `xl` preview size and open a zoomable viewer with the original; external images are never loaded by themselves and show a placeholder that opens the URL in the browser; SVG is never decoded. The cache is per account, memory only, bounded at 32 MB, refuses responses above 25 MB and images above 40 megapixels (checked from the header before decoding), and is dropped with the avatar cache when the account goes.
  - **Details and comments online, on demand.** `TaskDetailsRepository.details` is the one expanded read, nothing stored (the schema stays v6). The sheet loads it when it opens; the follow bell takes its state from it, so opening a task still costs one request; offline the sheet shows one quiet line with Retry and is otherwise complete. When the result says unread, `markRead` follows, fire and forget. Comments load when "Show comments" is tapped, a page at a time, sorted by `(created, id)` in the order of `comment_sort_order` with a session toggle; a reply quote names the quoted author when that comment is loaded. Reactions show as read-only chips with the names in their label. Attachments list a thumbnail (`md`) for previewable images and an icon otherwise, with size and the cover badge; only images open (downloads are step 26).
  - **The sheet** adds the colour bubble, the identifier computed from the local project (the server echoes a stale one after a move), the project path as a breadcrumb that opens a project, the watched project, the checklist summary, sorted labels, authenticated assignee avatars and a metadata line (created by, updated, done); the separate "Done at" row is gone. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): no identifier shows; the breadcrumb sits in the header beside the actions, or on a line of its own below them when it does not fit there whole, the colour bubble before the title, and the open or done state on a line under the title.*
  - **Deep links on the app's own scheme:** `vikunja-dev.coderoftime.vikunja://task/<id>` and `…://task/<IDENT-12>`, with an optional `?account=` that links the app makes itself can carry. Android: the sign-in `CallbackActivity` filter is narrowed to host `oauth` and path `/callback`; a pass-through `TaskLinkActivity` owns the `task` host and hands the link to the one `MainActivity` in the app's own task, so another app cannot start a second engine; `MainActivity` drops the link on a Recents relaunch and on restore. iOS: a `CFBundleURLTypes` entry; ASWebAuthenticationSession keeps catching its own callback (checked on the simulator). Flutter's deep linking stays on: a binding observer registered before go_router's own takes every URI of the scheme before it can be routed (go_router's `onEnter` was tried and rejected because it makes the first frame blank), a redirect guard sends a stray one to the splash, `overridePlatformDefaultLocation` keeps cold starts on the splash, and the start link is read once from the platform's default route. No link is logged.
  - **One opener.** `TaskOpener` serves notification taps, deep links, the inbox and task-link pills: it waits until the app is past the splash and the first sync, chooses the account (the link's `account`, else the active one when it has the task locally, else the signed-in accounts that have it, else the active account online), asks before any switch a link triggers, resolves the task through `TaskResolver` (local first, by id or by lower-cased identifier and index; else online by id, or by index with the numeric project id, following the 307; an all-digit identifier that is not local, `.` or `..` is not found without a request), stores a task found online like a duplicate, closes modal routes and shows the sheet. The inbox now opens a task that is not local by reading it online.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **flutter_widget_from_html_core with hooks.** Making it safe needs a sanitizer in front, a custom widget factory for images, overrides for links and colours and builders for mentions, checklists and pills, which leaves little of its value; it also imports the legacy Material library (D2), and its next version does not resolve with cupertino_ui 1.1.
  - **app_links and a second scheme.** A native dependency and a parallel path that requires switching Flutter's deep linking off on both platforms; the single scheme with narrowed filters also hardens sign-in, whose callback filter caught every link of the scheme.
  - **Storing attachments, comments and reactions.** Offline lists of names would need a schema change, sync writes and purges, while thumbnails, files and comment bodies need the server anyway; attachment metadata can be stored with step 26.
  - **Loading comments with every open.** One more request per sheet for something that is often not read; the count is part of the details read, so the section can say how many there are.
- **Consequences:**
  - Every property of a task is visible; descriptions and comments look like the web's, including checklists, mentions, task links and attachment images, and nothing in them can run, load a remote resource without a tap or reach another host with the token.
  - Opening a task online costs one read, plus one per comment page shown, one mark-read when it was unread and one per visible attachment image (cached in memory); offline, the synced part of the sheet is complete.
  - Links open tasks from other apps, from notifications, from the inbox and from descriptions through one path; a link can switch accounts only after the user confirms.
  - Not built: editing descriptions and toggling checkboxes (step 25), uploading, downloading and opening files (step 26), writing comments and reactions (step 27), a read-only mode from `max_permission` or archived projects, comment permalinks, App and Universal Links for web URLs (self-hosted domains cannot be verified).
  - Tests: the oracle, hostile and fuzz suites of the parser, the 29 upstream and 38 extra `parseTaskIdFromUrl` vectors, the 23 upstream attachment-preview vectors, the link, reference and attachment-URL helpers; the ports with the fake HTTP adapter; the repositories, resolver, image cache and deep-link relay; widget tests for every node type, link scheme, image case, sheet section online and offline, the opener and deep links cold and warm with account switches, and the sheet at 200 % text in dark mode. Contract tests on both API versions: details, unread and mark read, comment pages and order, previews (PNG, text and SVG originals), by-index by id and identifier, the v2 307 after a move and the v1 404; in the app the resolver online and through the 307, details and comments through the repository. Checked on the Android emulator and the iOS simulator: sign-in on the narrowed filter and with the URL type, the full sheet, the image viewer, comments, cold and warm links by id and identifier, the 307 and a pill opening a sheet over the sheet.

## D45: App shell on wide Android screens, the colour scheme and dynamic colour, the web's display settings applied only when set, translated default names, the project tree with favourites, saved filters and archived projects, smart lists, a sticky offline banner

*Accepted 2026-10-19, step 8. Completes the shell of step 5 and applies the settings that [D43](#d43-task-lists-from-the-local-database-like-the-webs-a-view-snapshot-for-positions-and-server-only-filters-the-overviews-saved-filter-downloaded-by-the-sync-upcoming-with-the-web-presets-and-a-pinned-overdue-part) left open; the smart lists reuse its view snapshots. dynamic_color is the only new dependency; from 2.0.0 it builds on material_ui ([D2](#d2-material_ui-and-cupertino_ui)).*

- **Context:**
  - The shell had a bottom bar at every width and the brand seed in light and dark after the system; dates came in the device's formats, and the display settings the user picks on the web were not read. The Projects tab was the tree of active projects; archived projects, favourites and saved filters had no place. Offline, every list showed its own notice, which disappeared and came back with every retry.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0, both API versions, probed on 2026-10-19; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **`frontend_settings`** is stored verbatim and is `null` for a new user; the web merges its defaults over it. `date_display` (9 values, default `relative`), `time_format` (`12h`/`24h`, default `24h`), `color_schema` (`light`/`auto`/`dark`, default `auto`), `default_view` (`first`/`list`/`gantt`/`table`/`kanban`, default `first`). `week_start` (0–6) is a top-level setting whose default 0 cannot be told from "never set".
    - **The web's date display:** six numeric patterns followed by ` HH:mm` or ` hh:mm A` (padded, `AM`/`PM` in German too), two long forms (`October 19, 2026` / `19. Oktober 2026`, the second with the weekday) joined to the time with ` at ` / ` um `, and relative with "just now" under a minute; tooltips use the long form with the weekday. `week_start` only sets the first column of the date picker; the server's `now/w` starts on Monday regardless.
    - **Translated names** (`getProjectTitle`): id −1 "Favorites", the exact titles `Inbox` and `My Open Tasks`, and the four default view titles in the view switcher.
    - **The sidebar:** Favourites (the Favorites pseudo project while a favourite task exists, favourite saved filters, favourite projects), Saved filters, Projects. Archived projects appear only on the projects page behind "Show Archived"; the server marks every descendant of an archived project archived. The server's Favorites list includes favourite tasks of archived projects.
- **Decision:**
  - **Navigation rail** on Android and the other non-Apple platforms from 600 dp wide: the same four destinations with labels, the same 1.3 text clamp and the same tab behaviour (re-select pops to the root, an account switch resets, back goes to the first tab). The rail sits outside the shell's scaffold, so snackbars stay beside it. iOS keeps the tab bar at every width (iPad layouts are step 36).
  - **Colour scheme** from the active account's `color_schema`; `auto` and no signed-in account follow the system. The start waits for the settings behind the launch screen, so the theme does not flash.
  - **Dynamic colour** on Android 12 and later: the system palette, read at start and whenever the app returns to the front, becomes the light and dark colour schemes under the app's component themes. It is on by default; a device-wide "Use system colours" switch in a new Appearance section of the Account tab, shown only where a palette exists, brings back the brand blue. iOS keeps the brand blue. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the Appearance section shows for every signed-in user and holds the account's colour scheme too, as its Theme row, still saved to the server; each row says where it is stored.*
  - **Display settings only when set.** While `date_display` and `time_format` were never set, the app keeps its formats: relative in rows, the metadata line, comments and the inbox; the device's absolute format in the sheet, reminders, the edit page and the pickers; the device's 12 or 24 hours. Once set, one `MomentFormatter` applies them:
    - an absolute `date_display` (the six patterns and the two long forms) everywhere a date is shown: the rows, the sheet's dates and reminders, the metadata line, comments, the inbox, the edit page, the new-task sheet and the quick-add preview;
    - `relative` where the app is relative, with "just now" / "gerade eben" under a minute and the app's own wording beyond it; the absolute places keep the device format;
    - screen-reader labels and tooltips use the long form with the weekday, as the web's tooltips do;
    - an explicit `time_format` applies to every time shown and to the time pickers, 12 hours in German included, through a settings-aware `MaterialLocalizations` per picker (en and de, the app's languages) and `use24hFormat` on iOS.
    Upcoming's day headers and range, the Overview's groups and the "as of" and "last synced" notes keep their formats.
  - **Week start** 1–6 sets the first day of the Material date pickers; 0 keeps the locale's. Upcoming's presets stay on Monday weeks, like the server's date math (D43).
  - **Default view** is read and used by the project screen's view resolution: the remembered view, else the first list view of the setting's kind, else the first list view. Until the other view kinds exist (steps 20 and 29) this is always the first list view. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): there is no remembered view: the first view of the setting's kind, else the first view, opens every time; a guest's link names its view in the route.*
  - **Translated names** through one `projectDisplayTitle` (id −1, exact `Inbox`, exact `My Open Tasks`) at every place a project title is shown, local and activity notifications included; the view switcher translates the default view titles. Storage, quick-add matching and default-project detection keep the server's titles. Saved filters sort by title, and projects with equal positions too, ignoring case and accents like the web's `localeCompare`.
  - **Projects tab** in the sidebar's sections: **Favourites** ("Favourite tasks" while an open favourite task outside archived projects exists, decided on the device, then favourite saved filters and favourite projects), **Saved filters** (by title) and **All projects** (the tree). "Show archived projects" in the overflow menu and the collapsed branches are saved per account (`list_preferences` key `projects`); archived projects carry an archive icon. They stay out of pickers, defaults and quick add. An archived project shows the web's note, no New task button, and its rows without the done and star controls, which the server would refuse. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): "Show saved filters" in the same menu, saved per account under the same key, hides the Saved filters section and the favourite saved filters; every project shows as a folder in its colour.*
  - **Smart lists** at the negative ids. "Favourite tasks" comes from the local database: open favourite tasks outside archived projects and their subprojects, ordered like the Overview, unlike the server's list, which keeps archived ones. A saved filter is the server's answer, archived projects included, kept as the view snapshot `filter:<pseudo project id>`. It is downloaded with `filter_timezone` when the list opens and the snapshot is missing or older than five minutes, on pull, and when the device comes back online, with the project screen's downloading, offline and failed states, and a gone state when the sync no longer lists the filter. The sync stores `is_favorite` with the saved filters and purges the `filter:` snapshots of filters that are gone on every run. Rows show their project; done and star work as everywhere; New task opens without a project.
  - **Offline banner** in the shell instead of a notice per list: offline after a sync run of the active account fails with a network failure, online again after a run gets through, unchanged while a run is going, so retries do not make it flicker. It says "Offline — showing saved data" with the last sync time, sits above the bottom bar or below the content beside the rail, and syncs when tapped. Pages over the shell, such as the inbox, keep their own notice from the same state; a failed view or filter download alone shows the lists' own states, not the banner.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **The web's defaults when nothing is set.** `relative` and `24h` would change the app for everyone who never opened the web's settings, and 24 hours is the wrong default in many English locales; the device's formats are the better fallback, and the web's choices take over once they exist.
  - **Applying `week_start` 0 as Sunday.** The server cannot tell it from "never set", and Sunday weeks in a German picker would surprise everyone who never touched the setting.
  - **A connectivity plugin.** A native dependency that still cannot tell whether the server is reachable; the last sync outcome can.
  - **Evaluating saved filters on the device.** Step 24's filter engine; the snapshot gives the server's answer with the code the views already use.
  - **A rail or sidebar on iOS.** cupertino_ui has none and a Material rail looks foreign there; the iPad step decides.
- **Consequences:**
  - The app follows the user's web settings once they are set and looks like a native app until then.
  - Opening a saved filter costs one download every five minutes while it is open; Favourite tasks cost nothing and work offline.
  - The detail sheet still offers done and star for a task in an archived project; a full read-only mode is not built, nor editing the settings (step 30), the `language` and `timezone` settings for display (the device's are used), the time pickers' settings in languages other than en and de, or the Kanban, Table and Gantt views (steps 20 and 29).
  - Tests: the web's date display vectors for all nine displays in 12 and 24 hours, en and de, and with nothing set; every formatted place with and without settings; the pickers' first weekday and 12/24 hours; the view resolution for every default view; the translated names at every place, notifications included; the settings codec and store; favourites, the saved filter snapshot, its purge and `is_favorite`; the project tree preferences; widget tests for the shell at 360 and 800 dp on Android and iOS, the theme and dynamic colour with a mocked palette, the Projects sections and the archived toggle, the archived project, the smart lists in every state, the offline banner, and the accessibility smoke test at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de. Contract tests: the four settings on both API versions; in the app a saved filter through `refreshSavedFilter` against the server, favourites, and the settings written the web's way. Checked on the Android emulator: the rail in landscape, the theme from `color_schema`, dynamic colour on and off, the formats and the pickers in English and German with 12 and 24 hours and a Monday week start, the translated names, the Projects sections, both smart lists, the archived project and the offline banner.

## D46: Realtime in the foreground: one WebSocket for the active account while the app is in front, pushed notifications stored at once and a sync nudged for the task they name, re-auth on every token change, quiet fallback to polling

*Accepted 2026-10-19, step 17. Carries out the foreground WebSocket that [D33](#d33-incremental-sync-watermark-on-updated-with-a-120-s-overlap-keyset-over-updated-id-delete-detection-by-per-project-count-check-and-id-reconcile-daily-full-resync) and [D40](#d40-background-sync-one-periodic-job-for-every-signed-in-account-with-a-deadline-skipped-while-the-app-is-in-front-the-incremental-run-as-the-session-keep-alive-a-one-off-drain-after-a-background-done-server-notifications-synced-into-a-local-inbox-on-their-own-cadence-new-unread-ones-as-activity-notifications-the-first-run-silent) leave to step 17, with two corrections to their plan: the socket does not replace the 60 s sync timer, and the server's reminder notifications need no de-duplication.*

- **Context:**
  - Since step 16 the active account's server notifications reach the inbox through a poll about every five minutes while the app is open; a comment written on the web a minute ago shows up minutes later.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0, both API versions, probed on 2026-10-19; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **The socket** (`/api/v2/ws`, `/api/v1/ws` since 2.3.0) pushes only `notification.created` and, with the time-tracking licence, `timer.*`; **there are no task or project change events**. A pushed notification equals the item of `GET /notifications` field by field, for every kind that is stored: comments (mentions in comments included), assignments, mentions, tasks created or deleted in followed projects, projects created, team memberships and reminders. Events while disconnected are lost, and reads are never pushed.
    - **Auth:** a user JWT within 30 s; a bad token gets `invalid_token` and a normal close; the server closes at the token's expiry ("token expired") unless a refreshed token of the same session re-authenticates the open socket (2.7.0); a logout or revoked session closes that session's sockets ("session revoked"). The server pings every 30 s and drops a socket that does not answer.
    - **The upgrade shares the per-IP limit of login and registration** (10 a minute, refused upgrades included; 429 with `Retry-After`). No setting disables websockets and `/info` does not tell; only the upgrade's status does (404, 426 or 502 behind a proxy that cannot upgrade).
    - **The web** re-authenticates after every token refresh, reconnects with a backoff of 1 to 30 s, and on a notification only reloads its list.
    - The app's REST requests go through Cronet and NSURLSession; dart:io, the only WebSocket client without a new dependency, ignores system proxies and user-installed CAs on Android.
- **Decision:**
  - **One socket, for the active account, while the app is in front** (`realtimeConnectionProvider`, gated like the foreground sync scheduler): after the account's first sync, never while it needs a new sign-in or is being signed out, never in a background isolate, only on servers with the WebSocket capability. It opens on start and resume, closes normally on hide and pause, and moves with an account switch; the socket closes before our own logout request, so the server's "session revoked" that follows is ignored. Inactive accounts keep their poll and the background job is unchanged (D40). `HomeShell` keeps this provider and the other per-account services alive from `initState`; listening in `build` had restarted the 60 s timer and pulled every account's activity on each tab switch since step 16.
  - **Transport** in `vikunja_api` on dart:io without a new dependency: `IoRealtimeConnector` does the upgrade by hand so that a refused one reports its status and `Retry-After`, checks `Sec-WebSocket-Accept`, sends the app's User-Agent and no Origin, gives up after 15 s, and pings every 25 s to notice a dead connection; a drop without a close frame reports no close code. `realtimeUri` uses the API version the account negotiated, keeping the sub-path and port. Nothing logs frames or tokens; the app logs states, statuses and close codes at FINE.
  - **Sequence:** upgrade, then the account's current token, `auth`, `auth.success` within 10 s, `subscribe notification.created`, live; a catch-up of the inbox runs alongside, because events while disconnected are lost.
  - **Events:** a pushed notification is stored at once like a polled one, as handled (the active account in front never gets OS notifications, D40), so the inbox and the bell badge update by themselves; a reminder is stored as the poll stores it, never shown and never counted, so it needs no de-duplication against the local reminder. Comments, assignments, mentions, created and deleted tasks and created projects request one incremental sync of the active account, debounced by 2 s and coalesced with a run in progress; team memberships and reminders request nothing. The 60 s sync timer and the inbox poll stay: the socket carries no task changes and no reads.
  - **Re-auth:** every 20 s the connection reads the account's token (which the session manager refreshes within its 60 s margin) and re-authenticates when it changed; a token of another session (a new sign-in) reconnects instead, and a re-auth without `auth.success` within 10 s closes and reconnects, which also covers servers older than 2.7.0. The session manager is unchanged.
  - **Failures fall back to polling, quietly and within the shared limit:**
    - `invalid_token` and "session revoked" go through the session manager's `refreshAfterRejection`; a newer token reconnects once, anything else stops the socket until the next resume, and a revoked session ends inside the manager's refresh as for HTTP.
    - Drops, "token expired", "auth timeout", 1013, 5xx, a TLS handshake cut off and connection errors reconnect after 2 s doubling to 60 s with ±25 % jitter, reset after a minute live and on resume; six attempts in a row that never reach `auth.success` stop until the next resume.
    - A 429 waits its `Retry-After`, at least 2 s (doubling with every 429 in a row) and at most 5 minutes.
    - Any other refused upgrade (404, 426, 3xx and the like) or a TLS error before any socket of the account was live means the server or a proxy cannot do it: no more attempts until the app starts again or the account changes. After a socket was live the same answers count as temporary.
    - While the shell's offline state says offline, nothing is tried; when it turns online, at once.
  - **Nothing new on screen:** the badge, the inbox and the lists simply update sooner, and the offline banner stays bound to sync runs.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **The socket as the sync trigger in place of the 60 s timer** (the plan of D33): the socket carries no task changes, so web edits would wait for the next notification.
  - **Re-reading the notifications on every event**, as the web does: a request per event for data the event already holds.
  - **Sockets for every signed-in account:** each costs an upgrade from the shared limit and a connection; the inactive accounts' poll is enough for an account not on screen.
  - **web_socket_channel or cupertino_http's socket:** a new dependency, and no status code for a refused upgrade; the hand-made upgrade on dart:io shows 429 and `Retry-After`.
- **Consequences:**
  - While the app is open, a comment, an assignment or a mention made on the web is in the inbox within a second and the task it names is synced within a few seconds; checked on the emulator against the test server (about 1.5 s for a comment, the assignee's avatar on the row within 4 s, the catch-up after a resume).
  - Each opening of the app costs one upgrade and one inbox pull; re-auth costs nothing beyond the token refresh REST needs anyway; a server or proxy that cannot upgrade costs at most a few attempts per app session. On Android a server behind a private CA or reachable only through a system proxy or VPN route that dart:io ignores keeps working through polling.
  - Not built: time tracking events (licensed), sockets for inactive accounts or in the background, showing the server's reminders, a switch to turn realtime off.
  - Tests: the codec on the frames recorded from 2.7.0, `realtimeUri`, the connector against a loopback server (101, 429 with `Retry-After`, 426, 404, a wrong accept key, timeouts, close codes, drops and resets), the connection's state machine with fake sockets and timers (gating, lifecycle, the sequence and catch-up, storage, the debounced sync per kind, re-auth and its timeout, every failure class, the limits, the offline pause, a late close after sign-out), the provider and the shell (no reconnect on a tab switch). Contract tests on both API paths: auth, `auth_required`, an API token refused, a comment pushed equal to `GET /notifications`, logout closing with "session revoked"; in the app a comment reaching the inbox repository through the real socket.

## D47: Kanban board from per-view board snapshots, moves and positions through the outbox with the done bucket's rules, accessible drag and drop

*Accepted 2026-10-19, step 20. Carries out the kanban read path [D30](#d30-kanban-board-reads-deferred-to-step-20) deferred to this step, and settles its open point: the bucket tables were not created in step 6, so schema v7 adds them. Extends the view snapshots of [D43](#d43-task-lists-from-the-local-database-like-the-webs-a-view-snapshot-for-positions-and-server-only-filters-the-overviews-saved-filter-downloaded-by-the-sync-upcoming-with-the-web-presets-and-a-pinned-overdue-part) to boards and the outbox of [D34](#d34-outbox-local-first-writes-serial-sender-coalescing-retry-classes-sync-interplay-pending-fields-win-until-sent-pending-deletes-hidden-v1-full-replace-emulation) and [D35](#d35-creates-through-the-outbox-temporary-negative-ids-with-remap-createupdatedelete-coalescing-lost-response-recovery-by-title-within-a-1-h-window) to moves.*

- **Context:**
  - The project screen offered list views only; a project's Kanban views, which every new project gets with To-Do, Doing and Done, were invisible in the app.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0, both API versions, probed on 2026-10-19; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **Reads:** v1 answers a kanban view's tasks endpoint with the buckets and their tasks, v2 has `…/buckets/tasks`; `per_page` and `page` apply per bucket (at most 50), `count` is the bucket's total. One more page of one bucket comes from the view's tasks endpoint with `bucket_id = N`, where every task reports bucket 0. Tasks are ordered by position, then id.
    - **Writes:** a move (`…/buckets/{b}/tasks`, v1 POST, v2 PUT) keeps the task's position, so the web sets the position right after it (`/tasks/{id}/position`); a position below 0.01 makes the server recalculate the whole view, and equal positions are spread apart. **Neither a plain move nor a position write bumps `updated`**, so the incremental sync never sees them.
    - **The done bucket:** a move into it marks the task done and puts it into the done bucket of every other kanban view; a move out marks it open in that view only; a repeating task is reopened into the default bucket; marking done anywhere moves the task into every done bucket, marking it open into the default bucket; a full done bucket makes the done update fail (412, 10004).
    - New tasks land on top of their bucket in every view. A full bucket (`count >= limit`) refuses moves and creates with 412 code 10004; filter-mode views have synthetic buckets and refuse moves; archived projects refuse writes with 3008.
- **Decision:**
  - **Kanban views join the project screen.** `shownViewsOf` offers list and kanban views; `default_view = kanban` opens the board ([D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner)). The board replaces the list body; the app bar keeps refresh and the overflow menu without sort and show done.
  - **Layout:** columns as wide as the board less 80 dp, at most 280 dp, 12 dp apart (step 20 built them less 48 dp, at most 340 dp, 8 dp apart; the design refresh of [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list) changed them, and this line was corrected on 2026-10-28, [D69](#d69-large-screens-one-window-layout-helper-readable-widths-of-840-dp-for-lists-and-640-dp-for-settings-and-forms-with-scrolling-margins-dialogs-at-most-560-dp-and-ios-sheets-at-most-640-dp-only-on-tablet-sized-windows)), in a horizontal scroll that snaps to columns on a phone and shows several side by side when there is room, clear of the safe-area insets; the board fills the height below the notes. A column has its header (title, the done marker with the web's hint, `count/limit` in the error colour when full, else the count), its cards, "Load more" for a partly loaded bucket (also on reaching the end), and "Add a task".
  - **Card** like the web's: done badge, identifier, due date in the user's format (red when overdue and open), title, progress, labels, priority from the minimum and only while open, description and repeat icons, assignees, checklist summary, task colour, the not-yet-synced mark; one screen-reader node built like the row's. A tap opens the detail sheet. Cover images and comment and attachment icons are not built. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the card shows no identifier, and its label reads none.*
  - **Drag and drop:** a long press lifts the card; the column under the finger shows where it will land; the board and the column scroll near the edges, the board does not snap while a card is held and settles on the column that took it. A drop computes the position from the cards above and below it as shown (the web's `calculateItemPosition`, except that an empty column gets 65536 instead of 0, which would recalculate the whole view), never below the last loaded card of a partly loaded column. A full column refuses a card from elsewhere with a haptic and the web's limit text. **For screen readers** every card offers "Move to <column>" for each other column (numbered when two columns share a title), "Move up" and "Move down", and the result is announced. Filter-mode boards and archived projects are read-only.
  - **Board snapshots (schema v7):** `kanban_buckets` holds a view's buckets with title, limit, count, position and pages loaded; `list_snapshot_tasks` gains the bucket. A download replaces both in one transaction under `view:<id>`: one board request for the first page of every bucket, then the further pages a bucket had loaded before, so what was scrolled to stays. The screen downloads when the board opens and the snapshot is missing or older than five minutes, on pull, when the device comes back online, and quietly 2 s after its own placements have left the outbox. The sync never downloads boards; it purges the snapshots and buckets of views that no longer exist, which also ends the leak of list-view snapshots.
  - **The board model** (`buildBoard`, `vikunja_logic`) joins the snapshot with the local tasks: a queued placement wins; a done task shows in the done bucket and an open one leaves it for the default bucket (the leftmost when none is set), as the server will do; tasks created here, changed after the download or moved into the project show on top of the default bucket; tasks deleted or moved away are hidden; the filters the device can evaluate apply as in D43; a filter-mode board shows its download as it is. A sent placement updates the stored board at once, counts included.
  - **Moves through the outbox:** `TaskChanges.kanban` maps a view to a bucket and a position, merged per view, folded behind an unsent create, kept as pending changes until sent. A later project move or a change that flips `done` drops the placements queued before it, so the server never gets a move for the wrong project or the opposite done state. A drop into the done bucket queues `done: true` with the placement, one out of it `done: false` with the placement; a repeating task dropped into Done is only marked done and placed in the default bucket without a position. The sender writes the body (with done) first, then per view the move and, when the answer's bucket is the target, the position. 10004, a missing bucket, 3008 and 403 become sync problems with their own wording; discarding one puts the card back.
  - **Add a task per column:** the new-task sheet with quick add creates the tasks and places them on top of that column, as many as its limit leaves room for (the rest stay in the default column, which the server fills regardless, and the snackbar says so); in the done column they are created done.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Downloading boards in the sync:** a request per kanban view and run for data that only matters while the board is open, as for list views (D43).
  - **Every task of every bucket on each download:** a done column with hundreds of tasks would cost several requests every five minutes; the web shows the first page too and loads more on scroll.
  - **A separate outbox operation for moves:** queueing, deletes, failures and sync problems all assume one update per task; a field of `TaskChanges` rides those paths, as labels and relations do.
  - **Placing new tasks with `bucket_id` on the create:** it applies to one view only and would change the create's recovery match (D35); a placement queued behind the create works offline and in every view.
  - **The web's 0 for an empty column:** it triggers a recalculation of every position in the view.
- **Consequences:**
  - A project's Kanban boards work like the web's, offline from their last download, and moves made offline are sent in order.
  - An open board costs one request every five minutes plus one per further page loaded, and each move up to three requests (a read of the task when nothing else changes, the move and the position) plus a quiet download afterwards.
  - Not built: creating, renaming, deleting and limiting buckets and choosing the done and default bucket (step 21), collapsing columns, ordering list views and projects (step 22), Table and Gantt (step 29), kanban views of saved filters, cover images, comment and attachment icons, a read-only mode from `max_permission` (a refused move becomes a sync problem). In filter mode a task the server lists in two buckets shows once.
  - Tests: the web's position vectors; `buildBoard` in every rule; the domain merge rules; the bucket codecs on fixtures recorded from 2.7.0 for both versions; the sub-writes and their order; the v6 to v7 migration; downloads, appends and the purge; the outbox (fold behind a create, project moves, done flips, failures); widget tests for the switcher, the board, cards, drag between and within columns, refused drops, the semantics actions, load more, add a task per column, read-only boards and the landscape layout; the accessibility smoke test at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de, with the rail. Contract tests on both API versions: per-bucket paging and counts, load more, move and position, the done bucket, the limit, filter mode and a repeating task; in the app the local board against the server's and placements drained by the outbox. Checked on the Android emulator: the board, a drag to Doing and into and out of Done with the server following.

## D48: Bucket management online, applied to the local board at once, from a column menu, with a guard against the server's orphaned tasks

*Accepted 2026-10-19, step 21. Completes the board of [D47](#d47-kanban-board-from-per-view-board-snapshots-moves-and-positions-through-the-outbox-with-the-done-buckets-rules-accessible-drag-and-drop); handles writes the way creating a label ([D36](#d36-labels-and-assignees-as-id-set-deltas-in-the-task-update-sent-through-their-endpoints-after-a-read-before-write-diff-reminders-as-the-whole-list-label-creation-online-only)) and duplicating ([D41](#d41-relations-as-deltas-in-the-task-update-sent-through-the-relation-endpoints-after-a-read-before-write-diff-temporary-ids-on-both-ends-resolved-at-send-time-the-inverse-rows-kept-locally-duplicate-and-subscriptions-online-only-subtasks-from-indented-quick-add-lines)) do, online and without the outbox.*

- **Context:**
  - The board of step 20 showed the server's columns but could not change them.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0, both API versions, probed on 2026-10-19; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **Buckets:** create v1 `PUT` / v2 `POST`; update v1 `POST` / v2 `PUT` **replaces title, limit and position together** (whatever is left out becomes 0) and answers with an incomplete bucket; delete moves the bucket's tasks to the default bucket, or the leftmost when none is set, keeping their positions and ignoring the limit, and clears the view's default or done id when it was that bucket; the last bucket cannot be deleted (10003). v1 lets ids in the body override the URL. Positions are stored as sent.
    - **Done and default bucket** are fields of the project view: v1 `POST` and v2 `PUT` replace the whole view (leaving out `view_kind` turns the board into a list), v2 `PATCH` merges only what is sent. Choosing a done bucket changes no task. Only project admins may change them (403 for write shares, which may manage buckets); archived projects refuse bucket changes (3008); a view change shows up in `GET /projects`, bucket changes do not.
    - **Upstream bug:** deleting the bucket the tasks fall back to (the leftmost while no other default is set) leaves its tasks in no bucket at all.
    - **The web:** the title is edited in place; the column menu has Limit, Done bucket, Default bucket, Collapse and Delete (disabled for the last bucket, with a confirmation); "Create a bucket" sits at the end; columns are dragged to reorder.
- **Decision:**
  - **Online, applied at once.** `BucketRepository` calls the server and nothing is queued: the actions need server ids, change where queued moves land and are rare. On success it writes the answer into the local board in one transaction (a new column with count 0; renamed, limited and moved columns keep their stored count; a deleted column's cards move to where the server puts them; the view's done and default ids are written to `project_views` alone), then downloads the board quietly. Offline, or on a refusal, nothing changes and a message says why: no connection, the change could not be confirmed, only project admins can choose the done or default column, a board needs at least one column, the column no longer exists, the project is archived, no permission. Every success is announced to screen readers.
  - **Requests:** the bucket update always sends title, limit and position, and no body carries an `id` or `project_view_id`. The view change is a merge-patch on v2 and, on v1, a read of the view followed by writing it back whole with only the bucket ids changed, so kind, filter, title and position survive. A bucket already gone counts as deleted.
  - **The column menu** sits behind an options button in each header ("Options for <column>"): Rename, Limit ("Not set" when none), Done column and Default column (checked when they are, with the web's hints; choosing a checked one clears it), Move left and Move right (where possible), Delete (disabled for the last column). Rename, the limit and a new column use an input sheet; titles are trimmed and must not be empty, which is stricter than the server; a limit below the current count is allowed and shows red. Delete asks first, naming the column, where its tasks go and, when needed, the column that becomes the default. "Create a column" is a tile one column wide at the end of the board, so the phone's column snapping stays even; a new column goes to the right. Filter-mode boards and archived projects show none of this.
  - **Reordering** by Move left and Move right in the menu and as screen-reader actions, with the position from the new neighbours (also when they share a position or the first is at 0, where the web's formula would land on the wrong side; a move that cannot land is not offered). Columns are not dragged: on a phone that would compete with dragging cards and with the snapping board.
  - **Guard against orphaned tasks:** before deleting, the app reads the view from the server; when the column to delete is the one tasks fall back to and holds tasks (when it looks empty, the board is downloaded first to be sure), the app first makes the leftmost remaining column the default, then deletes; when the delete fails, the old default is put back. When the first call is refused (a write share is not an admin), nothing is deleted.
  - **The board model follows the server's done rule more closely:** a done task moves into the done column only when it was marked done after the board's download (its `doneAt`, else `updated`, is newer, or a done change is still queued), and an open task leaves the done column only when it changed after the download; tasks that were done before a done column was chosen stay where the server keeps them. The download now takes its time before the request.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Bucket changes through the outbox:** temporary bucket ids would have to be remapped in queued moves and snapshots, and a move queued into a column deleted offline would have nowhere to go.
  - **The v1/v2 full view replace for done and default:** one missing field breaks the view; v2's merge-patch and v1's read-then-write cannot.
  - **Collapsing columns:** a phone shows one column at a time; it would also break the even snapping.
  - **Editing the title in the header:** the keyboard would cover the board and fight the snapping and the drop targets; a sheet does not.
- **Consequences:**
  - A board can be set up completely in the app, while online.
  - Each change costs its request plus a board download; a guarded delete one view read and up to two more requests.
  - Not built: collapsing and dragging columns, managing columns offline, a permission model from `max_permission` (a refusal shows as a message). Moves still queued into a deleted column fail as the existing sync problem.
  - Tests: the write ports on fixtures recorded from 2.7.0 for both versions (verbs, bodies, 304, the v1 view kept whole, the error codes); the repository against a fake server with each action compared to a fresh download, the guard in every branch, the plans for deletion and moves; the DAO additions; the refined done rule; widget tests for the menu, the sheets, the confirmation, the tile, the moves and announcements and read-only boards; the accessibility smoke test at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de. Contract tests on both API versions: create, rename keeping limit and position, limit, reorder (also in front of a column at 0), delete moving tasks, the last column, done and default set and cleared without changing the view or any task; in the app each action against the server leaves the local board equal to a fresh download and the guard keeps the tasks. Checked on the Android emulator: the menu, a limit set and cleared, a column created and deleted, with the server following.

## D49: Manual ordering: list views by drag through the outbox, projects reordered and nested online, never a project position below 0.1

*Accepted 2026-10-20, step 22. Writes the positions that [D43](#d43-task-lists-from-the-local-database-like-the-webs-a-view-snapshot-for-positions-and-server-only-filters-the-overviews-saved-filter-downloaded-by-the-sync-upcoming-with-the-web-presets-and-a-pinned-overdue-part) only reads, with the drag of the board ([D47](#d47-kanban-board-from-per-view-board-snapshots-moves-and-positions-through-the-outbox-with-the-done-buckets-rules-accessible-drag-and-drop)); moves projects in the tree of [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner) online like the column changes of [D48](#d48-bucket-management-online-applied-to-the-local-board-at-once-from-a-column-menu-with-a-guard-against-the-servers-orphaned-tasks).*

- **Context:**
  - Lists sorted "Manually" showed the server's order but could not change it, and the project tree could not be rearranged.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0, both API versions, probed on 2026-10-19; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **Task positions in a list view** use the board's endpoint (`/tasks/{id}/position` with the view), do not bump `updated`, are per view and shared by everyone using it; done tasks hidden by the view's filter keep and share positions. The web lets a list be dragged only under the "Manually" sort, moves subtasks with their parent and takes the position from the visible neighbours.
    - **Projects:** a new project's position is its id × 65536, one number for all levels; `GET /projects` orders by position. v1 `POST` and v2 `PUT` replace the project, clearing colour, identifier, favourite and archived state left out; v2 `PATCH` changes only what is sent. **A position below 0.1 renumbers every sibling under that parent — for top-level projects, according to the source, those of every user on the instance.** A parent change keeps the position; cycles, the own project, pseudo projects and archived parents as parent are refused (3011, 3010, 3009, 3008); a parent change needs admin rights on the project and the new parent, a reorder write rights. Project moves bump `updated`, so the sync sees them.
- **Decision:**
  - **Which lists can be reordered:** a list view sorted "Manually" that has been downloaded once, in a project that is not archived (itself or above), not a smart list; only the top-level rows of the open part, each with its nested subtasks as one block. The Done part, subtasks on their own and the leaving copies of just-completed tasks stay put.
  - **List drag** as on the board: a long press lifts the row (tooltips of its controls are off while it can be lifted), a line shows where it will land, the list scrolls near its ends; the position comes from the rows above and below as shown. Tasks without a downloaded position get made-up positions, and a move among them also writes theirs, so the list keeps the order shown until the server's answer and the server gets that order. Screen readers get "Move up" and "Move down" on the row, offered only where the order changes, and announced.
  - **Positions through the outbox:** `TaskChanges.positions` maps a view to a position, merged per view, dropped by a later project move, kept by a done change, folded behind an unsent create, overlaid on the manual order until sent. The sender writes them after the body and the column moves, one request per view; the stored list takes the sent position; the screen downloads the view quietly 2 s after its own positions have left the outbox. A refusal reads "This list can't be reordered" or "The list no longer exists"; discarding it puts the row back.
  - **Projects** are reordered and nested in "All projects" (Favourites and Saved filters stay as they are), online: `moveProject` sends a v2 merge-patch with position and parent, or on v1 reads the project and writes it back whole with only those changed; then the answer is written locally and a quiet sync follows. A long press lifts a project with its subtree; a drop between rows makes it a sibling at the level the line's indent shows; a drop onto a row makes it that project's last child (a collapsed one opens). The project shows in its new place, faded, until the server answers. Refused without a request: onto itself or its subtree, into or out of an archived project, and any place whose position would fall below 0.1 (the port refuses it too). Screen readers get "Move up", "Move down", "Move out of <parent>" and "Move into <project>". A shared project whose parent the user cannot see keeps that parent when reordered. Failures have their own words: only admins may move a project under another one, no permission, archived, not possible there, the project is gone, needs a connection, could not be confirmed.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **List positions as kanban placements:** a placement always moves a column first, is dropped by a done change and needs a column on the stored side; a field of its own avoids all four.
  - **`SliverReorderableList`:** its screen-reader actions sit on a node around the row instead of the row's own, cannot be switched off per row, announce nothing, and its order snaps back until the database write lands.
  - **Project moves through the outbox:** projects have no offline write path yet (project editing is step 23), and a queued parent change could become a cycle or land in a project archived meanwhile.
  - **The full project replace on v2:** a single field left out clears it; the merge-patch cannot.
- **Consequences:**
  - Lists and the project tree can be ordered as on the web; lists offline, projects online.
  - A reorder costs a read of the task and one request per view, plus a quiet download; a project move one request (two on v1) plus a sync.
  - Not built: ordering favourites and saved filters, dragging in smart lists, ordering in table and Gantt views (step 29), a permission model from `max_permission` (a refusal shows as a message).
  - Tests: the domain merge rules; the position sub-write and the project port on fixtures recorded from 2.7.0 for both versions (bodies, 304, the v1 project kept whole, the 0.1 floor, the error codes); the outbox threading, the overlay and `applySentPositions`; the move planners for lists (made-up positions) and projects (levels, refusals, the floor); widget tests for both drags, the actions and announcements, the in-flight project and the failures; the accessibility smoke test at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de. Contract tests on both API versions: list order after positions, a hidden done task, a project reorder, nest and un-nest keeping every other field, the cycle, own-parent and archived-parent refusals; in the app a reordered list drained by the outbox and a moved project equal to the server. Checked on the Android emulator: a task dragged to the top of a list and two projects moved and back, with the server following and the project's colour and identifier kept.

## D50: Project management and views online, applied to the local database at once, from the project screen's menu

*Accepted 2026-10-20, step 23. Extends the online writes of [D48](#d48-bucket-management-online-applied-to-the-local-board-at-once-from-a-column-menu-with-a-guard-against-the-servers-orphaned-tasks) and [D49](#d49-manual-ordering-list-views-by-drag-through-the-outbox-projects-reordered-and-nested-online-never-a-project-position-below-01) to the rest of project and view management; the Projects tab of [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner) gains its entry point.*

- **Context:**
  - Projects and views could only be created and changed on the web.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0, both API versions, probed on 2026-10-20; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **Projects:** create v1 `PUT` / v2 `POST`, with the four default views (List with `done = false`, Gantt, Table, Kanban with To-Do, Doing and Done); update v1 `POST` and v2 `PUT` replace the project, v2 `PATCH` changes only what is sent; delete is hard and recursive, also for subprojects of other users; duplicate (v1 `PUT` / v2 `POST …/duplicate`) copies tasks, done ones included, labels, reminders, comments, attachments, relations inside the project, views, columns and positions, and the shares only on request, but no subprojects. The favourite is per user and goes through the update.
    - **Rules:** the identifier has at most 10 characters, is stored upper-case and must be unique on the whole instance (3007); **changing it changes every task's identifier without bumping the tasks' `updated`**. Archiving cascades to all subprojects; unarchiving a parent unarchives them all; a child under an archived parent cannot be unarchived (3016); the owner's default project cannot be archived (3013); an archived project refuses edits, favourites and task writes (3008) but not view changes. Whitespace titles and colours like `zzzzzz` are accepted.
