    - **Views:** create, update and delete need admin rights; a kanban view gets the manual mode with the three default columns; invalid filters are refused (4024, 4019, 4016); positions are kept as sent; deleting the last view is allowed; view changes do not bump the project's `updated`.
    - **The web:** project menu Edit, Views, Background, Share, Duplicate, Archive, Subscribe, Webhooks, Create project, Delete (hidden for the default project); an archived project offers Un-Archive only; the new-project form asks for title, parent and colour.
- **Decision:**
  - **Online, applied at once.** Every action calls the server; on success the local database changes at once — a new project with its views, only the changed fields, the archive flag down the subtree, a deleted subtree purged with its tasks, their queued changes, list snapshots, columns and list preferences — and a sync follows that never joins one already running (`refreshAfterWrite`), so a run in flight cannot undo the write. Offline and refusals show a message; nothing changes locally.
  - **Entry points:** "New project" in the Projects tab's app bar and in its empty state; the project screen's menu in the web's order for what the app offers: Edit, Views, Duplicate, Archive, Follow, New subproject, Add to or Remove from favourites, Delete (hidden for the user's default project). An archived project offers Unarchive and Follow; unarchiving under an archived parent is disabled with the reason.
  - **Forms:** new project (title, parent, colour, then the project opens); edit page (title, identifier with its 10-character limit and a hint that task identifiers follow, colour, parent outside the project's own subtree); duplicate (parent, "Also copy its shares", on as on the web, then the copy opens after a sync brought its tasks). Titles are trimmed and required, limits count code points as the server does. The description is not edited here; that waits for the editor ([D18](#d18-tiptap-description-editor-in-a-webview), step 25).
  - **Confirmations:** archive and unarchive with the web's meaning, also naming the user's default project when it is inside; delete counts every task it would delete, in subprojects too, and says it is for everyone.
  - **Views page:** the project's views in order with their translated title and kind, Table and Gantt marked as shown on the web for now; create, edit, Move up and Move down, delete (not the last view). The form: title, kind, "Show done tasks" for lists (mapped to the web's `done = false`), the filter in the web's syntax with the server's error shown and announced, the column mode for boards (filter-mode columns are set up on the web). A move sends only the position, so a view changed on the web meanwhile is not overwritten.
  - **Local consequences the server leaves out:** an identifier change rewrites the stored task identifiers; an unarchived subtree and a copy whose sync failed are pulled whole on the next run; a stored project below position 0.1 is lifted to at least 0.1 in the same update, so the server does not renumber its siblings.
  - **Failure wording** for no connection, could not be confirmed, no permission, admin only, archived, archived parent, default project, identifier taken, invalid filter and not found.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Project management through the outbox:** temporary project ids would have to flow into tasks, views, columns and the outbox itself; deletes and duplicates only make sense with the server's answer.
  - **Allowing the last view to be deleted, as the server does:** the app would have nothing to show for the project.
  - **The web's delete count of direct children only:** it understates what a recursive delete removes.
- **Consequences:**
  - Projects and their views can be managed completely in the app while online, except descriptions, backgrounds, sharing and webhooks.
  - Each action costs its request (two on v1 for updates) plus a sync; a duplicate waits for that sync before the copy opens.
  - Not built: description editing (step 25), backgrounds, sharing and link shares (step 28), webhooks, editing filter-mode columns, Table and Gantt (step 29), managing projects offline.
  - Tests: the domain types; the ports on fixtures recorded from 2.7.0 for both versions (bodies, 304, the v1 objects kept whole, the error codes, the 0.1 floor); the repositories against a fake server (local writes, identifier rewrite, archive cascade, subtree purge, duplicate after the sync, the guards); widget tests for the entry points, the menu, the forms, the confirmations, the views page and the failures; the accessibility smoke test at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de. Contract tests on both API versions: create, each field changed alone without losing the others, 3007, 3008, 3013, 3016, favourites, the archive cascade, duplicate, a recursive delete, views created, changed, moved and deleted, the filter errors, a write share refused; in the app each action leaving the local database equal to a fresh sync. Checked on the Android emulator: a project created with a colour, its identifier set, a filtered view added (an invalid filter refused first), the project duplicated and the copy deleted, the project archived, unarchived and deleted, with the server following.

## D51: One filter engine on the device with the server's semantics, saved filter definitions synced on change and managed online, a filter builder, offline search

*Accepted 2026-10-20, step 24. Carries out what [D43](#d43-task-lists-from-the-local-database-like-the-webs-a-view-snapshot-for-positions-and-server-only-filters-the-overviews-saved-filter-downloaded-by-the-sync-upcoming-with-the-web-presets-and-a-pinned-overdue-part) and [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner) left to step 24: lists, boards ([D47](#d47-kanban-board-from-per-view-board-snapshots-moves-and-positions-through-the-outbox-with-the-done-buckets-rules-accessible-drag-and-drop)), saved filters and the Overview no longer depend on the server for their filters. The views form of [D50](#d50-project-management-and-views-online-applied-to-the-local-database-at-once-from-the-project-screens-menu) gets the builder; saved filter changes go online like [D48](#d48-bucket-management-online-applied-to-the-local-board-at-once-from-a-column-menu-with-a-guard-against-the-servers-orphaned-tasks).*

- **Context:**
  - Lists evaluated only the default filters (`done = false`, `done = true`, none) on the device; any other view filter, every saved filter and the Overview's filter came from a download, showed changes made here only after the next one, and were empty offline until downloaded once. Saved filters could not be created or changed in the app, and there was no search.
  - Verified facts (the server's filter code and the web read at `e7d7f17`, probed on Vikunja 2.7.0 with both API versions on 2026-10-20; 267 vectors from the source and 445 observed results):
    - **Grammar:** a value runs to the next `&`, `|`, `(` or `)`; `&&` and `||` fold strictly from the left, only parentheses group; consecutive AND-joined range conditions on labels or reminders must hold for the same label or reminder; field names are exact snake_case; errors 4016, 4017, 4019, 4024, 4033.
    - **Semantics:** a missing date fails every comparison, `!=` included; `filter_include_nulls` adds missing values and, for whole numbers with `=`, `<` and `>`, also 0; labels by id and assignees by exact username match by existence, so `!=` and `not in` include tasks without any; `percent_done` runs from 0 to 1; `project` never includes subprojects; `like` uses `%` and `_` and folds ASCII case only; dates follow go-datemath (`now/d+1w`, quarters, business days, epoch milliseconds) with fallbacks, in `filter_timezone`, else the server's zone; saved filters use the owner's timezone setting.
    - **Scope:** a project view covers its project, archived tasks included; all tasks and saved filters cover the projects that are not archived plus the user's favourite tasks.
    - **Saved filters:** created, changed and deleted through `/filters` (v1 `PUT`/`POST`, v2 `POST`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`); their definition is only in `GET /filters/{id}`, not in the project list, which carries their `updated`.
    - **Search:** a case-insensitive substring of title or description, `#n` for an index; identifiers are not searched.
- **Decision:**
  - **One engine in `vikunja_logic`:** `parseTaskFilter` and `matchesFilter` implement the server's rules exactly and pass every collected vector and observed result; contract tests on both API versions compare the engine's tasks with the server's for 62 filters, the dated ones in three timezones. Two deliberate differences: a date equal to a bound matches as written (on SQLite the server is off by one there), and filters the server answers with a 500 (`position`, range comparisons on people, business-day rounding) are refused with 4019. The expression reader is a translation of the server's fexpr library (BSD 3-Clause) and the date math follows go-datemath (ISC); both notices are in `packages/vikunja_logic/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`.
  - **Timezone:** a saved filter's own, else the user's timezone setting, else the device's zone, through `package:timezone`.
  - **Where it applies:** list views with any filter (the view's snapshot only orders a manual list), boards (matching tasks created or changed here show at once), saved filters and the Overview's saved filter, each with the server's scope. Filters that use `now` are evaluated again every minute and on resume, so "due today" turns over at midnight. A filter the engine refuses, one naming `bucket_id` or a saved filter whose definition is unknown keeps the downloaded list, with its notice; downloads of saved filters the device evaluates are no longer made and old ones are removed.
  - **Definitions in the sync:** the project phase keeps each saved filter's `updated` and reads the definition of new or changed ones only; a failed read keeps the previous definition.
  - **Saved filter management** online, applied at once: "New saved filter" ends the Saved filters section (and is in the Projects menu); a saved filter's screen offers Edit, Add to or Remove from favourites and Delete. The form: title, description (plain text; the web's HTML is shown as text and kept when untouched), the filter, "Include tasks which don't have a value set" (the web's wording) and favourite; new filters start with the web's defaults. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): while "Show saved filters" is off, "New saved filter" is in the Projects menu only.*
  - **The filter builder** (saved filters and the views form): the web's syntax with operators in bold and values underlined (no backgrounds, so selection and caret stay visible), suggestions under the text for fields, operators, labels, projects, people and named date shortcuts, kept in view above the keyboard; label and project titles are sent as ids, ids without a local title are kept; the engine's error appears after a short pause or on leaving the field and blocks saving; a live count of matching tasks on the device, read out when it changes.
  - **Global search:** a search action in the Overview, Upcoming and Projects app bars opens one field with results grouped as tasks (title or description with full Unicode case folding, `#12`, and `PROJ-12` as an app addition), projects, saved filters and labels, open tasks first; offline and instant from an index kept up to date as tasks change; a label opens its open tasks with the saved filters' scope; the last query comes back when Search opens again. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the search's start text no longer names `#12` and `PROJ-12`, which the search still finds.*
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Keeping the server-only path for anything but the default filters:** the lists would stay stale and empty offline, and saved filters would cost a download per open.
  - **Parsing with a grammar of our own instead of the server's reader:** the left fold, the value scanning and the range merge only come out right by reading filters the way the server does.
  - **The web's defaults for case and time:** `like` and the timezone follow the server's observed behaviour; the web adds nothing there.
  - **A chip-based builder:** much more screen for the same language; a text field with suggestions keeps every filter the web can write.
- **Consequences:**
  - Every list, board and saved filter works offline and follows changes made here at once, as on the web while it is online.
  - A saved filter costs one request when it is created or changed elsewhere, instead of a download per open and per sync for the Overview.
  - Evaluating 5,000 tasks takes about 50 ms on the test machine; the search index rebuilds in about 50 ms and updates in a few.
  - Not built: the server's ParadeDB full-text search, `uid` filters, a chip-based builder, searching comments and attachments, teams in search (step 28), an in-app page of open-source licences (the notices file is the record until the store release).
  - Tests: all vectors and observed results in `vikunja_logic`, the date math with several timezones and DST, the transforms; the saved filter ports on fixtures recorded from 2.7.0; the definition sync, the local sources with their scopes and fallbacks, relative-date ticks, the search index; widget tests for the builder, the saved filter form and menu, the search page and the views form; the accessibility smoke test at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de. Contract tests on both API versions: the engine against the server, saved filter CRUD and a saved filter's list. Checked on the Android emulator: search (case, `WORK-3`, projects), a saved filter built with a project title and a live count of 3, stored with the project's id, its list evaluated on the device and following a task marked done at once, then deleted.

## D52: Descriptions edited in a bundled TipTap page in a locked-down WebView with a native toolbar, checklists ticked in place, no silent overwrite of a description changed on the web

*Accepted 2026-10-20, step 25. Carries out [D18](#d18-tiptap-description-editor-in-a-webview); keeps the native read-only rendering of [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme); keeps the promise of [D34](#d34-outbox-local-first-writes-serial-sender-coalescing-retry-classes-sync-interplay-pending-fields-win-until-sent-pending-deletes-hidden-v1-full-replace-emulation) that the description would not stay last-write-wins; adds the project description that [D50](#d50-project-management-and-views-online-applied-to-the-local-database-at-once-from-the-project-screens-menu) deferred.*

- **Context:**
  - Descriptions were read-only in the app; checklists in them could not be ticked, and a description change would have overwritten one made on the web meanwhile.
  - Verified facts (the web read at `e7d7f17`, probed on Vikunja 2.7.0 with both API versions on 2026-10-20): the web edits with TipTap 3.31.3 on ProseMirror; the order of its 21 extensions decides the schema; custom nodes for mentions, task links, task items with `data-task-id`, quotes of comments, attachment images (`data-src`) and coloured table cells; a WebView serialises like the browser. The web autosaves 5 s after a change, saves at once on Save and on a ticked checkbox, stores an emptied description as `<p></p>`, ticks by `data-task-id` and re-saves the whole description normalised. Mentions are suggested after `@` from the project's users; task links come from pasted task URLs. The server has no preconditions on writes.
- **Decision:**
  - **Our own bundle, the web's output.** `app/editor/` builds one self-contained page, `app/assets/editor/editor.html`, from the TipTap and ProseMirror npm packages at the web's versions with our own configuration of the web's extensions (written from its behaviour; the web frontend's AGPL code is not used). It leaves out what does not change stored HTML or belongs to later steps: code highlighting (also avoiding highlight.js), slash commands, Markdown paste, image upload, paste and drop, and floating popups. Acceptance: all 123 recorded web outputs of step 10 come out of our editor byte for byte (Node with jsdom). `tool/build_editor.sh --check` rebuilds it and fails on any difference; CI runs it. Everything bundled is MIT, listed in `app/assets/editor/NOTICES.md`.
  - **Locked down:** the page loads from the app's assets, a content security policy allows only its own hashed script and inline styles and no network at all (images are drawn as labelled placeholders and never loaded, so no token reaches the page), every navigation is refused, messages carry a per-load token both ways, file access and debugging are off. The bridge exchanges `init`, commands, `state`, mention queries and the HTML; a page that was not changed is never written back.
  - **The editor page:** a full-screen page over the task or project, opened from an Edit button by the description in the detail sheet ("Add description" when empty) and from a Description row on the task and project edit pages, not for archived projects. A **native toolbar** above the keyboard (bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, code, heading levels, bullet, numbered and check lists, quote, code block, link, table menu, divider, undo, redo, clear formatting) shows the active formatting; links are set in a native sheet; `@` opens a native list of the project's people (the locally known ones offline). Save is explicit; leaving with changes asks; the page follows the app's theme, dark mode, text size and language. An unsaved edit is kept as a draft when the app goes to the background and offered again next time. When the description changed underneath while editing, Save asks: Overwrite, Reload or Keep editing.
  - **Checklists ticked in place:** a tap on a checkbox in the detail sheet replaces only that item's `data-checked` value (and the input's `checked` in TipTap's exact shape) in the HTML the view was built from; it is queued through the outbox, so it works offline, with a haptic and an announcement; a tap on a view that is no longer current is refused. Comment checklists stay read-only (step 27).
  - **No silent overwrite:** a queued description change remembers the description it started from and, for ticks, which items it ticked (by `data-task-id`, else position and text). When it is sent, the app compares that with the server's current description (v1 from the read it makes anyway, v2 with one read): unchanged → sent as queued; changed and only ticks → the ticks are applied again to the server's description; changed after an edit → a sync problem "The description was changed on the web too" with **Compare versions**, **Keep my version** and **Use the web version**. Project descriptions are saved online with the same check and the same question.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **The web's own editor code:** AGPL; and its popups and highlighting are not needed for the stored HTML.
  - **A native rich-text editor:** it would have to reproduce TipTap's schema and serialisation exactly, which only TipTap itself does.
  - **Autosave as on the web:** an explicit Save fits the app's edit pages and avoids writes while typing offline.
  - **Last write wins for descriptions:** a tick on the phone would erase a paragraph written on the web.
- **Consequences:**
  - Descriptions are edited with the web's formatting and saved in the web's form, offline too; ticking a checklist is one tap.
  - The app grows by the WebView plugin (which imports the legacy Material library, [D2](#d2-material_ui-and-cupertino_ui)) and a 0.5 MB page; Node is needed only to rebuild that page.
  - On v2 a web change landing between the check read and the write is still overwritten; the server offers no preconditions.
  - Not built: image upload, paste and drop (step 26), editing comments and their checklists (step 27), Markdown paste, slash commands, code highlighting, collaborative editing; the hardening review of the WebView is step 35. An in-app licences page that lists `NOTICES.md` and `packages/vikunja_logic/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` ([D51](#d51-one-filter-engine-on-the-device-with-the-servers-semantics-saved-filter-definitions-synced-on-change-and-managed-online-a-filter-builder-offline-search)) is still missing.
  - Tests: the Node tests (the 123 oracle vectors, the schema order, the bridge protocol with tokens, task-link rules, images untouched, the built page's policy); the tick and rebase helpers with fuzzing; the outbox, sender and conflict paths; the project description check; widget tests through a fake bridge and a fake WebView platform (toolbar, Save, leaving, drafts, mentions, the lockdown settings, entry points, ticks, the conflict sheet); the accessibility smoke test. Contract tests on both API versions: a description round trip, a tick round trip, a web change between queueing and sending (ticks rebased, an edit becomes a conflict, both answers), and the project description check. Checked on the Android emulator: a checklist item ticked with the server following, the editor showing every kind of content and saving an addition in the web's normalised form (a software-rendered emulator was too slow for the WebView; with host GPU rendering it ran smoothly).

## D53: Attachments uploaded through the outbox in their own lane, opened, shared and saved through the platform, cover and delete online, and an own Android share target

*Accepted 2026-10-20, step 26. Extends the outbox of [D34](#d34-outbox-local-first-writes-serial-sender-coalescing-retry-classes-sync-interplay-pending-fields-win-until-sent-pending-deletes-hidden-v1-full-replace-emulation) and [D35](#d35-creates-through-the-outbox-temporary-negative-ids-with-remap-createupdatedelete-coalescing-lost-response-recovery-by-title-within-a-1-h-window) by a second kind of write, turns the read-only attachments of [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme) into managed ones, uses the online pattern of [D48](#d48-bucket-management-online-applied-to-the-local-board-at-once-from-a-column-menu-with-a-guard-against-the-servers-orphaned-tasks) for cover and delete, and lets the editor of [D52](#d52-descriptions-edited-in-a-bundled-tiptap-page-in-a-locked-down-webview-with-a-native-toolbar-checklists-ticked-in-place-no-silent-overwrite-of-a-description-changed-on-the-web) insert images. The iOS share extension with the App Group of [D15](#d15-app-id-devcoderoftimevikunja) comes in the iOS phase.*

- **Context:**
  - Attachments could only be looked at; nothing could be added, and other apps could not share into Rurana.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0, both API versions, probed on 2026-10-20; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **Upload:** v1 `PUT` / v2 `POST /tasks/{id}/attachments`, multipart field `files`; the answer `{errors, success}` may hold `null` lists, and per-file errors carry no name. The limit is `max_file_size` (a config string, enforced rounded down to whole MiB); one byte over gives a per-file 4012 inside a success, a body over the limit plus 2 MiB a 413 with 4035 or a broken connection. Any type is accepted and sniffed; names keep unicode, lose directories.
    - **Download** needs the Bearer header, supports ranges; previews are PNGs that ignore EXIF orientation. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): on 2.7.1 previews are JPEGs, PNGs only with transparency, and a file without a preview is 422 code 4038; EXIF orientation there is not checked.* **An upload never bumps `task.updated`**, a delete does.
    - **Cover:** deleting the cover attachment leaves `cover_image_attachment_id` dangling, and every later v2 update of the task fails with 4020 until the cover is cleared.
    - Archived projects refuse uploads, deletes and covers (3008); read-only shares may download only; `task_attachments_enabled=false` hides all routes. Description images are `<img data-src=".../api/v2/tasks/{t}/attachments/{a}" src="#" id="tiptap-image-{t}-{a}">`.
  - The share-receiving plugins for Flutter store files under names the sending app chooses, read `file://` paths and need CocoaPods; the "open file" plugins add permissions or expose app folders.
- **Decision:**
  - **Uploads through the outbox, in their own lane.** "Add" in a task's Attachments section picks files (an own Android picker that copies off the main thread with a cap; `file_selector` on iOS), photos or the camera (`image_picker`). Each file is checked against the smaller of the server's limit and the app's ceiling of 50 MiB, moved into the account's uploads folder under a random name and queued as one `uploadAttachment` operation, offline too. A second sender loop sends uploads beside the main queue, so edits are not held up; one file per request, with timeouts from one to four minutes by size; behind an unsent create it follows the new id; an answer that got lost is recognised by name, size and uploader within an hour; three timeouts in a row, a file too large, a task gone or archived become sync problems (Discard deletes the stored copy, a lost copy offers only Discard); background runs leave uploads alone. Pending uploads show as rows with Cancel.
  - **Per attachment:** Open (images in the viewer; other files downloaded into the cache and handed to another app through a FileProvider), Share, Save to device (the system's "save as"), Make cover or Remove cover, Delete with the web's confirmation. Cover and delete are online and refresh the details; deleting the cover clears it first. Downloads stream to disk with the Bearer header for the account's own server only, resume, can be cancelled, are named from the sanitised `file.name` and are purged per account and after three days.
  - **No dangling cover:** an update answered with 4020 is sent again once with the cover cleared.
  - **Images in descriptions:** the editor's Image button (online, not for archived projects) uploads the picked or photographed image at once and inserts it in the web's form; the page keeps showing a labelled placeholder.
  - **Share to Rurana on Android:** an own pass-through activity accepts text and files from other apps, forwards them to the app's one activity, which takes a share once (never again from Recents or after a restore); only another app's `content://` files are read (no `file://`, not the app's own), copied off the main thread under random names with the size cap; a share without permission for a file falls back to the files it may read, else to the text. The app waits for its start and first sync, asks for the account when there are several, then offers **New task** (title from the subject or first line, the rest as description with links, project, files) or **Add to task** (local search; files queued, text appended to the description with its base, D52). It all works offline through the outbox.
  - **Gating:** no uploads, deletes or covers in archived projects or when the server has attachments off; downloads always.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **A share-receiving plugin:** unsafe file names, `file://` reads and CocoaPods; a small own activity is safer and fits the app's other pass-through activities.
  - **Uploads in the main queue:** a large file would hold every edit back for minutes.
  - **Several files per request:** errors carry no names and the body limit counts all files together.
  - **Storing attachment lists offline now:** the server does not bump `updated` on uploads, so a stored list would be stale; the details read online stays the source, pending uploads come from the outbox.
- **Consequences:**
  - Files, photos and shared content reach a task from the phone, offline too; files open, share and save like in any Android app.
  - The app grows by `image_picker`, `share_plus` and `file_selector` (iOS) plus own Kotlin; files up to 50 MiB are held in memory while they upload (the native HTTP adapter buffers bodies), so no real progress bar.
  - Not built: storing attachment metadata offline (schema v8 later), real upload progress, attachments on comments (step 27), the iOS share extension (iOS phase; the camera and photo usage texts are in place), HEIC conversion, recovering a photo when Android kills the app while the camera is open. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): the owner put the iOS share extension after 1.0, so iOS takes files only through the in-app pickers, which since 37b take the location out of a photo or video from the photo picker or the camera before it is queued.* *Note 2026-11-02, step 37c ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores)): on Android the photo picker's photos and videos, camera shots and every copy of a share now lose their location before they are queued or ready, where the removal can; a file picked as a file is passed on unchanged.*
  - Since 2026-10-28 a pick or "save as" open while Android relaunches the activity (system colours, contrast, wallpaper) keeps its result: the app's document picker and "save as" keep the waiting call with the kept engine and answer it from the relaunched activity, and the camera and the photo picker read image_picker's lost-data cache once the app is back (every 500 ms, up to 15 s). Known gaps: image_picker_android 0.8.13+25 drops a lost result that holds only an error, so a photo-picker pick that fails after a relaunch ends like a cancel after 15 s; a photo-picker pick whose copy into the app takes longer than 15 s (several GB, or media the provider must first download) also counts as a cancel, its copies stay in image_picker's cache directory, and a pick started meanwhile may receive them; a process that dies during a pick still loses it.
  - Tests: the ports on fixtures recorded from 2.7.0 (multipart bodies, `null` lists, every too-large path, downloads with resume), the file-name sanitiser; the outbox and the upload lane (each trap, overtaking, the create remap, adoption, timeouts, cleanup), the 4020 recovery, the downloads cache; widget tests for the section, menus, pending rows, the editor's Image button and the share sheet; the accessibility smoke test. Contract tests on both API versions: uploads incl. a unicode name and an empty file, a 21 MiB file refused, a download byte for byte with a resumed range, delete, the cover and its 4020, archived; in the app an upload queued offline and drained, and an edit after the cover was deleted on the server. Checked on the Android emulator: a text with a link shared from another app became a task with the web's link HTML; a `file://` share pointing at the app's own data was refused; a photo and a text file were picked, uploaded, set as cover, the cover deleted cleanly, and the text file opened in another app.

## D54: Account settings and profile edited in the app with writes that never wipe the web's settings, the avatar managed, password and email left to the web

*Accepted 2026-10-21, step 30. Makes the settings read since [D13](#d13-support-floor-230) and [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner) editable; keeps [D14](#d14-browser-sign-in-only) (the app never handles passwords); uses the photo pickers of [D53](#d53-attachments-uploaded-through-the-outbox-in-their-own-lane-opened-shared-and-saved-through-the-platform-cover-and-delete-online-and-an-own-android-share-target) for the avatar and fixes the avatars of [D43](#d43-task-lists-from-the-local-database-like-the-webs-a-view-snapshot-for-positions-and-server-only-filters-the-overviews-saved-filter-downloaded-by-the-sync-upcoming-with-the-web-presets-and-a-pinned-overdue-part) for the own account. Steps 27 to 29 come later, at the owner's choice.*

- **Context:**
  - Settings could only be read; the name shown came from the sign-in and never changed; the own avatar was shown nowhere.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0 on both API versions, and 2.3 and 2.4 in throw-away containers, probed on 2026-10-20; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **Writes:** v1 `POST` and v2 `PUT /user/settings/general` replace everything — an omitted field is reset and an omitted `frontend_settings` wipes every web setting; only v2 `PATCH` (2.7.0) merges deeply, also inside `frontend_settings`. `GET /user` carries the settings, the name, `is_local_user`, `auth_provider`, `pending_email` and `updated`, but not the email.
    - **Validation:** week start 0–6, the overdue time `H:MM`, a loadable timezone (1025), a writable, not archived default project (3017); name and language are not checked.
    - **Avatar:** providers default, initials, gravatar, marble and upload; an upload (PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP, TIFF, WebP; ≤ 50 MP) makes `upload` the provider; avatar answers are `no-store`, the change signal is the user's `updated`.
    - **Password and email:** changing the password ends every session, our OAuth refresh token included; the email change needs the password and a confirmation when the server sends mail; OIDC and LDAP users cannot change either and get their name from their provider at each login.
- **Decision:**
  - **Account settings, apart from device settings.** The Account tab opens with a profile header (own avatar, name, @username, server) and an "Account settings" row; the notification and appearance switches follow under "On this device". The Account settings page shows, in the web's order: Profile (name, avatar), Password and email, Defaults (default project, default view, Quick Add Magic mode, minimum priority, the Overview's saved filter, first day of the week), Time and language (timezone with the zones matching the device's offset suggested, the server language with a note that the app follows the device, date and time formats with a live example), Email notifications (reminders, the daily overdue summary and its time), Privacy (findable by name or email) and Appearance (colour scheme). Changes save at once, online; offline the page is read-only; refusals show at their field. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the page has no Appearance section, and its row on the Account tab no longer names the colour scheme, which is the Theme row of the tab's Appearance section, saved through the same code; on the tab, Sync follows the Accounts section under a heading of its own, and Server comes after Appearance.*
  - **Writes that never wipe:** on 2.7.0 a v2 merge-patch carries only the changed fields (web keys inside `frontend_settings`); older servers get a full write built from a fresh read of the raw settings, keeping every key the app does not know — never from the local store, which keeps only what the app uses. After each write the app reads `GET /user` again, stores settings and profile at once and syncs quietly; writes are sent one at a time. The name reaches the account registry, so the header, the switcher and avatars with initials change at once (also when another isolate's sync saw it first).
  - **Avatar page:** Default, Initials, Gravatar, Marble and "Your photo" (picked or taken, uploaded, which also selects it); the caches are evicted and every avatar of the account fetched again; a note says that the app draws initials where the server sends an SVG. The own avatar now shows in the Account tab and the account switcher.
  - **Password and email through the web:** for local users the two rows open the web's own pages in the browser; OIDC and LDAP users see that their provider manages them, and the rows stay hidden until the profile is known. A pending email change is shown. Returning from the password page, the app checks its session at once; a changed password has ended it, and the existing "Sign in again" flow takes over with queued changes kept.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Native password and email forms:** they would make the app handle passwords, against D14; the web already does it, and our session ends anyway.
  - **Writing the stored settings back:** the store keeps only the keys the app uses, so a full write from it would erase the web's other settings.
  - **Only the 2.7.0 merge-patch:** servers from 2.3 to 2.6 would be left read-only.
- **Consequences:**
  - The account's settings follow the user from the phone to the web and back; the web's own keys survive every write.
  - A settings change costs two requests on 2.7.0 and three on older servers.
  - Not built: TOTP, sessions, API tokens, CalDAV, data export and account deletion (step 31); native password and email forms; the web's frontend keys the app has no use for are kept but not shown; `default_due_time` is kept too but not yet applied, so a date-only pick still takes the breakpoint time of [D35](#d35-creates-through-the-outbox-temporary-negative-ids-with-remap-createupdatedelete-coalescing-lost-response-recovery-by-title-within-a-1-h-window)'s editing rules.
  - Tests: the domain types; the write port on fixtures recorded from 2.7.0 with "unknown keys survive" on all three paths, the avatar calls, the error mapping; the repository, the registry's display name across isolates, cache eviction; widget tests for the Account tab, the settings page, the avatar page and the browser rows; the accessibility smoke test. Contract tests on both API versions: every field, clearing, refusals leaving the settings unchanged, the avatar provider and upload; in the app a change leaving the server's raw settings equal except the changed field. Checked on the Android emulator: the name set and shown in the header and on avatars, the colour scheme set to dark with only that key written, both restored, and the avatar provider switched and back.

## D55: Security and integration settings: two-factor status, sessions and tokens in the app, a new token shown once, export and deletion through the web

*Accepted 2026-10-21, step 31. Keeps [D14](#d14-browser-sign-in-only) (the app never handles passwords or TOTP codes) and extends the Account settings page of [D54](#d54-account-settings-and-profile-edited-in-the-app-with-writes-that-never-wipe-the-webs-settings-the-avatar-managed-password-and-email-left-to-the-web); keeps secrets out of storage and logs as [D24](#d24-tokens-in-secure-storage-readable-after-the-first-unlock) does.*

- **Context:**
  - Two-factor authentication, sessions, API tokens, CalDAV, the data export and account deletion could only be managed on the web.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0 on both API versions, 2.3 and 2.4 in throw-away containers, mail flows on a throw-away server with a mail sink, probed on 2026-10-21; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **Our OAuth access token reaches every endpoint of this area**; API tokens reach none of them.
    - **A password or TOTP code is needed** to turn two-factor on (a code) or off (the password; also the only way to cancel a setup), to request or download an export and to request or cancel the deletion (the password, for local users); confirming a deletion needs the mailed link. Turning two-factor on ends every session, ours included. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): on 2.7.1 turning two-factor on needs the password and a code, turning it off or cancelling a setup the password and a code, and turning it on ends our session at once; the note under the row says that newer servers ask for a code.*
    - **Sessions:** a list with the User-Agent at creation, the IP, created and last active (moved only by a refresh); the current session is the `sid` of our access token; ending one stops its refresh at once, its access token works up to 10 minutes; there is no "end all others" call.
    - **API tokens:** `tk_` plus 40 hex characters, shown only in the create response; permissions per group from `/routes`, which differs by server; the expiry is required but a past date is accepted. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): 2.7.1 renamed five permissions (`notifications.create` to `notifications.mark_all_as_read`, `projects.views_buckets_put` to `projects.views_buckets_create`, `projects.views_buckets_post` to `projects.views_buckets_update`, `projects.views_buckets_tasks` to `projects.views_buckets_tasks_update`, `projects.background_get` removed), refuses the old names (400 code 14002) and migrates existing tokens; the task preset takes the move's new name where `/routes` offers it. Deleting a token that is gone answers 404 code 14017, which the app takes as deleted.* **CalDAV tokens:** 64 characters, shown once, no title or expiry; CalDAV takes the username with a CalDAV token, an API token with the CalDAV permission, or the password of a local user without two-factor.
    - **Export:** status with size and expiry (7 days), readiness only by email. **Deletion:** `GET /user` shows the scheduled date (confirmation plus 72 hours); v2 ignores `user_deletion_enabled`; without mail the confirmation never arrives, which the app cannot detect.
    - Servers before 2.7 send the two-factor secret with the status even while it is on.
- **Decision:**
  - **Everything that needs a password or code goes to the web's own page** in the browser, as in D54: two-factor setup and turning it off, export request and download, deletion request and cancel. Everything else is native. The app shows no password or code field, no QR code and never the two-factor secret.
  - **Account settings sections:** Security (two-factor status On, Off or "Setup not finished", only where the server offers it and for local users, opening the web with a note that turning it on signs out everywhere; Sessions with their count), Integrations (API tokens with their count; CalDAV where the server offers it), Your data (the export with its status), Delete account (last, only where the server allows it, a dialog with the consequences, the mail confirmation and the three days, then the web). *Note 2026-11-02, step 37c ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores)): the row now shows on every server, as Apple's Guideline 5.1.1(v) asks of an app that lets users create an account; where the server does not allow deletion it reads "Not allowed by this server" and opens a dialog that says to ask whoever runs the server, and nothing else. A deletion already scheduled still opens the web page to cancel it.* A scheduled deletion shows at the top of the page and below the Account tab's profile header with "Cancel deletion".
  - **Sessions page:** a device line read from the User-Agent (Rurana with version and system, browser and system, the raw text otherwise), IP, last active, "This device" on our own session (which cannot be ended there); ending one or all other sessions asks first; ending all goes one by one, stops at the first failure and says how many ended.
  - **API tokens page:** the list with expiry (expired ones in the error colour) and a detail sheet with the permissions and Delete; the form takes a title, an expiry of 30, 60 or 90 days or a picked day (valid through its end, in the device's zone; a past day is refused), and the permissions from `/routes` with the web's four presets and a mixed-state checkbox per group (groups start collapsed). **CalDAV page:** the address from the server root (not the web address) and the username, both with Copy, the password text matching the account (local without two-factor, with two-factor, or a sign-in provider), the token list with a delete confirmation (the web has none).
  - **A new token is shown once:** only in the state of the page that created it — never in a provider that outlives it, the database, the registry, logs or a route — in a selectable monospace text with Copy (Android marks the clip sensitive; the selection menu and the copy shortcut use the same path), with screenshots blocked (`FLAG_SECURE`) while it is visible, and one question when leaving without copying. Log redaction also covers a `secret` key and `otpauth` links.
  - **Two-factor status over http:** not read from servers before 2.7 over an unencrypted connection, because the answer would carry the secret; the row then says so and still opens the web.
  - **Returning from the web's two-factor page** runs the session check at once, so a session ended by turning it on asks to sign in again right away. A deleted account (`GET /user` 404 code 1005) ends the session the same way.
  - **Waiting for a page the app opened:** go_router's push futures never complete once Android reports the route list back, as it does when the app is opened again (flutter/flutter#128122). Every screen that waits for a page it opened — the new pages here, and the description editor's callers since step 25 (the task detail sheet, the task edit page and the project edit page) — now waits for the pushed route's own pop (`pushPage`), which survives that.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **In-app two-factor setup** (QR or secret plus a code field) and **in-app password fields** for export and deletion: against D14; the web already does them.
  - **Native export download and deletion for sign-in provider accounts**, which need no password: a second path for a minority that the web covers for everyone; the download would need a POST download and a new shareable cache folder.
  - **A "never expires" token choice:** the web offers none; the server accepts a far date, so it stays possible on the web's API.
- **Consequences:**
  - The account's security and integrations are managed from the phone except where a password or code is required; one-time secrets never land anywhere but on screen and in the clipboard.
  - Every page here is online only; offline they show their offline state.
  - Not built: native two-factor and password flows (D14), bot users' tokens (step 33), the web's prefilled token links, webhooks, feeds and migration (step 33), refreshing the server's capabilities while signed in (a missing route reads as "not offered"), the sensitive clipboard and screenshot blocking on iOS (iOS phase). *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): the owner chose no screenshot blocking on iOS: a one-time token or a revealed secret hides while the screen is recorded or mirrored instead. The sensitive clipboard of [D66](#d66-a-threat-model-uncaught-errors-redacted-and-never-printed-in-release-builds-the-apps-data-out-of-backups-and-device-transfer-routes-pushed-by-the-platform-taken-only-as-app-scheme-links-widget-links-and-notification-taps-switching-accounts-only-with-a-per-install-key-exported-components-and-dependencies-checked-in-ci) is built, but not yet run.*
  - Tests: domain types; the device description vectors; the port on fixtures recorded from 2.7.0 with secrets scrubbed, every method on both API versions; the repository (current session, ending all others with a partial failure, refusals before a request); widget tests for every section, page, the form, the one-time view (no secret in logs or provider values), the deletion dialog and notes, and the results after the platform reports the route list back (the API tokens page and the settings page, the description editor opened from the sheet, the task edit page and the project edit page); the accessibility smoke test. Contract tests on 2.7.0: sessions with the current one and refresh-stable `sid`, another session ended, tokens created, listed and deleted, a CalDAV token and an API token with the CalDAV permission authenticating a `PROPFIND`, export and two-factor status. Checked on the Android emulator: sessions listed with this device marked and one ended; API tokens created with a preset and a picked day, shown once with screenshots blocked, copied, used against the server and deleted; a CalDAV token created, used for a `PROPFIND` (207) and deleted; the export status ready after a request; the deletion dialog; the two-factor row opening the web and the session check on return. Found and fixed there: lists that did not reload after the app was reopened during the form (the same cause lost the description editor's "Saved" and the sheet's refresh, fixed too and checked on the emulator), and deletes that failed on the server's unmodifiable lists.

## D56: Design language after the owner's mockups: a blue tonal scheme with an amber accent, grouped cards, one task row for every list

*Accepted 2026-10-21, outside the roadmap's steps. Keeps [D2](#d2-material_ui-and-cupertino_ui) (material_ui and cupertino_ui), dynamic colour and the colour scheme of [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner), the task sheet of [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme) and the board rules of [D47](#d47-kanban-board-from-per-view-board-snapshots-moves-and-positions-through-the-outbox-with-the-done-buckets-rules-accessible-drag-and-drop).*

- **Context:** the app was plain Material 3 with default component styles. The owner provided mockups of eight screens (a Material 3 concept for a Vikunja client: Overview, Upcoming, new task sheet, a project's list view and menu, task detail, Kanban, settings) and asked for the app to look like them without copying them blindly and while staying a typical Flutter app.
- **Decision:**
  - **Colour:** the brand scheme (used when the system palette is off or missing, and on iOS) is a tonal scheme with primary roles from the Vikunja blue `#1973FF`, cool grey-blue surfaces and an **amber tertiary** for highlights (favourite stars, high priority). Dynamic colour stays on by default and replaces the whole scheme; every component reads scheme roles.
  - **Surfaces and structure:** grouped content sits in 12 dp cards (`surfaceContainerLow`) with inset dividers; section headers are primary-coloured with an optional count; settings lists have a 24 dp outlined leading icon on every tile and a divider between sections; switches show a check in the thumb (Material only); short exclusive choices are segmented buttons.
  - **One task row for every list** (Overview, Upcoming, project lists, smart lists, search, labels): a square checkbox, the title, one wrapping meta line (priority flag, due date with a calendar icon in the overdue/today/later tone, the identifier in a monospace face, the project with its colour dot, label pills in the server's colours, checklist count), the percent-done bar, an avatar stack and the favourite star. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): no identifier; the project after a folder in its colour, muted without one.*
  - **Screens:** the Overview starts with a search pill and a greeting (date, "Good morning, …", the tasks left for today, the bell); Upcoming has the range presets in its app bar, a day strip to pick one day and a timeline of days; a project shows its done/total progress, its views as tabs, "Show done" and the sort as chips and a grouped overflow menu; Kanban columns are cards with a count badge (red over the limit) and elevated cards; the task sheet has a project chip, a large title, a "Mark as done" button and the properties in an attribute card; the new task sheet has a headline, a project field and filter chips; the Account tab ends with "Sign out" and the version. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the task sheet's project chip sits in its header, where the identifier was; the new task sheet has the due date and a priority as two rows of chips and expands for more options; the Account tab has one Appearance section with the theme, and Server below it.*
- **Not copied:** a Teams tab (teams are step 28; Account stays the fourth tab), an inline Quick Add field and a project grid on the Overview (the button and the composer stay the way to add), a checkbox on Kanban cards (the done bucket keeps its rules), the task detail as a full page (it stays a sheet). Kept although the mockup has none: the favourite star on rows. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): since the owner's review, not copied either: the task identifier the mockups show in the row, on the Kanban card and in the task's header; a project's colour dot (in the project field and the task's project chip), where every project now has a folder in its colour; and the new task sheet's row of reminder, repeat and attachment buttons, whose reminders and repeat are under More options (the app's own `* + @ !` toolbar, which the mockups never had, is gone too).*
- **Consequences:**
  - Every list and settings page shares the same widgets (`SectionCard`, `TaskRow`, `PriorityFlag`, `DueBadge`, `LabelPill`, `AvatarStack`, `CountBadge`, `SearchPill`, `SettingsSection`); new screens use them.
  - Navigation labels on Android scale text up to 1.15× so that the bold selected label fits in German at 200 % text.
  - `tool/seed_demo_data.py` fills an emptied test server with the demo projects and tasks used for checks on the emulator.
  - Tests: the theme in light and dark (scheme roles, scrolled app bars), the shared widgets, the task row's meta line and its layouts, the Overview header and counts, the day strip and timeline, the project progress counts and tabs, the board's count badge, the task sheet's button and attribute card, the Account tab's sign-out, and the accessibility smoke test at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de. Checked on the Android emulator with the brand scheme and the system palette, light and dark: Overview, Upcoming, Projects, a project's list and Kanban, its menu, the task sheet, the new task sheet and the Account tab, and ticking a task from a row.

## D57: Comments written in the app, online, with a draft kept on the device; reactions on tasks and comments; comment counts and the unread dot on rows

*Accepted 2026-10-21, step 27. Extends the read-only comments and reactions of [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme) and edits comments in the editor page of [D52](#d52-descriptions-edited-in-a-bundled-tiptap-page-in-a-locked-down-webview-with-a-native-toolbar-checklists-ticked-in-place-no-silent-overwrite-of-a-description-changed-on-the-web); styled after [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list).*

- **Context:**
  - Comments and reactions could only be read, the comments section appeared only when a task already had comments, and rows did not show comments at all.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0 on both API versions, probed on 2026-10-21; the web read at `e7d7f17`):
    - **Comments:** v1 `PUT`/`POST`/`DELETE`, v2 (2.4.0 and later) `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE` on `/tasks/{t}/comments`; oldest first, newest first with `order_by=desc`, 50 per page. Writing needs write access; only the author may edit or delete; archived projects refuse every write (3008). A comment write bumps the task's `updated` and marks the task unread for everyone else; an edit's answer lacks the author and the creation time.
    - **Format:** the web's TipTap HTML: paragraphs, mentions as `<mention-user data-id="{username}" data-label="{name}" data-mention-suggestion-char="@">`, replies as `<blockquote data-comment-id>` with the quoted mentions turned into text. The server stores and returns HTML unchanged, hostile markup included.
    - **Reactions:** add with v2 `POST` (v1 `PUT`) `/{tasks|comments}/{id}/reactions`, remove with `POST …/reactions/delete`; any text of up to 20 code points; needs write access; never bumps `updated` and notifies nobody, so the sync cannot see them.
    - **Unread:** one flag per task and user, set by someone else's new comment, cleared only by mark-read; with `comment_count` an expand on every task read since 1.0.
- **Decision:**
  - **Online writes, a draft kept offline:** comments and reactions are written online and applied to the open sheet at once, then a quiet refresh. The composer's text, its mentions and the reply it quotes are kept per account and task on the device (the 50 newest per account, deleted on sign-out), so nothing typed is lost; offline the Send button is off with a note.
  - **A composer pinned under the task sheet** with @-mention suggestions from the project's people, sending the web's HTML byte for byte (escaped paragraphs, mention nodes, the reply quote rebuilt from what the editor schema keeps, so a quoted comment's hostile markup is not re-posted). The comments section is always there where the server offers comments ("No comments yet"); read-only and archived tasks get no composer.
  - **Each comment:** avatar, name, time, "edited", its reactions and a menu with Reply, Copy link (the web's `#comment-{id}` link) and, for one's own comments, Edit (the full editor page with mentions) and Delete (asked first; the paging adjusts so "Load more" skips nothing).
  - **Reactions** on the task and on each comment: chips toggle one's own reaction, long press says who reacted, "Add reaction" offers eight quick emoji and a field for any other. Read-only users see the chips disabled.
  - **Counts and unread on rows:** schema v8 stores `comment_count` and `is_unread`, which every stored task read now expands (the migration forces one full sync to fill them); rows and board cards show the count, in primary with a dot when unread. Opening a task marks it read and clears the dot at once.
  - **Live and linked:** an open sheet reads its details and loaded comments again when the task changes on the server; notifications and inbox entries about a comment open the task at that comment.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Comments through the outbox:** the thread itself can only be read online; a queued comment needs lost-answer recovery for little gain over a kept draft.
  - **The rich editor inside the sheet for new comments:** too heavy for a quick reply; formatting stays available when editing.
  - **Syncing reactions:** they never change `updated`, so the sync could not keep them right.
- **Consequences:**
  - A task read on the web or on another device keeps its unread dot here until the task changes or the daily full resync.
  - A comment needs one request; editing on v1 two (a read first).
  - go_router drops a push's `extra` that is not JSON once Android reports the route list back; the comment editor's route therefore keeps the comment it was pushed with.
  - Not built: a formatting toolbar in the composer, images in comments, reactions for link-share guests (step 32), comment search.
  - Tests: the comment HTML vectors (each also loaded into the bundled editor and serialised back unchanged), the write ports on fixtures recorded from 2.7.0, the schema v8 migration and the sync's expands, the draft store, the controllers' refresh and write ordering, widget tests for the composer, replies, edits, deletes, reactions, counts and the unread dot, opening at a comment, the keyboard cases in the accessibility smoke test. Contract tests on 2.7.0 with two users: create, edit and delete as author, another user refused, reactions on a task and a comment, unread set and cleared, counts in a view list. Checked on the Android emulator: unread count and dot after another user's mention, the inbox opening the task at that comment, a reaction, a reply with a mention stored exactly as the web writes it (the other user notified as mentioned), an edit in the editor page and "edited", a delete, another user's new comment appearing in the open sheet, the dot gone after reading, and a draft typed offline kept across closing the sheet and sent once online.

## D58: Server HTML parsed within budgets counted inside package:html; a document past one ends with an ellipsis

*Accepted 2026-10-21, a hardening task after step 27. Bounds the parser of [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme) and the reply quotes of [D57](#d57-comments-written-in-the-app-online-with-a-draft-kept-on-the-device-reactions-on-tasks-and-comments-comment-counts-and-the-unread-dot-on-rows).*

- **Context:**
  - Descriptions and comments are HTML that the server stores unchanged, hostile markup included. The app parses them on the UI isolate (the task sheet, comment previews and reply quotes, checklist taps, the description editor's check for an empty comment) and in the outbox's checklist rebase, which also runs in headless isolates.
  - package:html 0.15.7 has several paths whose time grows with the square of the input. Measured on a laptop:
    - Most tokens walk the stack of open elements: 50,000 nested `div`s took 30 s, 20,000 nested lists 20 s.
    - Markup misplaced in a table goes before the table after a search through its siblings: 82 s for 950 KB.
    - The adoption agency moves a block's children one by one, searching for each: 65 s for 1 MB.
    - Formatting elements reopened before each text, or cloned by the adoption agency, multiply the elements and their attributes: 14 million elements came from 950 KB, 500 KB of attributes ran out of memory, and one long link reopened in every paragraph made a reply quote of gigabytes.
    - Each formatting element is compared, attribute by attribute, with up to 256 earlier ones in the list of active formatting elements, and markers that table cells leave behind make that list grow, while the adoption agency searches it from the start: 2 s to 9 s per MB.
    - A numeric character reference past 64 bits (`&#99999999999999999999;`) throws, so 26 bytes broke the task sheet for everyone.
  - The converter added its own: removing TipTap's blank text nodes and moving nested lists one at a time (24 s and 13 s), and joining adjacent text pairwise (9 s). The composer trimmed the end of a web address in quadratic time (17 s for 40,000 dots).
  - A first guard modelled the tree builder on the raw text and blanked tags past a depth. Two review rounds bypassed it (CDATA outside foreign content, cells outside a table, foreign content, its own blanking) and found it blanking legitimate markup. A fixed depth limit in its place cut off ordinary mail threads of 40 replies.
- **Decision:**
  - `parseBoundedHtml` (`vikunja_logic`) parses with package:html itself, through subclasses of its tokenizer and tree builder (implementation imports: the package offers no hooks). They count the real parser's work against budgets:
    - the entries walked in the stack of open elements (once per token) and in the list of active formatting elements (per formatting element, times its attributes, and per round of the adoption agency): 2^24;
    - the elements created, the reopened and cloned ones included: 2^18;
    - the characters of their attributes, copies included: 2^24;
    - the sibling positions searched to move nodes, for foster parenting and for the children the adoption agency actually moves: 2^28;
    - at most 1,024 elements open at once, for package:html's recursive serialiser.
  - The parse stops between tokens, before an element is inserted or before a round of the adoption agency, never while a node is out of the tree. Rich text shows what was parsed until there, then a paragraph `…`, and `RichDocument.truncated` is set; checklist items before the cut tick as before. A reply quotes such a comment up to the cut, then `…`, and its preview ends with ` …`.
  - Below the budgets the result is package:html's own. The converter still flattens what is nested deeper than 96 elements to its text.
  - A numeric reference past 64 bits reads as U+FFFD, as package:html reads one past U+10FFFF.
  - The converter removes blank text nodes, moves lists nested in a list and joins adjacent text each in one pass; the composer trims the end of a web address in one pass.
  - The outbox's checklist rebase parses the description once for all queued taps and applies them from the last item to the first. When an item's change could reach the next item, which only malformed HTML allows, it parses again after each tap as before.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Modelling the tree builder on the raw text** (the first guard): it is never exact by construction, it was bypassed and it blanked too much.
  - **A fixed depth limit:** the cost is the depth walked per token, not the depth itself; a limit low enough to be cheap cut off mail threads.
  - **Parsing in a background isolate with a time limit:** every synchronous caller would have to become asynchronous; it remains possible as an addition.
  - **Keeping the rest of a document past a budget as plain text:** it needs a second pass that knows which content stays hidden, for documents that are hostile or broken.
- **Consequences:**
  - A document past a budget shows only its beginning in the app. The rest stays on the server and in the web, and its checklist items cannot be ticked in the app.
  - An update of package:html can break the subclasses at compile time; the tests pin package:html's output below the budgets.
  - Of 90 hostile and ordinary shapes of up to 1 MB, measured on a laptop, the slowest takes 1.0 s (end tags deep in the stack, through the parser as through the reply quote) and every other at most 0.7 s, against 0.13–0.48 s for 1 MB of plain paragraphs, breaks or list items.
  - Tests:
    - package:html's own output for every recorded description and comment and for deep, misnested and large documents of the real world (a mail thread of 60 replies, unclosed bold headings and fonts, a link, font or bold closed out of order, a link opened inside a link, a 1 MB editor document);
    - each budget at its edge, the stop outside an adoption agency's move, numeric references against package:html;
    - the truncated document with its checklist, its reply quote (a plain-text one keeping its paragraph) and preview;
    - 27 hostile inputs of up to 1 MB, through the parser and the reply quote, each under 3 s and truncated or not as expected; among them every bypass of the first guard and every path above;
    - the address trimming and the checklist rebase against the former code, the rebase also with 100 taps on a 1 MB checklist;
    - a mutation check in which switching off any budget, hook or fix fails the tests.

## D60: Labels managed online and applied locally; teams, project shares and link shares online only; the project permission stored and every screen shaped by it

*Accepted 2026-10-26, step 28. Adds label management to the label creation of [D36](#d36-labels-and-assignees-as-id-set-deltas-in-the-task-update-sent-through-their-endpoints-after-a-read-before-write-diff-reminders-as-the-whole-list-label-creation-online-only) and [D38](#d38-quick-add-the-web-grammar-in-vikunja_logic-with-d19s-fixes-weekday-and-ordinal-dates-at-the-default-time-and-an-unknown-project-kept-in-the-title-a-live-composer-labels-created-online-and-assignees-looked-up-on-submit-the-task-through-the-outbox), which stays online only; manages labels like the columns and projects of [D48](#d48-bucket-management-online-applied-to-the-local-board-at-once-from-a-column-menu-with-a-guard-against-the-servers-orphaned-tasks) and [D50](#d50-project-management-and-views-online-applied-to-the-local-database-at-once-from-the-project-screens-menu), and teams and shares online only like the settings pages of [D55](#d55-security-and-integration-settings-two-factor-status-sessions-and-tokens-in-the-app-a-new-token-shown-once-export-and-deletion-through-the-web); stores the permission through the project diff of [D33](#d33-incremental-sync-watermark-on-updated-with-a-120-s-overlap-keyset-over-updated-id-delete-detection-by-per-project-count-check-and-id-reconcile-daily-full-resync); styled after [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list).*

- **Context:**
  - Labels could only be created, online, from the label picker and quick add. Teams and a project's shares could only be managed on the web. The app did not know the user's permission on a project, so it offered every change and worded the server's refusal afterwards.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0 on both API versions with fresh users, probed on 2026-10-26; the server and the web read at 2.7.1):
    - **Labels:** v1 `PUT`/`POST`/`DELETE`, v2 (2.4.0 and later) `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`. v1's update and v2's `PUT` replace title, description and colour together; v2's merge-patch keeps the rest and answers 304 when nothing changes. Only the creator, or the owner of the creator's bot, may change or delete a label; lists carry no `max_permission`. An update of a deleted label is a 403 like someone else's, a delete of one a 404 code 8002. A search that is a number, or numbers with commas, is an id lookup, so a label titled "2026" is never found by its title. Changing or deleting a label bumps no task's `updated`; from 2.7.0 a delete also takes the label off every task. Before 2.7.1 any colour of up to 7 characters is stored.
    - **Teams:** the members come only inside the team, and the create answer has none. The admin toggle flips the flag whatever the body says and answers someone outside the team with `200 {id: 0}`. The server lacks guards: the only admin may leave while others stay, or demote themselves, and nobody can manage the team afterwards; only the last member is refused (6006). v1's update and v2's `PUT` reset an `is_public` they are not sent, and an empty description is not stored (the old text stays). Teams from a sign-in provider (`external_id`) refuse only member removal (6010), and their name ends in ` (Provider)`. Public teams count only with `public_teams_enabled`. The user search finds the exact username in any case and a name or an email only for people who allow it, without paging and never with the email.
    - **Sharing:** only a project admin changes user, team and link shares; the user and team lists need read access, link shares admin (from 2.7.0). Change answers have `id: 0` and a zero `created`. Since 2.6.0 a team the reader cannot see comes without its name. Link shares cannot be changed (405); the hash is 40 characters and is the link's long-lived secret; the web's link is `{frontend_url}share/{hash}/auth?view={id}`. Without `link_sharing_enabled` the routes do not exist (404). From 2.7.0 whoever loses access also loses their task assignments, subscriptions and webhooks in those projects, which bumps no `updated`.
    - **The permission:** `max_permission` (0 read, 1 write, 2 admin) comes with every project of `GET /projects` only with `expand=permissions` (since 1.0, so 2.3 has it), else null (possibly 0 before 2.6); a v1 single read has it in the `x-max-permission` header. A share change bumps the shared project's `updated` but not its children's, whose inherited permission changes too; a change of team membership or a link share bumps nothing.
- **Decision:**
  - **Owner decisions of 2026-10-26:** Labels and Teams open from a new "Organise" section on the Account tab, after the account settings and before Accounts. QR codes come from the `qr` package 4.0.0 (pure Dart, BSD-3-Clause, depends only on `meta`), the step's one new dependency. Offline label creation is deferred again, so the online-only creation of D36 and D38 stays.
  - **Labels, managed online and applied locally:**
    - A change or a delete goes to the server at once, and the answer is stored right away: a change replaces the stored label; a delete removes the row, which takes the label off every task through the foreign key, and forgets its queued adds in the outbox, in one transaction, as the sync's purge does. A quiet `refreshAfterWrite` follows. A failure changes nothing, except an update answered "gone" (8002), which removes the label here too.
    - v2 sends a merge-patch of the changed fields only (a 304 is read back); v1 reads the label and posts it back whole with the change on top, so another client's colour or description that the app cannot parse survives. Colours go without `#`; an empty one clears the colour.
    - Only the creator's labels offer Edit and Delete (`created_by` is the user), because lists carry no permission. A label made by the user's bot is not recognised (`User` has no bot owner); the server's refusal is worded instead.
    - A search the server would read as ids pages the whole label list and matches title and description on the device, so quick add and the lost-answer lookup find an existing "2026" instead of creating a second one.
    - Labels page: "Your labels" and "From others" (labels on shared tasks), a search field above 12 labels, "New label" (off while offline), pull to refresh as a sync. The form: a title of up to 250 code points; the web's palette as swatches, "No colour" and a custom hex value, a new label starting with a random palette colour; the description in the editor page of [D52](#d52-descriptions-edited-in-a-bundled-tiptap-page-in-a-locked-down-webview-with-a-native-toolbar-checklists-ticked-in-place-no-silent-overwrite-of-a-description-changed-on-the-web), saved online with nothing compared, as for comments. Delete asks first and says that the label leaves every task. A tap on a label, here or on the task sheet, shows its open tasks.
  - **Teams, online only and never stored**, like the settings pages of D55: read on open and on pull to refresh; a page counts as offline only when its own last request found no connection, before that the sync's state decides; every change reads the team again.
    - Teams page: the member count and the badges Admin, Public (where the server has public teams) and "Managed by {provider}" or "Managed externally". New team: a name and, where enabled, "Public team"; then its page.
    - Team page: the description; for admins of a team that is not external, Edit (the name and the public switch, which always sends the stored value back) and "Edit description" through the editor page; the members with "You", Admin or Member; for admins a row menu with Make admin or Make member and Remove, never on one's own row; "Add member" through the people search (300 ms debounce, the latest query wins, a hint that the exact username always works).
    - Guards the server lacks (`TeamGuards`): nobody demotes or removes themselves from their row; the only admin with other members is asked to make someone else admin first; the last member is offered "Delete team" instead of Leave; external teams offer no edit, add or leave. Leave reads the team first, so the guard runs on its current members.
    - The toggle: an answer with id 0 is settled by reading the team, which gives the member's flag, or `TeamMemberMissing` for someone not in it, because older servers likely answer every toggle that way.
    - v1 updates post name, description and `is_public` from a fresh read; v2 sends a merge-patch. A cleared description is written as the editor's `<p></p>`, which 2.7.0 stores on both versions.
    - A create is read back for its members; when that read fails, the created team is returned with its creator as the only admin, so a retry creates no second team.
    - Leave, Remove and Delete ask first with the access-cleanup notice: on servers from 2.7.0 the three things a person loses in the projects they can no longer reach (task assignments, subscriptions, webhooks), on older ones one sentence. After leaving or deleting, the page goes back to the Teams page and a quiet `refreshAfterWrite` follows; the incremental run reads every project page, so projects reached only through the team go.
    - The server's refusals are worded: already a member (6005), the last member (6006), a team managed by a sign-in provider (6010, in the app's own words, since the web has none), an unknown user (1005), no permission, an invalid name.
  - **Sharing:** "Share" in the first group of a project's menu, for every real project that is not archived, opens `/home/project/:id/share`.
    - People: avatar, name ("You" on one's own row) and permission. Admins change a row through an option sheet with the three permissions and Remove, which asks first with the cleanup notice; lowering one's own share asks first too.
