"Add person" opens the people search (without oneself, the owner and those already shared), then the permission (Read only by default), and sends one request.
    - Teams: an initials avatar (without a provider suffix), the name or "Hidden team" for a team the server hides, the member count and the permission, with the same actions; "Add team" offers the user's teams, and public ones where enabled, that are not shared yet.
    - Links, only where the server has link sharing and only for admins: the name or "Link without a name", the permission, "Password protected" with a lock, "Created by {name}". A tap opens the link's sheet: the view it opens (the project's views in their order, the first by default), Share, Copy link, QR code and Delete ("Anyone using this link loses access at once."). "Create link" takes a permission (Read only by default), an optional name and an optional password (obscured, autofilled as a new password), then shows the same sheet as "Link created", without Delete. Links cannot be changed.
    - The link is `{frontend}share/{hash}/auth`, with `?view={id}` (`linkShareUrl`), on the base the app already used for web links: the frontend URL when it is http(s), else the server root, never http for an https server.
    - Share goes through the system share sheet (`LinkShareActions` over share_plus), Copy through the sensitive clipboard of D55, the selection menu's Copy too. The QR code is dark on white in both themes, with a quiet zone of 4 modules and the link selectable below it; a link that needs a QR version above 10 says "This link is too long for a QR code" and keeps Share and Copy. Screenshots are not blocked: the link is meant to be shared.
    - The hash appears only inside the link on screen, in the QR code, the share sheet and the clipboard; the password only in its field and the create request. Neither is logged, put in a route, kept in a provider that outlives its page or stored, and the share types print neither.
    - Others see people and teams read-only, with "Only project admins can change who has access". A change of the user's own share or of any team share is followed by a quiet `refreshAfterWrite`, so the stored permission follows; other changes touch nothing here. A link share already gone counts as deleted.
  - **The permission stored:** schema v9 adds `projects.max_permission`, filled through `expand=permissions` on the sync's project list, so no request is added. The project diff compares it through `Project ==`, so a change of the permission alone is written although `updated` did not move. Null means unknown and keeps today's behaviour: offer the change, word the refusal. In a v1 single read the header wins over the body, which servers before 2.6 fill with 0. Write answers never carry it, so the app keeps the stored value when it applies one; a created or duplicated project is unknown until the next sync. The migration deletes `last_full_sync_at`, so the first run after the upgrade is a full one.
  - **Every screen shaped by it** (`ProjectAccess`, by the server's rules; `projectAccessProvider` follows the stored row):
    - the project menu: Edit, Archive or Unarchive and the favourite (the server takes it as a project change) need write; Views and Delete need admin; New subproject needs write; Duplicate needs read, and its parent picker lists only projects the user may write;
    - "New task", the manual order of lists, and the board's drag and column menu need write; "Done column" and "Default column" need admin;
    - the project pickers for new and moved tasks and for the parent of a new, moved or duplicated project leave out read-only projects, and so does the default project (the new task sheet, the Android share target, quick add's fallback);
    - the task sheet of a read-only project offers no Mark as done, Edit, Delete, Add subtask, overflow menu, description editing, checklist ticks, relation changes, attachment changes or favourite; comments and reactions follow the stored permission until the details arrive, and the stored permission wins over an earlier read in the same run;
    - in rows of a read-only project the checkbox, labelled "Read only", and the star are off; each subtask's checkbox follows its own project;
    - a read-only project's screen says "Read only" under its progress line;
    - archived projects keep their own rules on top.
  - **Inbox:** an entry about being added to a team opens the team's page when the notification names the team.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **A Teams tab**, as in the mockups: D56 kept Account as the fourth tab, and teams are opened rarely.
  - **An own QR encoder:** error correction and masking are a lot of code to pin by tests; `qr` is small, pure Dart and depends only on `meta`.
  - **Storing teams and shares:** adding a member or toggling admin changes neither the team's `updated` nor its ETag, so the sync could not keep them right; they are management pages, read when opened.
  - **Following permission changes through `updated`:** a share change bumps only the shared project, not its children, whose inherited permission changes too, and team membership and link shares bump nothing.
  - **Refreshing `/info` while signed in**, to follow link sharing, public teams and the frontend URL: a request per run for settings that rarely change; a link share call answered 404 says that link sharing is off.
  - **Gating labels on `max_permission`:** lists do not carry it, and a read per label would cost a request each.
- **Consequences:**
  - The app offers what the server allows, and a permission changed on the web reaches the screens with the next sync. Until the first sync after the upgrade, a full one, every change is offered as before. The sync costs no extra request.
  - Not built: offline label creation (deferred by the owner); teams in the global search (teams are not stored); refreshing `/info` while signed in; link-share guests opening links in the app (step 32); share counts in the project menu; changing link shares (the server cannot); a cleanup list per project (the server offers none); the iOS share sheet's anchor (the iOS phase). *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): the anchor was built in step 28 after all (`sharePositionOrigin` in `link_share_actions.dart`, as for the attachment rows since step 26); the iPad simulator showed an attachment's share sheet as a popover at its row, while a link share's was not opened there.* A tap on the OS notification about a team membership still opens the inbox. Not gated: reordering and favouriting projects in the Projects tab, and quick add's `+project` naming a read-only project.
  - Known gaps: an assignee the server's access cleanup removed stays on the device until the daily full resync, because the removal bumps no `updated`. A team whose create answer is lost can still be created twice; only a failed read after the create is covered. A user with write access who deletes the default column while it holds tasks is refused with the admin message, because the app first sets a new default. The id-0 toggle answer is unverified on 2.3; public teams and the teams of a real sign-in provider could not be tested on the test server.
  - Tests:
    - the domain and the logic: the link builder with the view and the base rule, the provider suffix, the team guards as a table, the label and team name rules, `LabelChanges`, and no share type printing a hash, password or name;
    - the ports on 139 responses recorded from 2.7.0 by `tool/capture_sharing_fixtures.dart` (names, emails, hashes and passwords replaced), every method on both versions, the failure mapping, `expand=permissions`, v1's header, the id-0 toggle and a failed read after a create;
    - the schema v9 migration from v8 and from v1; both runs storing the permission and an incremental run writing a project whose only change is the permission; the repositories: label changes and deletes applied here with the queued adds forgotten and the quiet sync, a failure leaving the row, the sync after a change of one's own access, the user search, the id-like label search; `ProjectAccess`;
    - widget tests: the Organise section, the Labels page, the label form, the label pills, the Teams page with its badges, the team page (member actions, guards, leave and delete with the notice by server version, offline only after its own failed request), the share page (admin and read-only, a person added with a permission, the hidden team row, links by capability and admin, a link created, copied through the sensitive clipboard fake and shared through the fake, the QR sheet, a link too long for a QR code, no hash or password in the logs), the gating of the project screen, the board's columns, the pickers, the task sheet and the rows, and the results after Android reports the route list back; the accessibility smoke test for every new screen at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de;
    - contract tests on 2.7.0 with fresh users, at most three per file: in `vikunja_api` labels (only the changed fields, another client's colour kept, an unchanged label read back, a long title refused, another user refused, a label deleted twice, a numeric title found), teams (a member reading as member, members added, toggled and removed, 6005, 6006, 1005, the public flag kept, the description cleared as `<p></p>`), the user search, user, team and link shares with their errors, and the acceptance on the ports; in the app labels, teams and sharing through the repositories, and the acceptance through the sync: a project shared read-only is stored as read and the gating says read only, raised to write it is stored as write after the other user's next incremental run, also on its child whose `updated` did not move, and unshared it is purged with its child and its task, on both API versions;
    - in total 3,968 app tests, 324 in `vikunja_domain`, 2,966 in `vikunja_logic` and 1,607 in `vikunja_api` pass, skipped ones not counted; the contract runs against the server pass with 339 tests in `vikunja_api` and 186 in the app.
  - Checked on the Android emulator: the Organise section with the label count; a label created with a palette colour, its colour changed and the change on the server, the label deleted there too; a label opening its open tasks; a team created, the second test user found by username and added, made admin, the only admin's Leave stopped with its question, the team deleted after the cleanup notice; a project shared read-only by the second user showing "Read only", no "New task" and a menu without Edit, Views and Delete, then, raised to write on the server, the note gone and "New task" back after the next sync; the share page with a person, a link created with a name, its QR code read by a scanner as the same link, which signed in to the project read-only, and the link deleted on the server. Found and fixed there: the Labels, Teams and share pages added the status bar's height above their lists a second time (the padding was read from the page's context, above the app bar), now covered by a test each.

## D61: The Table view as a detailed list sorted like the server, and the Gantt view as a read-only timeline with the web's filter, range, rows, bars and arrows, both from the local database

*Accepted 2026-10-26, step 29. Carries out [D20](#d20-scope-cuts-and-desktop-adaptations) (the table becomes a detailed list, Gantt a read-only timeline) and draws the Gantt arrows that [D41](#d41-relations-as-deltas-in-the-task-update-sent-through-the-relation-endpoints-after-a-read-before-write-diff-temporary-ids-on-both-ends-resolved-at-send-time-the-inverse-rows-kept-locally-duplicate-and-subscriptions-online-only-subtasks-from-indented-quick-add-lines) left to this step. Both views read the lists of [D43](#d43-task-lists-from-the-local-database-like-the-webs-a-view-snapshot-for-positions-and-server-only-filters-the-overviews-saved-filter-downloaded-by-the-sync-upcoming-with-the-web-presets-and-a-pinned-overdue-part) through the filter engine of [D51](#d51-one-filter-engine-on-the-device-with-the-servers-semantics-saved-filter-definitions-synced-on-change-and-managed-online-a-filter-builder-offline-search); the table's rows are the task row of [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list), and "New task" follows the stored permission of [D60](#d60-labels-managed-online-and-applied-locally-teams-project-shares-and-link-shares-online-only-the-project-permission-stored-and-every-screen-shaped-by-it).*

- **Context:**
  - The app showed list and Kanban views only. Table and Gantt views were left out of the tabs, a `table` or `gantt` default view fell back to the first view shown, and the Views page, the view form and the default view in the account settings marked both kinds as shown on the web only.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0 on both API versions with fresh users, probed on 2026-10-26; the server and the web read at 2.7.1; v1 and v2 answered alike):
    - **Views:** a new project gets List (`done = false`), Gantt, Table (no filter) and Kanban. Neither a table nor a Gantt view stores anything of its own. The web keeps the table's columns and default sort once for the browser, and the sort and the Gantt range per view, in local storage. The tasks endpoint ignores a view's stored sort and ANDs its filter with the request's, `(request) && (view)`, with include-nulls if either asks.
    - **The web's table:** the flat tasks endpoint with the comment count and the unread flag, without `expand=subtasks`, so subtasks are rows of their own and done tasks stand among the open ones with a Done badge; there is no "show done". 16 columns in a fixed order, six shown by default (#, Done, Title, Labels, Assignees, Due date). 11 of them sort, `index desc` by default; a header click cycles descending, ascending and off, Ctrl-click sorts by several, and a sort on a hidden column is left out of the request. The priority cell shows every value, Unset and done tasks included. An unset date shows `-`, an overdue one is not red, and the progress is the raw float (`28.999999999999996%`).
    - **The server's table order:** the request's keys, then the view's positions ascending, then id ascending. Every key puts missing values last in both directions; priority 0 and progress 0 are values. `done_at` is set only by an update that marks a task done, so a task created done has none. SQLite orders titles by bytes (`Apple, Date, _under, apple`). The positions of a table view created with its project run newest first.
    - **The web's Gantt request:** the range runs from today − 15 to today + 55 days at local midnight (71 days), "today" taken once when the page loads. The filter is `((start_date >= "F" && start_date <= "T") || (end_date >= "F" && end_date <= "T") || (due_date >= "F" && due_date <= "T") || (start_date <= "F" && end_date >= "T"))` with the range's local dates; include-nulls is "Show tasks without dates"; `filter_timezone` is the timezone setting, sent even when empty; `expand=subtasks`; sorted by start date, done and id descending; every page.
    - **The server's answer:** a bare date is midnight in `filter_timezone`, or in the server's zone when that is empty. So a task that starts on the range's last day after midnight matches only through another clause, and the spanning clause ignores the due date. Include-nulls adds "or missing" to every comparison, so nearly every task matches, and the web drops the bars outside the range itself. The subtasks expand appends every descendant of the matching roots, in any project and unfiltered. A saved filter combines the request in its own way (the server's test GO2).
    - **What the web draws:** a tree with each task once, under its first parent, indented up to four levels; a parent without dates takes its subtasks' span. Rows outside the range are dropped. Bars are rounded to local days, an end before noon ending at the start of its day; a start alone runs 7 days and fades out, an end alone 7 or 8 days and fades in; a task without dates (with the toggle) runs from today to today + 7, grey and dashed; a done task is at half opacity and struck through; parents have end markers and a translucent band over their group. Arrows show `blocking` (solid, red) and `precedes` (dashed, grey), one per pair and kind, re-routed to a collapsed ancestor and spread by min((n − 1)·6, 24) px. A chevron collapses a parent, not saved. There is no zoom, no empty-state text and no today line, and a task opens on a double click.
    - **The web's quirks:** bar widths, arrows, group bands and the chart width are counted in milliseconds, so they are a day off across a DST change; "today" stays fixed in a tab left open over midnight; "Reset" is not translated.
- **Decision:**
  - **Both views shown:** one predicate, `isShownInApp` in `project_list_model.dart`, shows every kind the app knows. Table and Gantt views are tabs in the web's order, and the user's default view and the remembered view pick them too. The "web only" texts are gone from the Views page, the view form and the account settings. "New task" is offered as on a list, by the stored permission. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): there is no remembered view; the default view picks them.*
  - **No downloads for them:** both read the list path of D43 (`watchProjectList` over `LocalFilters`), and pull to refresh runs the sync. Only a view whose filter the engine refuses downloads its snapshot as a list view does (`downloadsSnapshot`): on open, on pull and after an edit, with D43's notes and states.
  - **The table, a detailed list:**
    - Flat rows as on the web: subtasks are rows of their own and done tasks stand among the open ones. A task marked done here keeps its row and its place while its Undo is offered.
    - **Columns per account** (`table_columns`), like the web's single choice: a Columns sheet lists the 16 columns in the web's order, the six defaults until chosen, with "Default columns" to go back. The title always shows, so one column at least stays. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): 15 columns without the task number ("Number") and five defaults; a stored choice naming `index` loads without it.*
    - **One sort key per view** (`table_sort`, the web's `field:dir`): a Sort sheet offers "Default (Highest number first)", which is `index:desc`, and the other 21 pairs of column and direction. An unknown stored value is the default. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the sheet offers "Default (newest first)", still `index:desc`, and the 20 other pairs without the number's; a stored `index:asc` reads as the default.*
    - **The server's order** (`compareTasksForTable`): the key first, missing values last in both directions, priority and progress 0 as values, open before done, no done-at for an open task or one created done, and a task created here numbered after every server task, as it will be once sent. Ties go newest first (id descending). This stands in for the view's positions, which are not downloaded; it is their order in a table view created with its project. Titles compare ignoring case first, the order of D43, where SQLite compares bytes.
    - **A sort on a hidden column** gives way to the default for the rows, the Sort chip and its check mark, as the web leaves it out of its request. The stored sort stays and applies again once its column shows. Picking a sort for a hidden column shows that column, for every table view, because the pick would change nothing visible otherwise.
    - **"Show done"** only on a view whose filter is `done = false`, as for lists. A view without a filter shows its done tasks among the open ones, as on the web.
    - **The row** is the task row of D56 with the chosen fields: the identifier; the Done badge on a done task only; the project with its colour dot; the priority by the table's rule, every value including Unset and on done tasks, in the web's three colours (Unset and Low in the primary colour for the web's blue, Medium in amber, High and up in the error colour with a circled exclamation mark); the labels; the assignees; the comment count when above 0, with the unread dot; the due, start, end, done-at (on done tasks only), created and updated dates, each after its column's icon, formatted by the display settings in a neutral colour and never red, an unset one left out instead of `-`; the progress as a ring and a whole percent, not the web's float; the creator's avatar with the name, since a phone has no tooltip. The checkbox and the star stay as on every row. A tap opens the task sheet. Each row is one screen-reader node that names every shown field. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): no identifier; the project after a folder in its colour.*
  - **The timeline, read-only:**
    - **Which tasks:** the web's filter for the range (`ganttFilterText`, character for character) is ANDed with the view's own filter as the server does it, `(range) && (view)`, with include-nulls if "Show undated" or the view asks (`LocalFilter.compileWithRequest`). It is evaluated on the device in the zone of the timezone setting, else the device's (`locationForFilters`), like D43 and D51. The engine also has the saved-filter rule, which no screen uses yet. Where the engine refuses the view's filter, the view's last download stands in, narrowed to the range evaluated here with include-nulls from "Show undated" or the view.
    - **The web's quirks** are copied where they decide which tasks show: a task starting on the last day after midnight shows only through another clause; the spanning clause ignores the due date; with "Show undated" every task with a missing date matches and the range trims it afterwards; a subtask with two parents shows once; a bar ending at midnight of the range's first day keeps its row. They are not copied where they are drawing mistakes: days are counted as calendar days (`CalendarDate`), so no bar, arrow or band is a day off across a DST change; "today" is computed again; "Reset" is translated.
    - **The range per view**, stored as "default" or as a custom from and to, so that the default follows today: 15 days back to 55 ahead. "Today" comes from `todayProvider`, which follows the clock ticks: it is taken when the screen opens or resumes and turns over at midnight while the screen is open. A Date range chip opens a sheet with "Default (15 days back, 55 ahead)", "Custom range…" (the day-range picker of Upcoming, moved to `features/common/`) and "Reset to defaults". Reset is offered only while the range or "Show undated" differs from the default, and resets both, not the zoom.
    - **"Show undated"** ("Undatierte zeigen"; the tooltip has the long form) is the include-nulls switch, stored per view.
    - **Rows** (`layoutGantt`): the matching tasks and every descendant of them, in any project. The top-level rows are those without a parent among them, by start date (missing last), open before done, then newest first. Each is followed by its subtasks in relation order, depth first, each task once under its first parent, indented up to four levels. Tasks caught in a relation cycle become top-level rows of their own. A parent without dates takes the earliest start and the latest end (else due date) of its subtasks. A row is left out when it has no dates while "Show undated" is off, and when its bar lies wholly outside the range.
    - **Bars** in calendar days of the device's zone: start and end (else due) rounded to their days, an end before noon ending at the start of its day; a start alone runs 7 days and fades out; an end or due date alone starts 7 days before its day and fades in; without dates (with "Show undated") from today to today + 7, neutral and dashed; a parent's span from its subtasks dashed; at least one day wide. The fill is the task's colour or the scheme's primary container, with the text colour by contrast. A done bar is its colour mixed with the surface, so its title stays readable, and the title is struck through.
    - **Titles** sit in the bar when they fit, on a partial bar only on its opaque part (the first 60 % or from 40 % on), else after the bar. When a bar starts left of the screen, its title and a parent's chevron follow the visible part of the chart; the tap target and the screen-reader node stay with the bar.
    - **Parents** get small end markers and a 48 dp chevron that collapses their subtasks, not stored, as on the web; the chevron shows only where a subtask row would show. Collapsing hides the subtasks placed under that row; a collapsed parent outside the range hides nothing. A band behind a parent and the subtask rows below it is as wide as all their bars (`GanttLayout.groupSpans`, the web's group bands).
    - **Arrows** for `blocking` (solid, in the error colour) and `precedes` (dashed, in the outline colour) run from the right edge of one bar to the left edge of the other, one per pair of tasks and kind, only between rows that show: a hidden task's arrows go to its outermost collapsed ancestor that shows, and an arrow to itself is dropped. Several arrows on one end spread by min((n − 1)·6, 24) dp. There is no toggle, as on the web.
    - **The chart:** one horizontal scroll with a two-row header (month and year; weekday over day number, today filled), day lines with weekends tinted, the rows, today's line and the arrows. It opens with today in its left third, and on today again after the range changes; "Go to today" scrolls back. Rows are 48 dp (more with large text) and built only while on screen; the header builds only the days on screen, and its text stops growing at 150 %. A layout is computed once per source, range, day, "Show undated" and collapse.
    - **Zoom** by the − and + buttons or by two fingers, from 20 to 72 dp per day (36 by default), stored per view. A pinch saves when the fingers lift, on top of the latest preferences, and holds the vertical scroll and pull to refresh while it lasts.
    - **Read-only:** a tap opens the task sheet and a long press does nothing; dates are changed on the edit page (D20).
    - **Empty and download states:** "No tasks between {from} and {to}" with a hint to choose another range or to show undated tasks. A view whose filter the engine refuses has D43's states before its first download and the note that the server applies its filter.
    - **Screen readers:** one node per row with the title, "subtask of …", done, the dates ("{from} to {to}", "starts …, open end", "ends …, open start", "due …, open start", "no dates, estimated" or "dates from its subtasks") and the expanded state with an action to collapse or expand. The header, the grid, the bands and the arrows are left out.
  - **Preferences** in the account's `list_preferences`: per view under `list:view:<id>`, beside the list's sort and show done, the table's `table_sort` and the timeline's `{range: "default" | {from, to}, without_dates, day_width}`; per account `table_columns`, the web's column keys. Each field decodes on its own, a bad one as its default, and deleting a view forgets them with the rest.
  - **The logic** is pure Dart in `vikunja_logic`, pinned by the web's vectors and the server's observed answers: `TableSort`, `TableColumn` and `compareTasksForTable`; `CalendarDate`; `GanttRange`, `defaultGanttRange` and `ganttFilterText`; `layoutGantt` and `ganttArrows`.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **A landscape data table** with a column per field: it fits a tablet, not a phone, and D20 chose a detailed list; the row shows the same fields.
  - **Columns per view:** the web keeps one choice for every table, and a choice per view would have to be made again in every project.
  - **A sort by several keys** ("then by"): the web needs Ctrl-click for it; one key per view covers a phone.
  - **Downloading the table view's positions** for exact ties: one request per 50 tasks every five minutes while a table is open, for a tie-breaker that already matches tables created with their project; the web table cannot reorder.
  - **Asking the server for the timeline**, as the web does: it would not work offline nor show a change made here at once, and the engine gives every answer the server gave in the probe.
  - **The web's day counts in milliseconds and its fixed "today":** a bar a day too wide after a DST change, and a range that stops moving in a screen left open overnight.
  - **Storing the default range as dates:** it would stop following today.
  - **The list row's priority rule** (D43: from the minimum priority, open tasks only): the column promises the priority, so it shows every value, as the web's table does.
  - **Editing in the timeline** (dragging or resizing a bar, the add-task row): D20 keeps it read-only, and dates are changed on the edit page.
  - **An agenda view:** Upcoming already is one.
  - **A toggle for the arrows:** the web has none.
  - **Painting the whole chart on one canvas:** the header days and the rows are widgets built only while on screen, so each row is a screen-reader node and the tests find them; the grid, today's line, the bands and the arrows are painted.
- **Consequences:**
  - Every view the server creates opens in the app, offline too, and follows a change made here at once, at no extra request. Only a table or Gantt view whose filter the engine refuses costs a download, like a list view.
  - A table's ties go newest first where the server goes by the view's positions. The two differ in a table view created after its tasks (oldest first there) and in one whose positions were changed through the API.
  - Not built: editing dates in the timeline and the web's add-task row (D20); a landscape data table; a sort by several keys; downloading table and Gantt positions where the filter is evaluated here; an agenda view; a toggle for the arrows; Table and Gantt views of saved filters (a saved filter opens as one list, and the saved-filter rule exists only in the engine); ParadeDB's relevance order.
  - Known gaps:
    - The timezone a view's own filter may carry on the server, which wins over the request's there, is not modelled, as before this step; the API cannot set it.
    - With an empty timezone setting the server evaluates in its own zone and the app in the device's, as in D43.
    - A view whose filter the engine refuses can list slightly other tasks than the server with "Show undated" on: its download asked with the view's own include-nulls only.
    - Titles sort ignoring case, while SQLite compares bytes (Postgres and MySQL use their collation, not checked); the contract test avoids titles that differ only in case.
    - Servers before 2.7 were not checked; the app applies 2.7's filter and tree rules to them.
    - At 200 % text on a 320 dp screen the table's chips, and on a 360 dp phone the timeline's zoom and today buttons, run past the edge; the row scrolls sideways, as the list's chips do.
  - Tests:
    - `vikunja_logic`: the table sort, every key in both directions, against the server's probe orders (done-at with a second probe), and the hidden-column fallback; `CalendarDate`; the default range and the filter text; the web's tree, bar and arrow vectors (TT1–TT5, RB1–RB6, RA1–RA6) and the rows it draws, in calendar days; the group bands; the 32 observed server runs and the extra ones, with the server's own GO1 and GO2, through the engine; in UTC, Europe/Berlin, America/Los_Angeles and Asia/Kolkata; 1,000 tasks laid out in under 50 ms.
    - the app: the preferences codec field by field and `forget`; the view predicate and the default view; the request combination, and the observed server runs through the app's engine on a test database with the top-level order; the timeline source with the refused-filter fallback and no downloads; `todayProvider` at midnight; widget tests for the table (the tabs, the Sort and Columns sheets, the hidden-column fallback, the fields and the priority rule, "Show done", a task marked done keeping its place, the download and empty states, German) and its rows; for the timeline the header and today, bars by case, the fades, a done bar's contrast, titles and chevrons following the visible part, today's line and an arrow, the screen-reader nodes, collapse, tap and long press, the range sheet and Reset, the default range moving at midnight, undated bars, zoom by buttons and by pinch without a refresh, "Go to today", the empty and download states, a range of decades, German; a performance test (1,000 tasks: the layout within its budget and fewer than 30 rows built in the first frame); the accessibility smoke test with a table showing every column and a timeline, at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de.
    - contract tests on 2.7.0 with one fresh user and the probe's tasks, on both API versions: the timeline equal to the server's answer to the web's Gantt request (the same tasks, the top-level rows in its order) in UTC, Europe/Berlin and America/Los_Angeles, for two ranges, with and without undated tasks; a Gantt view's own `done = false` ANDed; 14 table sorts in the server's order, missing values included; table and Gantt views never downloaded.
    - in total 4,072 app tests, 324 in `vikunja_domain`, 3,148 in `vikunja_logic` and 1,607 in `vikunja_api` pass, skipped ones not counted; the contract runs against the server pass with 339 tests in `vikunja_api` and 194 in the app.
  - Checked on the Android emulator: a project created with the server's four default views showing List, Gantt, Table and Kanban as tabs; the table sorted by number, highest first, with the Done badge on a done task; Priority, Start and End date turned on in the Columns sheet and shown with their own markers, Unset priorities in the web's colour, the dates neutral; the table sorted by start date, earliest first, in exactly the server's order for the same request (missing dates last, newest first among them); the Gantt view on today − 15 to + 55 days with the month and day header, today highlighted and its line, bars for start and end, start only (fading out) and due only (fading in), a done task muted and struck through, a parent with its subtask under it, the blocking and precedes arrows; "Show undated" adding an undated task as a dashed bar from today for a week; a tap on a bar opening the task sheet. Found and fixed there: the group band behind a parent and its subtasks covered only the parent's own bar in the subtask rows; it now spans the parent row and the subtask rows below it, as wide as all their bars, as the web's `parentGroupBands` does (`GanttLayout.groupSpans`, with a test).

## D62: Create account, password reset and invite links through the browser sign-in page; link-share guests as an account kind with token renewal and a reduced UI

*Accepted 2026-10-26, step 32. Carries out the re-scope of [D14](#d14-browser-sign-in-only): the app still never handles an account password or a TOTP code, and a link share's password, which is no account password, is taken for one request and never stored. Adds a second kind of account to those of [D16](#d16-multi-account-in-the-first-release) and [D37](#d37-account-switcher-a-sheet-from-an-avatar-button-and-an-accounts-section-a-switch-resets-the-navigation-the-most-recently-active-account-follows-a-removal-inactive-outboxes-drain-in-the-foreground), kept alive by the background runs of [D22](#d22-session-keep-alive-through-background-sync) and [D40](#d40-background-sync-one-periodic-job-for-every-signed-in-account-with-a-deadline-skipped-while-the-app-is-in-front-the-incremental-run-as-the-session-keep-alive-a-one-off-drain-after-a-background-done-server-notifications-synced-into-a-local-inbox-on-their-own-cadence-new-unread-ones-as-activity-notifications-the-first-run-silent) and left without the realtime of [D46](#d46-realtime-in-the-foreground-one-websocket-for-the-active-account-while-the-app-is-in-front-pushed-notifications-stored-at-once-and-a-sync-nudged-for-the-task-they-name-re-auth-on-every-token-change-quiet-fallback-to-polling); takes links through the share target of [D53](#d53-attachments-uploaded-through-the-outbox-in-their-own-lane-opened-shared-and-saved-through-the-platform-cover-and-delete-online-and-an-own-android-share-target) and shapes a guest's screens through the stored permission of [D60](#d60-labels-managed-online-and-applied-locally-teams-project-shares-and-link-shares-online-only-the-project-permission-stored-and-every-screen-shaped-by-it); styled after [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list).*

- **Context:**
  - The server overview offered only "Continue to sign-in". A new account, a forgotten password and an admin's invitation needed the web, without a hint from the app. The Account tab showed nothing of the server's message of the day, demo mode or legal links. A link share, which step 28 creates, opened only in the web: the Connect screen took a pasted link for a server address, and the share target offered a shared one only as a new task.
  - Verified server facts (Vikunja 2.7.0 on both API versions with fresh users, probed on 2026-10-26; the server and the web read at 2.7.1):
    - **The sign-in page:** the OAuth authorize page is the web's login page. It links to "Create account" when `registration_enabled` and to "Forgot your password?" with local accounts, during a pending sign-in as without one. The pending request survives the move to the register page in the same tab, so after a registration without email confirmation the web signs in and returns to the app with a code (register, login, authorize and token observed on the API). With a mailer a new account must confirm its email first (412 code 1012), and the mailed link opens in the normal browser.
    - **Password reset:** `get-password-reset` mails a link to the web's reset page, valid for 24 h. A reset deletes every session of the user, the app's OAuth session included. `/info` has no mailer flag, and the request answers "Token was sent." without a mailer.
    - **Invite links** (2.7.0, v2 only, licensed `user_invites`): `{frontend}register#invite-link=<token>`. The web reads and removes the fragment before its router runs, so an invite registration cannot carry the app's sign-in and ends on the web's home page. `POST /api/v2/invite-links/check` is public and answers `{name, teams, skip_email_confirm}`, or 404 code 2005 for an invalid, expired, used up or unlicensed invite. The register call with the invite works even with registration closed.
    - **Notices:** `motd` (plain text, shown by the web only to signed-out pages), `demo_mode_enabled` (only informational; the web shows a banner on every page) and the legal links (http(s) only).
    - **Link share sign-in:** `POST /shares/{hash}/auth` (v1; v2 from 2.4.0, the body optional) answers the token, the project id and the permission, and echoes the hash. 412 code 13001 means the password is needed, 403 code 13002 that it is wrong, 404 code 3006 an unknown or deleted share; without `link_sharing_enabled` the route does not exist (404 without a code). It shares the unauthenticated limit of 10 requests a minute and IP with sign-in and registration.
    - **The token:** a JWT of type 2 with the share id, the hash, the project id, the permission, `sharedByID`, `iat` and `exp`, valid 72 h (`service.jwtttl`), without a refresh token or a session. `POST /user/token` (v1 and v2) renews it: the claims stay, a fresh 72 h, the old token stays valid, outside the sign-in limit. An expired token cannot be renewed (401 code 11). The server reads the share on every request, so the database's permission counts, not the claim.
    - **A share gone** (deleted, deleted with its project, or replaced to change its password, since shares cannot be changed): v1 answers 400 code 13003 on every request, v2 401 without a code. Renewal fails, a new sign-in with the hash gets 3006, and `/token/test` still answers 200.
    - **What a guest may do:** a read share reads the project, its views, tasks, comments, reactions, attachments and the labels used in it; every write is a 403. A write share also writes tasks, comments (edits and deletes only its own), attachments, existing labels, assignees, relations within the project and buckets, and may rename and archive the project. No guest may create labels, react, mark tasks read, search users (v1 401 code 1027, v2 403 code 1023), use saved filters, subscriptions, notifications, teams, shares or user settings, create or delete projects, see child projects or open the WebSocket. A guest's favourites are accepted and kept for the guest only.
    - **Identity:** the server shows a guest as the pseudo user `-shareId`, `link-share-<id>`, named "<name> (Link Share)" or "Link Share", in English, in `created_by`, `author` and `GET /user`.
    - **The project list** gives a guest only the shared project, with `max_permission` null even with `expand=permissions`; a single read has it (v1's header, v2's body).
- **Decision:**
  - **Owner decisions of 2026-10-26:** both halves now, onboarding and the link-share guest, despite the overlap with the parked session hardening (below). A share link comes in by paste or through the Android share target; no QR scanner and no new dependency. No schema change.
  - **Onboarding through the browser's sign-in page** (D14 kept: no registration, reset or confirmation form, no pasted token):
    - Server overview: under "Continue to sign-in", "Create account" where the server allows registration (`Capability.registration`) shows a dialog with the hint "On the sign-in page, choose Create account. You come back here afterwards." (Cancel, "Continue to sign-in") and then runs the normal browser sign-in. "Forgot password?" with local accounts (`Capability.localAuth`) opens the web's `get-password-reset` in the external browser (`passwordResetPageUri`, on `webFrontendBase`). Both are off while a sign-in runs. A server with invite links but no registration says "Registration needs an invitation." The sign-in methods card says that the sign-in page also creates accounts or resets passwords, as far as the server allows.
    - The overview's action bar stays pinned at the bottom only on a screen at least 480 dp times the text scale tall; otherwise the buttons scroll with the content. In German at 200 % the pinned bar with both new buttons took 472 of 640 dp.
    - Invite links: a pasted `{base}register#invite-link=<token>` on the Connect screen, or one shared to the app, probes the base and checks the invite (`InviteLinksPort.check`). A card shows its name, its teams and whether the email needs confirming. "Create account in the browser" opens the invite in the external browser; "Continue to sign-in" goes to the server overview. 404 code 2005: "This invitation is invalid, expired or used up". A server without the route (before 2.7.0, local accounts off, no v2) gets the card without the check. The token is never shown or logged.
    - Account tab, Server section, for every account: a demo-server note in the amber accent, the MOTD card and the legal links, from the `/info` snapshot stored at sign-in (D60: no refresh while signed in).
  - **The guest account:**
    - `Account.linkShare` (`AccountKind.linkShare`) with the share id, the project id and the share's name. Its `userId` is `-shareId` and its username `link-share-<id>`, as the server writes the guest, so the outbox's upload recovery and the guest's own comments match without changes.
    - Account id: the first 32 hex characters of `sha256("<server root>|share|<share id>")`, apart from every user's id. The same link opened again replaces the guest's session instead of adding a second guest.
    - Registry: a guest entry has `kind: link_share`, `link_share_id`, `project_id` and `share_name`, and no `user_id`. A build before guests fails to read it and keeps it untouched, instead of taking it for a user whose tokens are gone.
    - Name: the share's name, else the project's title, else `link-share-<id>`. The title is read once at sign-in; an unnamed guest's name then follows the title from each sync's project list (`AccountRegistry.updateGuestTitle`). The server's "Link Share" never replaces it.
    - Secrets: `LinkShareStore` keeps the JWT and the hash, the link's long-lived secret, in secure storage under keys of its own. The hash is written first and deleted first. The share's password is never stored or logged; a share with a password asks for it again when the hash alone cannot sign in. The start-up check counts a guest as signed in while its hash is stored.
  - **The guest session** (`LinkShareSession`, an `AccessTokenSource` per guest beside the session manager, held by `LinkShareSessions`):
    - The stored token is renewed through `POST /user/token` once half its lifetime has passed. The server does not rotate, so concurrent renewals are only de-duplicated. A failed renewal keeps the old token while it is valid and waits 5 minutes before the next try.
    - An expired token is replaced by signing in again with the stored hash. A 429 blocks that for its `Retry-After`. A container the OS started (the background runner, the notification action isolate) tries it at most once, so a password the user has to type never spends the shared limit every half hour.
    - A 401 code 11 is not answered with a renewal, which passes the same token check: a newer stored token is taken, else the session signs in again.
    - 13001 or 13002 on that sign-in end the session, since the password is not stored. A gone share ends it too. Either marks the account `needsReauth`.
    - The server clock offset comes from each token's `iat` and stays in memory.
    - After writing a token the session reads the hash again and takes the token back when a sign-out in another isolate deleted it meanwhile.
    - Dispatch at the seams: `AccountTokens` hands a guest's requests to its session, so the transport, the sync and the outbox need no other change. The session check, the background runner's settle and retire, and the sign-out get a guest branch that never touches `SessionManager`; the sign-out skips the server's logout. The account settings repository refuses a guest as the server would (403 code 1023).
  - **A share gone:** `LinkShareGone`, a `SessionEnded` with the reason `linkShareGone`, never a 401 code 11.
    - v1's 400 code 13003 is mapped by `normalizeError` for every client. v2's 401 without a code becomes one only through `LinkShareGoneInterceptor` on the guest's client, and only for a request that carried a link-share token, because a user can get that answer too. At the share endpoints, 404 code 3006 and a 404 without a code (link sharing off) are one too; the `code` tells them apart.
    - The outbox stores and reloads it as a `SessionEnded` with that reason, so code that reads the outbox checks the reason.
    - The session check reads `GET /user` through the guest's session on a client without the interceptor, and maps v2's 401 without a code itself, because `/token/test` passes a deleted share's token.
    - A 401 code 1027 (the endpoint needs a user) stays an ordinary failure and never ends a guest's session; the guest's screens do not call those endpoints.
    - A missing password, a wrong one and an invalid invite keep the existing failure types: a `DomainError` 412 code 13001, a `Forbidden` code 13002, a `NotFound` code 2005.
  - **Sync for a guest:** no account phase, so no current user or settings are stored for it. `GuestReadClient` reads the shared project once (`GET /projects/{id}`) when the project list brings it without a permission, one request per run, so the stored permission shapes a guest's screens through D60's `ProjectAccess` like a user's. Labels and tasks sync as for users.
  - **Gates beyond D60** (`activeAccountIsGuest`, `accountIsGuest`):
    - No activity sync (in front, for other accounts, in the background runs, where the 403 would make every run partial), no inbox, bell or activity switch, no realtime socket. Local reminders stay.
    - Hidden: the account settings (header, tile, routes), Organise, new projects, new and edited saved filters, favourites on tasks and projects, reactions, Follow, and the project menu's Edit, Archive or Unarchive, Share, Duplicate and New subproject, also for write shares, which the server lets rename and archive. Delete and Views are hidden even for admin shares: the project is its owner's.
    - The label picker offers no "Create label" and says why; quick add creates no labels and looks no one up. The people pickers and @-mentions offer only people already on the device.
    - `AppRoutes.isClosedToGuests` sends a guest from the account settings, teams, inbox, label management, the saved-filter form and the project's edit, description, views and share pages to the Overview. A label's task list stays open.
    - Kept: browsing, task edits by the stored permission, comments with Edit and Delete on the guest's own (the web never offers those), attachments, local reminders, and the list, table, timeline and Kanban views.
  - **Guest entry:**
    - Connect screen, at first start and in Add account: a pasted share link is recognised before any probe (`parseLinkShareUrl`: an explicit http(s) scheme, any path before `share/`, the web's guest address with `#share-auth-token=`, a link inside surrounding text). The progress line shows it shortened, never the hash, and the hash stays out of the screen's state.
    - The link's server: a stored account's server whose web frontend is the link's base, else the base probed like a typed address (an http server asks first). When no Vikunja server answers there, "Which server is it?" asks for the server's address and the link opens once it is entered. Offline, timeouts and TLS errors stay ordinary connection errors.
    - "Open a shared link" under "Try the public demo" opens a sheet with a field and Paste. Only the address field, Continue and the known-server chips carry a pending share on; the demo button does not, so a share's hash never goes to try.vikunja.io.
    - Android share target: a shared text with a share link brings up "Open as guest" before the signed-in and signed-out branches, signed in also "Make it a new task instead". An invite link goes to the invite check. A share with files is never taken for a link.
    - Password sheet (13001, 13002): obscured with show and hide, no autofill, "Wrong password" inline with the field kept focused, and the note that Rurana does not save the password.
    - `GuestSignIn` signs in, writes the secrets and only then replaces the session of the same share, so the old one keeps answering until the new one exists and a failed write leaves it working. It upserts and activates the account and remembers the link's project and view; after the first sync the guest lands on the shared project in that view. "Open the link again" (`reopen`) uses the stored hash and server and stays where the user is. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the landing names the view in the project's route (`?view=<id>`), as a project no longer remembers the view it showed last.*
    - Link sharing switched off answers without a request only for a freshly probed server on which no account is stored, since a stored account's `/info` is a snapshot. Otherwise the server's 404 says it.
  - **Guest UI:**
    - The account tile, the switcher button ("Accounts, guest in …"), the Account tab's header, the Accounts section and the share sheet's account line show a link icon instead of an avatar, the guest's name and "Guest · {host}".
    - The Account tab drops Edit, the header tap, the account settings, Organise and the deletion note. A Guest note gives the share's permission as the last sync stored it, what it allows and what needs an account. Sign out reads "Remove guest access", and its dialog names the guest and the server. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the Account tab has no Theme row either; a guest follows the system.*
    - The Overview greets a guest without a name.
    - An ended guest gets the banner "Guest access ended" with "Open the link again", which asks for the password where the share has one; a gone link and link sharing turned off get their own card. It shows on the Account tab and the first-sync screen. The tile's badge, the background notification ("Guest access to {name} ended", not the made-up username) and the sync's failure cards and snackbars say "Open the link again" instead of "Sign in again".
  - **Logging:** `describeUriForLog` replaces the segment after `share/` and `shares/` (a numeric share id stays), and `redactSecrets` also the `"hash"` JSON key. The link-share client's `Dio` logs no bodies: the requests carry the hash and maybe the password, and the answers echo the hash. No parsed link, token or share type prints its secret.
  - **The parked session hardening:** the package reserved as D59 (not in this log) is parked in a stash. The six auth files it changes, `session_manager.dart`, `token_store.dart`, `browser_sign_in.dart`, `auth_gateways.dart`, `secure_store.dart` and `oauth_token_client.dart`, were not touched; the guest code lives in new files. The package also changes `account_registry.dart`, `accounts_without_tokens.dart`, `auth_providers.dart`, `background_sync_runner.dart`, `repository_providers.dart`, `session_check.dart`, `realtime_connection_providers.dart`, `account_tab.dart`, `reauth_banner.dart` and `sync_status_section.dart`, their generated files, the ARB and l10n files, `auth_fakes.dart`, four of their tests and these docs. Step 32 changed them too, mostly in small hunks (in `repository_providers.dart` the sync engine's user callback moved under the guest condition), so applying the package will conflict there.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **A QR scanner** (`mobile_scanner` with ML Kit): a camera permission, native code and app size. The phone's camera opens a QR code's link in the browser, which shares it to Rurana.
  - **Native registration, reset or confirmation forms, or a pasted reset token:** they take an account password, against D14; the web's pages do them.
  - **Opening the web's register or reset page directly in the sign-in sheet** (`register#redirect=…`): it relies on an internal mechanism of the web that cannot be tested without a browser, and it would change `browser_sign_in.dart`, a file of the parked package. The hint before the normal sign-in does the same.
  - **Storing the share's password** to sign in again silently: a token renewed by the syncs does not expire, and the app would hold a secret for someone else's project. Opening the link again asks once.
  - **The guest inside `SessionManager` and `TokenStore`:** both are built around the rotating refresh token, and both are files of the parked package.
  - **The permission from the token's claim:** the server ignores it and reads the share on every request, so a lowered permission would not show.
  - **Tolerating the account phase for guests:** the server's pseudo user would rename the guest to "Link Share", and it has no settings.
  - **The web's guest layout** (only the project, no navigation): the app keeps its shell and hides what the server refuses, so the shared project works like any other, in the Overview, Upcoming and the search too.
  - **Renewing after a 401 code 11:** the renewal passes the same token check and fails too.
  - **Checking a guest's token with `/token/test`:** it answers 200 for a deleted share.
  - **Opening http(s) share links straight from other apps** (App Links): impossible for self-hosted domains.
  - **Refreshing `/info` while signed in** for the notices: rejected in D60.
- **Consequences:**
  - A link share opens in the app as an account of its own beside the user's accounts, synced offline and kept alive by the same background runs. While its token is renewed, a guest never types a password again; a deleted share ends its access at the next request.
  - A guest's sync costs one request more per run; a user's nothing.
  - New accounts and password resets still happen on the web's pages; the app leads there.
  - Not built: a QR scanner (owner: paste and the share target); an iOS share extension (iOS phase); native registration, reset or confirmation forms (D14); storing the share's password; refreshing `/info` while signed in (D60); a guest's `GET /user` on servers that refuse it beyond skipping the account phase; activity and realtime for guests (the server refuses them); opening http share links straight from other apps (no App Links for self-hosted domains); the re-sign-in banner's note that a password change signs you out (it would change an existing string and `reauth_banner.dart`); translating the English "Link Share" name others see on a guest's tasks and comments. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): the owner put the iOS share extension after 1.0; on iOS a link is pasted through "Open a shared link", as on the iPad simulator.*
  - Known gaps:
    - The app does not record why a guest's access ended, so the banner covers both a password needed and a link deleted. A tap tells them apart.
    - While the password sheet is open, the banner's button reads "Waiting for sign-in…".
    - A request already inside the old session when "Open the link again" replaces it can still end as `SessionEnded`, as with user sessions.
    - A guest whose link needs opening again gets one sign-in attempt per app start when something asks for its token.
    - The invite token stays in the Connect screen's state while the screen is open, for "Create account in the browser".
    - The description editor's @-mention gate for guests has no test of its own (it runs in a WebView); the comment composer's is tested.
    - Not probed: an admin link share, link sharing switched off (read in the source), a real 72 h expiry, servers before 2.7 (on 2.3 the share endpoints exist on v1 only, and the guest's `GET /user`, which the session check reads, is verified on 2.7.0 only), the sign-in sheet surviving a trip to the mail app for a confirmation or a reset, and an invite that passes the check (the test server has no licence; the 200 is the source's shape).
  - Tests:
    - `vikunja_logic`: the share and invite link parsers with round trips through `linkShareUrl`, the web's guest address, links inside text and the invite's fragment form. `vikunja_domain`: `Account.linkShare`, `LinkShareGone`, `InvitePreview`.
    - `vikunja_api`: the sign-in client, the claims, the gone interceptor (v1's 13003 through `normalizeError`, v2's 401 without a code only with a link-share token, 1027 left alone) and the invite port, on 21 responses recorded from 2.7.0 by `tool/capture_link_share_fixtures.dart` (hashes, tokens and passwords replaced).
    - the app: the registry codec (a guest round trip, an older build keeping the entry), `LinkShareStore` and its delete order, the guest session (renewal at half-life, expiry and a new sign-in, 13001 ending it, `LinkShareGone` on v1 and v2, de-duplication, 429, at most once headless, a sign-out during a renewal), the `AccountTokens` dispatch, accounts without tokens, a sign-out deleting a guest's secrets, the session check, the background runner skipping activity and settling guest sessions, no realtime for a guest, the guest read client storing the permission from one read and following the title, the redaction, `GuestSignIn` (the frontend and API host fallback, reopening, the secrets written before the session is replaced, a failed write), the guest's session-ended notification; widget tests for the overview's new actions and their pinning, the server notices, the invite flow, the guest entry by paste, by "Open a shared link" and by the share target, the password sheet (focus, keyboard, the password in no log, secure store or preference), the demo button sending no sign-in, the guest's Account tab, tile, header and banner, and every gate, an admin guest's project menu and the closed routes included; the accessibility smoke test with the new screens at 200 % text in dark mode, en and de. Each test of the review's fixes fails with its fix reverted.
    - contract tests on 2.7.0 with one fresh user per file, the share sign-ins paced for the server's limit: in `vikunja_api` on both API versions a read link without a password, a password link asking for it and refusing a wrong one, the guest reading only the shared project without its permission in the list, renewal, a broken token refused, a deleted link ending access (v1 13003, v2 401 without a code, renewal gone, a new sign-in 3006), the invite check's 404 code 2005 and the `/info` notice fields; in the app the acceptance: a read guest's syncs store the project read-only and `ProjectAccess` forbids writes, a write guest with its password edits a task through the outbox and the owner sees it, the token renewed through `POST /user/token`, and a deleted share ending the session, the syncs and the edit on both API versions.
    - in total 4,276 app tests, 332 in `vikunja_domain`, 3,163 in `vikunja_logic` and 1,663 in `vikunja_api` pass, skipped ones not counted; the contract runs against the server pass with 353 tests in `vikunja_api` and 198 in the app.
  - Checked on the Android emulator with the release build: a read-only link share of the project "Lesen" sent to Rurana through the Android share target from another app offered "Open as guest"; after the unencrypted-connection question the guest landed on the shared project, read only (the note, no New task, no favourite star, the checkbox off); the guest's Account tab showed the link avatar, "Guest · localhost:34527" and the note on what a read-only guest can do, without account settings or Organise; a second guest from a read & write share of the same project ticked a task done and the server showed it done; deleting that share on the server ended the guest's access with the "Guest access ended" banner and "Open the link again"; both guests were removed through "Remove guest access" (the confirmation names the guest and the server); on the server overview "Create account" and "Forgot password?" appeared under "Continue to sign-in" with the hint in the sign-in methods card, and "Forgot password?" opened the server's get-password-reset page in the browser. Not checked on the device: registering through the sign-in page (it needs a new account's password typed into the browser) and a password-protected share (covered by widget and contract tests).

## D63: Home-screen widgets from JSON snapshots the app writes through an in-repo plugin; Glance widgets on Android with Done through a headless Dart worker; a WidgetKit extension on iOS with an App Group outbox and no token

*Accepted 2026-10-27, steps 18 and 19 (iOS built; its device test comes at the end of the project). Carries out the home-screen widgets of [D20](#d20-scope-cuts-and-desktop-adaptations), which replace the desktop's quick-entry window, and keeps [D24](#d24-tokens-in-secure-storage-readable-after-the-first-unlock): the extension gets no keychain group and no token, so the App Group of [D15](#d15-app-id-devcoderoftimevikunja) is the only id derived for it. Adds one table to each account's database of [D31](#d31-one-drift-sqlite-file-per-account-shared-across-isolates-deleted-on-sign-out); a widget's Done goes through the outbox of [D34](#d34-outbox-local-first-writes-serial-sender-coalescing-retry-classes-sync-interplay-pending-fields-win-until-sent-pending-deletes-hidden-v1-full-replace-emulation) with the steps of the notification Done of [D39](#d39-local-notifications-a-scheduler-per-signed-in-account-plans-from-the-local-database-and-diffs-against-a-rolling-window-with-stable-keys-exact-alarms-when-allowed-done-through-the-outbox-from-a-background-isolate-without-a-token-refresh-snooze-local-only-permissions-asked-once-after-the-first-sync), now one shared function, and the background runs of [D40](#d40-background-sync-one-periodic-job-for-every-signed-in-account-with-a-deadline-skipped-while-the-app-is-in-front-the-incremental-run-as-the-session-keep-alive-a-one-off-drain-after-a-background-done-server-notifications-synced-into-a-local-inbox-on-their-own-cadence-new-unread-ones-as-activity-notifications-the-first-run-silent) import and publish too. Widget links use the app scheme of [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme) and switch accounts through the switcher of [D37](#d37-account-switcher-a-sheet-from-an-avatar-button-and-an-accounts-section-a-switch-resets-the-navigation-the-most-recently-active-account-follows-a-removal-inactive-outboxes-drain-in-the-foreground). The snapshots use the display settings, translated names and smart lists of [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner), the stored permission of [D60](#d60-labels-managed-online-and-applied-locally-teams-project-shares-and-link-shares-online-only-the-project-permission-stored-and-every-screen-shaped-by-it) and the guest rules of [D62](#d62-create-account-password-reset-and-invite-links-through-the-browser-sign-in-page-link-share-guests-as-an-account-kind-with-token-renewal-and-a-reduced-ui); the widgets are styled after [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list).*

- **Context:**
  - The owner postponed the widgets on 2026-10-13 until after the web-parity phase. On 2026-10-26, after accepting step 32, they took them up, before step 33, which moved to the back.
  - What existed: a notification's Done from a background isolate without a token refresh (D39), the background runs (D40), task links on the app scheme whose relay drops every other host (D44), the step 13 "New task" shortcut, the App Group id reserved by D15, and D24's rule that extensions get no keychain group.
  - Verified platform facts (research of 2026-10-26, with a Glance probe and an iOS spike built here):
    - **home_widget 0.10.0** (2026-09-17), the common Flutter package for widgets: its Android configuration flow starts a second `FlutterActivity`, so a second engine and bootstrap on the same databases and session managers; its Android worker reports success before the Dart callback has finished; issue #318 (the callback not running after the app was killed) is open; `saveWidgetData` commits SharedPreferences on the platform thread (ANR issues #438 and #401). On iOS its interactivity worker never completes in the extension's process, so only `WidgetCenter.reloadTimelines` would have been used.
    - **Glance 1.2.0** (stable since 2026-08-26) needs the Compose compiler plugin at the Kotlin version (2.4.0) and `buildFeatures.compose`; the plugin alone fails the build, because it wants the Compose runtime that Glance brings. With Glance's own transitive versions R8 removed its RemoteViews translator; with WorkManager 2.11.2, which the app already has through workmanager, it stays. Text sizes in `sp` become RemoteViews SP sizes, so the system font scale applies. A probe grew by about 0.85 MB per ABI.
    - **Android:** from Android 12 a widget can be placed without its configuration step (`configuration_optional`) and set up later (`reconfigurable`). Android 15 and later grey out a force-stopped app's widgets until the app is opened again; on Android 14 and earlier an activity tap still works after a force-stop.
    - **iOS:** a widget button's `AppIntent` runs in the extension's process. The xcodeproj gem, which comes with CocoaPods, adds a WidgetKit target without the Xcode GUI; its embed phase must come before Flutter's "Thin Binary" phase. The simulator signs locally with simulated App Group entitlements; `--no-codesign` embeds none, so it only proves that the code compiles. Access tokens live 600 s. Whether a free Personal Team may use App Groups is unverified.
    - **Shared files:** Apple's TN2408 names atomic writes of flat files as the safe way to share container data; a process suspended while it holds a lock is killed.
- **Decision:**
  - **Owner decisions of 2026-10-26:** both platforms now. Android is checked on the emulator; iOS is built now (the simulator build) and tested on a device at the end of the project.
  - **Data: the app writes, the widgets only draw** (`app/lib/core/widgets/`):
    - Kinds (`WidgetKind`): Today (overdue and due today, like the Overview's first sections, and like the Overview through its saved filter when one is picked), Upcoming 3, 7 or 14 days (the overdue tasks and those due within the days), Overdue, Favourites, Project (its first list view's filter and the sort last left there, done tasks hidden), Label (its open tasks through the filter engine), Saved filter (the engine of D51; not for guests), and the New task button.
    - One snapshot per shown list (`WidgetSnapshot`), versioned JSON with dates as epoch milliseconds: the state (`ok`, `needsReauth`, `guestEnded`, `accountRemoved`, `gone`), title and subtitle, `generatedAt`, `validUntil` (the next local midnight at the latest) and `changesAt` (the due instants inside the range), at most 30 rows and the count of the rest, the empty and message texts, and the links. A row has the task id, title, a day-level due label with its tone (`overdue`, `today`, `later`, `none`), the priority, the project with its colour, whether Done is offered (the stored permission, read-only and archived projects without it), a screen-reader label and its link. A fingerprint that ignores `generatedAt` keeps unchanged content from being written again.
    - Keys are `<accountId>.<part>` (`today`, `overdue`, `favorites`, `quick_add`, `upcoming_<days>`, `project_<id>`, `label_<id>`, `filter_<id>`): safe file names, and the account first, so a sign-out finds its files.
    - One catalog per account (`WidgetCatalog`) for the native pickers: the account's name, host and guest flag, the lists with their keys, the projects without archived ones (nested ones with their parents' path, and whether they take tasks), labels and saved filters. `host.json` holds the active account and the "Use system colours" switch.
    - Dart formats every text with the widget account's own display settings and translated names (`WidgetTexts`): due labels that stay right until midnight ("Today, 14:30", "Tomorrow", a weekday, a date) or the absolute format picked on the web, never "in 2 hours", which goes stale between updates. The device's 24-hour switch and the locale are noted for headless isolates.
    - No file holds a token or other secret. They hold task titles, which the home screen shows.
  - **The in-repo plugin** `plugins/rurana_widgets` (Dart, Kotlin and Swift; SwiftPM, no podspec): a workspace member and a path dependency of `app`. `packages/*` stay pure Dart ([D1](#d1-flutter-3475-pub-workspace-with-pure-dart-packages)); the purity check covers only `packages/*`.
    - The files are read and written in Dart (`dart:io`, a temp file and a rename). Kotlin and Swift only report the directories, list the placed Android widgets, redraw and pin.
    - `takeActions()` and `ackActions(ids)` serve both platforms (the spec had an iOS-only outbox call): entries stay until acknowledged, so the caller de-duplicates by id.
    - A redraw is an explicit `ACTION_APPWIDGET_UPDATE` broadcast to the two receivers by class name, so the plugin needs no Glance dependency. On iOS `reloadTimelines(ofKind:)` for both kinds.
    - `requestPin(provider, snapshotKey, config)`: the launcher's pin callback writes the new widget's config natively.
    - `MemoryRuranaWidgets` is the fake for app tests.
    - Its Gradle file has no `buildscript` or `allprojects` block (the repositories come from the app's settings), so the build's warning about plugins that apply the Kotlin Gradle plugin themselves still lists only cronet_http, flutter_web_auth_2 and workmanager_android.
  - **Publishing:**
    - One `WidgetPublisher` per signed-in account while the shell runs, like the notification schedulers. It watches the account's tables, its registry entry and the sync state, publishes 1 s after the last change and at most 3 s after the first, again at midnight while the app runs and on resume, writes only what changed and redraws only the widgets that show it. A change in the app reaches a widget within 5 s.
    - It writes the lists on screen (Android: the configured keys plus the active account's Today for a widget not set up; iOS: the request markers the extension leaves) and, on iOS, the smart lists and the New task button always. `publish(config)` writes one list although no widget shows it yet: Save on the setup page and "Add" before the pin use it.
    - The background runs import the widgets' taps before the accounts' turns (the turns drain them) and publish after each account's turn, an account that needs a new sign-in included.
    - A sign-out or removal deletes the account's snapshots, catalog and waiting taps through the account data wiper; the widgets still on screen say "Account removed".
  - **Done from a widget, one function for notifications and widgets** (`ExternalDone` in `core/outbox/`): mark the task done through the outbox (a temporary id followed to the server's), cancel its notifications, redraw the widgets before anything waits for the network, drain for at most 20 s with session managers that never refresh (a rejected token queues the one-off drain of D40 on Android), plan the notifications again and redraw once more. A notification's Done is otherwise unchanged; it now also redraws the widgets.
    - **Exactly once:** `WidgetTapImport` queues each tap's Done and records its id in `imported_widget_actions` in one transaction of the account's database, so a tap read again after a crash between that commit and the deletion of its file is skipped, and a repeating task never moves twice. Taps of an unknown account are dropped. Then the taps are acknowledged, which deletes them, and the widgets redrawn; acknowledging first would bring the row back. Recorded ids are kept for 30 days. The same table covers Android's queue.
    - **Android:** the Glance checkbox runs `MarkDoneAction`. An untick does nothing. `WidgetActionQueue.add` refuses a row already hidden: queued, claimed, or pending from a newer list than the one the row came from, so a repeating task can be ticked again once a newer list shows it. A tap rewrites the whole `widget_actions.jsonl` (a temp file and a rename, under an in-process lock) instead of appending, because the app claims the queue by renaming it, and an append could land in a file the app has already read and be lost; a rewrite can at worst duplicate a tap, which the table drops. The row hides at once (`android-pending.json`) and `WidgetActionsWorker` (unique work, `APPEND_OR_REPLACE`, expedited on Android 12+) starts a headless engine with `widgetActionsMain` from `core/widgets/widget_entry_points.dart` (re-exported by `main.dart`, so the AOT build keeps it), waits up to 3 minutes for `finished` on `dev.coderoftime.vikunja/widget_worker`, redraws and destroys the engine. The Dart side builds the notification action's container, imports the taps and publishes every account.
    - The same worker runs with an empty queue only to publish: once per key and process when a widget finds no snapshot (placed from the launcher while the app is closed), and once per `validUntil` when it draws a stale one (after midnight). An inexact alarm that does not wake the device redraws at the earliest `changesAt` or `validUntil`, so a task due today turns overdue on time.
    - **iOS:** `MarkDoneIntent` (`Button(intent:)`, iOS 17), compiled only into the extension, writes one file per tap, `outbox/<uuid>.json` with the account id, the task id and `createdAt` in whole milliseconds (Foundation's milliseconds carried a fraction, which made the plugin drop the file as malformed until the integration pass; the plugin now also rounds one), as `<uuid>.json.tmp` and a rename (the plugin skips `.tmp` files; Foundation's atomic write picks temp names we do not control). It records the task as hidden in `ios-pending.json` and reloads the timeline; a second tap on the task while its file exists is ignored. No network, no keychain, no SQLite, no lock. The app imports the outbox on start, on resume and in every background run.
    - **No `widget/done` link** (found in the review): the planned foreground fallback is gone, because any app, and on iOS any web page, can open the app scheme and the account id (`sha256(serverRoot|userId)`, first 32 hex characters) can be derived, so a link that writes would let others mark tasks done. After a force-stop on Android 14 or earlier the row's tap opens the task, which the user marks done in the app; a tap that reached the queue is imported at the next app start. A later fallback may open the task sheet, never write.
    - The acceptance is reworded: Done from the widget works with the app in the background or swiped away; after a force-stop on Android 14 or earlier the row opens the task, which is marked done in the app; on Android 15 and later the launcher greys out a force-stopped app's widgets until the app is opened again (platform behaviour).
