On iOS, Done with the app terminated reaches the server at the next app run, in front or in the background, which step 19's acceptance allows; the extension never touches SQLite or the network.
  - **Links and launch:** widget links are `<scheme>://widget/…`: `task/<id>?account=`, `new-task[?account=][&project=]`, `list?account=&kind=[&id=][&days=]`, `account?account=` (a session that ended), `open[?account=]` and `configure?appWidgetId=` (`core/links/widget_link.dart`). No link writes.
    - `DeepLinkRelay` passes `widget` links to a source of their own (`widgetLinks`); every other host except `task` is still dropped.
    - `WidgetLinkListener` switches to the widget's account without asking, unlike D44's task links, because a widget belongs to one of the user's own accounts and a link can reach nothing else; an unknown account or a malformed link is dropped. It opens the task sheet (or "This task is gone"), the New task sheet (`TaskOpener.requestNewTask`, shared with the step 13 shortcut), the Overview, Upcoming, a project, Favourites, a saved filter, a label's tasks, the account's Account tab or the setup page.
    - `MainActivity` is exported, as the launcher activity always was, so another app can send it a widget link too; it can only switch among the user's own accounts and open a screen.
  - **Widgets page and setup page** (`features/widgets/`):
    - The Account tab gets a "Home screen" section (on this device) with "Widgets", which opens `/home/widgets`: what a widget shows, the steps for Android or iOS, a note that the home screen shows task titles, the placed Android widgets (one not set up yet included) with their setup, and on Android 8+ where the launcher can pin, "Add a widget" with Today, Upcoming 7 days, Overdue, Favourites and the New task button (`requestPinAppWidget` through the plugin, the list published first). A project, label or saved filter is chosen in the widget's setup afterwards. A launcher that declines is a note on the page, announced.
    - The setup page is `/home/widgets/configure?appWidgetId=…`, not `/widgets/configure`, so it waits behind the sign-in, the first sync and the notification setup like every page; native code reaches it through the `widget/configure` link, because a plain route path would be lost on a cold start. It offers the account (with several), the list, the days, or a project, label or saved filter from a picker (no saved filters for a guest), with a live preview; Save writes the config and publishes. Opened from the launcher (`&from=widget`), Save then moves the app to the back through `moveTaskToBack` on the device channel; `SystemNavigator.pop()` would finish the activity and lose its state.
  - **Android widgets** (Glance, `app/android/.../widgets/`):
    - Two widgets: the list (`TasksWidget`, 3 × 2 cells by default, resizable, `reconfigurable|configuration_optional`: Android 12+ places it showing the active account's Today until it is set up) and the New task button (`QuickAddWidget`, 2 × 1).
    - `SizeMode.Responsive` with four layouts; the smallest shows only the header, centred. The header: title, subtitle, the count and "+" for a new task. Rows are at least 48 dp with a 48 dp checkbox where Done is offered, the title and a line with the due label in its tone's colour (an overdue date with a warning icon, so not by colour alone), the priority and the project with its colour dot. "+N more", empty and message states and a sign-in banner; on a configured widget, "Account removed" and "gone" open its setup. Every text size is in `sp`; a count without its spoken label reads a native plural. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the project follows a folder tinted with its colour, `onSurfaceVariant` without one (on iOS the `folder` symbol, `.secondary` without one); no row's label reads an identifier.*
    - The state definition reads the files on every update, so a new snapshot shows at once; removing a widget deletes its config.
    - Theme: the system colours on Android 12+ unless the app's switch is off, else the brand colours of D56 in light and dark.
    - `WidgetConfigureActivity` (exported for the launcher's configuration step) checks that the widget id is the app's, sets `RESULT_OK` and opens `widget/configure` in `MainActivity` in the app's own task (`NEW_TASK`, `SINGLE_TOP`, `CLEAR_TOP`), like `TaskLinkActivity`; no second Flutter engine. Taps follow only the app's own widget links.
    - Static preview layouts and images serve every version; no generated previews for Android 15+.
    - Fixed strings (picker name and description, a few states) in `res/values(-de)/strings.xml`; everything else comes translated in the snapshot.
  - **New native dependencies (dependency note):** `androidx.glance:glance-appwidget:1.2.0` (Apache-2.0) and the Compose compiler plugin `org.jetbrains.kotlin.plugin.compose` 2.4.0, which must equal the Kotlin version, with `buildFeatures.compose`; `androidx.work:work-runtime:2.11.2`, already in the app through workmanager and now declared, because the worker needs it to compile and below 2.11.2 R8 stripped Glance's translator; no `glance-material3`, which pulls in Material 3 for Compose. Test only, never shipped: `junit:junit:4.13.2` and `org.json:json:20260814`, because the JVM tests parse with the real org.json while android.jar has stubs (the first draft's 20180813 is flagged by scanners for CVE-2022-45688 and CVE-2023-5072). `app/android/app/proguard-rules.pro`, referenced from the release build type, keeps every `ActionCallback`'s no-argument constructor, which Glance calls by reflection.
  - **iOS extension** `app/ios/RuranaWidgets/` (plain Swift; no Flutter, no plugin, no SQLite, no network):
    - Bundle id `dev.coderoftime.vikunja.RuranaWidgets`, iOS 17.0; the app stays at 15.0. App Group `group.dev.coderoftime.vikunja` in the new `Runner/Runner.entitlements` and in `RuranaWidgets.entitlements`.
    - Added by `tool/add_widget_extension.rb`, committed and idempotent (xcodeproj gem; the embed phase before "Thin Binary"; it keeps the file list in sync, deleted languages included, and prints nothing secret).
    - Two kinds: `RuranaTasks`, an `AppIntentConfiguration` with the account, the list kind and its days, project, label or saved filter from the catalogs (a nested project shows "in *parent*"), and `RuranaQuickAdd` with the account and an optional project that takes tasks. A new widget shows the app's active account (`host.json`), not the first one.
    - The extension takes every snapshot key from the catalog and never builds one. It leaves an empty marker `requests/<key>` for each list it shows, so the app knows which project, label or saved filter lists to publish, and removes a marker older than 24 h whose widget is gone.
    - Timeline entries now, at every `changesAt` and at `validUntil`, reloaded `.after(validUntil)`; a task due today turns overdue on time without the app.
    - Small, medium, large and the iPad's extra large, and three lock-screen sizes; dark mode, tinted rendering and Dynamic Type (fewer rows rather than smaller text; the header stays and "+N more", a native string, counts every row not shown).
    - Fixed strings in `en.lproj` and `de.lproj` `Localizable.strings`.
  - **Schema v10:** `imported_widget_actions` (`id` primary key, `imported_at`) in each account's database; the migration creates it, with nothing to migrate.
  - **`core/` imports `features/` for the first time:** the snapshot builder reuses the Overview's grouping, the project list model, the project tree and access, the default project, the display settings, the date formatter and the task meta texts, rather than moving them out of the features.
  - **Logging:** no task title, snapshot or link is logged; Dart logs counts and failure types, Kotlin failure types.
  - **The parked session hardening:** the package reserved as D59 (not in this log) is still parked in a stash. Its six auth files, `session_manager.dart`, `token_store.dart`, `browser_sign_in.dart`, `auth_gateways.dart`, `secure_store.dart` and `oauth_token_client.dart`, were not touched. `background_sync_runner.dart` (the import and the publishing) and `repository_providers.dart` (the wipe step) are in its file list and changed here in small hunks, so applying the package will conflict there.
  - **CI:** the test job also runs the plugin's tests, the Android build job the Kotlin unit tests (verified locally; the CI run itself is untested).
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **home_widget:** a second Flutter engine for its Android configuration screen on the same databases, a worker that reports success before Dart finishes, the open issues #318, #438 and #401, and on iOS only `reloadTimelines` would be used.
  - **A token mirror for the iOS extension**, as the research proposed (the access token in the App Group's keychain group, a direct `PATCH` from the extension): access tokens live 600 s and the extension must never refresh, so the mirror would rarely be valid; it would amend D24 for a second copy of a secret; the next app run meets step 19's acceptance.
  - **SQLite in the App Group**, read by the extension: a database shared across processes with its locks (a suspended process holding one is killed), the extension's memory limit, and a second reader of the schema in Swift.
  - **A second Flutter engine for the Android configuration**, as home_widget does it: a second bootstrap on the same databases and session managers, which `TaskLinkActivity` exists to avoid. A native hop into `MainActivity` does it.
  - **A `widget/done` link** as the Android fallback after a force-stop: any app or web page could mark tasks done.
  - **Appending to the Android queue:** a tap could be lost to the app's claim by rename.
  - **Formatting dates and texts natively:** two more copies of the display settings and the Overview's day rules, which would drift from Dart.
  - **Relative due labels** ("in 2 hours"): stale between two updates.
  - **Refreshing through `GlanceAppWidgetManager` in the plugin:** a Glance dependency in the plugin; a broadcast to the receivers does the same.
  - **Glance 1.3.0-alpha** (compileSdk 37, AGP 9.2) and **`glance-material3`** (Material 3 for Compose).
  - **Generated previews for Android 15+:** the static preview serves every version.
  - **Confirming the account switch for widget links**, as D44 does for task links: a widget shows one of the user's own accounts; a question on every tap would be noise, as it would be for a notification tap.
- **Consequences:**
  - The home screen shows one list of an account on Android and iOS, and a change in the app reaches it within seconds. On Android a tick reaches the server through the worker within seconds with the app in the background or swiped away; on iOS at the next app run or background run.
  - The app writes a few small files after each change; no request is added.
  - Not built: editing tasks from a widget beyond Done; a widget per comment or notification; Live Activities; iOS Control Center controls; a token in the extension; configuring an iOS widget from inside the app (iOS has no API for it; Edit Widget on the home screen does it); lock-screen widgets on Android; a project for the Android New task button (the iOS one has one); lists across accounts; generated previews for Android 15+; the iOS 27 extra-large portrait size; the app lock (step 35).
  - Known gaps:
    - iOS lists are not refreshed while the app is closed beyond the background runs; a list configured while the app is closed shows "Open Rurana to load this list" until the app or a background run publishes it.
    - On iOS, day labels like "Tomorrow" can be stale after midnight until the next publish; the switch from today to overdue happens on time.
    - Task titles show on the home screen, where anyone who sees the phone can read them, until the app lock of step 35.
    - Unticking is no undo: the first tick marks the task done; the widget offers no Undo.
    - Once Android 12+'s quota for expedited work is used up, the worker runs as ordinary work, maybe later, and the system may stop it early; the tap stays queued for the next worker run or app start.
    - Below Android 12, a change of dark mode keeps the old colours until the next redraw.
    - A notification's Done can be sent before the widgets' redraw, because `setDone` starts its own drain; the order does not matter.
    - iOS still reads a bare number where a count has no spoken label; Android has a native plural. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): closed: the extension's `Localizable.stringsdict` (en, de) has `%lld tasks` for a count without the app's label, and `+%lld more` is a plural now.*
    - Left for the iOS device test: the configuration fields per list kind, the project picker following the chosen account, the extension seeing which widgets are placed, and the large widget's "+N more" layout (only compiled). The device test needs a development team on both targets with the App Group registered; a free Personal Team may not allow App Groups. A lock-screen release build may need `DEAD_CODE_STRIPPING=NO` (a known WidgetKit issue). *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): the Tasks widget showed real data from the App Group on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator (iOS 26.5), and a Done from it reached the app; the simulator signs the App Group itself, so the team's App Group, `DEAD_CODE_STRIPPING` and the rest of this list wait for a signed build and the device test.*
  - Tests:
    - the app: the kinds, keys, snapshot and catalog JSON (versioned, epoch milliseconds, a reader refusing another version, the fingerprint); the builder per kind on seeded data (Today like the Overview, the next day at local midnight, Upcoming with the overdue pinned, Overdue outside archived projects, a label and a saved filter through the engine, Favourites, read-only and archived projects without Done, a project gone, an ended session, 30 rows and the rest counted, the absolute date format, calendar days across the end of daylight saving); the catalog (archived projects left out, nested projects named, no saved filters for a guest); the publisher (a second of quiet, at most 3 s, within 5 s, nothing rewritten unchanged, stale snapshots at midnight, a sign-out leaving "Account removed", an isolate without a publisher); the shared Done (the notification handler's 11 tests unchanged, an expired token queuing the drain); the import (once each, read again after a crash before the acknowledgement, a failed write kept, a task gone, the redraw after the acknowledgement); the worker's entry point and container; the plugin-backed host; the links (parsing, the relay, while running, on a cold start, during the first sync, across accounts, a removed account, a `widget/done` link neither switching nor writing); the widgets page and the setup page (Android and iOS, a launcher that cannot pin or declines, placed widgets, a widget not set up, Save from the launcher, guests, pickers, a failed save); the accessibility smoke test with both pages at 200 % text in dark mode on a small phone, en and de; the v9 to v10 migration and the schema at v10; two background runner tests. Each test of the review's fixes fails with its fix taken out.
    - the plugin: 20 tests over a fake channel, an outbox file in the extension's exact shape and a fractional `createdAt` among them.
    - Kotlin: 28 JVM tests (`WidgetModelTest`, `WidgetActionQueueTest`, `ListContentTest`). iOS: the extension compiles in `flutter build ios --simulator --debug` and `--no-codesign`, and `xcode_project_test.dart` passes.
    - in total 4,392 app tests (198 contract tests skipped), 332 in `vikunja_domain`, 3,163 in `vikunja_logic`, 1,663 in `vikunja_api`, 20 in `rurana_widgets` and 28 in Kotlin pass; the contract runs against the server pass with 353 tests in `vikunja_api` and 198 in the app. The arm64 release APK is 33.2 MB.
  - Checked on the Android emulator (API 36, release build): the Widgets page under the Account tab ("Home screen" section) with the kinds and "Add a widget"; "Add" on Today opened the launcher's pin dialog with the widget preview and placed a 3 × 2 Today widget; it lists the overdue and today tasks of the active account with a warning icon on overdue dates, priority and the project with its colour, and the count in the header; ticking "Buch zurückgeben" in the widget hid the row, the worker ran the Dart entry point and the server showed the task done; a tap on a row opened the task sheet in the app; "Mark as done" there removed the row from the widget by the next screenshot, 7 s after leaving the app; after the tasks were reopened on the server the widget showed them again after the app's sync; the New task widget (2 × 1) opened the New task sheet. Found and fixed there: the list widget showed only its header, because the header's title column used `fillMaxHeight` inside a row of wrapped height, which stretched the header over the whole widget and left the list 0 high (it now wraps, and the smallest layout centres its header); and R8 removed the no-argument constructor of the Glance action callback that Glance creates by reflection, so a tick failed with `NoSuchMethodException` (now kept by `proguard-rules.pro`). Not checked on the device: the setup page from a launcher's reconfigure, the fallback after a force-stop on Android 14 or earlier, dark mode and font scaling (covered by tests), and everything on iOS (only built: `flutter build ios --simulator --debug` with the RuranaWidgets extension; the owner decided the device test comes at the end of the project).

## D64: An app lock over the UI, not encryption, with biometrics or the device's screen lock; device privacy settings for the app switcher, screenshots, notification content and widget titles

*Accepted 2026-10-27, step 35a (Android checked on the emulator; the iOS device test comes at the end of the project). Keeps [D24](#d24-tokens-in-secure-storage-readable-after-the-first-unlock): the lock is a gate over the UI, and the tokens stay readable after the first device unlock, so the background runs of [D40](#d40-background-sync-one-periodic-job-for-every-signed-in-account-with-a-deadline-skipped-while-the-app-is-in-front-the-incremental-run-as-the-session-keep-alive-a-one-off-drain-after-a-background-done-server-notifications-synced-into-a-local-inbox-on-their-own-cadence-new-unread-ones-as-activity-notifications-the-first-run-silent), the notification actions of [D39](#d39-local-notifications-a-scheduler-per-signed-in-account-plans-from-the-local-database-and-diffs-against-a-rolling-window-with-stable-keys-exact-alarms-when-allowed-done-through-the-outbox-from-a-background-isolate-without-a-token-refresh-snooze-local-only-permissions-asked-once-after-the-first-sync) and the widget worker of [D63](#d63-home-screen-widgets-from-json-snapshots-the-app-writes-through-an-in-repo-plugin-glance-widgets-on-android-with-done-through-a-headless-dart-worker-a-widgetkit-extension-on-ios-with-an-app-group-outbox-and-no-token) keep working while the app is locked. Carries out the unlock on the notification Done that D39 left for the app lock, closes D63's known gap of task titles on the home screen, and gives the `FLAG_SECURE` of [D55](#d55-security-and-integration-settings-two-factor-status-sessions-and-tokens-in-the-app-a-new-token-shown-once-export-and-deletion-through-the-web) a switch of its own. Adds two columns to D39's `scheduled_notifications` in each account's database of [D31](#d31-one-drift-sqlite-file-per-account-shared-across-isolates-deleted-on-sign-out). The entry paths that wait behind the lock are the task links of [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme), the widget links of D63, the share target of [D53](#d53-attachments-uploaded-through-the-outbox-in-their-own-lane-opened-shared-and-saved-through-the-platform-cover-and-delete-online-and-an-own-android-share-target), the New task shortcut of step 13 and D39's notification taps. The settings page is open to the link-share guests of [D62](#d62-create-account-password-reset-and-invite-links-through-the-browser-sign-in-page-link-share-guests-as-an-account-kind-with-token-renewal-and-a-reduced-ui) and styled after [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list).*

- **Context:**
  - Roadmap step 35 was "Security hardening": threat model, app lock, TLS pinning, mTLS, custom headers, audits. On 2026-10-27 the owner split it into 35a (this entry), 35b (connection profiles per server) and 35c (audits and the threat model), with one commit and one acceptance each, and postponed the diagnostics export that [D20](#d20-scope-cuts-and-desktop-adaptations) placed in step 35.
  - What existed: the tokens in secure storage readable after the first unlock (D24) and unencrypted databases (D31); D39's note that the app lock may add `authenticationRequired` to the notification actions; D63's widgets with task titles on the home screen "until the app lock of step 35"; D55's `FLAG_SECURE` while a one-time token shows, set and cleared by that view alone; every outside entry into the UI going through `TaskOpener` or `ShareIntentListener`, which already wait for the sign-in, the first sync and the notification setup.
  - Verified platform facts (research of 2026-10-27, from the Flutter SDK and plugin sources and the platforms' documentation):
    - **local_auth 3.0.2** refuses a plain `FlutterActivity` (`NOT_FRAGMENT_ACTIVITY`): BiometricPrompt needs a `FragmentActivity`, and the plugin's README asks for an AppCompat theme at the app's minSdk 24. Its Android prompt texts (`AndroidAuthMessages`) live in `local_auth_android`.
    - **Prompts and the lifecycle:** below Android 11 the system's PIN screen stops the activity, and Face ID makes an iOS app inactive, so lifecycle events during a prompt are no real hide. A BiometricPrompt shown before the activity resumed risks fragment state errors, and one that reaches Android after the activity saved its state is dropped without an answer, after which local_auth reports `authInProgress` until the app restarts. LAContext fails while the app is inactive.
    - **Recents:** Android 13's `Activity.setRecentsScreenshotEnabled(false)` shows the window background in Recents while the user's own screenshots keep working. Below 13 only `FLAG_SECURE` or a cover drawn before the system takes the thumbnail hides the content, the cover on a best-effort basis.
    - **Notifications:** flutter_local_notifications 22.3.x has no public version of a notification; `NotificationVisibility.private` still shows the content title, which is the task title; `secret` keeps the notification off a secure lock screen. On iOS the app cannot force the lock-screen preview off; Settings → Notifications → Show Previews decides. `DarwinNotificationActionOption.authenticationRequired` makes an action ask for the device unlock.
    - **WidgetKit:** `.privacySensitive()` redacts a view on a locked device and in StandBy. A widget's `AppIntent` runs on a locked device too unless its `authenticationPolicy` requires authentication.
    - **System back:** `WidgetsBinding.handlePopRoute` asks the observers in the order they registered, and the router's back button dispatcher registers only when the router mounts, below `MaterialApp.router`'s `builder`. From Android 14 back can come as a predictive back gesture instead, which every observer is offered and each page route's gesture detector takes and pops on commit; no observer can veto it. With a `FlutterFragmentActivity`, a framework that reports `canHandlePop: false` turns off the engine's back callback, and Android handles back itself.
- **Decision:**
  - **Owner decisions of 2026-10-27:** the split of step 35; the lock's default timeout 1 minute; "Block screenshots" as a switch of its own, not tied to the lock; the diagnostics export later, not in step 35; the backup exclusion on both platforms in 35c.
  - **A gate over the UI, not encryption.** Data at rest stays as D24 and D31 describe. Nothing without a UI waits for the lock: the OAuth callback, the notification action isolate, the widget worker and the iOS widget outbox import, the background runs, the realtime connection and the schedulers. The threat model of 35c is to record this.
  - **Settings, per device** (`core/security/`): `PrivacySettings` with `appLock` (off), `lockAfter` (immediately, **1 minute** by default, 5 or 15 minutes, 1 hour), `blockScreenshots` (off; Android only), `notificationContent` (show everything, hide on the lock screen, never show task titles; show everything by default) and `widgetTitles` (on). One preferences key `privacy.settings`, JSON with `version: 1`: unknown fields are ignored and an unknown choice falls back to its default, but another version or a field of the wrong type makes the value unreadable. The same for every account; sign-out leaves it.
    - **Fail closed:** when the read throws or the value does not decode, the rest of the app gets the most private value of every switch for that launch (the lock on, Block screenshots on, no task titles in notifications or widgets), because the user may have chosen any of them; nothing is written back. The settings page then shows the defaults with a note, and saving overwrites. Until the settings are read the lock controller starts locked; `bootstrap.dart` awaits the read (at most 5 s) before `runApp`, so the first frame is already locked.
  - **Unlocking with what the device has** (`DeviceAuthenticator` over local_auth): biometrics or the device credential (`biometricOnly` off, so the screen lock stays a way in when biometrics lock out; `persistAcrossBackgrounding` off, because the lock prompts again on its own return; `sensitiveTransaction` off). The results: success; cancelled (by the user, the system or a timeout, and iOS's `false`); no credentials; locked out; a prompt still in progress; unavailable (logged by local_auth's code name only). Android's prompt has the app's name as title, a hint and the reason, all localized; iOS shows the reason, and `NSFaceIDUsageDescription` (en, de) explains Face ID.
    - **Android:** `MainActivity` extends `FlutterFragmentActivity`; `LaunchTheme` and `NormalTheme` get AppCompat parents (`Theme.AppCompat.Light.NoActionBar`, at night `Theme.AppCompat.NoActionBar`) and keep their items; the manifest declares `USE_BIOMETRIC`, which local_auth merges in anyway.
  - **When it locks** (`AppLockController`, one `AppLifecycleListener`):
    - Always after a cold start while the lock is on; "unlocked" is never stored.
    - When the app was out of sight for `lockAfter` or longer, by the wall clock (deep sleep counts), and whenever the clock went back. "Immediately" locks on the hide, so the first frame back is already locked. `isLockedNow()` evaluates the time out of sight at once, so a link or tap handled before the app's show event still waits.
    - While a prompt is up, lifecycle events neither lock nor cover; otherwise "Immediately" would lock again behind every PIN screen. Time out of sight during a prompt that then fails counts from the prompt's first hide. After a return from a prompt that hid the app, a prompt with no answer within 2 s counts as cancelled and its late answer is ignored.
    - It prompts on its own once per return while locked, only once the app has stayed resumed for 300 ms; a return within 3 s of a prompt cancelled out of sight does not prompt again, so a cancel cannot bring the prompt back in a loop. The lock screen's Unlock button prompts again.
    - Cancelled stays locked; locked out, a stuck prompt ("close Rurana completely and open it again") and unavailable stay locked with a message and the Unlock button.
    - **No screen lock any more:** when the device answers that it has no credentials, the app lock turns itself off, unlocks and says once "App lock was turned off because this device has no screen lock". Removing the screen lock takes the device credential, so this helps no attacker, while failing closed would leave the owner only a reinstall.
    - Turning the lock on and off needs a successful prompt (off also when the device has no screen lock any more); changing the time does not. Without a screen lock on the device a lock that is off cannot be turned on: the switch is disabled with "Set a screen lock on this device first", and the app asks the device again on every resume.
  - **Excursions don't relock** (`AppExcursions`, `AppRoundTrips`): the round trips the app starts itself do not lock it: the file, photo, camera and avatar pickers, Android's document picker, "save as", the share sheet (files and link shares), "open with", the browser sign-in, the notification permission request, the system's notification and exact-alarm pages and the launcher's pin dialog. `roundTrip` serves calls that answer when the user comes back and lasts until the app is resumed, because Android hands a picker's result over just before the resume and an excursion that ended out of sight would lock at once with "Immediately"; `launchAway` serves calls that answer as soon as the other screen opens and lasts until the app has left and come back, or ends at once when nothing opened. Nested and overlapping excursions are counted. One lasts **10 minutes** at most: a longer one counts as a hide from its start. External links and the web's settings pages are no excursion: the user may wander off there, so the normal timeout applies.
  - **The gate** (`AppLockGate`): the outermost widget of `MaterialApp.router`'s `builder`, around `NotificationTapListener` and the other listeners. The app stays mounted below without pointers, semantics, focus or tickers, so open sheets and unsaved edits survive; above it the opaque lock screen (the logo, "Rurana is locked", the error as a live region, Unlock in view at 200 % text) or the privacy cover. No router redirect, which would replace the pages below.
    - **Entry paths wait:** links, widget taps (the setup page's `widget/configure` included), the New task shortcut, shares and notification taps wait while `isLockedNow()` and open after the unlock, the last request winning as before; a share stays in Android's `ShareInbox` meanwhile.
    - **System back while locked** never pops the routes below. The gate's observer registers before the router's back button dispatcher, so a pop stops there and moves the app to the back. From Android 14, or with an unknown version, back can come as a predictive back gesture, so there the gate holds back the navigators' reports and tells the platform that the framework does not handle back, and `MainActivity`'s own back callback, switched on while locked, moves the task to the back. Before 14 back always arrives as a pop, so there the framework keeps handling it.
  - **App switcher and screenshots:**
    - Android 13 and later: `setRecentsScreenshotEnabled(false)` while the lock is on, through the device channel; no Flutter cover there, which would only blank the app whenever it loses focus (the notification shade, split screen).
    - Android 7 to 12 and iOS: the privacy cover (the logo on the launch background) while the app is inactive and the lock is on, not during a prompt or an excursion; best effort on Android, which the page says, pointing to Block screenshots.
    - **Block screenshots** (Android only): `FLAG_SECURE` on the whole window, out of screenshots, screen recordings and Recents. `SecureDisplay` is reference-counted: the window is secure while the switch is on or any `SecureHold` is held, which D55's one-time secret view now takes, so neither clears the other's protection.
  - **Notification content** (reminders, due dates and activity notifications):
    - **Show everything:** as before.
    - **Hide on the lock screen:** Android posts them with `NotificationVisibility.secret`, so nothing of them appears on a secure lock screen. iOS cannot be forced by the app: the page says to set Show Previews to When Unlocked and opens the app's notification settings.
    - **Never show task titles:** generic texts on both platforms, "Task reminder", "Task due" and "New activity" with "Open Rurana to see it", without a project, identifier, name, comment or account label. The payloads stay, so a tap and Done still work. The "Sign in again" notification holds no task data and stays as it is.
    - A change of the content or of the app lock reschedules every account (`reconcile(force: true)`: every pending notification cancelled and scheduled again, the snoozes kept). Schema version 11 records each scheduled row's privacy (`hidden_on_lock_screen`, `done_needs_unlock`, migrated from version 4 on with false, what the OS was told before) and the diff compares it like the texts, so an entry another isolate scheduled with older settings is scheduled again by the next run; headless containers read the settings anew for every run. A change to a content that shows less also withdraws the account's delivered reminders and activity notifications; nothing is posted again.
    - **iOS:** every `initialize` registers `task` and `task_locked`; in `task_locked` Done has `authenticationRequired` and the snoozes stay plain (they only move a local notification, D39). Notifications use `task_locked` while the app lock is on. Android cannot ask for the unlock per action with this plugin; with "Hide on the lock screen" the actions are not on the secure lock screen at all.
  - **Widgets:** "Show task titles in widgets", on by default. Off, a list's snapshot keeps its title, count, links and "+" but has no rows, no "+N more" and no due instants, and a non-empty list shows the count and "Open Rurana to see them" where the rows were; both renderers draw that without a change, and without rows there is no Done from the widget. The publishers republish when the switch changes, and while it is off each run deletes the account's snapshots outside the current set (of a widget removed meanwhile, of a list an iOS widget picks next). In the iOS extension `.privacySensitive()` follows the accessibility labels of the rows, the small widget and the Done button, so that the labels, which name the task, are redacted too, and `MarkDoneIntent` has `authenticationPolicy = .requiresAuthentication`, both always. Android widgets are home-screen only, so their Done stays as it is.
  - **Settings UI** (`features/privacy/`): a "Privacy" section on the Account tab (on this device, between Notifications and Appearance) with "App lock and privacy" and "App lock: Off" or "App lock: On · after 1 minute"; `/home/privacy`, open to guests. The page: the app lock switch, "Lock after" while it is on ("Rurana always locks after a restart"), a line on the app switcher (best effort on Android 12 and older or an unknown version), Block screenshots on Android, the three notification choices (on iOS with the hint and the settings button for "Hide on the lock screen"), the widget titles switch; failures under the switches, announced.
  - **Dependencies (dependency note):** `local_auth: ^3.0.2` (BSD-3-Clause), resolved to local_auth 3.0.2 with local_auth_android 2.3.0 and local_auth_darwin 2.0.5, the dependency planned in [research/flutter-stack.md](research/flutter-stack.md) §3.16; `local_auth_android: ^2.3.0` named directly for `AndroidAuthMessages`, the localized Android prompt title and hint, at the version local_auth already pulls in, so nothing new enters `pubspec.lock`; `androidx.appcompat:appcompat:1.8.0` (Apache-2.0) declared in the Android build, because biometric 1.1.0 would otherwise pull in 1.2.0. local_auth_android 2.3.0 and local_auth_darwin 2.0.5 were less than two weeks old when the step was built.
  - **Logging:** no settings value, prompt text or task title is logged: failures by type, local_auth's by its code name, an unreadable value by the type of the error.
  - **The parked session hardening:** the six auth files of the package reserved as D59 were not touched. `core/auth/auth_providers.dart` changed in one line (the browser sign-in gets the excursions), which will conflict when the package is applied.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **A lock route behind a router redirect:** it replaces the pages below, so open sheets and unsaved edits would be lost, and every entry path would race it.
  - **Encrypting the data behind the lock** (a database key released by biometrics): the background runs, notification actions and the widget worker could not work while the app is locked, and D24 would change.
  - **A monotonic clock for the timeout:** it stops in deep sleep, so a phone asleep overnight would come back unlocked.
  - **Ending an excursion when the call answers:** Android delivers a picker's result just before the resume, which with "Immediately" locks right after every picker, and a settings page or "open with" answers before the user has even left.
  - **Blocking predictive back from Dart:** no observer can veto the gesture, so from Android 14 the activity's own callback moves the task to the back.
  - **`NotificationVisibility.private`:** it still shows the content title, the task title, and the plugin has no public version to put in its place.
  - **Clearing the stored plan rows on a forced reschedule**, as the spec had it: the rows hold the snoozes.
  - **`FLAG_SECURE` tied to the lock:** it also blocks the user's own screenshots; the owner wanted a switch of its own.
  - **The Flutter cover on Android 13 and later:** the system already leaves the app out of Recents, and the cover would blank the app at every loss of focus.
  - **Staying locked when the device has no screen lock any more:** the owner could only reinstall, while an attacker gains nothing from the rule.
  - **`biometricOnly`:** biometrics lock out after failed attempts; the screen lock must remain a way in.
- **Consequences:**
  - Rurana can sit behind the device's screen lock or biometrics: locked after every restart and after the chosen time away, with links, widget taps, the shortcut, shares and notification taps waiting behind the lock, and pickers, the camera, the browser sign-in, the share sheet, settings pages and the pin dialog not locking it for up to 10 minutes. The lock guards the screen against someone holding an unlocked phone, not the data at rest.
  - Notifications and widgets can stay off the lock screen and the home screen; on iOS a Done from a locked notification or widget asks for the unlock.
  - Known gaps:
    - On Android 12 and older the app switcher cover is best effort, and there and on iOS the cover also shows whenever the app becomes inactive (the notification shade, split screen, Control Center).
    - With settings that could not be read, the page and the Account tab show the defaults ("off") although the app is locked for that launch; the page's note says why.
    - Once Android has dropped a prompt silently, local_auth stays in progress until the app restarts; the lock screen says so.
    - With "Immediately", cancelling the PIN screen of the turn-off prompt on Android 7 to 10 locks the app.
    - A background run that started before a change finishes with the old settings; the next run repairs it.
    - Switching back to "Show everything" does not bring withdrawn notifications back.
    - A launcher that answers the pin request but shows nothing leaves the excursion open: no lock for up to 10 minutes.
    - Since 2026-10-28 the activity keeps its engine when Android relaunches it (system colours, contrast, wallpaper): with "Immediately", such a relaunch while the app is in sight (for example the wallpaper changed from the other half of split screen) locks and prompts, as the cold start it replaced did.
    - The Android 7 to 10 paths around the PIN screen are covered by unit tests only.
  - Not built: the iOS device test, which comes at the end of the project (Face ID and the passcode, the cover in the app switcher, `task_locked`'s Done and `authenticationPolicy` asking for the unlock, the redaction in StandBy and of the VoiceOver labels); a native `SceneDelegate` cover on iOS, the fallback should the Flutter cover come too late for the app switcher's snapshot; the diagnostics export (later, not in step 35); the test notification on the Account tab, which ignores the privacy settings (visibility and iOS category); per-account settings; a PIN of the app's own. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator (iOS 26.5) the lock with Face ID worked (turned on, "Immediately", unlocked with a matching face), and the Dart cover showed in the app switcher, so no `SceneDelegate` cover is needed so far; the passcode, Done asking for the unlock and the redaction wait for the device test.*
  - Found in the acceptance on the Android emulator and fixed:
    - Under the AppCompat theme Android drew its grey default focus highlight over the focused `FlutterView`, so about 7 % grey over the whole app, as soon as a key press left touch mode, and kept it. `MainActivity` now turns `defaultFocusHighlightEnabled` off on the `FlutterView` (in `onPostResume`, API 26 and later).
    - On Android 14 and later the gate leaves back to the platform while locked, and when the task was not started from the launcher (a task link, a notification, a share) Android then closed the activity and its state. `MainActivity` now has its own `OnBackPressedCallback`, switched on by the gate while locked (the device channel's `setBackMovesTaskToBack`, `AppBackgrounding.setBackMovesToBack`), which moves the task to the back.
  - Tests: the settings' JSON and store (unknown fields and choices, another version, an unreadable value failing closed); the controller with a fake clock (a hide and a show, the clock moved back, "Immediately", a cold start, the settings loading, the prompt guard, time out of sight during a prompt, a silent prompt, the prompt loop, excursions nested and past 10 minutes, no credentials turning the lock off, locked out and cancelled staying locked); the authenticator's mapping and prompt texts; `SecureDisplay`'s holds; the round trips and every excursion call site, the pin dialog included; the gate (content covered, semantics and focus excluded, pointers absorbed, back not popping the route below with a pop and with a predictive back gesture, the platform told while locked, the cover on `inactive` but not during a prompt or an excursion); `TaskOpener` and shares waiting and running after the unlock; the describers in every mode in en and de, the gateway's categories and visibility, forced runs keeping snoozes, the privacy in the diff, the withdrawal, the activity notifications; widget snapshots without titles and the deletion of the others; the privacy page (enabled only after a successful prompt, disabled without a screen lock, open to guests) and the Account tab; the accessibility smoke tests of the lock screen and the page; the v10 to v11 migration and the schema at v11. Each test of the review's fixes fails with its fix taken out. In total 4,607 app tests (198 contract tests skipped), 332 in `vikunja_domain`, 3,163 in `vikunja_logic`, 1,663 in `vikunja_api`, 20 in `rurana_widgets` and 28 in Kotlin pass. The arm64 release APK is 33.9 MB.

## D65: Connection settings per server address in the app's transport: a certificate pinned in the TLS handshake or a trusted CA certificate, a client certificate, access headers, and unencrypted connections only with consent; failing closed

*Accepted 2026-10-27, step 35b (Android checked on the emulator; the iOS device test comes at the end of the project). The owner-facing choices 1 to 9 below were taken without the owner and are open in the review. Corrects the consequence of [D5](#d5-dio-with-native_dio_adapter) that pinned or mTLS servers need "a per-server switch to `IOHttpClientAdapter`": one adapter in both base `Dio`s applies every server address's settings below all interceptors and sends only pinned, CA-trusted and client-certificate addresses through a `dart:io` client of their own, and the pin is checked in the TLS handshake, not with dio's `validateCertificate`, which [research/flutter-stack.md](research/flutter-stack.md) §3.4 proposed and which runs only after the request was sent. Carries out the per-host cleartext opt-in that [D8](#d8-cleartext-policy) left to a one-time dialog: the consent is now recorded per server address and enforced in the transport, for requests and the socket. Keeps [D14](#d14-browser-sign-in-only): the stored PKCS#12 password is a file password, not an account password. Keeps [D24](#d24-tokens-in-secure-storage-readable-after-the-first-unlock): header values and the client certificate live in secure storage with its accessibility, and a store that cannot be read yet fails closed like a network error. Account ids derive from the server root ([D25](#d25-account-registry-in-sharedpreferences-without-secrets)), so no `http` account is upgraded to `https`. The browser sign-in of [D23](#d23-browser-sign-in-through-flutter_web_auth_2-client-id-and-redirect-uri) stays outside the settings; the hint before it uses D23's sign-in page (`frontend_url`). The socket of [D46](#d46-realtime-in-the-foreground-one-websocket-for-the-active-account-while-the-app-is-in-front-pushed-notifications-stored-at-once-and-a-sync-nudged-for-the-task-they-name-re-auth-on-every-token-change-quiet-fallback-to-polling) gets the same settings and still falls back to polling where they do not reach. The link-share guests of [D62](#d62-create-account-password-reset-and-invite-links-through-the-browser-sign-in-page-link-share-guests-as-an-account-kind-with-token-renewal-and-a-reduced-ui) use the settings like users and are the only way into a server behind a service-token-only Cloudflare Access app. Picking a certificate file is a round trip of [D64](#d64-an-app-lock-over-the-ui-not-encryption-with-biometrics-or-the-devices-screen-lock-device-privacy-settings-for-the-app-switcher-screenshots-notification-content-and-widget-titles) that does not lock the app, and showing a secret holds D64's `SecureDisplay` (the `FLAG_SECURE` of [D55](#d55-security-and-integration-settings-two-factor-status-sessions-and-tokens-in-the-app-a-new-token-shown-once-export-and-deletion-through-the-web)). The screens are styled after [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list).*

- **Context:**
  - On 2026-10-27 the owner split step 35 into 35a ([D64](#d64-an-app-lock-over-the-ui-not-encryption-with-biometrics-or-the-devices-screen-lock-device-privacy-settings-for-the-app-switcher-screenshots-notification-content-and-widget-titles)), 35b (this entry) and 35c, accepted 35a the same day and asked for 35b next.
  - What existed: every HTTP request of the app went through one of the two base `Dio`s that `createDio` builds (`dioProvider`, `authDioProvider`), and every account `Dio` borrows their adapter, the frozen token client and session manager included; only the realtime socket used a plain `dart:io` `HttpClient` (D46). Cleartext was a one-time "Unencrypted connection" dialog, with nothing recorded per server address and nothing enforced below it (D8). The failure texts called trusting a self-signed certificate in the app and custom proxy headers "planned".
  - Verified platform facts (research of 2026-10-27 from the dio, Dart SDK, BoringSSL, Chromium and cupertino_http sources, re-run with Dart 3.13.5 on the development Mac, and the Docker servers below):
    - **dio 5.11.1's `validateCertificate`** runs after the request headers and body were sent and the response headers arrived, and only for a chain the `SecurityContext` already accepted: an impostor would get the token first, and a self-signed server fails before it is ever asked.
    - **Pinning in the handshake works in `dart:io`:** with `SecurityContext(withTrustedRoots: false)` no chain verifies, so `badCertificateCallback` is asked on every new TLS connection, before any HTTP byte, with the leaf (BoringSSL's `X509_STORE_CTX_get0_cert`; on Apple platforms `chain[0]`). A pin together with a trusted CA never reaches the callback: the chain verifies and the request gets its 200.
    - **Only `dart:io` can pin or present a client certificate:** Cronet goes on with a handshake without a client certificate and has no per-request trust override; cupertino_http 3.1.0 has no binding for authentication challenges. `dart:io` ignores Android's user-installed CAs (dart-lang/sdk#50435, open), reads no OS proxy and speaks HTTP/1.1 only.
    - **Redirects:** `dart:io` copies every header except six sensitive ones to a cross-origin redirect target, so an access header would go to whichever host a redirect names. No caller follows a redirect.
    - **PKCS#12:** BoringSSL reads it in both `useCertificateChainBytes` and `usePrivateKeyBytes`, AES-256 with PBKDF2 and the legacy 3DES/RC2 format alike, and needs the password whenever a context is built. A wrong password, an empty file or garbage throws a `TlsException` or an `ArgumentError`, a password longer than 1,023 characters an `ArgumentError`.
    - **Header values:** `dart:io` refuses a value with a non-ASCII character, such as a no-break space, with a `FormatException` that quotes the value.
    - **TLS 1.3 refuses a client certificate after the client finished its handshake:** `dart:io` reports it on the first read as an `HttpException`. Against Caddy 2.11.7 its text names the alert BoringSSL received, `TLSV1_ALERT_CERTIFICATE_REQUIRED` without a certificate and `TLSV1_ALERT_UNKNOWN_CA` for one from another CA; a `dart:io` server sends no alert and only closes ("Connection closed before full header was received", "Connection reset by peer"), as a stale keep-alive connection or a network change also do.
    - **Apple's TLS rules apply to custom anchors:** under `flutter test` on macOS a CA-trusted server leaf valid for 7,300 days fails and one valid for 800 days passes. iOS evaluates with the same SecTrust (SAN, EKU serverAuth, at most 825 days).
- **Decision:**
  - **Owner-facing choices taken without the owner (2026-10-27), open in the review:**
    1. The pin is the SHA-256 of the leaf certificate's DER, checked in the TLS handshake, never with dio's `validateCertificate`.
    2. A CA certificate (PEM) can be trusted per server address instead, for servers whose leaves are short-lived (Caddy's `tls internal`, step-ca) and for client-certificate servers on a private CA, which `dart:io` would not trust through Android's user CAs. Installing the CA in the OS stays the advice for header-only and plain private-CA servers, which keep the native adapter.
    3. The settings belong to a server address, the origin: scheme, lower-case host and effective port. Removing the last account, user or guest, of an origin asks in the existing confirmation whether to "Also forget this server's connection settings" (on by default); the Connection screen can forget them too; settings made on the Connect screen without an account stay until they are forgotten.
    4. The PKCS#12 file password is stored next to the file in secure storage, because `dart:io` needs it at every context build. It is a file password, not an account password (D14).
    5. Unencrypted connections need a consent per origin: recorded when the user continues past the "Unencrypted connection" question, given once to the existing `http` accounts and guests, revocable, and enforced in the transport, with the pre-consent `/info` probe as the only exception and `ws://` included. No `http` account is upgraded to `https` (D25).
    6. A Cloudflare Access app that allows only service tokens lets the app reach the server but cannot sign a user in through the browser (D14); link-share guests, which use only the API, still work. The headers editor says so.
    7. The browser legs are outside the settings: a hint before the browser sign-in says what the browser will meet.
    8. The unit tests get a separate committed set of long-lived dummy certificates; the Docker certificates are generated by a committed script into a gitignored folder.
    9. Pinned, CA-trusted and client-certificate origins take `dart:io`'s side effects (HTTP/1.1, no OS proxy); header-only origins stay on Cronet and NSURLSession.
  - **Where it applies** (`app/lib/core/network/`): `createDio(…, profiles:)` wraps the platform adapter in a `NetworkProfileAdapter`. `dioProvider` and `authDioProvider` each own one over the shared, keepAlive `networkProfilesProvider`, which depends only on the two stores, so an edit changes the table in place and never rebuilds a `Dio`, which would tear down running syncs. As the adapter it sits below the retry, auth and log interceptors, so no object above the transport ever holds a header value. The base `Dio`s now also set `followRedirects: false`; no caller relied on following. The four containers (the UI, the background runs, the notification actions, the widget worker) need no override: each loads the table on its first request.
  - **Per request** (`NetworkProfileAdapter.fetch`):
    - An `http` origin without consent: only the pre-consent probe passes, a request with `preConsentProbeKey` in its `extra` (set by `vikunja_api`'s `/info` probes) that is a `GET` of a path ending in `/api/v1/info` or `/api/v2/info` without an `Authorization` header. It goes out without any part of the profile. Every other request fails with `CleartextBlockedException`.
    - No settings, or only a consent: the platform adapter, unchanged.
    - Headers only: the platform adapter, with the headers merged into a copy of the request options (a header the request already has wins, ignoring case) and redirects off.
    - A pin, a CA or a client certificate: a `dart:io` `IOHttpClientAdapter` per origin, cached by revision, pin, CA and client certificate, with its `SecurityContext` built and checked when it is created; a context `dart:io` refuses means "needs re-entry". A replaced adapter is closed without force, so requests in flight finish, and a request that meets an adapter already closed is sent once more on the current one.
    - Every error leaves with the caller's own request options, which never held a header value. The app's own exceptions pass as they are. A `FormatException` or `ArgumentError` on a request with headers means that the transport refused a stored value ("needs re-entry"). Any other error whose text quotes a stored header value or the password keeps its kind and loses its text (`secret_scrubbing.dart`); only values of 8 characters or more count, because shorter ones turn up by chance in ports, errno numbers and host names.
  - **Pinning:** with a pin or a CA the context has no system roots. The pin callback accepts only the origin's own host and port with the pinned SHA-256. Any other leaf is recorded as the origin's last mismatch (its fingerprint, subject, issuer, validity and the time it was seen; once per certificate; the revision stays) and the handshake fails like any rejected certificate. Nothing is ever trusted automatically. A pin and a trusted CA exclude each other; stored settings with both count as damaged.
  - **CA trust:** a PEM file of at most 64 KB without a private key, checked against the server before it is saved (`CertificateProbe.verifyWithCa`): trusted; not issued by it, with the server's leaf shown and the offer to trust that leaf instead; not checkable, with "Save anyway"; not a certificate file. The PEM is public, so it stays in the metadata. The editor says that on iPhone and iPad the server's leaf must name the server and be valid for at most 825 days.
  - **Client certificates:** a `.p12` or `.pfx` file of at most 64 KB from the app's document picker (Android's has no type filter, so only the check tells), validated at once by building a context the way the transport will, else "Wrong password or unsupported file". The legacy format is accepted. The picker's copy in the cache is deleted whatever happens. The page says that the certificate is presented only to its origin and that the key and the password stay encrypted on the device, and that a server certificate from a private CA needs that CA or a pin in the app too, because certificates installed in the device settings do not apply to `dart:io`.
  - **Access headers:** at most 10. A name must be an RFC 7230 token and none the stack, Vikunja or the socket set themselves (`Host`, `Authorization`, `Cookie`, `Content-Length`, `Content-Type`, `Content-Encoding`, `Transfer-Encoding`, `Connection`, `Upgrade`, `User-Agent`, `Accept`, `Accept-Encoding`, `Origin`, `Range`, `If-Range`, `Expect`, `TE`, `Trailer`, `Keep-Alive`, `Cache-Control`, and every `Proxy-*` and `Sec-*`), and no name may repeat, ignoring case. A value is stored trimmed and may hold only visible ASCII, with spaces and tabs inside, at most 4 KB ("Only visible ASCII characters are allowed"). Presets: "Cloudflare Access service token" (`CF-Access-Client-Id`, `CF-Access-Client-Secret`) and "Custom header". Only the names in the metadata are sent, and only to their own origin.
  - **Unencrypted connections:**
    - "Continue anyway" on the Connect screen records the consent before the connection goes on, so a shared `http` link opens as a guest on the first answer; a consent that cannot be written ends in the unexpected-failure text, never in a request without it.
    - On its first load the table gives the consent once to every registry account, user or guest, on an `http` origin that has no entry yet (`cleartextMigrated`). It never changes an existing entry, so a revoked consent stays revoked.
    - The Connection screen of an `http` origin shows the consent with "Revoke", whose confirmation says that the accounts there stop working until unencrypted connections are allowed again, or "Allow unencrypted connection", which asks the existing question. Headers can be added only while the consent exists, with the note that they travel unencrypted; headers stored before a revoke stay listed as not sent, with Remove.
  - **Storage** (`NetworkProfiles`):
    - Metadata in preferences under `network.profiles`: JSON with `schemaVersion: 1`, `cleartextMigrated`, a revision counter and one entry per origin key (`https://host:443`, always with the port; an IPv6 host in brackets): the pin with the certificate's details and `trustedAt`, the CA certificate's file name, PEM and import date, the client certificate's file name and import date, the header names, `cleartextConsentAt`, `lastPinMismatch`, and a revision that grows with every edit and is never reused for an origin forgotten and set up again. Unknown fields and entries are written back as they were.
    - Secrets in secure storage, one entry per origin, `network_profile.<id>.secrets`, named by the first 32 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the origin key because secure storage cannot be listed: the header values, the PKCS#12 file and its password. Secrets are written before the metadata and deleted after it, so the metadata never names secrets that were never written. Settings with only a pin, a CA or a consent never read secure storage, so they work before the first unlock.
    - Storage is the truth across isolates: every write reads the stored JSON afresh under a lock shared by the isolate and changes only its own origin; the metadata is cached for at most 30 s or until this isolate writes; successful secret reads are cached per origin and revision.
  - **Fail closed:** no request leaves without the settings its origin has.
    - Preferences or secure storage that cannot be read (before the first unlock, or a platform error): `ConnectionProfileLockedException`, read again after 5 s at the earliest and failing fast meanwhile; transient, like D24's locked Keychain. The texts say "not available until the device has been unlocked once", never "locked", which belongs to the app lock.
    - Damaged metadata (not JSON, a newer schema, an entry that cannot be read, a pin with a CA): `ConnectionProfilesDamagedException` for every origin and every edit until "Reset connection settings" (`resetAll`). A reset first writes empty settings, then deletes the secrets of every origin it can name (those of the old settings and of the registry's accounts), and gives no consents again. A read that fails is not damage.
    - Secrets missing or not JSON, a named header without a value, a client certificate without its file or password, or a context `dart:io` refuses: `ConnectionProfileIncompleteException`, shown as "needs re-entry".
  - **Failures** (`vikunja_domain`, `vikunja_api`): the five exceptions (`CleartextBlockedException`, `ConnectionProfileLockedException`, `ConnectionProfileIncompleteException`, `ConnectionProfilesDamagedException`, `ClientCertificateRefusedException`) print only their class and origin. `transportFailure` maps them by type, never by text, to `ConnectionSettingsFailure` (`cleartextBlocked`, `locked`, `incomplete`, `damaged`) and `TlsFailure(clientCertificateRefused: true)`. Neither is connection-level, so the connector stops at the `https` address and never tries `http`, and a connection-settings failure is the most informative of all. After the connect they are `NetworkFailure`s with the new problems `clientCertificate`, `cleartextBlocked`, `profileLocked`, `profileIncomplete` and `profileDamaged`: queued writes stay queued, because nothing left the device; the retry interceptor does not retry them, nor `tls`; the offline banner and notices leave them out and a failure card shows them instead.
  - **Client-certificate refusals:**
    - Text markers for what the platforms report: Chromium's `ERR_BAD_SSL_CLIENT_AUTH_CERT` and `ERR_SSL_CLIENT_AUTH_CERT_NEEDED` (Cronet's internal -117 and -110), NSURLErrorDomain -1205 and -1206, which no longer count as a rejected server certificate, and the alerts BoringSSL reports as received, under their real names as recorded: `TLSV1_ALERT_CERTIFICATE_REQUIRED`, `TLSV1_ALERT_UNKNOWN_CA`, `TLSV1_ALERT_ACCESS_DENIED`, `SSLV3_ALERT_BAD_CERTIFICATE`, `SSLV3_ALERT_UNSUPPORTED_CERTIFICATE` and `SSLV3_ALERT_CERTIFICATE_REVOKED`, `_EXPIRED` and `_UNKNOWN`; a server sends these only about the client's certificate. Not `SSLV3_ALERT_HANDSHAKE_FAILURE`, which any cipher or version mismatch sends.
    - On the `dart:io` path an `HttpException` before the response starts a check that sends no HTTP: a second handshake (`SecureSocket` with the profile's own context and pin check, 5 s), then up to 3 s for the server to hang up. A server that ends that connection, or whose handshake fails with one of the alerts, confirms the refusal (`ClientCertificateRefusedException`); data, silence or a socket error do not, and the original error stands. The result is kept for 10 s per origin. The check runs for every `https` origin on `dart:io`, so a client-certificate server is recognised also while no certificate is stored.
  - **Realtime** (`ProfileRealtimeConnector` in `core/realtime/realtime_providers.dart`): the socket is no `Dio` request, so the connector applies the origin's settings to each upgrade: the consent for `ws://`, the headers on the upgrade request (the socket's own headers win, the app's `User-Agent` comes last), and for a pin, a CA or a client certificate a `dart:io` client from the profile through the new `IoRealtimeConnector(createHttpClient:)`. Errors follow the adapter's rules; an error naming a client-certificate alert becomes `ClientCertificateRefusedException`. `RealtimeConnection` gives up until the next resume on each of the five exceptions. `realtimeConnectionProvider` waits until the origin's settings were read once and starts the socket again after every edit or revoke, but not after a recorded mismatch. Link-share guests have no socket (D62).
  - **Connect screen** (`features/onboarding/`, `features/connection/`):
    - A failure the settings can fix gets its action on the error card: a rejected certificate "View certificate" (the trust page, or the review when the origin has a pin); a refused client certificate "Add client certificate" ("Replace client certificate" when one is stored); a login proxy (`BehindLoginProxy`, or `NotAVikunjaServer` with 401 or 403) "Add access headers"; settings that need re-entry "Open connection settings"; settings not available yet only the text and Try again; damaged settings "Reset connection settings" after a confirmation. A shared link or an invite that fails this way gets the same.
    - The controller keeps the target (an address, a shared link, a shared link with the server address typed for it, an invite) while its failure is one the settings can fix, and runs it again once an editor saved a change (`retryAfterConnectionChange`): a shared link opens as a guest without being sent again.
    - "Connection options" under the address field opens the settings of the origin the typed address gives ("Settings for https://host:443"), disabled while the address is invalid, and says "Saved settings" when that origin has some. This is how settings without an account are found again.
  - **Connection screen** (`/connection?origin=…`, a top-level route that no redirect touches, signed in or not): from "Connection" in the Account tab's Server section (users and guests; it shows what is stored or what needs attention), from "Connection options", from every action on the Connect screen, and from "Open connection settings" on the data failure cards, the sync status, the first-sync screen, the server overview and the guest banner.
    - An `https` origin: the trusted certificate with its fingerprint and date, or the trusted CA certificate, or "Check the server's certificate"; the client certificate's file name and date with Replace and Remove; the header names with hidden values, Edit and Remove; Forget, whose confirmation says that the accounts there stop working until the settings are entered again and that the consent goes too. Removing a trust, a client certificate or the headers asks first.
    - The trust page: the origin; the SHA-256 as 32 hex pairs in rows of four, selectable, with a plain copy and one screen-reader label; issued to and by, "Self-signed"; the validity, with a warning when expired or not yet valid; the hint to compare with `openssl x509 -noout -fingerprint -sha256 -in cert.pem`; that the browser will warn as well; that the app trusts it only for this origin; "Trust this certificate" and "Use a CA certificate instead", with a note when the one replaces the other. A certificate valid for less than 100 days gets a warning and puts the CA first; one the device already trusts gets a note and no filled Trust button.
    - "The server's certificate changed" after a mismatch, with Review: a fresh probe shown with the recorded certificate and whether they match; "Accept new certificate" pins the certificate probed now, never the record, and says so when the two differ; "Keep current certificate" clears the record.
    - Secret fields (header values, the file password) are hidden, without suggestions, autocorrect or autofill, take a paste but offer no copy or cut, and hold `SecureDisplay` while a value is shown.
    - Damaged settings show only the notice and the reset; settings not available yet the text and Try again.
  - **Before the browser sign-in** (`confirmBrowserLeg`, before "Continue to sign-in" and at the four places with "Sign in again"; merged into the existing question of "Create account"): it looks at the API's origin and at the sign-in page's origin, where D23 opens the authorize page (`frontend_url` when usable). It shows the pinned fingerprint to compare when the sign-in page's origin is pinned, a note that the browser may warn when that origin trusts a CA, the client-certificate note ("the browser needs the client certificate installed in the device settings too, or the sign-in page must be reachable without it") and "the browser goes through the access login itself" when the API's settings have those, and "Sign-in opens host" when the two origins differ, with Continue and Cancel. Without a pin, a CA, a client certificate or headers on either origin it shows nothing; a consent alone needs no hint.
  - **Removing the last account of a server:** the remove and sign-out confirmation gets the checkbox "Also forget this server's connection settings" (on), with what is stored below it ("Unencrypted connections allowed", "Trusted certificate, Access headers"), only when settings exist and no other account, user or guest, uses the origin. `SignOutController.signOut(…, forgetConnectionSettings:)` forgets them after the sign-out and only when still no account uses the origin; a failure is logged by type and never blocks the sign-out. The checkbox is adaptive, because the Material one asserts in a Cupertino dialog.
  - **Texts:** failure texts for the five problems in the connect, sign-in, link and data failure messages, and for a pin mismatch; the suggestions that called certificates and proxy headers "planned" now point to the connection settings. English and German.
  - **Logging:** no header value, certificate byte or password is logged; failures are logged by type. Redaction gains the names `CF-Access-Client-Id`, `CF-Access-Client-Secret` and `CF-Access-Token` with their values, because a 32-hex id is too short for the rule on long runs. `ProfileSecrets` appears in no `toString` and no metadata JSON.
  - **Docker `tls` profile** (`test_servers/`, opt-in, so CI, a plain `up -d` and `tool/contract_tests.sh` never start it): a second Vikunja 2.7.0 (`vikunja-2-7-tls` with its own dummy secrets, `publicurl` `https://localhost:34528/`, plain HTTP on 34531 for seeding from the host) behind Caddy 2.11.7 (`tls-proxy`): 34528 with a self-signed leaf; 34529 requiring a client certificate from the test client CA, with a server certificate from the test CA; 34530 with an access-header gate like Cloudflare Access, which answers a missing or wrong token with a 302 to a reserved host. Caddy keeps no access log and no autosaved config and offers no HTTP/3. `tool/gen_tls_test_certs.sh` (bash 3.2, OpenSSL 3 or newer, LibreSSL refused) writes the dummy certificates, a random PKCS#12 password and random header values into the gitignored `test_servers/tls/generated/` (EC P-256, SAN `DNS:localhost, IP:127.0.0.1`, 397 days) and prints only the self-signed fingerprint; `--renew-selfsigned` replaces only that leaf, for the mismatch check. `tool/wait_for_test_servers.sh --tls` also checks that every gate refuses a client without its credential. `tool/tls_e2e.py` seeds a project, a task and a link share (printing ids only), sets up `adb reverse` and hands the share link to the app without printing it. Start, stop and the emulator matrix: [test_servers/tls/README.md](../test_servers/tls/README.md).
  - **Fixtures** (`app/test/fixtures/tls/`, from `tool/gen_tls_test_certs.sh --fixtures`): a committed dummy set, "never trusted anywhere", without the CA keys, with a fixed dummy password and a manifest. The CAs, the self-signed leaf and the client certificates are valid for 20 years, the CA-chained server leaf for 820 days, because macOS applies Apple's 825-day limit to CA-chained leaves even with custom anchors. `fixtures_expiry_test.dart` fails 60 days before a CA-chained leaf expires and names the command.
  - **No new dependency:** `crypto` was a direct dependency already.
  - **The parked session hardening:** the six auth files of the package reserved as D59 were not touched; nothing here needed them, because every request passes `createDio`'s adapter. `account_tab.dart`, `sync_status_section.dart` and `realtime_connection_providers.dart` are in its file list and change here (besides the ARB and generated files), so applying the package will conflict there.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **dio's `validateCertificate`**, as research/flutter-stack.md §3.4 proposed: it runs after the request was sent and only for a chain already accepted.
  - **A per-server switch of the `Dio`'s adapter or a `Dio` per profile**, D5's plan: the account clients borrow the base adapter, and rebuilding a `Dio` on an edit would tear down running syncs. One adapter over a table that changes in place covers every request, the frozen auth code included.
  - **An interceptor instead of the adapter:** the interceptors, the retry and auth interceptors among them, and every `DioException` see and keep the request options, so header values would travel into failures and logs.
  - **Everything on `dart:io`:** header-only servers would lose Android's user CAs, the system proxy and HTTP/3 for nothing.
  - **SPKI pins:** they survive a renewal that keeps the key, but need DER parsing, users cannot compare them with what `openssl` or a browser shows, and Caddy makes a new key at every renewal by default.
  - **A pin together with a CA:** the CA verifies the chain, so the pin would never be checked.
  - **TLS of the app's own through `HttpClient.connectionFactory`:** the callback already sees the leaf before any byte; kept in reserve in case the iOS test finds a gap.
  - **All settings in secure storage, pins included**, as the research's storage note had it: pins, CA certificates and consents are public, and in preferences settings without secrets work before the first unlock.
  - **`Proxy-Authorization`** (Authelia's basic-auth alternative): Apple says not to set it on NSURLSession; every `Proxy-*` header is refused.
  - **A failure type of its own for refused cleartext that is not retried**, as the spec first had it: the outbox would mark queued writes as rejected for good, while nothing left the device; a `NetworkFailure` keeps them queued.
  - **The probe exception by its marker alone:** the request must also be a `GET` of an `/info` path without `Authorization`.
  - **Reporting every error that quotes a secret as "needs re-entry":** short values turned up by chance in the texts of ordinary network errors; such an error now keeps its kind and loses its text.
  - **Accepting a changed certificate from the recorded mismatch:** the record may be old; Accept pins a certificate probed just then, which the user compares.
  - **Every `HttpException` after the handshake as a refused client certificate, or `SSLV3_ALERT_HANDSHAKE_FAILURE` as a marker:** stale keep-alive connections, network changes and cipher mismatches look the same; the second handshake tells them apart.
  - **An `http` → `https` upgrade of an account:** the account id derives from the root (D25), so it would be a new account with a new sign-in anyway.
  - **A list of all stored settings** for those without an account: "Saved settings" on Connection options finds them by address; a list under Privacy is a possible follow-up.
  - **A separate sheet for the options of a typed address:** one screen and one route serve every way in.
  - **Committing the Docker certificates:** they would expire and set off secret scanners, and CI does not need them; the unit tests have their own committed dummy set.
- **Consequences:**
  - A server with its own certificate, a private CA, a client-certificate gate or an access proxy such as Cloudflare Access works in the app, for requests, the socket and guests, and an `http` server only with a consent the user can revoke. A changed certificate stops every request to its server until the user reviews it.
  - Pinned, CA-trusted and client-certificate origins run on `dart:io`: HTTP/1.1, no system proxy (a VPN still works), no Android user CAs (the pin or the CA in the app takes their place). Header-only origins keep Cronet and NSURLSession with all of these.
  - The browser legs (the sign-in, "Sign in again", "Create account", and the web pages the app opens) do not use the settings. A pinned or private-CA server shows the browser's own warning at every sign-in, because the browser session is ephemeral (D23). The pin protects the app's own traffic, the code exchange included; the password typed into the browser is protected only by the user's check there. A client-certificate server needs the certificate installed in the OS for the browser too, or a sign-in page reachable without it; an access proxy shows its own login in the browser every time, and one that allows only service tokens leaves only guests.
  - The settings are per origin: two servers under one origin share them, and the connector's `:3456` candidate is another origin.
  - After a reset of damaged settings, `http` accounts need the consent again. Secrets of origins that neither the old settings nor the registry name stay in secure storage, which cannot be listed.
  - Known gaps:
    - A header-only origin behind a private CA keeps the socket on `dart:io`'s default trust, which on Android ignores user CAs, so realtime falls back to polling (D46); trusting the CA in the app moves the origin to `dart:io` and fixes it.
    - The socket recognises a refused client certificate only by the alert's name; a server that just closes leaves it retrying with backoff.
    - Cronet's and NSURLSession's client-certificate texts are not recorded: on the emulator the test CA was trusted in the app, which runs on `dart:io`. Should they be classified wrong, "Connection options" still leads to the settings.
    - nginx answers a missing or refused client certificate with an HTTP 400 page after a completed handshake (unverified); it is not classified as a client-certificate failure.
    - On Android the trust page cannot tell that the device trusts a certificate through a user CA, which `dart:io` does not see, so it offers Trust as usual.
    - A background run in flight during an edit fails once with the old settings and succeeds at the next run.
    - A settings write that hangs blocks the later writes of its isolate.
  - Not built: SPKI pins; `Proxy-Authorization`; the `http` → `https` upgrade; a list of all stored settings (a possible follow-up under Privacy); recognising nginx's HTTP 400; a client certificate from the OS key store (Android KeyChain, an iOS profile identity); user CAs for `dart:io`; the iOS device test, which comes at the end of the project (handshake pinning and PKCS#12 under SecTrust, CA trust under the 825-day rule, headers through NSURLSession, ASWebAuthenticationSession's certificate warning, the Cupertino dialogs). *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): there is no iOS check of the connection settings yet; it waits for the device test, and so does the reserve of the app's own TLS (Rejected alternatives).*
