  - Tests: `OriginKey` (IPv6, the string form); the header rules (a no-break space, zero-width characters, `Sec-Foo`, `Range`); the model and its JSON (a pin with a CA damaged, unknown fields kept); `NetworkProfiles` (two instances over one store: a new pin not reverted by another's stale mismatch record, a revoke surviving another's migration; the migration once; a failed read locked and read again after 5 s; settings with only a pin or a consent never reading secure storage; revisions never repeated; the write order; the reset); `validateClientCertificate` with the recorded `dart:io` texts; `profileHttpClient` and the adapter against local TLS servers from the fixtures (a pin accepted, and refused before any request byte reaches the server; the host and port check, IPv6 included; CA trust; a client certificate with, without and from another CA through a `SecureServerSocket` that requires one; the legacy file; headers only for their origin, a request header winning, redirects off; errors without secret headers or quoted secrets; a revision closing the old adapter and a closed one retried once; the cleartext block with the probe exception; a throwing mismatch hook; a server that hangs up on requests but not on a handshake not taken for a refusal; a check whose handshake outlasts its timeout); `dioProvider` the same instance after edits; the realtime connector (headers on the upgrade, a pinned client, the five exceptions giving up until resume, a restart after an edit); a test that no dummy header value or password reaches a log; the probe and `verifyWithCa`; in the packages every classification with the recorded alert names, -1205 and -1206 no longer a server-certificate rejection, the connector stopping after the `https` probes for each exception, the probe marker without credentials, the retry interceptor; widget tests of the trust page and the review, the client certificate import, the headers editor, the Connection screen (revoke, forget, reset, the `http` rules, the route open signed out and before the first sync), the Connect screen's action per failure and the share → trust → guest retry, the order of the consent, the browser hint (same and different origins, nothing without settings), the remove-last-account checkbox (also on iOS), the picked copy deleted, a shown secret holding `SecureDisplay`; the accessibility smoke test of the new pages; the fixture expiry guard. The opt-in Docker test (`tls-docker`, 14 tests, skipped unless `RURANA_TLS_DOCKER=1`) runs the adapter, the connector, a guest sign-in and the socket against 34528 to 34531. Each test of the review's fixes failed before its fix. In total 4,867 app tests (198 contract and 14 Docker tests skipped), 337 in `vikunja_domain`, 3,163 in `vikunja_logic`, 1,719 in `vikunja_api`, 20 in `rurana_widgets` and 28 in Kotlin pass, and the 14 Docker tests pass with the profile up. The arm64 release APK is 34.2 MB.
  - Checked on the Android emulator against the Docker `tls` profile, with link-share guests: on 34528 the rejected certificate, "View certificate" with the fingerprint the script printed, "Trust this certificate" and the guest reading the shared project; after `--renew-selfsigned` the failed sync, "Certificate changed" on the Account tab's Connection row, the review with the certificate presented now, and "Accept new certificate"; on 34529 the test CA trusted in the app, then "localhost:34529 needs a client certificate" with "Add client certificate", the `.p12` with its password, and the guest; on 34530, the CA again trusted in the app, "Server is behind a login page" with "Add access headers", a wrong secret failing the same way and the right one letting the guest in; on 34527 a guest after the unencrypted-connection question with the consent recorded, "Revoke" failing the sync with "Unencrypted connections to localhost:34527 are not allowed on this device", and removing the guest with "Also forget this server's connection settings" ("Unencrypted connections allowed" below it); on 34528's server overview, "Continue to sign-in" showing the hint with the pinned fingerprint. Not checked on the device: a user's browser sign-in, which needs a password typed into the browser, and with it a user's socket (covered by the Docker test); the header-only path through Cronet with a user CA and Cronet's client-certificate texts; everything on iOS.

## D66: A threat model; uncaught errors redacted and never printed in release builds; the app's data out of backups and device transfer; routes pushed by the platform taken only as app-scheme links; widget links and notification taps switching accounts only with a per-install key; exported components and dependencies checked in CI

*Accepted 2026-10-27, step 35c (Android checked on the emulator; the Swift is written but not built, and its checks come with the iOS device test at the end of the project). Decisions 1 to 6 below and the widget-link key in notification taps were taken without the owner and are open in the review. Adds the threat model, [threat-model.md](threat-model.md), which the residual risks below link to. Corrects [D24](#d24-tokens-in-secure-storage-readable-after-the-first-unlock) and [D31](#d31-one-drift-sqlite-file-per-account-shared-across-isolates-deleted-on-sign-out) where they say that `android:allowBackup="false"` keeps the tokens and the database out of backups: on Android 12 and later it stops cloud backup only, and on some manufacturers' devices not a device-to-device transfer; data extraction rules now stop both, and on iOS the databases and the widget files are out of backups too, while the preferences stay in them, so D31's "on iOS it is part of the device backup" no longer holds for the database. Moves the diagnostics export, which [D20](#d20-scope-cuts-and-desktop-adaptations) placed in step 35, to a later step, as the owner decided on 2026-10-27 ([D64](#d64-an-app-lock-over-the-ui-not-encryption-with-biometrics-or-the-devices-screen-lock-device-privacy-settings-for-the-app-switcher-screenshots-notification-content-and-widget-titles) recorded the postponement). Corrects [D63](#d63-home-screen-widgets-from-json-snapshots-the-app-writes-through-an-in-repo-plugin-glance-widgets-on-android-with-done-through-a-headless-dart-worker-a-widgetkit-extension-on-ios-with-an-app-group-outbox-and-no-token)'s reason for switching accounts on a widget link without asking, as for a notification's tap, because a widget shows only the user's own accounts: any app, and on iOS any web page, can send such a link, and on Android any app can make up a notification tap, so neither a widget link nor a tap of [D39](#d39-local-notifications-a-scheduler-per-signed-in-account-plans-from-the-local-database-and-diffs-against-a-rolling-window-with-stable-keys-exact-alarms-when-allowed-done-through-the-outbox-from-a-background-isolate-without-a-token-refresh-snooze-local-only-permissions-asked-once-after-the-first-sync) switches without the install's widget-link key any more. Rewords principle 5 of the [architecture](architecture.md#1-principles) from "never refresh tokens" to "never refresh user tokens; a guest token may be renewed, re-authentication at most once", which is what [D62](#d62-create-account-password-reset-and-invite-links-through-the-browser-sign-in-page-link-share-guests-as-an-account-kind-with-token-renewal-and-a-reduced-ui) built. Corrects the cause that [D55](#d55-security-and-integration-settings-two-factor-status-sessions-and-tokens-in-the-app-a-new-token-shown-once-export-and-deletion-through-the-web), [D57](#d57-comments-written-in-the-app-online-with-a-draft-kept-on-the-device-reactions-on-tasks-and-comments-comment-counts-and-the-unread-dot-on-rows) and [D60](#d60-labels-managed-online-and-applied-locally-teams-project-shares-and-link-shares-online-only-the-project-permission-stored-and-every-screen-shaped-by-it) give for go_router's push futures that never complete and its dropped `extra`s, "once Android reports the route list back, as it does when the app is opened again": it is go_router decoding its route list again, as on a state restoration or a refresh (flutter/flutter#128122); the engine pushes only an intent's data on Android and app-scheme URLs on iOS, and since this step no route the platform pushes reaches go_router. Carries out the WebView review that [D18](#d18-tiptap-description-editor-in-a-webview) and [D52](#d52-descriptions-edited-in-a-bundled-tiptap-page-in-a-locked-down-webview-with-a-native-toolbar-checklists-ticked-in-place-no-silent-overwrite-of-a-description-changed-on-the-web) left to step 35 and the sensitive clipboard on iOS that D55 left to the iOS phase. Keeps D24: every secret stays in the Keychain or the Keystore.*

- **Context:**
  - The owner accepted 35b ([D65](#d65-connection-settings-per-server-address-in-the-apps-transport-a-certificate-pinned-in-the-tls-handshake-or-a-trusted-ca-certificate-a-client-certificate-access-headers-and-unencrypted-connections-only-with-consent-failing-closed)) on 2026-10-27 and asked for 35c, the audits and the threat model, the last part of step 35 as split in D64.
  - What existed: a log redaction grown since step 2+3 and no log sink in release builds (`Level.OFF`), but Flutter's default error handler, which prints an uncaught error's details unredacted, in release builds too; domain `toString`s that printed titles; `allowBackup="false"` only, and nothing excluded from iOS backups; an app-scheme observer in the router that took the app's own links but let every other route the platform pushed reach go_router; widget links and notification taps that switched accounts without asking; a widget setup page for any id; the WebView locked down by D52 without tests of its refusals and its page's policy; no advisory scan in CI; no threat model. An audit of the code after 35b (logs, backups, deep links, the WebView, the widgets and the exported components, the dependencies) found the gaps; a check of the spec (31 findings) and a review of the result (8 findings) refined how they were closed.
  - Verified platform facts (research of 2026-10-27 from the Android and Apple documentation and the Flutter SDK, flutter_local_notifications 22.3.2, Glance 1.2.0, osv-scanner-action 2.7.0 and Chromium 141 sources):
    - **Backups on Android 12 and later:** `allowBackup="false"` turns cloud backup off, but on devices of some manufacturers not a device-to-device transfer; that takes `dataExtractionRules` with a `<device-transfer>` section, and a mode without rules is fully on except for `cache` and `no_backup`. Android 16 QPR2 adds `<cross-platform-transfer>`, for a transfer to iOS. Below Android 12 `allowBackup="false"` covers both.
    - **Backups on iOS:** every data container, the App Group's included, is backed up except `Caches`, `tmp` and what is marked `isExcludedFromBackup`; a restore or a move to another device can clear the mark. `ThisDeviceOnly` Keychain items never reach another device but come back to the same one from an encrypted backup.
    - **Routes pushed by the platform:** Flutter's Android embedding pushes the data of any intent that reaches `MainActivity`, which is exported for the launcher, as a route, whatever its scheme, a bare path included; iOS pushes only URLs of the app's scheme. The app never navigates itself that way: its links use its scheme, and notifications, shares and the quick action have channels of their own. The most valuable target was `/connection?origin=…&edit=certificate`, open while signed out, whose certificate page probes the origin it names as soon as it opens.
    - **Notification taps:** flutter_local_notifications reports a `SELECT_NOTIFICATION` intent with a `payload` extra, which any app can send to `MainActivity`, as a tap; the app then switched accounts and marked the activity read. On iOS taps come only from `UNUserNotificationCenter`.
    - **Uncaught errors:** `FlutterError.presentError`, the default handler, prints the error's details, in release builds its text and up to 100 stack frames, and the engine prints "Unhandled Exception: …" itself when `PlatformDispatcher.onError` is missing, returns false or throws.
    - **Glance 1.2.0** gives the rows of a lazy list one collection template, an empty mutable `PendingIntent` that the widget host fills in.
    - **osv-scanner-action 2.7.0** (the tag points to commit `b1d267ae…`): `fail-on-vuln` is on by default, `scan-args` are split by lines, and the inner actions run the scanner image `ghcr.io/google/osv-scanner-action:v2.7.0` by its tag. **Gradle's wrapper** checks `distributionSha256Sum` only when it downloads the distribution.
- **Decision:**
  - **Owner decisions of 2026-10-27:**
    - Backups are excluded on both platforms: Android's data extraction rules for cloud backup and for device-to-device transfer; on iOS `isExcludedFromBackup` on `Library/Application Support/accounts` and on the App Group's `widgets/` folder.
    - The diagnostics export comes later, not in step 35. Of the logging, only the gap of uncaught errors printed unredacted in release builds is closed now.
    - One commit and one acceptance for 35c.
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-10-27), open in the review:**
    1. **A widget-link key:** 32 random bytes per install, which every link of the app's widgets carries; a widget link without it never switches accounts silently. A fresh install gets a new key; an iOS restore brings the old one back with the backup, which only the backup's owner has.
    2. **Guests renewed in headless containers stay as they are** (the audit's item W1): a renewal rotates nothing, and a guest is signed in again at most once per container (D62). Principle 5 says "never refresh user tokens" now.
    3. **No redaction at capture yet:** there is no log buffer. The error hooks redact before anything is printed, and the threat model sets the rules for the later buffer ([threat-model.md §10](threat-model.md#10-constraints-for-the-later-diagnostics-buffer)).
    4. **A sensitive clipboard on iOS:** the one-time secrets of D55 and copied share links stay on the device (`localOnly`) and expire after 2 minutes, through the app's own iOS channel; Android keeps `IS_SENSITIVE`. Written now, built in the iOS phase. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): built with Xcode 27.1, not yet run; the same channel now also says when the screen is captured, so a one-time secret hides meanwhile.*
    5. **No Dependabot** while there is no git remote: an OSV scan, `npm audit` and an informational `flutter pub outdated` run in CI, the scan also weekly.
    6. **No dependency upgrades in this step:** `pub outdated` shows routine updates only and no advisory.
    - Also taken without the owner: **the key in notification taps**, with the same rule of asking instead of a silent switch, because on Android any app can make up a tap (below).
  - **Uncaught errors** (`bootstrap/logging_setup.dart`, called by all four entry points: the UI, the background runs, the notification actions, the widget worker): `configureLogging` installs `FlutterError.onError` and `PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError` in every build, before the release return. Both log through `Logger('Flutter')` and `Logger('Uncaught')`, the text redacted when the record is made; a release build prints nothing (`Level.OFF`), a debug build the redacted line and up to 20 stack frames through the existing sink. `FlutterError.presentError` is never called, in any mode. Each hook catches everything: a text that cannot be built becomes the error's type, a failed log is swallowed, and the dispatcher hook returns true even then, so the engine never prints the error itself. What the hooks do depends only on `printToConsole`, so tests can emulate release; `resetLoggingForTest` restores the handlers that were there before.
  - **Redaction** (`core/logging/redaction.dart`) also covers e-mail addresses (in free text, after the rule that keeps the host of `user:pw@host`, as a JSON key and as `email=`), `invite-link=` and `share-auth-token=`, a URL's fragment in free text (the first key's name stays readable), the widget-link key as `?k=`, `&k=` and `"k":`, and a JWT cut off after its header and a JSON secret without its closing quote, as a `FormatException` quotes them, cut at 75 characters. The Cloudflare Access names of 35b stay.
  - **No titles in `toString`:** `Task` prints its id and identifier, `Project`, `Label` and `TaskAttachment` their id, `ProjectView` its id and kind. `Account`, `User` and `UserProfile` keep the username; `vikunja_logic`'s `ParsedTaskText` and `IndentedLine` keep their text for test diffs, and no log call may interpolate any of them.
  - **Native logs:** `MarkDoneAction` logs only the failure's type, like the rest of the Kotlin; the Swift logs nothing.
  - **Leak tests:**
    - `app/test/security/token_leak_test.dart` drives the transport and the token refresh, a pasted share link with a password and an invite, widget links and notification payloads, with markers for tokens, JWTs, hashes, invites, `tk_` and CalDAV tokens, e-mail addresses, titles and the widget-link key, and also feeds what they throw to both hooks: no 20-character piece of a marker may appear in a record's message, its error or a printed line. The header values and the PKCS#12 password of 35b are checked on their own paths and, quoted by an uncaught error, against the hooks of a release build, which must print nothing; pattern redaction cannot know an arbitrary header value.
    - `app/test/security/log_hygiene_test.dart` reads the shipped Dart, Kotlin and Swift of the app, the packages and the plugin: no print outside the debug sink; no secret-named value (tokens, codes, verifiers, states, passcodes, header values, `linkKey`), content, link or person interpolated into a log call; no secret field and no title, name, description, file name or text in a domain `toString`; Kotlin logs failure types only, the Swift none. Its allowlists name only the debug sink and the realtime server's error code; a self-test lists names the rules must catch.
    - `app/test/bootstrap/logging_setup_test.dart` tests the hooks: release builds print nothing, an error whose text cannot be built, the reset. The 35b test `no_secrets_in_logs_test.dart` stays and shares its fixture reader (`tlsAccessValues`).
  - **Backups, Android:** `android:dataExtractionRules="@xml/data_extraction_rules"` beside `allowBackup="false"`. The rules exclude all nine domains (`root`, `file`, `database`, `sharedpref`, `external` and their `device_` forms) with `path="."` in `<cloud-backup>` and in `<device-transfer>`, with no `<include>` and no `<cross-platform-transfer>`. No `fullBackupContent`: the app's minSdk is 24, and below Android 12 `allowBackup="false"` covers both.
  - **Backups, iOS:** `excludeAppDataFromBackup` (`core/platform/backup_exclusion.dart`, behind `BackupExclusion` with a fake for tests) hands `<application support>/accounts` (the databases and the queued uploads) and the plugin's `widgetsDirectory()` (the App Group's `widgets/` with the snapshots, catalogs, configs, `outbox/`, `requests/` and `host.json`) to the app's channel, nothing else of the App Group. `bootstrap.dart` calls it on iOS after the first frame of every UI start, because a restore or a move can clear the mark; best effort, a failure logged by its type.
  - **The app's own iOS channel** (`DeviceChannel` in `app/ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift`, so `project.pbxproj` stays as it is): `dev.coderoftime.vikunja/device`, the name of the Android device channel, registered in `didInitializeImplicitFlutterEngine` through `engineBridge.applicationRegistrar.messenger()`, so only the UI engine has it; the background and notification engines get the plugin registrant only. `excludeFromBackup` creates each folder and sets `isExcludedFromBackup`, a failure naming its error type, never a path; `copySensitive` puts the text on the pasteboard with `localOnly` and an expiry 120 s ahead; everything else is not implemented.
  - **The sensitive clipboard** (decision 4): `PlatformSensitiveClipboard` calls `copySensitive` on iOS too and falls back to a plain copy, logged by type ("Copied plainly instead"), when the channel does not answer, so copying works before the Swift is built. The call sites (the one-time secret view of D55, `link_share_clipboard.dart`) are unchanged.
  - **What stays in iOS backups**, recorded as a limitation: the preferences, which hold the description and comment drafts, the account registry, the connection metadata of D65 (pins, CA certificates, header names, consents), the privacy settings, the install marker and the widget-link key. Restored to a new device, the registry and the metadata come back without databases, widget files or Keychain items: accounts need a new sign-in, and settings with secrets need re-entry. Restored to the same device from an encrypted backup, the `ThisDeviceOnly` Keychain items come back too, and the databases are synced again. iOS is not at parity with Android, which neither backs up nor transfers anything of the app.
  - **The Keychain and the entitlements** are pinned by static tests: the options of the frozen `secure_store.dart` (`first_unlock_this_device`, not synchronizable, no Secure Enclave) and both targets' entitlements (the App Group only, no keychain access group).
  - **Routes pushed by the platform** (`app/router/app_router.dart`): `_AppSchemeLinks`, registered before go_router's own observer, now takes every route the platform pushes: a URI of the app's scheme goes to the deep-link relay as before, anything else, another scheme or a bare path, is dropped. The top-level redirect sends a location with a scheme or a host to the splash, a backstop for app code that would `go()` to a full URI. `MainActivity` is unchanged. The test helper for flutter/flutter#128122 (`test/helpers/route_report.dart`) pushed a relative route through the binding, which the app now drops; it hands the route to the router's provider directly, as the binding did, and a canary test checks that it still breaks a plain push's future.
  - **The widget-link key** (decision 1, `core/links/widget_link_key.dart`): `widgets.link_key` in preferences, 32 bytes from `Random.secure()` in base64url without padding, compared in constant time after decoding (`widgetLinkKeyMatches`, false for a missing, malformed or wrong key). Only the UI start creates it, in `bootstrap.dart` before the registry's first read sets the install marker: without the marker a new key, else the stored one, replaced only when it is missing or broken. The headless containers build the account registry too, so they only read the key, and without one build links and payloads without it. Neither the key nor a link is logged.
    - **Carriers:** `WidgetLink.linkKey`, read from and written as `k`; every link of the Dart snapshot builder; and for the links that Kotlin and Swift build without a snapshot (the setup, Open and New task links, and on iOS the task and list links), the `linkKey` of `widgets/host.json`, which only the UI writes (`widgetHostSettings` through `WidgetHost.writeHost` to the plugin). Kotlin's `WidgetLinks.configureLink`, `openLink` and `newTaskLink` are pure string functions with a JVM test; Swift's `WidgetLinks.url` adds the key for every link. Links the widgets build before the app has written `host.json` carry no key, by design.
    - **Without the right key** (`WidgetLinkListener`, checked first): a task link goes through the task-link flow of [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme), which asks before it switches; list, Account tab, Open and New task links act only when they name no account or the active one; `widget/configure` needs no key, because the setup page decides. With the key, everything works as before.
  - **The key in notification taps:** `NotificationPayload.linkKey`, written as `k` and optional when read, so notifications scheduled earlier still parse. The scheduler, the activity notifier (activity, "more" and session notifications) and the test notification read the key and leave it out when there is none. `NotificationTapListener` with the key works as before. Without it, or with a wrong one, a tap never switches silently and marks nothing read: a payload that names a task (an id other than 0, since tasks created on the device have negative ids) goes through the task-link flow, which asks; session, inbox and test taps and task-less activities open only when their account is the active one. The notification actions are unchanged: the plugin's receivers are not exported, and only plain taps reach the UI. No forced reschedule: a notification scheduled before this step keeps its keyless payload until it fires or its text or time changes.
  - **The widget setup page** (`WidgetConfigurePage`): the form opens only when the plugin's `configs()`, which lists every placed widget of the app, configured or not, answers and contains the id; otherwise, and always on iOS, which sets widgets up through App Intents, "This widget can't be set up here" (en, de). A list link's days are clamped to 1–365 when it is parsed (`WidgetLink.maxDays`).
  - **Exported components** (`tool/check_exported_components.py`, run with `python3 -I`, standard library only): it compares the exported `activity`, `activity-alias`, `receiver`, `service` and `provider` elements of the merged release manifest (`exported="true"`, or an intent filter without `exported="false"`; any other value fails) with an allowlist in the script, each entry with its permission and each of its intent filters on its own (actions, categories, every attribute of its data elements). An addition, a removal or a change fails, and so do an `allowBackup` other than `false` and a missing rules attribute. `--self-test` runs 17 made-up manifests through it, `--print` shows the entries found. The allowlist, from a fresh release build, has 11 components: 7 open (`MainActivity`, `TaskLinkActivity`, `ShareTargetActivity`, flutter_web_auth_2's `CallbackActivity`, `WidgetConfigureActivity` and the two widget receivers) and 4 behind a permission (Glance's `GlanceRemoteViewsService` behind `BIND_REMOTEVIEWS`, WorkManager's `SystemJobService` behind `BIND_JOB_SERVICE`, its `DiagnosticsReceiver` and the `ProfileInstallReceiver` behind `DUMP`).
  - **The WebView review:** `webview_editor_bridge_test.dart` gets "a window.open main-frame navigation after ready is prevented" (`about:blank`, `data:`, `https:`, `intent:`), and `editor_page_policy_test.dart` pins the page's CSP directives, computes its `script-src` hash anew over the one inline script and finds no network load in the page. The channel keeps the per-load token of D52 in place of an origin check.
  - **CI and supply chain** (decisions 5 and 6):
    - `.github/workflows/osv.yaml`, a workflow of its own: one job calls `google/osv-scanner-action`'s reusable workflow at commit `b1d267ae…` (v2.7.0, its inputs read at that commit) on pushes to `main`, pull requests, by hand and on Mondays at 05:23 UTC, with `fail-on-vuln: true`, `upload-sarif: false` (true once the repository has code scanning) and `--config=osv-scanner.toml` over `pubspec.lock`, `app/editor/package-lock.json` and `app/android/app/gradle.lockfile`. No permission at the top level; the job gets exactly the `actions: read`, `contents: read` and `security-events: write` the called workflow asks for, without which GitHub does not start it. Its concurrency group includes the event's name, so a push and the weekly run never cancel each other.
    - `osv-scanner.toml` holds the accepted advisories, each with a reason and an `ignoreUntil` after which the scan fails again. One so far: GHSA-735f-pc8j-v9w8 (CVE-2024-7254, a stack overflow on nested unknown groups, fixed in 3.25.5) in `protobuf-javalite` 3.22.3, which ships with Cronet (`cronet_http` → `play-services-cronet`; since D67 `cronet-embedded` and `cronet-shared`, where Cronet also writes its engine and proxy options with it but parses nothing else). In the app only Cronet's `HttpFlagsLoader` parses with that library, for a flags file it reads from a package of the system image (`MATCH_SYSTEM_ONLY`), never for data from the network or another app; a crafted file could at worst crash it. Since D67 the app turns those flags off (`android.net.http.ReadHttpFlags=false`), so nothing in the app reaches the parser. DataStore and Glance bundle protobuf copies of their own, which the scan cannot match and which read only the app's own files. Accepted until 2027-01-31; the next dependency update fixes it. *Note 2026-10-29: protobuf-javalite left the app with the Cronet move ([D67](#d67-agpl-30-or-later-with-an-app-store-permission-contributions-under-the-dco-the-name-and-the-logo-reserved-no-f-droid-the-legal-notices-on-an-about-page-and-the-licence-page-the-source-code-published-before-the-first-store-release)'s notes), and its acceptance with it; `osv-scanner.toml` accepts nothing, so nothing expires.*
    - `ci.yaml`: `flutter pub outdated --no-dev-dependencies` at the end of the analyze-and-test job, informational (`continue-on-error`); `npm audit --package-lock-only --audit-level=high` in the editor job; the exported-components check, its self-test first, right after the release APK. No schedule there: a weekly build would find nothing the scan does not.
    - The Gradle wrapper pins `distributionSha256Sum`, that of `gradle-9.3.1-all.zip` as the cached zip and services.gradle.org give it. The wrapper checks it when it downloads, which in CI happens whenever a change of the properties file changes the cache key.
    - Gradle dependency locking for the `*RuntimeClasspath` configurations of `:app`, what ships plus the JVM tests (143 entries in `app/android/app/gradle.lockfile`), with `io.flutter:*` left out because the engine's artifacts change with every Flutter version. A changed Android dependency fails the build until `./gradlew :app:dependencies --write-locks` (in `app/android`) writes the lock again.
    - `app/test/ci/workflows_test.dart` checks the workflow files: every remote action and reusable workflow pinned to a commit with its `# v…` comment, `persist-credentials: false` on every checkout, permissions at the top of every workflow, the scan job's keys and its three permissions, `fail-on-vuln`, that the scanned lockfiles and the scripts a workflow runs exist, and no schedule in `ci.yaml`.
    - Local runs of 2026-10-27: `npm audit` found nothing in 118 packages; `pub outdated` lists 25 packages behind (10 direct, 15 transitive), none discontinued, retracted or under an advisory; the OSV API, asked with package names and versions only, found nothing among 196 pub and 118 npm packages and the protobuf advisory among the 143 Maven entries, which the audit's lookup of the six direct Android dependencies had missed.
  - **The threat model** ([threat-model.md](threat-model.md)): scope and assumptions, the assets with where they live and whether they reach backups, the actors and trust boundaries with a data-flow diagram, the entry points, STRIDE per boundary, the mitigations with their code, tests and decisions, the residual and accepted risks with theirs, the sign-off checklist (automated, on the Android emulator with the commands of the device-to-device check, and in the iOS phase), when to review it, and the rules for the later diagnostics buffer.
  - **No new dependency.**
  - **The parked session hardening:** none of the six auth files of the package reserved as D59 changed; of its file list only ARB and generated files changed here.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **A redacted copy through `FlutterError.presentError`:** every error would print twice, and the later buffer would need a second path; the redacting sink alone prints it once.
  - **Redaction at capture now** (decision 3): there is no buffer to capture into.
  - **Rotating the key on an iOS restore:** it needs a mark outside the backup, such as a file in `accounts/`; not taken.
  - **Creating the key where the account registry is built:** the headless containers build the registry too, and only the UI start may create or replace the key.
  - **The key in the snapshots and catalogs only:** Kotlin and Swift build several links without a snapshot, and Kotlin cannot read the app's DataStore preferences; `host.json` carries it to them.
  - **A forced reschedule** (a schema change) so that every scheduled notification gets the key: a keyless one asks or does nothing, never switches; left to the owner.
  - **Stripping foreign data from the intent in `MainActivity` too:** the Dart observer takes every push, and `MainActivity` keeps the intent handling of 35a and 35b as accepted.
  - **Letting a relative push through when it equals the current route:** no legitimate relative push exists.
  - **Setting the backup mark from the widgets plugin in every engine:** it would put the app's folder policy into the plugin; the window before the first UI start after a restore is accepted instead.
  - **Excluding the preferences on iOS too:** not part of the owner's decision; whether the drafts move out of them is the owner's question (the mark on the preferences file might not even stick, unverified).
  - **The scan as a job of `ci.yaml`:** that workflow's concurrency group, the same for a push to `main` and the schedule and cancelling the one in progress, would let them cancel each other.
  - **Locking every Gradle configuration**, as the spec check had it: it built too, but locked 369 entries with the build tooling of the Android Gradle plugin, which changes with every update of it, and the scan then found 18 advisories (netty, bouncycastle, guava, commons-lang3, httpclient) in test-platform configurations that never ship.
  - **Dependabot** (decision 5) and **dependency upgrades** (decision 6) in this step.
  - **Rows in a plain `Column` instead of Glance's lazy list** (the widget host finding below): the list would no longer scroll (at most 9 rows, with "N more"), it changes an accepted widget and needs an emulator check; recorded, left to the owner.
- **Consequences:**
  - Nothing of the app leaves an Android device through a backup or a transfer, and on iOS its databases, uploads and widget files do not either. An uncaught error is never printed by a release build and only redacted by a debug build. Another app can no longer push a route into the app, switch accounts silently with a made-up widget link or notification tap, or open the setup page for a widget that is not placed. CI fails when a plugin exports a component or changes an exported one, when a lockfile names a package with a known advisory, and when the editor's npm packages have a high or critical one.
  - Debug builds lose Flutter's diagnostics tree (the information collector, the widget context), the "Another exception was thrown" throttling and the IDE's structured errors: one redacted line with up to 20 stack frames instead, and the frames are not redacted. The red error screen stays.
  - The residual and accepted risks, each with its decision, are in [threat-model.md §7](threat-model.md#7-residual-and-accepted-risks); among them:
    - **The widget host acts as the app** (R17): through the list widget's collection template the launcher, or any app allowed to host widgets, can make the app start its activities, broadcasts and services, the non-exported ones included: Done or Snooze on any task of any signed-in account, a notification posted as the app, Glance's Done with any parameters. It also sees the widget-link key in the rows' links. Documented, not changed: the fix is the plain `Column` above.
    - **A second `MainActivity` in a caller's task** (R33): an app that starts `MainActivity` from its own activity without `FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK` gets a second Flutter engine on the same data inside its own task, behind its own app-lock gate. Documented, not changed: the fix (start again in its own task and finish) needs an emulator check started from another app's activity.
    - **Keyless links and taps** (R18, R19): a link without the key still acts in the active account; notifications scheduled before this step, and those a background run posts before the first UI start after the update, stay keyless until replaced (`diffSchedule` compares no payloads), and their taps ask or do nothing. A one-time forced reconcile would replace them; the owner's call.
    - **iOS backups** (R21–R23): the preferences listed above; an iOS restore brings the old widget-link key back; between a restore and the first UI start a background engine could create a folder under `accounts/` before the mark is set again, which is accepted because an app restored to a new device runs no background task before it is opened.
    - **Outside the hooks** (R24): the drift database isolate, which drift_flutter 0.3.1 spawns without an error port, prints its truly uncaught errors through the engine (its query errors return to the caller); errors before `configureLogging` runs; plugins' own prints and native logs.
    - **Supply chain** (R26, R27): the scanner image is pinned by its tag only; no workflow has run, so the weekly scan waits for a GitHub remote; native code that no lockfile describes (the bundled SQLite, the Flutter engine and the Dart runtime, Play Services' Cronet, since D67 embedded Cronet's native library) is followed by hand; the protobuf acceptance expires on 2027-01-31, when the scan fails again. *Note 2026-10-29: the acceptance was removed with protobuf-javalite in the Cronet move, so nothing expires.*
  - **Open question to the owner:** should the description and comment drafts, which are user content, leave the backed-up iOS preferences? That would be a change of its own.
  - Open in the review besides decisions 1 to 6: the key in notification taps, the protobuf acceptance, the lock scope of Gradle's runtime classpaths, the widget host's template (R17), the second `MainActivity` (R33) and a forced reconcile (R19).
  - Not built: the diagnostics export and with it redaction at capture (later); Dependabot; Gradle's dependency verification metadata; the SARIF upload (no code scanning yet); `actionlint`, which is not installed (the workflows were parsed with Ruby's YAML, and their structure is tested in Dart); the Swift build and the iOS checks of [threat-model.md §8.3](threat-model.md#83-final-ios-phase) (the backup marks, the sensitive clipboard, the key in `host.json` and in the extension's links, a tap of another account's notification). *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): the Swift builds with Xcode 27.1; on the iPad simulator `isExcludedFromBackup` was set on `accounts` and on the App Group's `widgets/`, and `widget/configure` showed "This widget can't be set up here". The sensitive clipboard, the key in `host.json` and in the extension's links and another account's notification tap wait for the device test.*
  - Tests: the hooks; the redaction rules; the leak and hygiene tests above and the domain `toString`s; the backup rules (both sections, all nine domains with `path="."`, no `<include>`, no `<cross-platform-transfer>`), the Keychain options, the entitlements, the backup exclusion and its channel calls; route injection (`route_injection_test.dart`: `x://y/home/account-settings/api-tokens/new`, the same path bare, and `/connection?origin=https://attacker.example&edit=certificate` with and without a scheme, signed out and signed in, through `handlePushRoute` and a raw `pushRouteInformation` message: handled, the location unchanged, no connection or certificate page, no certificate probe), the dropped push in `deep_link_test.dart` and the helper's canary in `organise_route_report_test.dart`; the key (a new one without the marker, kept with it, a broken one replaced, the comparison), the links with and without it in Dart and Kotlin (`WidgetLinksTest.kt`), the key in `host.json`, the listener and the tap listener without and with a wrong key (no switch, nothing marked read, a task through the account picker), a payload without `k`; the setup page (a foreign id, `configs()` failing, the form from the Widgets page) and the clamped days; the WebView's `window.open` case and the page's policy; the workflows; the exported-components check's self-test. The leak test, the route-injection test, the hygiene test's self-test and the checker's self-test each fail with their fix taken out. In total 4,955 app tests (212 contract and Docker tests skipped), 337 in `vikunja_domain`, 3,163 in `vikunja_logic`, 1,719 in `vikunja_api`, 20 in `rurana_widgets` and 33 in Kotlin pass. The arm64 release APK is 34.2 MB.
  - Checked on the Android emulator: the three route injections through `adb` (another scheme, `/connection?origin=…&edit=certificate`, a bare path) left the screen as it was; a widget account link without the key and a made-up `SELECT_NOTIFICATION` tap for the other account did not switch accounts; `widget/configure?appWidgetId=999` showed "This widget can't be set up here"; a list widget placed from the launcher opened its setup, and its rows and its setup links worked with the key; in the device-to-device test mode `bmgr` reported that the app "doesn't have any backup data"; the exported-components check reported "11 exported components as allowed; backups off." for the release build; the debug build for the emulator's ABI built with the Gradle lockfile. An uncaught error cannot be forced safely in a release build, so the hooks' tests stand in for a look at logcat; nothing was run on iOS.

## D67: AGPL-3.0-or-later with an app store permission, contributions under the DCO, the name and the logo reserved, no F-Droid; the legal notices on an About page and the licence page; the source code published before the first store release

*Accepted 2026-10-27, the licence step between steps 35 and 36 (Android checked with one release build; the emulator check is the main session's; nothing was built for iOS). Answers [Q3](open-questions.md#q3-licence-and-distribution). The owner's decisions are listed below; decisions 1 to 11 were taken without the owner and are open in the review. Closes the gap [D52](#d52-descriptions-edited-in-a-bundled-tiptap-page-in-a-locked-down-webview-with-a-native-toolbar-checklists-ticked-in-place-no-silent-overwrite-of-a-description-changed-on-the-web) left ("an in-app licences page … is still missing"). Corrects [D21](#d21-languages-english-and-german), whose `error.*` ARB import (step 2) was never built: error texts are written per error code in our own words. This entry explains choices; it is not legal advice.*

- **Context:**
  - Q3 was deferred on 2026-10-09. By now the app follows the web frontend's behaviour in many places (quick add, filters, dates, the editor's schema, error texts), and Vikunja is AGPL-3.0-or-later ("Copyright 2018-present Vikunja and contributors"). The project rule was to read the web for behaviour and never copy it.
  - Apple's App Store terms add restrictions that the GPL family does not allow. An additional permission under AGPL section 7 is the usual way out, but only copyright holders can grant it, and only for their own work: section 7 lets anyone remove the permission from a part it does not cover. Copied Vikunja text or code in the app would be such a part.
  - **The audit** (2026-10-27, against Vikunja v2.7.1, scripts outside the repository):
    - Texts: 2,085 keys per ARB file; 93 keys share 4 or more words or 25 characters with the web's translations. 15 keys were whole or close copies (24 values, 13 en and 11 de); 78 are stock phrases and functional labels.
    - Code: the Dart, Kotlin and Swift code has no copied passages; the ports re-express the web's behaviour. The editor page had five near-identical blocks from the web's TipTap code and one small function (E1–E6: the task link node and its paste handler, the task item ids, the image attributes, the quote's comment id, the task and attachment URL parsing). `vikunja_logic`'s rich-text parser translates parts of prosemirror-model (MIT), which no notice named. 38 test files and fixtures carry Vikunja's test vectors; they do not ship.
    - Brand: 135 image files are the llama logo or derived from it.
    - Notices: Flutter's own licence collection covers the Dart packages and the engine only. The app showed none of: the editor bundle's 44 MIT packages, the code `vikunja_logic` translates (fexpr, go-datemath, prosemirror-model), the 141 Android libraries of the release build, the Material Icons font.
  - The review of the first version of this step found: the editor rewrite not yet done; the open-source components inside Google's Play services libraries without notices on the device; desugar_jdk_libs (GPL-2.0 with the Classpath exception) without a source offer; the AGPL shown twice on the licence page, once under the workspace's internal name `vikunja_workspace`; a pull request able to change its own sign-off check; no guard against a release without the source link.
  - The review of the switch to embedded Cronet found: Cronet still applied HTTP flags from a system-image package, which could reconfigure the bundled stack or replace it with the platform's `HttpEngine`; the app's notice pointing to a list of logo files it does not contain; libc++, libc++abi and the Rust standard library linked into Cronet's native library without notices; the version test blocking a deliberate Cronet update; the bundled Cronet being a pre-stable trunk build. *Note 2026-10-29: LLVM's libunwind, linked the same way, was missed then; the Cronet move adds it (decision 5's note).*
- **Decision:**
  - **Owner decisions of 2026-10-27:**
    - **AGPL-3.0-or-later** for the whole repository: the app, the packages, the plugin, the tools and the documentation.
    - **An app store permission** under AGPL section 7 from the start, in the owner's words (`NOTICE.md`, "App stores"): distribution through an app store whose terms conflict with the AGPL, such as Apple's App Store, provided that the Corresponding Source is also available under the AGPL through a channel without such restrictions.
    - **Contributions under the Developer Certificate of Origin 1.1**: every commit of a contribution carries `Signed-off-by:`; contributors keep their copyright. Copyright line: "Copyright (C) 2026 Benedikt Lesch and the Rurana contributors".
    - **The name "Rurana" and the llama logo are not under the AGPL**: all rights reserved by Benedikt Lesch; a fork that distributes an app uses its own name and logo.
    - **F-Droid is not a distribution target**: no F-Droid flavour, so the bundled SQLite stays as it is.
    - **Embedded Cronet instead of Google Play services Cronet** (answers open question 1 of the first version): the app ships no Play services library, so contributors' code is combined only with free libraries and needs no further permission. The app's Gradle build makes the switch for every build, test and CI run, without a `--dart-define` ([D5](#d5-dio-with-native_dio_adapter)).
    - **The logo files may be copied unmodified** (answers open question 2 of the first version), in the owner's words in `NOTICE.md`: only as part of a copy of the repository or of its history, or of an unmodified build of the app published by Benedikt Lesch; no other right is granted, and an app built from modified source may not be distributed with them. The app's notice carries the sentence verbatim; the README, `branding/README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and the About page say it in short.
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-10-27), open in the review:**
    1. **`NOTICE.md`** as the notice file: copyright, the AGPL's "how to apply" notice (or later), the permission verbatim, the brand files by glob, the third-party material and the test files with Vikunja vectors (AGPL only, without the permission, not shipped).
    2. **Rewrite rather than attribute** whatever the audit found copied and shipped: the 15 ARB keys (en and de, in the tone of [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list)) and E1–E6 in `app/editor/src/extensions.js` and `links.js`, written again from the behaviour with the web's sources closed. What the stored HTML needs stayed: the node and attribute names, the task link's rank above the Link mark and its `a[href]` rule at priority 100, the plain-anchor conditions, `['a', attrs, href]`, an 8-character `data-task-id` per item, a positive integer `data-comment-id`, `data-src` with `src="#"` and `id="tiptap-image-<task>-<attachment>"`, and the attachment URL pattern. MIT, BSD, ISC and Apache material allows app stores and is attributed.
    3. **The licence page's sources:** `LicenseRegistry.addLicense` once at bootstrap, reading committed assets only when the page asks; Flutter's own collection keeps the Dart packages, the engine and the AGPL.
    4. **The workspace root package is named `rurana`** (was `vikunja_workspace`): Flutter's build lists a `LICENSE` beside every package of `package_config.json`, the workspace root's included, so the root `LICENSE` appears under `rurana`, and the app's notice joins it under the same name. No workspace member may carry a `LICENSE` or `NOTICES` file; a test checks it.
    5. **Cronet's licence file is copied into the repository:** the Cronet AARs carry none; their POMs name "Chromium and built-in dependencies" with the `LICENSE` that Chromium publishes beside each Cronet build's jars (bucket `chromium-cronet`, folder `android/143.0.7445.0/Release/cronet/`). `--refresh-cronet` downloads it for the locked cronet-embedded version into `tool/android_notices/cronet/`, with `source.json` (the addresses and SHA-256s), after checking that the folder's `cronet_impl_native_java.jar` is byte for byte the AAR's `classes.jar` (by hand, the other four Cronet artifacts' jars match too). The file holds Chromium's licence, which must be `chromium.txt`, and 44 sections of other projects' code in the native library: BoringSSL, QUICHE, zlib, Brotli, Zstandard, Abseil, Perfetto, LLVM's compiler-rt and libc, the Rust crates and others (43 names, 25 distinct texts). On the licence page each is its own entry; one with the text of a section above joins it. The file does not name what the native library links statically: Chromium's libc++ and libc++abi (`std::__Cr` symbols, `third_party/libc++abi` paths) and Rust's `core` and `alloc`. The generator adds them (`CRONET_SUPPLEMENT`): libc++ and libc++abi with LLVM's `LICENSE.TXT` texts (Apache-2.0 with LLVM exceptions, plus the legacy licences; `tool/android_notices/libcxx.txt` and `libcxxabi.txt`, copied from the Android NDK 29 toolchain notice), and the Rust standard library (MIT OR Apache-2.0) under the Apache-2.0 section. Until Cronet changes, the check needs neither Gradle nor the network. `android.md` is 214 KB, 26 KB compressed in the APK (729 KB and 117 KB with the Play services components of the first version). *Note 2026-10-29, the Cronet move ([D73](#d73-gitlab-ci-from-scripts-that-run-the-same-locally-images-and-downloads-pinned-the-version-from-the-release-tag-release-signing-from-protected-ci-variables-the-source-link-from-the-build-a-gitlab-release-with-the-apk-its-checksums-and-the-desugar_jdk_libs-source)): the AARs of `cronet-bundled` and `cronet` 500.x carry their licences themselves, in `third_party_licenses.txt` with an index of byte offsets, `third_party_licenses.json`. The entry "Cronet" is Chromium's licence file for the build, byte for byte the bucket's `LICENSE` of 155.0.8059.16, which now puts Chromium's own licence under a heading "The Chromium Project"; the other six entries are the Java dependencies of Chromium's build (the AndroidX annotation library and its experimental library, JSR 305, Kotlin, Protocol Buffers for Java, java_annotations). `--refresh-cronet` copies both files from the locked version's AARs in the Gradle cache into `tool/android_notices/cronet/`, with `source.json` (both AARs' SHA-256s, the Chromium build and commit, the day it became stable, the bucket's address, the files' SHA-256s), after checking that exactly `cronet` and `cronet-bundled` of one version are locked, that each AAR has the SHA-256 Google's Maven publishes, that the build in the native library's name is the one in `cronet`'s `ImplVersion` (with its commit), that `NativeCronetProviderSentinel` is there, that both AARs carry the same licence files and the index covers the text exactly, that Chromium Dash lists the build at that commit as a Chrome stable release for Android, and that the "Cronet" entry equals the bucket's `LICENSE` where the bucket has the build. The bucket's jars are no longer a check: the AARs' classes are shrunk by R8 and their native libraries differ from the bucket's. The 155 file names 49 components after Chromium's licence (51 headings, `CityHash` and `syn` twice), with 27 distinct texts; each index entry and component is its own entry on the licence page, or joins the section whose text it has. The 155 library still links libc++, libc++abi and Rust's standard library, and the files still name none of them. It also links LLVM's libunwind statically in every ABI (`libunwind:` messages and `N9libunwind` type names; so did 143), which neither the files nor the supplement named: the supplement adds it with libunwind's `LICENSE.TXT` (`tool/android_notices/libunwind.txt`; Apache-2.0 with LLVM exceptions plus the legacy licences, from Chromium's `third_party/libunwind/src` at the revision the DEPS of 155.0.8059.16 pins, `24a407d5…`). `android.md` is 284 KB, 33 KB compressed.*
    6. **A written offer for desugar_jdk_libs**, the one GPL-2.0 library in the APK (core library desugaring, which flutter_local_notifications needs): the licence page names its source and offers it for three years, from Benedikt Lesch, as GPL-2.0 section 3(b) asks. The owner confirms the wording and adds the contact before the first release.
    7. **"Licences" on the licence page in English:** Flutter's page says "Licenses"; a `MaterialLocalizations` override for English replaces only its title and counts.
    8. **The sign-off check comes from the pull request's base commit**, and merge commits need a sign-off too.
    9. **A release-only test for the source link** (below).
    10. **Cronet reads no HTTP flags and sends no telemetry:** `AndroidManifest.xml` declares the disabled, unexported placeholder service `android.net.http.MetaDataHolder` (Cronet looks it up by that name; the class does not exist) with `android.net.http.ReadHttpFlags` and `android.net.http.EnableTelemetry` set to `false`. Without it Cronet reads a flags file from whichever system-image package offers `android.net.http.FLAGS_FILE_PROVIDER` (on Google Play devices probably Play services; not checked on a device), turns its entries into overrides of Chromium's features and Cronet's settings, and with `Cronet_OverrideNativeCronetWithHttpEngine`, on Android 11+ with SDK extension 7, hands out the platform's `HttpEngine` instead of the bundled library. Telemetry is already off by default for embedded Cronet; the entry keeps it off if that default changes. *Note 2026-10-29, the Cronet move: Chromium 155 reads the same placeholder and keys (`CronetManifest` and `HttpFlagsLoader` at tag 155.0.8059.16, matched against the bytecode of `cronet` 500.1.0); with `ReadHttpFlags=false` it reads no flags file, so `Cronet_OverrideNativeCronetWithHttpEngine` and the other flags stay unset. Its telemetry default still hangs on a class lookup, so the entry stays. The 500.x AARs declare no permissions, so the app declares `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE`, which Cronet's network change notifier needs, itself (WorkManager's manifest brings it too).*
    11. **The app's notice says where the logo files are listed:** after the owner's sentence, which speaks of "the files listed below", `NOTICE.md` and the app's notice add "The files are listed in NOTICE.md at the root of the app's source code repository.", because the licence page has no such list. The owner's sentence is unchanged.
  - **The repository:**
    - `LICENSE`: the AGPL v3 text from gnu.org, unchanged (SHA-256 `0d96a4ff…abcb0`, checked by a test).
    - `NOTICE.md`; `CONTRIBUTING.md` (issues, pull requests and the checks, the DCO text, new strings in `app_en.arb` only, the brand, the style rules); the README's "License" section; a paragraph in `branding/README.md`; `app/editor/package.json` says `AGPL-3.0-or-later`. The packages and the plugin are not published and need no licence file of their own.
    - `.github/workflows/dco.yaml`, pull requests only, minimal permissions, actions pinned: it runs the pull request's `tool/check_dco.py --self-test`, then the base commit's copy of the script (`git show "$BASE_SHA:tool/check_dco.py"`, `--repository "$GITHUB_WORKSPACE"`) over `base..head`. Every commit, merges included, needs a `Signed-off-by:` with its author's name and email (letter case aside). The history before a pull request is not checked.
  - **What the app shows** (AGPL section 5(d)):
    - **About page** (`/about`, outside the shell, so signed out, for guests and for users): from the Account tab's version line and, signed out, from a button on the Connect screen. The app's name, version and copyright line; "free software under the AGPL-3.0-or-later with an additional permission for app stores"; no warranty; Licences; the source code; the name and the logo. en and de.
    - **The source code link** is one constant, `sourceCodeUrl` in `app/lib/app/legal.dart`, null while the repository is not public; the page then says the source code will be published with the first release. No placeholder address. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37a ([D73](#d73-gitlab-ci-from-scripts-that-run-the-same-locally-images-and-downloads-pinned-the-version-from-the-release-tag-release-signing-from-protected-ci-variables-the-source-link-from-the-build-a-gitlab-release-with-the-apk-its-checksums-and-the-desugar_jdk_libs-source)): `sourceCodeUrl` is now a build define, `String.fromEnvironment('SOURCE_CODE_URL')`, null when empty. Only the GitLab release job sets it, to the project's URL (`$CI_PROJECT_URL`); every other build shows no link, as before.*
    - **The licence page** (Flutter's `LicensePage`): under `rurana` the app's notice (the licence notice, the permission and the name and logo, worded as `NOTICE.md`; a test compares them) and the AGPL from Flutter's build; the editor's npm packages (`app/assets/editor/NOTICES.md`); the code `vikunja_logic` translates (`THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, now with prosemirror-model); the Material Icons font and the three copied icon shapes (CC BY 4.0, from the Flutter SDK's licence file); on Android only, the 140 Android libraries (Apache-2.0, BSD-3-Clause for protobuf and the five Cronet artifacts, GPL-2.0 with the Classpath exception plus the written offer for desugar_jdk_libs) and the code of other projects in embedded Cronet, with libc++, libc++abi and the Rust standard library. *Note 2026-10-29, the Cronet move: 136 libraries; BSD-3-Clause for the two Cronet artifacts, protobuf-javalite gone; libunwind joins libc++, libc++abi and the Rust standard library.*
  - **Generators and checks:**
    - `tool/build_editor.sh` writes `NOTICES.md` from what esbuild bundled and refuses a licence text with a line starting with ```, which would cut the text short on the licence page; `--check` compares both committed files.
    - `tool/build_android_notices.py` writes `app/assets/legal/android.md` from the lockfile's release classpath and `build.gradle.kts`, refuses unknown Maven groups, Google Play services libraries (`com.google.android.gms`), a bundled "external" AndroidX artifact without an entry and fenced lines, and fails when the stored Cronet licence file does not belong to the locked Cronet build, was edited, or starts with another Chromium licence than `chromium.txt`; it adds the components of `CRONET_SUPPLEMENT` that the licence file does not name itself; `--check` and `--self-test` run in CI's analyze-and-test job. *Note 2026-10-29, the Cronet move: it reads the stored `third_party_licenses.txt` and `.json` of the locked `cronet` and `cronet-bundled` version (decision 5's note), fails for any other `org.chromium.net` library in the release classpath and for a `source.json` whose build, commit, stable day, AAR hashes or bucket address `--refresh-cronet` could not have written, and lists the index's other entries as components of `cronet-bundled`.*
    - The release-only test `app/test/app/release_source_code_test.dart` (tag `release`, skipped unless run with `--run-skipped --tags release`) fails while `sourceCodeUrl` is null; both release build jobs run it on tag builds. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37a ([D73](#d73-gitlab-ci-from-scripts-that-run-the-same-locally-images-and-downloads-pinned-the-version-from-the-release-tag-release-signing-from-protected-ci-variables-the-source-link-from-the-build-a-gitlab-release-with-the-apk-its-checksums-and-the-desugar_jdk_libs-source)): the GitLab release job runs it with the build's define before it builds; it fails without the define.*
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Attributing the copied editor code and texts instead of rewriting them:** the AGPL allows the copy, but the app store permission could not cover it, and an App Store build would rest on it.
  - **The AGPL as an app asset of its own** (the first version's `agpl-3.0.txt`): Flutter's build already lists the root `LICENSE`, so the page showed the licence twice. A `NOTICES` file in `vikunja_logic` or a pubspec `flutter: licenses:` list would show internal package names the same way.
  - **Downloading Cronet's licence file at check time:** CI would need the network for the check; the committed copy keeps it offline and still catches a version bump.
  - **A `LICENSE` copy in each package and in the plugin:** they are not published on their own, and each copy would add an entry under an internal name to the licence page.
  - **A second section 7 permission for the Play services libraries** (the other answer to the first version's open question 1): the owner chose embedded Cronet, so the app ships nothing that is not free software.
  - **`--dart-define=cronetHttpNoPlay=true` on the build commands:** one forgotten define (a local `flutter run`, a test, a new CI job) would ship Play services again; the exclusion in the app's Gradle build cannot be forgotten.
  - **`dart:io` instead of Cronet:** it ignores system proxies and user-installed CAs on Android ([D5](#d5-dio-with-native_dio_adapter)).
  - **Keeping Cronet's HTTP flags** (Chromium's default): a system-image package could then change the bundled stack's features and settings and, on Android 11+ with SDK extension 7, replace it with the platform's `HttpEngine`, so "the bundled Cronet, updated with the app" would hold only on devices without a flags provider.
  - **A contributor licence agreement:** the owner chose the DCO.
- **Consequences:**
  - **Step 37 must, before the first store release:** publish the repository and set `sourceCodeUrl` (AGPL section 6, and the permission's own condition); add release tags to CI so that the release-only test runs; add the contact to the written offer and keep the desugar_jdk_libs source of each release (its exact commit recorded) beside the release's Corresponding Source; raise Cronet from the pre-stable 143.0.7445.0 to a build of a current Chrome stable release (licence, provider and `--refresh-cronet`); check the notices of the release build again. *Note 2026-10-29: Cronet is raised (the Cronet move, under "Embedded Cronet" below).*
  - **AGPL section 13** (remote network interaction) does not matter here: the app is conveyed to its users as object code, which section 6 covers. Section 13 concerns a modified program that users interact with remotely over a network; the app is not offered that way, and the Vikunja server is not part of this repository.
  - **F-Droid:** none; nothing in the build changes for it.
  - **Contributors' code and the permission:** contributions carry the same terms, permission included (`CONTRIBUTING.md` and the DCO's "license indicated in the file"). Tests with Vikunja vectors stay AGPL-only and never ship.
  - **Embedded Cronet** (owner decision above):
    - Every Android device gets Cronet, with or without Play services, so user-installed CAs and system proxies now work on de-Googled devices too ([D5](#d5-dio-with-native_dio_adapter)). It is always the bundled build as configured by the app: with the HTTP flags off (decision 10), no system-image package can change its features or settings or swap in the platform's `HttpEngine`.
    - The release APK built with `--target-platform android-arm64` grew from 34.5 MB to 52.2 MB: Flutter's build keeps the plugins' native libraries for arm64, armv7 and x86_64, and `libcronet.143.0.7445.0.so` is 6.5, 4.2 and 7.2 MB (stored uncompressed); the rest of the APK shrank by 0.16 MB (Play services' resources and the longer notices out, Cronet's Java classes in). A device gets only its own ABI from an app bundle (+6.5 MB on arm64). *Note 2026-10-29, the Cronet move: 54.5 MB, with `libcronet.155.0.8059.16.so` at 7.3, 4.4 and 8.3 MB.*
    - Chromium's network stack (Cronet 143.0.7445.0, built 2025-10-01) is now part of the app and no longer updated by Play services on the device: its security fixes come only with an app update that raises the version (`build.gradle.kts`, then `--refresh-cronet`). The test asks only for at least the version cronet_http names, so a newer one can be taken on purpose. Build 7445.0 is a trunk build from just after Chromium's M142 branch point (Chrome 142 stable shipped as 142.0.7444.x), not a stable release: it has none of the security fixes merged into the M142 or M143 stable branches for `net/`, BoringSSL or QUICHE. Newer Cronet is published as `org.chromium.net:cronet-bundled` 500.x (2026-10-01), still under Chromium's licence; the transitional `cronet-embedded` 500.x POM points to Google's SDK terms instead, so its licence needs checking before any upgrade. A switch to `cronet-bundled` means editing the artifact in `build.gradle.kts`, `embedded_cronet_test.dart` and `CRONET_LIBRARY` in `tool/build_android_notices.py`, and checking its provider and its licence file. **Trigger and owner:** step 37 raises Cronet to a build of a current Chrome stable release before the first store release; after that the owner reviews it at each Chrome stable release whose security fixes touch `net/`, BoringSSL or QUICHE, and at each dependency update ([threat model](threat-model.md) R26 and section 9). *Note 2026-10-29, the Cronet move ([D73](#d73-gitlab-ci-from-scripts-that-run-the-same-locally-images-and-downloads-pinned-the-version-from-the-release-tag-release-signing-from-protected-ci-variables-the-source-link-from-the-build-a-gitlab-release-with-the-apk-its-checksums-and-the-desugar_jdk_libs-source)): Cronet is `org.chromium.net:cronet-bundled` 500.1.0, which brings `org.chromium.net:cronet` 500.1.0: Chromium 155.0.8059.16, which reached Chrome Android Stable on 2026-09-23 (its commit `eb81d1e9…`, compiled into `ImplVersion`, is the one Chromium Dash records for that release). Google has published Cronet only under these two names since 500.0.1 (2026-07-29); the Maven version is no longer Chromium's, which the native library's name and `ImplVersion` carry, and the two POMs pin each other. Both POMs name "Chromium License and licenses of built-in dependencies" and come from Google's Maven repository (no `<organization>`; the AARs have `.asc` signatures, not verified, and the checks rest on Google's published SHA-256). `cronet-embedded` 500.x and the other transitional artifacts (`cronet-api`, `cronet-common`, `cronet-fallback`, `cronet-shared`, `httpengine-native-provider`) are empty, under the Android SDK licence, and pulled by the POMs: the app's Gradle build excludes them from every configuration, which also cuts cronet_http's `cronet-embedded` 143.7445.0 under `cronetHttpNoPlay`, so the release classpath's Cronet is exactly the two artifacts. cronet_http 1.10.0 stays: its Kotlin proxy compiles against 500.1.0, and all 124 of its JNI lookups in `org.chromium.net` resolve with the same descriptors in the AAR; in the APK all but three do: the `java.time.Duration` setters of `DnsOptions` (`setPersistDelay`, `setFreshLookupTimeout`, `setMaxExpiredDelay`), which core library desugaring rewrites to `j$.time.Duration` and which cronet_http's client never calls (it uses the `…Millis` setters, and jnigen looks a method up only when it is called), as with 143; 1.10.1 still names 143.7445.0. `NativeCronetProvider` (score 5, ahead of the platform's `HttpEngine` and Play services) is enabled by `NativeCronetProviderSentinel` in `cronet-bundled`, which its R8 rule and cronet_http's keep, so the bundled library is always chosen. Still behind the newest stable (157.0.8103.30 on 2026-10-28, and M155 had patch releases after .16), but a stable build. Google has published three versions in nine weeks so far (3 to 6 weeks apart), each 1 to 2.5 weeks after the Chrome stable release it is built from; raising it is a version in `build.gradle.kts`, the locks, one release build and `--refresh-cronet`, which refuses a build that is not a Chrome stable release ([releasing.md](releasing.md), R34).*
    - `protobuf-javalite` 3.22.3 stays: cronet-embedded and cronet-shared bring it, and the OSV acceptance of GHSA-735f-pc8j-v9w8 (D66) now names them. Its only parse in the app was of the HTTP flags file; with the flags off nothing reaches it, and Cronet only writes its engine and proxy options with it. The OSV API (2026-10-27) knows no advisory for the five Cronet artifacts at 143.7445.0. *Note 2026-10-29, the Cronet move: protobuf-javalite left the app with cronet-embedded and cronet-shared, and its OSV acceptance is removed; `cronet` 500.1.0 carries a newer copy shaded into `org.chromium.net.internal.com.google.protobuf` ("Protocol Buffers for Java" in its licences), which no lockfile names. The OSV API knows no advisory for `cronet` or `cronet-bundled` 500.1.0 (2026-10-29).*
    - image_picker_android's manifest still declares the disabled `com.google.android.gms.metadata.ModuleDependencies` service, a hint for Play services to install the photo picker backport; it names no class in the app.
  - **Open question to the owner:** the wording of the desugar_jdk_libs offer and its contact.
  - Not built: an F-Droid flavour; a licence page of our own; signing and store builds (step 37).
  - Tests: the editor's (266, among them the refused fenced licence text) and a comparison of the page before and after the E1–E6 rewrite (4,068 inputs and link contexts, byte-identical HTML); the generators' self-tests (Cronet's licence file split with a repeated name, comment markers and shared texts, the supplement joining a section or a block and never replacing a component the file names, a stored file of another version, an edited one and one with another Chromium licence refused, the refresh against a Gradle cache and a build folder made in the test (*since the Cronet move: the AARs' licence index with overlaps, gaps, a tail and a missing "Cronet" entry refused, the heading before Chromium's licence, and `--refresh-cronet` against a Gradle cache, Google's Maven, Chromium Dash and the bucket made in the test, each of its checks refused once, and a `source.json` with a malformed or missing build, commit, stable day, AAR hash or bucket address refused*), the Play services group refused, the offer after the GPL text, the DCO cases with signed and unsigned merges); `legal_files_test.dart` (the LICENSE hash, the permission verbatim, the owner's sentence on the logo files with the pointer to their list in `NOTICE.md` and the app's notice, every path `NOTICE.md` names, the README section); `app_licenses_test.dart` (the parser with names in backticks, every committed notices file parsed to as many entries as it has blocks, the root package as the only one with a licence file, the code of other projects in embedded Cronet on Android only, libc++, libc++abi and the Rust standard library among them (*since the Cronet move also libunwind and the AARs' Java entries*), no Play services library, the offer); `embedded_cronet_test.dart` (no Play services library in the release classpath, the exclusion in the Gradle build, the manifest's `MetaDataHolder` with the HTTP flags and telemetry off, embedded Cronet at least at the version cronet_http names, compared by number; *since the Cronet move: exactly `cronet` and `cronet-bundled` of the declared version in the release classpath, the transitional artifacts excluded, at least 500.1.0 and Chromium 155.0.8059.16, `source.json` written for the declared version, the Chromium build at least the one cronet_http names, and `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` declared*); `about_page_test.dart` (signed in, guest, signed out, the null source text, the licence page in British English); the workflow test that the DCO check is the base commit's.
  - The similarity check run again over `app/editor/src` after the rewrite found no block of 5 or more near-identical lines against the web's editor code or `parseTaskIdFromUrl.ts`; what matches is import lines, the node name with its rank, and the Link options.

## D68: Screen readers: heading levels on screen titles and section headers, one announcer with the app's own live region on Android, high contrast and Reduce Motion followed, and a route sweep that every route, pushed page and overlay must pass; WCAG 2.2 AA as the yardstick with written deviations

*Accepted 2026-10-28, step 36a (Android: one release build and two checks on the tablet emulator; the manual TalkBack pass could not be performed there and is pending; nothing was built for iOS). Decisions 1 to 8 below were taken without the owner and are open in the review, and so are the choices of the spec check listed after them. The first sub-step of step 36 as the owner split it on 2026-10-28 ([roadmap](roadmap.md)). Keeps [D2](#d2-material_ui-and-cupertino_ui): every change uses material_ui and cupertino_ui. Keeps the row layout of [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list), where an overdue due date now gets an icon of its own, and the navigation-label clamps of D56 (1.15× on Android's bar) and [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner) (1.3× on the rail), now listed as deviations. The screen-reader moves of [D47](#d47-kanban-board-from-per-view-board-snapshots-moves-and-positions-through-the-outbox-with-the-done-buckets-rules-accessible-drag-and-drop) and [D49](#d49-manual-ordering-list-views-by-drag-through-the-outbox-projects-reordered-and-nested-online-never-a-project-position-below-01) stay as they are; on Android their announcements now reach TalkBack through the app's live region. Takes the overdue icon of the home-screen widgets of [D63](#d63-home-screen-widgets-from-json-snapshots-the-app-writes-through-an-in-repo-plugin-glance-widgets-on-android-with-done-through-a-headless-dart-worker-a-widgetkit-extension-on-ios-with-an-app-group-outbox-and-no-token) into the app. Adds a rule for the strings of [D21](#d21-languages-english-and-german): new and changed keys get descriptions. [Q19](open-questions.md#q19-device-setup-for-the-manual-talkback-pass) stays open (below).*

- **Context:**
  - The research for step 36 (2026-10-28) audited the app's accessibility; the owner put 36a–36g on the roadmap that day and started 36a. A spec with decisions 1 to 8, a check of the spec (32 findings, none rejected outright, 9 accepted with changes), three areas built in parallel (headings and the announcer, the sweep, the fixes), a reconcile step and a review (7 findings confirmed, 2 rejected) shaped the result.
  - What existed: 41 `header: true` sites without a heading level, and app bar titles that Material marks as headers without one, so TalkBack and VoiceOver found next to no headings; 15 direct `SemanticsService.sendAnnouncement` calls next to a small `announce` helper; accessibility smoke tests of selected screens at 2.0× text in dark mode, Flutter's contrast guideline on the lock screen only, and no check that every screen is covered; nothing tested above 2.0× text, although iOS goes to 3.12×; no high-contrast theme; neither Remove animations nor Reduce Motion read; an overdue due date in rows told only by its colour once an absolute date format is set (D45); Gantt relations only drawn; the session, API token and CalDAV lists refreshed only by pulling.
  - Verified platform facts (research and spec check of 2026-10-28, from the Flutter engine and framework sources, material_ui 1.6.0, cupertino_ui 1.1.2 and probes):
    - **Headings:** Android and iOS make a node a heading only when its heading level is above 0; Android also treats a header-flagged direct child of a list as one, through `CollectionItemInfo`. Elsewhere `header: true` alone is no heading, and `headingLevel` alone leaves the header flag off. Material's AppBar wraps its title in `Semantics(header: true)` without a level and names the route with it on Android. Two header flags conflict and never merge: a `Semantics(header: true)` inside the title becomes a node of its own below an empty level-0 header, while a `headingLevel` alone merges into the title's node.
    - **Announcements:** Android's accessibility bridge always reports that it cannot announce, on every version, so `MediaQuery.supportsAnnounceOf` is false there; Android 16 deprecates `announceForAccessibility`. A live region speaks on Android only when its label changes, on iOS also when a node newly gets the flag; a node with an empty rect is dropped; a modal barrier's `BlockSemantics` drops only what was painted before it. Material's `InputDecorator` makes a field's error a live region wherever the platform cannot announce. flutter_test reports `supportsAnnounce` as true by default, but `FakeAccessibilityFeatures` defaults it to false.
    - **High contrast:** iOS Increase Contrast sets `highContrast`, and so does, from Android 14, the system Contrast setting at medium or high; `MaterialApp` then takes its `highContrastTheme` and `highContrastDarkTheme`.
    - **Motion:** Android's Remove animations sets `disableAnimations`; iOS Reduce Motion sets only `accessibilityFeatures.reduceMotion`, which `MediaQueryData` does not carry, and the framework's page and sheet transitions follow only the former. Scroll animations ignore both. `DraggableScrollableController.animateTo` refuses a zero duration.
    - **Text size:** iOS scales Dynamic Type up to AX5, 53/17 ≈ 3.12× (AX3 is 2.35×). Material's AppBar clamps its title at 1.34×.
    - **Test guidelines:** Flutter's contrast guideline finds texts through `find.text`, so colour-coded text inside a merged label is invisible to it, and its contrast and tap-target guidelines skip nodes off screen or at a scroll edge. With Roboto in tests, anti-aliasing makes the contrast guideline measure two background shades (a false 1.11:1); on iOS Material asks for the Cupertino font families anyway.
