  - The review of the first result found: section headings merged into the first row of their section, so that the heading became that row's button (A11Y-R1); form errors spoken twice (R2); pages pushed with the Navigator outside the completeness guard (R4); the search route named only by its hint on Android (R5); the iOS day-range sheet overflowing at 3.12× (C1); scenarios that no file ran (C3); the team name as a heading twice (C4). All are fixed below.
- **Decision:**
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-10-28), open in the review:**
    1. **WCAG 2.2 AA as the internal yardstick**, with the deviations below and no public conformance claim.
    2. **Heading levels:** level 1 for the screen and sheet titles, level 2 for section headers; the rich-text view keeps the description's h1–h6.
    3. **One announcer:** the platform's announcement where it has one, otherwise a polite live region of the app's own; every announcement goes through it.
    4. **iOS text up to 3.12×** without a global clamp, tested on six core screens: the Overview, a project list, the task sheet, the edit page, the new-task sheet and the Account tab.
    5. **The navigation-label clamps stay**, 1.15× on Android's bar and 1.3× on the iOS tab bar and the rail, as deviations; a long press shows the label as a tooltip.
    6. **High-contrast themes** when the platform asks for more contrast, light and dark; **Reduce Motion** followed where the app animates on its own.
    7. **A distinct overdue icon** beside the colour; the row layout stays.
    8. **ARB descriptions** from 36a on for new and changed strings, starting with "Screen reader: " for strings that are only spoken; new plurals put the number in the singular branch as well (`=1{{count} task}`). Existing strings wait for step 36f.
  - **Also taken without the owner, by the spec check and in the build, open in the review:**
    - App bar titles count as screen titles, at level 1, so Headings navigation now starts at each screen's title.
    - On Android every announcement goes through the app's live region, on every version and not only on Android 16, because the engine reports no announce support on any. Whether TalkBack speaks it as reliably is still unchecked (below).
    - High contrast also follows Android 14+'s Contrast setting, and with system colours on, the high-contrast scheme comes from the system's primary colour, not from the brand blue.
    - The iOS tab bar's inactive labels turn from Cupertino's grey (2.71:1 in light, 3.70:1 in dark) to `onSurfaceVariant`, a visible change, instead of a 1.4.3 deviation.
    - App-bar text actions (Save, Create, Upload) and the headers of the iOS picker and day-range sheets grow to 2.0× at most.
    - 2.5.7 is recorded as a deviation instead of adding a visible "Move to…" menu item.
    - The platform target sizes and the iOS alert contrast below are recorded as deviations instead of taller segments and alert colours always in high contrast, both visible changes.
  - **Headings:**
    - `ScreenTitle` (`features/common/presentation/screen_title.dart`) is the title of 41 app bars in 36 files: `Semantics(headingLevel: 1)` around the text, which merges into the app bar's own title node, so the title stays one node (header, label, level 1 and on Android the route's name). It asserts in debug builds that it sits in an `AppBar` that marks its title. The connect screen's add-account bar has no title.
    - The 40 `header: true` sites name their level in the same `Semantics`. Level 1: the titles the app draws itself (the lock screen, the Overview's header, the connect and first-sync screens, the notification setup, the task sheet, the new-task sheet, `PickerHeader` and the other pickers, the option sheet, the account switcher, the session and token detail sheets, the reaction picker, the quick-add help, the shared-link, description-conflict and widget-choice sheets). Level 2: section headers (`ListSectionHeader` and with it `SectionCard` and `SettingsSection`, `FormSectionTitle`, `DetailSectionHeader`, `SectionHeading`, `EmptyState`, a board column's header, the server header and message card, the account header, the certificate page's headings and others). The rich-text view takes the description's level, clamped to 1–6.
    - `SectionSemantics` (`section_semantics.dart`, `explicitChildNodes`) wraps a heading and its section in `SettingsSection`, a `SectionCard` with a title, Upcoming's days, the server message card, the task sheet's subtask, related-task, parent-task and attachment sections and the widgets page's "Add a widget" group. Without it, a group with a heading and a single tappable row merged into one node, and the heading became that row's button. Untitled cards stay merged as before: under the wrapper their plain texts would each become a stop of their own.
    - Two headings stay buttons, each with its reason in the sweep: the Account tab's account header (the signed-in account, one button that opens its settings) and the task sheet's title (it opens the edit page, as a tap on it does).
    - The search page's field is no heading (`excludeHeaderSemantics`) but still names the route on Android, with "Search" followed by its hint.
    - The team page's card shows the team's name as plain text again, so the page has one heading with that name.
  - **The announcer** (`features/common/presentation/announce.dart`):
    - `announce(context, text)`, or `Announcer.of(context)` taken before an `await` and its `say(text)` after it (account settings, the avatar crop, the share-link password, the client certificate). Where the platform announces (`MediaQuery.supportsAnnounceOf`, so on iOS), `say` calls `SemanticsService.sendAnnouncement`. Elsewhere, on Android always, the text goes to `AnnouncerHost`. Without a host, in a widget test of a page on its own, it calls `sendAnnouncement`; it never throws.
    - `AnnouncerHost` is mounted once, as the direct child of `AppLockGate` in `MaterialApp.router`'s `builder`, around `NotificationTapListener` and the routes: a `Stack` of the app and a 1×1 node (`liveRegion`, a container, focus blocked, no actions) painted after the routes, so neither a sheet nor a dialog hides it, while the lock and the privacy cover do. A repeated message is cleared for one frame and set again, so that it is spoken again; the region empties 5 s after the last message, so a node built again after an unlock or a theme change repeats nothing.
    - The 15 direct calls of `sendAnnouncement` are gone; 25 files announce through the announcer.
    - **Errors already shown in a live region are not announced again.** A new or changed error speaks itself, as a field's `errorText` where the platform cannot announce and as a `FormErrorText` on every platform; `say(text, onlyIfPlatformAnnounces: true)` then leaves the app's live region out, and the announcer speaks only an error whose text stays the same, such as the same input submitted again. This applies to the project identifier, the account name (the one account setting shown as `errorText`), the share-link password, the client certificate page and the access headers. Errors that are no live region, such as a filter's server error in the saved-filter and view forms and the other account settings, keep the announcer.
  - **High contrast** (`app/theme/app_theme.dart`): `brandScheme` takes a `contrastLevel` for each of its seeded parts, so the blend keeps its amber tertiary and neutral tones. `AppTheme.highContrastLight` and `highContrastDark` go into `MaterialApp.router` as `highContrastTheme` and `highContrastDarkTheme`, so they apply whenever the platform reports `highContrast`: iOS Increase Contrast, and Android 14+'s Contrast at medium or high. With brand colours they are the brand scheme at contrast level 1, `HighlightColors` kept; with system colours (`appColorSchemesProvider`) a scheme seeded from the system scheme's primary at contrast level 1, which keeps the user's hue.
  - **Reduce Motion** (`features/common/presentation/motion.dart`): `reduceMotionOf(context)` is true with Android's Remove animations (`disableAnimations`) or iOS Reduce Motion (the platform's `accessibilityFeatures.reduceMotion`), read at call time. The app then jumps instead of animating at its eight own sites: the board settling on a column and following a move, Gantt's "Go to today", the Android task sheet growing to full height (`jumpTo`, because the controller refuses a zero duration), the scroll to a comment, the filter suggestions, the connect screen's error card and the server overview's scroll to its error.
  - **Smaller fixes:**
    - **Overdue:** `DueBadge` (rows, cards, Upcoming) and the task sheet's due row show `Icons.error_outline` instead of the calendar for an overdue date, the icon the home-screen widgets use, in the same error colour, and the sheet's due row says "Overdue, due …" (`taskOverdueSemantics`).
    - **Gantt** (`TimelineSemantics`): a row's label ends with "blocks …", "blocked by …", "precedes …" or "follows …" for the arrows the chart draws (blocking and precedes, between rows that are both in the chart), at both ends, titles joined by commas. One text node over the painted header reads "Today, *date*; showing *range*"; it takes no touches and sits outside the horizontal scroll, where its rect would span the whole range.
    - **Refresh buttons** (`RefreshButton`, "Refresh") in the app bars of the session, API token and CalDAV lists, whose pull to refresh many screen-reader and switch users cannot reach.
    - **The iOS tab bar:** inactive labels in `onSurfaceVariant`.
    - **App-bar text actions** (`AppBarTextAction`): Save, Create and Upload at 8 places (task edit, project edit, saved-filter form, label form, description editor, avatar crop, API token form, widget setup) grow to 2.0× at most (`maxTextScale`), as iOS's own navigation-bar buttons stop growing at the accessibility sizes. At 3.12× the German "Speichern" overflowed the bar by 35 px at 375 pt and by 90 px at 320 pt.
    - **The iOS picker sheet** (`cupertino_picker_sheet.dart`): above 1.3× text the title gets a line of its own above Cancel and Done, which sit side by side when they fit and stack otherwise; title and buttons grow to 2.0× at most; the wheel never scales (cupertino_ui). Up to 1.3× it looks as before.
    - **The iOS day-range sheet** (`day_range_picker.dart`, from Upcoming's range button and a Gantt view's range controls): above 1.3× Cancel and Done stack when they do not fit and grow to 2.0× at most; the From/To control's text stops at 2.0× (the control keeps its native height), and each segment's two lines shrink to fit.
  - **Strings:** five new keys, the Gantt relations and summary, in en and de with "Screen reader:" descriptions; nothing else is new.
  - **The yardstick's deviations** (WCAG 2.2 AA; every check the sweep skips carries its reason in the test):
    - **1.4.4 Resize Text.** No global clamp. Below the 200 % that 1.4.4 asks for: Android's navigation bar at 1.15× (D56) and the iOS tab bar and the navigation rail at 1.3× (D45), each label shown by a long press (Flutter has no Large Content Viewer for tab bars); the offline banner at 1.3×; Upcoming's date column at 1.3× (out of the semantics, the rows carry the dates) and its day strip at 1.5×; the Gantt header at 1.5× ([D61](#d61-the-table-view-as-a-detailed-list-sorted-like-the-server-and-the-gantt-view-as-a-read-only-timeline-with-the-webs-filter-range-rows-bars-and-arrows-both-from-the-local-database)); avatar initials, reaction emoji and inline pills unscaled, while the text around them scales; Flutter's app bar title at 1.34×, for all 41 screen titles, ellipsized when long and spoken in full as the level-1 heading (not changed: opting out needs a toolbar that grows). At 2.0×, which meets 1.4.4 but not decision 4's 3.12×: the app-bar text actions and the iOS picker and day-range sheet headers above.
    - **2.5.7 Dragging Movements.** Moving a card to another column or within one, a task in a manually sorted list or a project among its siblings takes a drag, or a screen reader's custom actions (D47, D49); a pointer user has no single-pointer way. Single-pointer ways exist for a column's place (its menu's Move left and Move right), a project's parent (the parent picker on its edit page) and a task's project (the edit page). Follow-up for the owner: a visible "Move to…" item in the card, row and project menus.
    - **1.4.11 Non-text Contrast.** The light brand scheme's amber highlight (the star's fill, the high-priority flag) is about 1.82:1 on the surface. It is supplementary: the star has a `tertiary` outline at 6.18:1, and the flag always stands beside the priority's name. Every other scheme keeps it at 3:1 or more; the scheme test asserts it as the one named exception.
    - **1.4.3 Contrast (Minimum), iOS alerts.** `AdaptiveDialogAction` on iOS is a `CupertinoDialogAction` in Apple's system blue and red on the alert's translucent material: 2.86–4.40:1 measured, light worse than dark, as in every iOS alert. Under Increase Contrast the delete-task, sign-out and browser-hint alerts pass; the insecure-connection alert's "Continue anyway" over the light connect screen stays at 4.23:1. Alternative for the owner: the high-contrast colours always.
    - **Platform target sizes** (WCAG 2.5.8's 24 × 24 is met): the day cells of Flutter's Material date picker are 43.4 × 48 dp on a 360 dp phone, where Android asks for 48 dp (the due, start, end, reminder and Upcoming range pickers); Cupertino's sliding segmented control keeps its native 28 pt height, where Apple asks for 44 pt (the account settings' colour scheme, the iOS day-range sheet's From/To). Alternative for the owner: taller segments. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the colour scheme's sliding control is the Theme row of the Account tab now.*
    - **Beyond AA, 2.3.3 Animation from Interactions:** under iOS Reduce Motion the page and sheet transitions still slide, because the framework follows only `disableAnimations`, which iOS never sets; on both platforms the board's settle after a fling follows the finger.
    - **Framework headings without a level:** Flutter's `LicensePage` (About › Licences) marks its app bar titles as headers at level 0, so Headings navigation finds nothing there (1.3.1, 2.4.6). Changing that takes a licence page of our own.
    - **A new field error may be spoken twice on Android:** when a field also has a helper text (the share-link password, the project identifier), Material builds its error again when the error's fade-in ends, and Android takes the new live-region node's label for a change. The app's own announcement no longer adds to it; the manual pass records what TalkBack does.
    - **Checked at scheme level only:** colour-coded text inside merged row and card labels (due and overdue dates, priority, label pills), which Flutter's contrast guideline cannot see. A unit test checks onSurface, onSurfaceVariant, primary, tertiary and error on surface and on surfaceContainerHighest (at least 4.5:1), outline on surfaceContainer (3:1) and label pills over a grid of server colours (4.5:1), for the brand and the high-contrast schemes, light and dark. Dynamic schemes are not checked.
    - **Test-only allowances, not deviations:** `ScreenTitle`'s level without a header flag in the static rule; the favourite saved filter listed twice on the Projects tab ("Urgent Favourite": the same list under two headings, so one purpose, 3.2.4); two controls may share a name under different labelled ancestors or after different headings (technique H80); the contrast check skips a text that a scroll view cuts at that stop (matched by string, so the same string elsewhere at that stop too); the iOS date-time wheel is swept on a 540 pt wide surface, because the square test font needs 524 px for the German date column (with Roboto it fits at 320 pt); the two tap-target exceptions above hold only while a target stays at least 24 × 24.
    - **Added 2026-10-28 by step 36b ([D69](#d69-large-screens-one-window-layout-helper-readable-widths-of-840-dp-for-lists-and-640-dp-for-settings-and-forms-with-scrolling-margins-dialogs-at-most-560-dp-and-ios-sheets-at-most-640-dp-only-on-tablet-sized-windows)), read-only selectable text:** both tap-target guidelines skip a node that is read-only selectable text (a text field, read-only, whose only actions are a long press and focus), whatever its size, unlike the two exceptions above. The sweep at tablet sizes found it with the server overview's message of the day: one line high on a tablet, it is lower than the platform targets and than 24 × 24. WCAG 2.5.8's inline exception: the target's size is set by the line height of the text, and selecting is a text function, not a control. The other `SelectableText`s (the CalDAV, session and certificate details, a one-time secret, a share link) pass today and fall under the same skip. No UI change; alternative for the owner: a minimum height of 48 dp on Android and 44 pt on iOS, which makes a one-line message taller on phones.
  - **The guard: the route sweep** (`app/test/a11y/`):
    - **Scenarios** (`scenarios_shell.dart`, `scenarios_pages.dart`, `scenarios_account.dart`, `scenarios_onboarding.dart`, `scenarios_overlays.dart`) seed fakes with a fixed clock (`sweep_data.dart`) and reach their screen through the router or the app's show functions, never by tapping text. A route scenario must end on its GoRoute pattern, so a silent redirect fails, and every scenario must find its screen exactly once.
    - **The registry** `sweep_suites.dart` maps each `<name>_sweep_test.dart` (shell, pages, account, onboarding, overlays) to its scenarios. Only scenarios in it count, and the completeness test checks that every entry has its file, that every file sweeps its own entry and that there is no other sweep file.
    - **Routes** (`route_walk.dart`, through `router.configuration.locationForRoute`): every GoRoute with a builder needs a scenario. There are 44 GoRoutes today; the redirect-only `/home` is skipped, and `/splash`, an empty Scaffold, is exempt with its reason. Routes that build several screens need each variant: `/home/projects/:id` as list, Kanban, table, Gantt, favourites and saved filter, and `/connection` for https and http and as the headers, certificate and client-certificate editors. A stale scenario, exemption or variant fails too, and a unit test shows that a route without a scenario is named.
    - **Pushed pages and overlays:** every call in `app/lib` of `showAdaptiveDialog`, `showDialog`, `showModalBottomSheet`, `showCupertinoSheet`, `showCupertinoModalPopup`, `showDatePicker`, `showTimePicker`, `showMenu`, `MenuAnchor`, `PopupMenuButton`, `MaterialPageRoute`, `CupertinoPageRoute` or `PageRouteBuilder` (`overlay_sites.dart`, keyed by file, enclosing declaration and call) needs an entry in `overlay_inventory.dart`: a scenario of the overlay registry, or an exemption with a written reason; a call without one and an entry without its call fail. There are 63 calls today: 22 swept and 41 exempt, most of them popup menus of text items or alerts built like the delete-task dialog and opened in the smoke tests. The 18 overlay scenarios must equal the registry: the task sheet, the new-task sheet, the account switcher, the date-time, time and day-range pickers, the project, label, assignee and default-project pickers, the option sheet, the delete-task and sign-out dialogs, the quick-add menu, the Done snackbar, the one-time secret page, the avatar crop page and the image viewer. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): "the quick-add menu" is replaced by "the new-task sheet, more options"; 63 calls, 21 swept and 42 exempt.*
    - **States:** empty, the error card, the offline banner and one loading frame on the shell tabs, a project list and the session list; the lock screen in four states and the privacy cover; the connect screen's failure, the insecure-connection question, the browser hint and a failed sign-in; a guest's project list and task sheet.
    - **Matrix:** each scenario on its platforms (Android and iOS; the widget setup page on Android only) × light and dark × en and de, one test each with a 30 s timeout, with the square test font. On Android the Android tap-target guideline, on iOS the iOS one; on both the labelled tap target, text contrast, heading level (every header has a level from 1 to 6, framework nodes included, no exemptions), heading not a control, image label, action labels (unique within a node) and distinct tap labels. They run on the first frame and after each page of scrolling of the main vertical list, up to 10 stops.
    - **3.12×** (`text_scale_312_test.dart`): the six core screens of decision 4 on iOS at 53/17, on 375 × 667 and 320 × 568, in en and de, every vertical list scrolled to its end; any exception fails.
    - **Runtime:** 828 sweep tests in about 40 s wall time on the development Mac, the five files in parallel (shell 38.5 s, pages 29.1 s, overlays 27.3 s, account 21.8 s, onboarding 17.7 s); 871 tests in `test/a11y` with the 3.12× check, the completeness checks, the guidelines' own tests and the static rules. CI runs them in its plain `flutter test`, with no switch.
    - **Static rules** (`source_rules_test.dart`, over `app/lib` and the `lib` of the packages and the plugin): every `header: true` names a `headingLevel` in the same call, and every `headingLevel` comes with `header: true` (only `screen_title.dart` exempt, with its reason); no `SemanticsService.sendAnnouncement` or `announce` outside `announce.dart`. The scanner skips strings and comments and names file and line.
    - The accessibility smoke tests of earlier steps stay.
  - **No new dependency.**
  - **The parked session hardening:** none of the six auth files of the package reserved as D59 changed; of its file list, the ARB and generated files, `account_tab.dart` (its title) and `account_settings_page_test.dart` changed, which will conflict when the package is applied.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **`Semantics(header: true, headingLevel: 1)` in `ScreenTitle`**, as the spec check had it: two header flags never merge, so every app bar kept an empty level-0 header above its title.
  - **`excludeHeaderSemantics` on every app bar**, with the header and the route's name in `ScreenTitle`: a second setting at each of the 41 app bars, and one forgotten leaves a level-0 header.
  - **The announcer's host in the shell**, as the spec had it: it leaves the semantics tree under the 31 routes on the root navigator, under every sheet and dialog and in the connect flow, so most announcements would go silent on Android.
  - **`announceForAccessibility` on the Android versions before 16:** the engine reports no announce support on any version.
  - **Announcing every error:** an error that a live region shows already would be spoken twice.
  - **`disableAnimations` alone for Reduce Motion:** iOS never sets it. **`animateTo` with a zero duration** for the sheet: the controller refuses it.
  - **High contrast from `ColorScheme.fromSeed(contrastLevel: 1)` over the brand seed:** it would replace the amber tertiary and the neutral tones. With system colours on, **the brand's high-contrast scheme** would override the user's colours, and **the system scheme unchanged** would leave high contrast doing nothing for most Android users.
  - **Cupertino's grey on the iOS tab bar** with a 1.4.3 deviation.
  - **A global text clamp**, or icons in place of Save at large text on single screens.
  - **Roboto in the sweep:** false contrast failures from anti-aliasing, and iOS asks for other font families; real fonts are left to the manual pass.
  - **A smaller default matrix behind an environment variable:** the full matrix takes about 40 s.
  - **A scenario per dialog, sheet and menu call, and every state on every list:** the inventory's exemptions with reasons and representative states cover them.
  - **Another overdue icon** (`event_busy`, `warning_amber`): `error_outline` is what the widgets show.
  - **"Today" in every Gantt row's label:** one summary node says it once.
  - **A visible "Move to…" item in 36a:** a UI addition, left to the owner (2.5.7 above).
  - **`SectionSemantics` around untitled cards:** their plain texts would each become a stop, such as the team summary card, read today as one item.
- **Consequences:**
  - TalkBack's and VoiceOver's Headings navigation steps from each screen's title through its sections, in the app's sheets too, and a heading no longer turns into the button of the row below it. Announcements reach TalkBack on every Android version through the app's live region, also over sheets and dialogs and on the connect screens, and never while the app is locked. The app follows Increase Contrast, Android's Contrast setting, Remove animations and Reduce Motion; overdue no longer depends on colour alone; Gantt relations are spoken; the session, token and CalDAV lists refresh without a pull. A new route, pushed page or overlay call without a scenario or a written exemption fails the tests ([CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md#merge-requests)).
  - Known gaps:
    - Whether TalkBack speaks the live region as reliably as the former announcements is unchecked: right after a dialog closes, while it is still speaking and for a repeated message; so are a board move and a list move with TalkBack, and whether a new field error is said twice.
    - The editor WebView's content is out of the sweep's reach; whether TalkBack enters it is for the manual pass.
    - Real fonts are checked only by probes and the manual pass; the sweep uses the test font.
    - Text the Gantt painters draw may not follow iOS Bold Text (unconfirmed).
    - The Reduce Motion paths of the connect screen, the server overview and the filter field have no tests of their own, and neither do the highlight colours of the high-contrast schemes from system colours.
    - The widget setup page's preview is now read line by line under its "Preview" heading instead of as one item; a `MergeSemantics` would make it one again.
    - Tables in descriptions carry no row and column positions.
  - Not built: the manual TalkBack, Switch Access and Accessibility Scanner pass and the VoiceOver pass ([accessibility checklist](accessibility-checklist.md)); a visible "Move to…" item; a licence page of our own; descriptions for the existing ARB keys (step 36f).
  - Open in the review besides decisions 1 to 8: the spec check's choices above (high contrast from system colours, the tab bar's colour, the 2.0× caps, the 2.5.7 deviation), taller segmented controls, alert colours always in high contrast, the widget preview, and Q19, who runs the manual pass and where.
  - Tests: `app/test/a11y/` above; `announce_test.dart` (both paths, a node a screen reader can neither focus nor tap, a repeated message spoken twice, the region empty after 5 s, an announcer taken before its page closed, no host, the field-error mode on both paths, the region left in the tree under an open dialog); the live-region path (`useLiveRegionAnnouncements`, `announcerRegion` and `SpokenCount` in `test/helpers/announcer_region.dart`) for a checklist tick with the task sheet open, ending other sessions, the board, list and tree moves, the project identifier, the account name and default project, the share-link password (within the error's fade and after it), the client certificate and the access headers, each error spoken once per carrier; the region hidden while locked (`app_lock_gate_test.dart`); `heading_levels_test.dart` (an app bar title as one node at level 1 on Android and iOS, the route named on Android, the search route's name); `section_widgets_test.dart` (headings apart from their rows); the rich-text levels; `app_bar_text_action_test.dart` (the German edit page at 3.12× on 375 and 320 pt), `cupertino_picker_sheet_test.dart` (9 cases), `day_range_picker_test.dart`; `motion_test.dart` and the board's settle, the sheet's growth, the scroll to a comment and "Go to today" under both flags; `theme_test.dart` (high contrast light and dark, switching while the app runs, system colours) and the scheme contrast in `app_theme_test.dart`; the tab bar's contrast in `home_shell_test.dart`; the overdue icon in `task_meta_test.dart`, `task_row_test.dart` and `task_detail_sheet_test.dart`; the relations at both ends and the summary in `project_timeline_test.dart`; the refresh buttons on the three pages. The tests of the review's fixes fail on the old code, except where it was right already (the iOS paths of the share-link password and the access headers). In total 5,966 app tests (213 contract, Docker and release-only tests skipped), 337 in `vikunja_domain`, 3,163 in `vikunja_logic`, 1,719 in `vikunja_api`, 20 in `rurana_widgets` and 33 in Kotlin pass. The arm64 release APK is 52.3 MB, and the exported-components check reports 11 components as allowed.
  - Checked on the Pixel_Tablet emulator (Android 16, a Google Play image with TalkBack 16 preinstalled): **the manual TalkBack and Switch Access pass could not be performed.** TalkBack ignored touch and key events injected over adb (its focus did not move, and its Alt+arrow shortcuts had no effect), and logging its node tree needs a four-finger gesture. Checked without TalkBack: overdue rows show the circled exclamation mark in the error colour, and the app switches to the high-contrast scheme with the system Contrast setting (stronger outlines, a darker error colour). The manual pass ([checklist](accessibility-checklist.md)) is pending: by the owner on the tablet emulator with the host keyboard, or in the final device phase together with VoiceOver. Found in that check: changing the system Contrast, and likewise the wallpaper while system colours are on, makes Android relaunch the activity (configuration change 0x80000000, the assets paths, which an app cannot opt out of), and the app comes back at the Overview with its navigation lost. This is older than 36a, from the `FlutterFragmentActivity` and engine setup of step 35a or earlier; it is tracked as a task of its own and not fixed here (fixed on 2026-10-28: the activity keeps its engine across such a relaunch, see D64's known gaps). Nothing was run on iOS.

## D69: Large screens: one window-layout helper, readable widths of 840 dp for lists and 640 dp for settings and forms with scrolling margins, dialogs at most 560 dp and iOS sheets at most 640 dp, only on tablet-sized windows

*Accepted 2026-10-28, step 36b (Android: one release build and a check on the Pixel_9_Pro_XL emulator with tablet display sizes; nothing was built for iOS, and the iPad check comes with the iOS device test at the end of the project). Decisions 1 to 6 below were taken without the owner and are open in the review, and so are the choices of the spec check and the review listed after them; the main session's decisions after the build are listed apart, and one of them amends decision 2. The second sub-step of step 36 ([roadmap](roadmap.md)). Partly carries out what [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner) left to step 36 ("iPad layouts are step 36"): lists, settings, forms, dialogs and sheets get readable widths on tablets, while Android keeps the rail from 600 dp and iOS the bottom tab bar at every width; the task detail pane follows in step 36c and the projects list–detail in 36g. Keeps the cards, rows and margins of [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list), which are centred as they are, and the Table view of [D61](#d61-the-table-view-as-a-detailed-list-sorted-like-the-server-and-the-gantt-view-as-a-read-only-timeline-with-the-webs-filter-range-rows-bars-and-arrows-both-from-the-local-database) as a list of task rows, now as wide as the List view. Corrects the board widths that [D47](#d47-kanban-board-from-per-view-board-snapshots-moves-and-positions-through-the-outbox-with-the-done-buckets-rules-accessible-drag-and-drop) and the architecture page gave, which the design refresh of D56 had changed. Adds a deviation to [D68](#d68-screen-readers-heading-levels-on-screen-titles-and-section-headers-one-announcer-with-the-apps-own-live-region-on-android-high-contrast-and-reduce-motion-followed-and-a-route-sweep-that-every-route-pushed-page-and-overlay-must-pass-wcag-22-aa-as-the-yardstick-with-written-deviations), with a dated line there, and runs its sweep at tablet sizes too. Keeps [D2](#d2-material_ui-and-cupertino_ui): every change uses material_ui and cupertino_ui.*

- **Context:**
  - The research for step 36 (2026-10-28) found every screen but the connect, first-sync, notification-setup and lock screens stretched across a tablet's window: at 1280 × 800 beside the rail a task row was about 1,170 dp wide, a Material dialog with a long text grew towards the window's width less 80 dp, and on an iPad every Cupertino sheet spanned the window, the task sheet's "Mark as done" button included. Only Material bottom sheets had a cap, M3's 640 dp, and only those four screens had widths of their own: the connect screen 480 dp, the other three 420 dp. A few widgets read the window's width where they meant their own: `ListSectionHeader`'s trailing text and the Gantt header and rows before their first scroll. The owner put 36a–36g on the roadmap that day, accepted 36a with its manual pass still open and asked for 36b next.
  - A spec with decisions 1 to 6, a check of the spec by two critics (26 findings: 17 accepted, 8 accepted with changes, 1 left to the main session), three areas built in parallel (the helpers, overlays, shared widgets and tests; the 840 dp screens and the project views; the 640 dp screens), the main session's decisions after the build and a review shaped the result.
  - Verified facts (research and spec check of 2026-10-28, from material_ui 1.6.0, cupertino_ui 1.1.2, flutter_test and probes):
    - **Window sizes:** every large phone in landscape is at least 840 dp wide (the Pixel 9 Pro AVD at 952 × 427, an iPhone Pro Max at about 956 × 440), so a rule by width alone takes it for a tablet. The Pixel Tablet is 800 × 1280 and 1280 × 800, an 11" iPad 834 × 1194 and 1194 × 834, a 13" iPad 1024 × 1366 in portrait, an iPad mini 744 × 1133. flutter_test's default surface is 800 × 600, and 32 app test files run at it.
    - **Dialogs:** Material 3's `Dialog` is at least 280 dp wide with no maximum; it takes `dialogTheme.constraints` when the call passes none, and so do the date and time pickers, whose landscape and large-text layouts are wider than 560 dp (the time picker 572 dp in landscape, the date picker 992 dp at 200 % text).
    - **Sheets:** M3's bottom sheet is at most 640 dp wide by default, and no call or theme of the app changes that. `showCupertinoSheet` sets no width; an `Align` or `Center` around its content loosens the constraints and the sheet shrinks to its content (a probe showed a 71 × 200 card); the route's drag listener is translucent across the window. `showCupertinoModalPopup` places its child at the bottom with loose constraints.
    - **Scroll views:** a vertical `ListView` without a padding applies the media query's top and bottom padding itself, and an explicit padding drops it. A theme width for snackbars would change phones too.
    - **Tests:** with the square test font the Android rail is 128 dp wide at 1280 × 800, not 80, so the tests measure it instead of assuming it.
- **Decision:**
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-10-28), open in the review:**
    1. **One window-layout helper** for Material 3's width classes (compact below 600 dp, medium 600–839, expanded from 840) and for two panes (at least 840 dp wide and 480 dp high, for 36c), from the window's size; the navigation rail stays at 600 dp (D45). Widgets that size themselves read their own constraints, not the window.
    2. **Readable widths:** 840 dp for lists, the Overview, Upcoming, search, the inbox and the project tree; 640 dp for settings, forms, the Account tab, detail pages and the server overview. The content is centred, and the scroll view keeps the whole width, so the margins scroll. The board, the Table view and Gantt use the whole width (the Table view since amended, below).
    3. **Dialogs at most 560 dp** through the dialog theme; **Material bottom sheets** keep M3's 640 dp; **iOS sheets show their content at most 640 dp wide, centred** (the task detail, the pickers, the option sheet, the account switcher, the project and account pickers, the share and open-link sheets). Should a centred iOS sheet look wrong at the iPad check, iOS gets Material-style sheets from medium width; recorded, not built.
    4. **The iPad keeps the bottom `CupertinoTabBar`**; no sidebar.
    5. **No golden images:** geometry checks in widget tests and screenshots on the emulator, as CI renders on Ubuntu and development runs on macOS.
    6. **Phones unchanged:** on a phone in either orientation and on the 800 × 600 test surface nothing moves, and the 32 test files that run at that surface pass without a change.
  - **Taken by the main session after the build (2026-10-28), also without the owner and open in the review, except the last:**
    - **The Table view at 840 dp,** like the List view; this amends decision 2, and the board and Gantt keep the whole width. A table is a list of the same task rows with no real columns ([D61](#d61-the-table-view-as-a-detailed-list-sorted-like-the-server-and-the-gantt-view-as-a-read-only-timeline-with-the-webs-filter-range-rows-bars-and-arrows-both-from-the-local-database)); at the whole width its rows were about 1,150 dp wide, and the width jumped between the List and Table tabs.
    - **The gate below, with its cost:** windows 600–839 dp wide and 480–899 dp high, such as an Android split screen in portrait or an iPad's 2/3 split, get no caps.
    - **The server's message of the day keeps its look;** the sweep skips read-only selectable text in both tap-target guidelines, a deviation added to D68 with a dated line.
    - **The two iOS wheel popups** (the date-time picker and the day-range sheet) are capped at 640 dp like the sheets; the iPad check may reverse it.
    - **Widgets that size themselves measure their own width,** as decision 1 says: `ListSectionHeader`'s trailing text takes at most 40 % of the header's width, which beside the Android rail (at 740 × 360, 800 × 600 and 952 × 427) is 32 dp less than 40 % of the window.
    - **Snackbars stay as they are on tablets,** across the content beside the rail; a width of their own is left for later and is not put to the owner now.
  - **Also taken without the owner, by the spec check and the review, open in the review:**
    - **The gate:** every cap applies only when the window is not compact in height and is expanded in width or in height (`capsWidths`: at least 480 dp high, and at least 840 dp wide or at least 900 dp high). This replaces the spec's "on iOS from medium width" for the sheets and keeps decision 6 as written: phones in landscape are less than 480 dp high, and 800 × 600 is neither wide nor high enough.
    - **The inset goes outside each screen's own layout:** the 840 or 640 dp include the cards' 16 dp margins, so a card is 808 or 608 dp wide, and dividers and tiles inside keep their places. The inset adds to a screen's safe-area padding; no window with side insets passes the gate.
    - **The wrappers stay in the tree, with no inset while the gate is off,** so a rotation across the gate keeps every screen's state.
    - **A project's tabs and progress line sit in the 840 dp band for every view,** so they do not move when the view changes, while the tabs' divider runs across the whole app bar (review); the controls under the tabs of the List, Table and Gantt views sit in the band in every state, loading and failure included (review).
    - **The Overview's header** scrolls with the list and follows the band; the other tabs' app bar titles stay at the window's edge.
    - **An iOS sheet becomes a card only with at least 48 dp beside it** (review): below that it spans the window as on a phone, because slivers of the page at its sides would look like a misplaced sheet. Half a 13" iPad (683 × 1024, 21.5 dp beside a card) gets the whole width, an iPad mini in portrait (744 × 1133) a card with 52 dp margins.
    - **The date and time pickers keep their own width** under the dialog cap: `pickerFormatsBuilder` gives them Flutter's own dialog bounds back, and a source scan makes every picker call use it; `ReadableWidth.dialog` and `defaultDialogConstraints` are shared constants (review).
    - **What keeps the whole width:** app bars with their sync and saving progress bars, the shell's offline banner, snackbars, the floating action buttons at the window's bottom right, the board with its notes, the Gantt timeline, the description and comment editors, the image viewer and Flutter's licence page.
  - **The helper** (`app/lib/app/layout/window_layout.dart`): `WindowLayout.of(context)`, from `MediaQuery.sizeOf`, with `sizeClass` (`WindowSizeClass.compact`, `medium`, `expanded`), `compactHeight` (below 480), `expandedHeight` (from 900), `twoPane` (from 840 wide and not compact in height) and `capsWidths`; every window threshold of the app is a constant there. `usesNavigationRail` reads the medium width, `ProjectAppBarBottom.showsProgressIn` the compact height, and the server overview pins its actions from the medium height, all as before.
  - **Readable widths** (`app/lib/app/layout/readable_width.dart`):
    - `ReadableWidth.list` (840), `form` (640), `sheet` (640), `dialog` (560) and `defaultDialogConstraints` (at least 280, Flutter's own).
    - The inset on each side is max(0, (available − max) / 2) while `capsWidths`, else 0. `available` is the scroll view's own width: beside the Android rail the window less the rail, on pages over the shell and on iOS the whole window. The gate comes from the window, the amount from the constraints.
    - `ReadableWidth` pads a box; `ReadableWidth.builder` hands the content width and the inset to a `ListView` or `SingleChildScrollView`, which takes the inset into its padding and adds the media query's top and bottom padding back; `SliverReadableWidth` (`.group` around a `SliverMainAxisGroup`) pads the slivers of a `CustomScrollView`, with `NoticeSliver`, `SliverFillRemaining` and `SliverSafeArea` working inside. No scroll view sits in a `ConstrainedBox`, `Align` or `Center`, so the margins scroll, and the scrollbar and the refresh and overscroll indicators stay at the window's edge.
    - `CenteredScrollView` and `RefreshableList.buildRefreshable` take a `maxWidth`, so the loading, empty and failure states and their notices share the band.
    - The insets (list / form): at 1280 × 800 on Android 180 / 280 dp on a tab beside an 80 dp rail and 220 / 320 dp on a page over the shell; at 800 × 1280 0 / 40 and 0 / 80; on an 11" iPad 177 / 277 in landscape and 0 / 97 in portrait; on a 13" iPad in portrait 92 / 192.
  - **The screens** (`app/test/a11y/readable_widths.dart` gives every route scenario its class, and the sweep checks it):
    - **840 dp, 9 screens:** the Overview, Upcoming, the project tree, a project's List view with its loading, empty, failure and "project gone" states, the Table view, favourites and saved filters, a label's tasks, search and the inbox; and for every view of a project its tabs, its progress line and the controls under them.
    - **640 dp, 28 screens:** the Account tab, for guests too; account settings, the avatar and its crop, sessions, API tokens, CalDAV and the data export (`SecurityListBody`), the API token form and its one-time secret; the connection pages (the address's settings, access headers, certificate, client certificate); privacy; About (not the licence page); the widgets page and widget setup; labels, the label form, teams and a team; project edit, views and share; the saved-filter form; task edit; the server overview, whose pinned actions follow the band while the bar's background keeps the whole width.
    - **Their own caps, unchanged:** the connect screen (480 dp), first sync, the notification setup and the lock screen (420 dp).
    - **The whole width:** as listed above.
  - **Dialogs and sheets:**
    - **Dialogs:** a `Theme` around `AppLockGate` in `MaterialApp.router`'s `builder` (`vikunja_app.dart`) sets `dialogTheme.constraints` to 280–560 dp while `capsWidths`, for every Material dialog of the app (13 `showAdaptiveDialog` sites and every `AlertDialog`, `SimpleDialog` and `Dialog`). The static `AppTheme` is unchanged, and with the gate off dialogs keep their old bounds, at least 280 dp and at most the window less 80. `pickerFormatsBuilder` (`app/lib/app/l10n/picker_localizations.dart`) now always returns a builder, which also sets the dialog bounds back to `defaultDialogConstraints`, so the date and time pickers keep their sizes. iOS alerts keep Cupertino's fixed width.
    - **Material bottom sheets** (11 `showModalBottomSheet` sites) keep M3's 640 dp at every size.
    - **iOS sheets:** `showReadableCupertinoSheet` (`app/lib/app/layout/readable_sheet.dart`) is the app's one call of `showCupertinoSheet` and wraps the content of its 8 callers in `ReadableSheetWidth`: padded to at most 640 dp, centred and clipped to a card with 12 dp top corners while the margin beside it is at least 48 dp (`minMargin`), else the whole width without a clip, as on a phone. The card takes the height it is given, so a drag down closes the sheet, from the margin too, and its list scrolls as before. The two wheel popups (`cupertino_picker_sheet.dart`, `day_range_picker.dart`) keep `showCupertinoModalPopup` and wrap their content in the same widget. Full-width buttons such as "Mark as done" follow the card.
    - **The overlay registry** of D68 lists the presenter's own call as an exemption ("the shared iOS sheet presenter; its callers are listed") and the 8 callers under its name: 64 calls, 22 swept and 42 exempt. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): 63 calls, 21 swept and 42 exempt: the toolbar's priority menu is gone.*
  - **Widgets that measure themselves:** `ListSectionHeader` takes its trailing cap from its own width (unchanged on portrait phones, except beside Upcoming's date column, where it shows only a count); `TimelineChart` hands its own width to `TimelineHeader` and `TimelineRow`, which used the window's before the first scroll; the project list and the tree measure the content width for the identifier column and for a row lifted to be dragged. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the identifier column and its measuring are gone; the lifted rows still measure.*
  - **The sweep on tablets** (`app/test/a11y/`): every scenario also runs on its platform's tablet, Android at 1280 × 800 and 800 × 1280 and iOS at 1194 × 834 and 834 × 1194, in dark mode, in English and German: once with the guidelines and the width checks, once at 200 % text, where the screen only has to show without an exception at every scroll stop.
    - For an 840 or 640 dp route the main scroll view spans the available width, and every `SectionCard`, `TaskRow`, `ListTile` or `TextField` under it lies in the band (the data export and the certificate review check their status cards and fingerprints instead, with the reason); a screen with its own cap stays within it; the board, the timeline and the editor take the whole width.
    - A dialog's surface is at most 560 dp (the pickers excepted), a Material sheet's at most 640 dp, an iOS sheet's content at most 640 dp and centred.
    - A completeness test fails for a route scenario without a class and for a class without a scenario. The server overview's sign-in scenario now scrolls the list until the button is built.
  - **No new strings and no new dependency.**
  - **The parked session hardening:** none of the six auth files of the package reserved as D59 changed; of its file list, `account_tab.dart` (its readable width) and the docs changed, which will conflict when the package is applied.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Caps by width alone,** as the spec first read: every landscape phone is at least 840 dp wide and would have got margins and centred sheets.
  - **A gate on height alone** (not compact in height): it also covers medium windows such as an iPad's 2/3 split, but moves the 800 × 600 test surface (40 dp margins for forms on Android, 80 on iOS, and narrower iOS sheets), against decision 6.
  - **A `ConstrainedBox`, `Align` or `Center` around a scroll view:** the margins would not scroll, and the scrollbar and the refresh indicator would sit at the band's edge; around an iOS sheet's content an `Align` shrinks the sheet.
  - **The dialog cap in the static `AppTheme`:** it would narrow dialogs on landscape phones (872 dp at 952 × 427). **The cap only in the app's own dialog helpers:** Flutter's dialogs and every other `AlertDialog` would stay wide, and decision 3 names the theme.
  - **The date and time pickers under the cap:** their landscape and large-text layouts need more than 560 dp.
  - **A minimum height for the message of the day,** 48 dp on Android and 44 pt on iOS: it would fix the target for real users too, but makes a one-line message taller on phones (decision 6).
  - **A snackbar width in the theme:** it would change phones; a width set per window in the shell is left for later.
  - **The Table view at the whole width,** as decision 2 first had it (above).
  - **The window's width for `ListSectionHeader`** until 36c's panes need their own.
  - **An iOS card with narrow margins,** such as 21.5 dp on half a 13" iPad.
  - **The 200 % pass in German only:** the tablet axis costs about 64 s, within the 90 s aimed for.
- **Consequences:**
  - On a tablet, lists and the project tree stay at most 840 dp wide and settings, forms and detail pages at most 640 dp, centred, and a drag or a pull in the margin scrolls or refreshes the list; dialogs are at most 560 dp wide, and on an iPad the task sheet, the pickers and the other sheets are centred cards of 640 pt. The board and Gantt use the whole width. Phones in either orientation and the 800 × 600 test surface look as before, except that beside the Android rail a section header's trailing text may wrap up to 32 dp earlier (above). A rotation keeps every screen's state.
  - Known gaps:
    - Windows 600–839 dp wide and 480–899 dp high get no caps: an Android split screen in portrait, the Pixel Tablet's 50/50 split in landscape (about 640 × 800), an iPad's 2/3 split (about 790 × 834). Their forms reach about 760 dp beside the Android rail and 839 pt on iOS, and their iOS sheets fill the window.
    - Snackbars span the content beside the rail, about 1,170 dp at 1280 × 800.
    - Pages over the shell, such as settings, forms, search and the inbox, still cover the rail or the tab bar, now with their content centred in the window; the task detail stays a sheet until 36c.
    - The floating action buttons stay at the window's bottom right, beside the band.
    - The Overview's header follows the band, the other tabs' app bar titles do not.
    - The description and comment editors have no width of their own, and images in a description still decode at the window's width.
    - Nothing was looked at on an iPad: the cards, the wheel popups as cards and decision 3's fallback are unchecked there. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): on the iPad Pro 13-inch simulator (iOS 26.5) in both orientations the sheets showed as centred cards of 640 pt and stay, and decision 3's fallback is not needed; a tap beside a card did nothing there and closes the sheet since 37b (widget tests). The wheel popups as cards were not looked at; an iPad mini and 11", Split View and Slide Over are still unchecked.*
  - Not built: the task detail pane (36c) and the projects list–detail (36g); a sidebar or a top tab bar on iPad; popovers for the iOS wheels; a snackbar width; a width for the editor page; layouts that follow a hinge.
  - Open in the review besides decisions 1 to 6: the main session's and the spec check's choices above, among them the gate and its cost, the Table view at 840 dp, the 48 dp rule for cards and what keeps the whole width; the wheel popups and decision 3's fallback, which the iPad check decides: at 744 × 1133 (an iPad mini in portrait, a card with 52 dp margins), at 683 × 1024 (half a 13" iPad, a sheet across the window), and at 834 × 1194, 1194 × 834 and 1024 × 1366.
  - Tests: `test/app/layout/window_layout_test.dart` (5: the size classes, the gate, the insets beside the Android rail and without, the iOS card at an iPad's centre, no inset without a bounded width) and `readable_width_test.dart` (9: the box, the builder, the slivers with a notice, `SliverFillRemaining` and `SliverSafeArea` inside, a child's state kept across a resize, nothing moving at 952 × 427 and 800 × 600, a state's notice and content in one band); `large_screen_guard_test.dart` (7: at Android 360 × 740, 740 × 360, 952 × 427 and 800 × 600 and iOS 375 × 667, 844 × 390 with the notch and 800 × 600, the rects of an Overview card and row, an Account tab tile, a task edit field, the task sheets and a long dialog, recorded on an export of step 36a's commit (2d70ef1), where the file passed, and unchanged since); `large_screen_lists_test.dart` (45: the Overview at the 11 sizes of the matrix, Android 360 × 740, 740 × 360, 952 × 427, 640 × 800, 800 × 1280 and 1280 × 800 and iOS 375 × 667, 844 × 390, 834 × 1194, 1194 × 834 and 1024 × 1366; every list screen on a phone and a tablet; a drag and a pull in the margin; the Overview's header from loading to data; a lifted list row and a lifted project as wide as their rows; the tabs, the progress line and the controls in one place for every view, and the divider across the app bar; the Table's rows as wide as the List's; the board and the timeline at the whole width; the timeline's width from its own box); `large_screen_forms_test.dart` (26: the Account tab, account settings, sessions, task edit and the server overview with its pinned actions, on tablets and phones; a drag in the Account tab's margin); `large_screen_overlays_test.dart` (28: dialogs of 280–560 dp on a tablet and as before at 952 × 427 and the other Android sizes; the time picker at 572 dp and the date picker at 992 dp at 200 % text, in English and German and for every date picker of the app; the Material task sheet at 640 dp; the iOS task sheet a centred card with Mark as done inside at 1194 × 834, 834 × 1194 and 1024 × 1366, the whole width and unclipped at 375 × 667, 844 × 390, 800 × 600 and 683 × 1024, a card at 744 × 1133; a drag on the card and in the margin closes it, and its list still scrolls; the wheel popup's Cancel and Done in its card; source scans: no theme or call changes the Material sheets' 640 dp, every date and time picker uses `pickerFormatsBuilder`, every iOS sheet goes through the presenter and every wheel popup through `ReadableSheetWidth`); `list_section_header_test.dart` (2: 144 dp at 360 dp, 336 dp in the band at 1280 × 800); a crop test in `avatar_page_test.dart`; the sweep's tablet axis and its completeness test. In total 7,757 app tests (213 contract, Docker and release-only tests skipped) pass in about 4 minutes, 2,528 of them in `test/a11y` in about 1.5 minutes in the last run (86 s; measured back to back, the 871 tests of 2d70ef1's export took 48 s and the 2,528 of this step 112 s, so the tablet axis adds about 64 s); 337 in `vikunja_domain`, 3,163 in `vikunja_logic` (35 skipped), 1,719 in `vikunja_api`, 20 in `rurana_widgets` and 37 in Kotlin pass; analyze and format are clean. After the Table view's change the large-screen, sweep and project tests (2,974) passed again.
  - Checked on 2026-10-28 on the Pixel_9_Pro_XL emulator with display overrides for 800 × 1280 dp (portrait), 1280 × 800 dp (landscape) and 636 × 800 dp (one half of a 1280 × 800 split; real split screen was not exercised). In landscape the Overview and a project's List and Table views were 840 dp wide (cards of 808 dp inside their 16 dp margins) and centred beside the rail, Privacy 640 dp, a dialog 560 dp in the middle and the account switcher sheet 640 dp. In portrait the lists filled the 720 dp beside the rail, which is less than 840, the Account tab was 640 dp wide and the dialog 560 dp. At 636 × 800 nothing was capped, as designed. Nothing was clipped, and the app did not crash. The board and Gantt could not be opened, because the emulator's guest account had lost its access; the widget tests cover them. The Pixel_Tablet emulator was not used, as it needs the owner's PIN. Nothing was run on iOS.

## D70: Task detail pane: on windows at least 840 dp wide and 480 dp high a task opens beside the list, one pane per tab with a back stack and a selected row, the sheet everywhere else; back through the pane first, and pages in a tab without Android's predictive back

*Accepted 2026-10-28, step 36c (Android: one release build and a check on the Pixel_9_Pro_XL emulator with tablet display sizes; nothing was built for iOS, and the iPad check comes with the iOS device test at the end of the project; no foldable device, no gesture navigation and no TalkBack were used). The owner chose the pane before the step; how it works, decisions 1 to 12 below, was decided without the owner and is open in the review, and so are the choices of the spec check and the review listed after them; the main session's decisions are listed apart, and one of them deviates from the roadmap row. The third sub-step of step 36 ([roadmap](roadmap.md)). Amends [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list): its "the task detail as a full page (it stays a sheet)" now holds below two-pane windows, while on them the detail is a pane beside the list, still not a page. Carries out for the task detail what [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner) left to step 36 ("iPad layouts are step 36") and [D69](#d69-large-screens-one-window-layout-helper-readable-widths-of-840-dp-for-lists-and-640-dp-for-settings-and-forms-with-scrolling-margins-dialogs-at-most-560-dp-and-ios-sheets-at-most-640-dp-only-on-tablet-sized-windows) to 36c, with the split on a hinge that D69 did not build; the projects list–detail stays with 36g. Keeps the detail of [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme) as it is, in a sheet or in the pane, and its opener, whose links, widget taps and notification taps now open in the Overview's pane where it fits. Keeps [D64](#d64-an-app-lock-over-the-ui-not-encryption-with-biometrics-or-the-devices-screen-lock-device-privacy-settings-for-the-app-switcher-screenshots-notification-content-and-widget-titles): the lock is a gate over the UI and closes no pane. Keeps the switch transition of [D37](#d37-account-switcher-a-sheet-from-an-avatar-button-and-an-accounts-section-a-switch-resets-the-navigation-the-most-recently-active-account-follows-a-removal-inactive-outboxes-drain-in-the-foreground), which now also closes every pane, and the comment drafts of [D57](#d57-comments-written-in-the-app-online-with-a-draft-kept-on-the-device-reactions-on-tasks-and-comments-comment-counts-and-the-unread-dot-on-rows), which carry a draft from the pane into the sheet. Keeps D69's `WindowLayout.twoPane`, its readable widths for the list beside the pane and its snackbars across the content. Marks the selected task on the board cards of [D47](#d47-kanban-board-from-per-view-board-snapshots-moves-and-positions-through-the-outbox-with-the-done-buckets-rules-accessible-drag-and-drop) and the timeline rows of [D61](#d61-the-table-view-as-a-detailed-list-sorted-like-the-server-and-the-gantt-view-as-a-read-only-timeline-with-the-webs-filter-range-rows-bars-and-arrows-both-from-the-local-database). Runs the sweep of [D68](#d68-screen-readers-heading-levels-on-screen-titles-and-section-headers-one-announcer-with-the-apps-own-live-region-on-android-high-contrast-and-reduce-motion-followed-and-a-route-sweep-that-every-route-pushed-page-and-overlay-must-pass-wcag-22-aa-as-the-yardstick-with-written-deviations) with the pane open. Keeps [D2](#d2-material_ui-and-cupertino_ui): every change uses material_ui and cupertino_ui.*

- **Context:**
  - When the owner split step 36 on 2026-10-28, they decided that the task detail becomes a pane beside the list on wide windows ("a column beside the list"), which amends D56. They accepted 36b the same day and asked for 36c. Until then the detail was a sheet at every size: a Material bottom sheet of at most 640 dp on Android and, since 36b, a centred card of 640 pt on an iPad, over a list that 36b had centred at 840 dp.
  - A spec with 13 decisions, a check of the spec by two critics (23 findings: 10 accepted, 13 accepted with changes, none rejected; 9 choices left to the main session), the core built first, then the selection and the tests with the sweep in parallel, the main session's decisions and a review (one medium and six low findings, all fixed with tests) shaped the result.
  - Verified facts (spec check of 2026-10-28, from the local Flutter 3.47.7, go_router 18.0.2, material_ui 1.6.0 and probes; CI pins Flutter 3.47.5):
    - **Predictive back in the tabs:** the app sets no page transitions theme, so Android uses `PredictiveBackPageTransitionsBuilder`. Its gesture detector takes a back swipe for any route that is current in its navigator, without asking whether that navigator is offstage or covered. With gesture navigation on Android 14 and later this gave three bugs at every size, phones included: with the task sheet over a project page, a swipe popped the project page and left the sheet; with search over a project page, one swipe closed both; on the Overview, a swipe popped a page of the Projects tab that was not shown, and nothing visible happened. A `PopScope` or a `BackButtonListener` in the shell never saw these swipes. Without the detector, a committed swipe goes the way of the back button, which asks what is in front first.
    - **The router:** go_router asks the root navigator and every current branch navigator, deepest first. `StatefulShellRoute.indexedStack` reports the branch navigators to the root observers, so `RootModalRoutes` already sees a tab's popup menu. The shell route's own `matchedLocation` stays as it is while a page is pushed over the shell and when it pops, and changes with a `go` to another location in the branch.
    - **Window sizes:** `WindowLayout.twoPane` is at least 840 dp wide and 480 dp high, from `MediaQuery.sizeOf`, which the keyboard does not change. An Android tablet in landscape (1280 × 800), an 11" iPad in landscape (1194 × 834), a 13" iPad in both orientations (1366 × 1024 and 1024 × 1366) and an unfolded foldable (about 841 × 701) are two-pane. An Android tablet in portrait (800 × 1280), an 11" iPad in portrait (834 × 1194), an iPad mini (744 × 1133), Split View and Slide Over, every phone in either orientation and the 800 × 600 test surface are not.
    - **Contrast** (a probe against `AppTheme`): `secondaryContainer` as a row tint fails in both high-contrast schemes (onSurface 2.34:1 and 1.77:1, primary and error 1.56:1). `surfaceContainerHighest` keeps every text colour of a row at 4.99:1 or more in all four schemes. A tint alone stands out from the cards by only 1.17:1 (light) and 1.39:1 (dark), so it is no indicator by itself, while `primary` against the cards and the tint is at least 4.99:1.
    - **Drafts:** when a detail for the same task mounts in the frame another one leaves the tree, such as a fresh entry under a new key, its `initState` reads the comment draft after the old State is deactivated but before it is disposed.
- **Decision:**
  - **Taken by the owner before the step (2026-10-28):** the task detail becomes a pane beside the list on wide windows, amending D56.
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-10-28), open in the review:**
    1. **When:** the detail opens in a pane when the window is two-pane, not in tabletop posture, and the task is opened from a screen inside a tab other than Account: the Overview, Upcoming, the smart lists and a project's List, Table, Kanban and Gantt views. Pages over the shell keep the sheet: search, the inbox and a label's tasks. Everywhere else it is the sheet as before, on both platforms, so phones in either orientation and the 800 × 600 test surface do not change.
    2. **One pane per tab:** the Overview, Upcoming and Projects tabs each have a pane with a stack of the tasks opened inside it, at most 10, the oldest dropped. A tab switch shows that tab's pane, or none; the Account tab never has one. Only the top task is built.
    3. **Layout:** in the shell, beside the tab's content and below every page over the shell: rail, list, a 1 dp divider in `outlineVariant`, pane. The pane takes 40 % of the content beside the rail, at least 360 and at most 480 dp; the list keeps D69's 840 dp band in what is left. The offline banner spans list and pane, the tab's floating action button stays left of the pane, and on iOS the pane sits above the tab bar. The pane is on the page's surface colour, keeps clear of the status bar itself, and takes the keyboard as the shell's Scaffold does, with no inset of its own. While the pane shows, the list loses its end-side safe-area padding and the pane its start-side padding, mirrored right to left.
    4. **The same detail:** the pane shows `TaskDetailSheet`, with a few changes. The close button closes the pane. A related task, a subtask, the parent, a task link and the duplicate's "Open" open inside the pane, and a back arrow before the identifier ("Back to *title*") returns to the task before. Delete leaves the task, closes the pane after the last one and says "Task deleted" on the screen beside it. Edit, the description editor and the comment editor open over the shell with the pane under them. The header row and the 4 dp progress line are pinned above the pane's list, so close and back stay in reach; in the sheet they scroll as before. Tasks in the pane change without an animation. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the back arrow comes before the project chip, which took the identifier's place, and the close button reads "Close details".*
    5. **Selection:** the row, board card or timeline row of the task in the current tab's pane is marked and says "selected" to screen readers. A row gets a `surfaceContainerHighest` fill and a 4 dp `primary` bar on its start edge; a board card the fill and a 2 dp `primary` outline inside its corners; a timeline row a translucent band of the fill and a 2 dp `primary` outline around its bar. Only the pane's top task is selected, and rows on pages over the shell, in sheets and inside the pane never are.
    6. **Back:** first a page, sheet, dialog or menu over the shell or in the tab; then the task before in the pane; then the pane; then the tab's own pages; then D45's tab rule (back to the first tab). This holds for the back button and the swipe alike.
    7. **Resets and the location rule:** any change of the active account (a switch, or the sign-out of the active one) closes every pane, and a sign-out removes them with the shell. A tab's pane closes when that tab's own location changes: another project, up to the tree, another smart list, a `go` elsewhere in the branch. Pages over the shell, another view of the same project, Upcoming's ranges and a list's filters and sorts keep it. Selecting the current tab again closes its pane.
    8. **Links, widget taps and notification taps** (`TaskOpener.showTask`): the rules of D44 stay (the open dialogs and sheets close, another tab goes back to the Overview). Then the task opens anew in the Overview's pane, at its comment for a notification about one, where the pane fits and nothing covers the shell; else in a sheet as before.
    9. **Resize:** when the window stops being two-pane (a rotation, a split screen, a freeform resize, a fold into tabletop posture), the current tab's task opens as a sheet with the same task and its comment draft. Its stack and the other tabs' panes are dropped. A sheet stays a sheet when the window grows. A resize that keeps two panes keeps the pane, its stack and both scroll offsets.
    10. **Hinge and posture:** a vertical fold or hinge inside the content that has a width or is half opened places the split on it, the list on the start side and the pane after it, when both keep at least 320 dp; else the normal split applies. A half-opened horizontal fold (tabletop) gives no pane, as on a phone. A pure function next to `WindowLayout` computes it.
    11. **Accessibility:** while the pane shows, the list and the pane are regions of their own for screen readers and keyboards, the list first. The pane's task title keeps its level-1 heading beside the screen's own level-1 title: two regions, which D68's heading rules allow. Opening, changing and closing the pane announce nothing. Focus moves into the pane on every open and change, and back to the row on close. The sweep runs scenarios with the pane open.
    12. **iPad:** the same pane at two-pane sizes, so in landscape on every iPad and in portrait on the 13" iPad (1024 × 1366). An 11" iPad in portrait (834 pt wide, below 840), an iPad mini in portrait (744 pt), Split View, Slide Over and an iPhone in landscape keep the sheet. This replaces the spec's "portrait 834+".
  - **Taken by the main session (2026-10-28), also without the owner and open in the review:**
    - **The lock keeps the pane.** This deviates from the roadmap row, which said that "the lock reset it". D64's gate keeps sheets and pages, unsaved edits included, under it, and the pane is kept the same way: while the app is locked, back moves the app to the back and leaves the pane, and after the unlock the pane, its stack and a comment draft are there.
    - **Pages inside a tab without Android's predictive back preview, at every size** (M1 of the spec check). The tab navigators get `FadeForwardsPageTransitionsBuilder`, the transition a tap already showed, so a swipe inside a tab goes the way of the back button. This is the one change visible on phones: a page inside a tab, such as a project or a smart list, no longer shows the page below while it is swiped back. Pages over the shell keep the preview. In return the three Android 14+ bugs above are gone. The alternative, the change only on two-pane windows, would have kept them on phones.
    - **The other defaults of the spec check:** snackbars span list and pane (M2), as they spanned the content in D69: a row's Done with Undo, "Saved" after the edit page and "Task deleted" from the pane; the pane's own messages (a comment posted, copied, failures) stay in the pane. Back inside the pane restores the scroll offset as far as the content allows, while the comments are collapsed again and the details read again (M3). The selected look as in decision 5, for the owner to judge (M4). After a resize while a page, sheet or dialog covers the shell, the task waits and opens as a sheet once the shell is in front again (M5). Focus moves into the pane, as a sheet's route takes it, and returns on close (M6). The pane's header is pinned, unlike the sheet's (M7). The back arrow's tooltip names the task, "Back to *title*", rather than "Previous task" (M8). Other tabs' panes are dropped when the window shrinks and do not come back when it grows (M9).
  - **Also taken without the owner, by the spec check and the review, open in the review:**
    - **The location rule** compares the shell route's own `matchedLocation`, never the router's, which names the page over the shell.
    - **A row's task already on top opens nothing new; the opener always makes a fresh entry,** so a notification's comment applies, and a draft typed just before is kept.
    - **A task deleted in one tab's pane leaves the other tabs' panes too** (review), and a stack never shows the same task twice in a row after a delete.
    - **The selection follows a queued create** (review): the row stays selected once the server names the task, and a tap on it keeps the pane as it is.
    - **The pane's focus node adds no semantics node** (review, the medium finding: it made one focusable node over the whole pane, which read the header's identifier with the whole pane outlined). The back arrow's icon is left out of the semantics, so the button is read once, by its tooltip (review). *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the header holds no identifier; in the pane the first Tab reaches the project chip, which a screen reader reads alone after the back arrow.*
    - **Focus after a close** goes to the row last used to open a task, while that row is still built; else it stays in the list (review).
    - **The timeline's selected band is translucent** (60 %), so the day lines and weekends stay visible under it (review).
    - **A second string, "More for *title*",** as the tooltip of the pane's menu (review): the pane's menu and the project page's menu were both "More" under the same heading.
    - **On a hinge** the gap is the feature's width with no divider, and the pane may then be wider than 480 dp.
  - **The pane's state** (`_HomeShellState`, `features/home/presentation/home_shell.dart`): four stacks of `TaskDetailPaneEntry` (the task, a comment to show once, the scroll offset), one per branch, each with the location it was opened at and the focus node to return to. They are plain State of the shell, changed only in `setState` and the lifecycle methods, and they go with the shell on sign-out. A row opens a stack of one; a task inside the pane pushes; back pops; close clears; a delete removes the task from every tab's stack and closes a pane left empty.
  - **Reaching it** (`features/tasks/presentation/task_detail_pane.dart`):
    - `TaskDetailPaneScope`, an `InheritedModel`, wraps only the branch navigators, so screens inside a tab find it, while pages over the shell, sheets, dialogs and the pane itself do not. It carries `available` (two-pane, no tabletop, not the Account tab), the selected task id and `open`.
    - `openTaskDetail(context, task:)` reads the scope without a dependency and opens the pane when it is available, else `showTaskDetail` as before. The tabs' seven call sites use it: the Overview, Upcoming, the smart lists through `DueSectionsList`, a project's list, table, board and timeline. Search keeps `showTaskDetail`; a label's tasks shares `DueSectionsList`, finds no scope and opens the sheet. `showTaskDetail` itself is unchanged.
    - `TaskDetailPaneScope.isSelected(context, taskId)` is an aspect dependency, so a change of the pane's task rebuilds only the old and the new row.
    - `TaskDetailPanes` (`taskDetailPanesProvider`, kept alive) holds the shell for the opener; the shell attaches in `initState` and detaches only itself in `dispose`.
  - **Layout:** the shell's `PopScope` holds a `BackButtonListener` around the `Row` of rail and `Scaffold`. In the Scaffold's body, under `OfflineBannerArea`, a `LayoutBuilder` builds `_TabsBesidePane`, a `Row` that is always there: the tabs (`Expanded`, a `MediaQuery` without the end-side padding while the pane shows, a `Semantics` that is a container while the pane shows, a `FocusTraversalGroup`, the scope, a `Theme` for the branches, the navigation shell), then the `VerticalDivider` or the hinge's gap, then the pane at its width. The navigation shell keeps its place in the tree in every state, so opening or closing the pane keeps the tabs' pages and scroll offsets.
  - **The pane** (`TaskDetailPane`): a `Material` on `surface`, the status bar's inset as its own top padding, a `Semantics` container without a label, a `FocusTraversalGroup` and a `Focus` with the shell's pane node, and the top entry's `TaskDetailSheet` under `ObjectKey(entry)`. Its `ScrollController` starts at the entry's offset and writes it back, with `keepScrollOffset: false`, as the page storage would be shared by every entry.
  - **The seam** (`TaskDetailPaneHost`, the `pane` parameter of `TaskDetailSheet`; without it the sheet's code paths are as before):
    - close calls `close`; `_open` (related tasks, subtasks, the parent, task links in descriptions and comments, the duplicate's "Open") calls `openOther`; delete calls `deleted(taskId)` and shows "Task deleted" on the root messenger, in the shell's Scaffold; Edit pushes the edit page without a pop.
    - The header row and the progress line are built above the list. `back` and `backTitle` (the stored task's title, else the entry's) give the back arrow, an `IconButton` with `BackButtonIcon` and the tooltip "Back to *title*"; the menu's tooltip is "More for *title*".
    - The description and comment editors, a label's tasks, the project breadcrumb, dialogs, pickers, menus and the image viewer open from the pane on the root navigator, as from the sheet. There is no navigator inside the pane.
    - The comment draft is also saved in `deactivate`, so a fresh entry for the same task (the opener's) that replaces the pane's detail in the same frame reads it; the sheet after a resize opens a frame later, after the pane has saved the draft.
  - **Back:**
