    - A `Theme` around the branch navigators maps Android to `FadeForwardsPageTransitionsBuilder`, derived once per parent theme, so no predictive back detector claims a swipe inside a tab.
    - `BackButtonListener` comes after `AppLockGate`'s observer and before go_router's delegate. It pops the pane's stack, or closes the pane, only when the shell's route is current, the pane shows, and `RootModalRoutes.isOpenOn` (new) finds no menu or other pageless route open on the current tab's navigator.
    - `PopScope.canPop` includes `!paneShown`, so Android hears that the app handles back while a pane is open; `onPopInvokedWithResult` keeps only the tab rule.
  - **Resets:** `_onActiveAccountChanged` clears every stack and a pending sheet on any change of the active account; on a switch that happens in the same `setState` as `_closePreviousAccount`'s reset, so no frame shows the old account's task. `didUpdateWidget` compares the current tab's `shellRouteContext.routerState.matchedLocation` with the stack's origin and clears the stack when they differ. `_selectTab` on the current tab closes its pane.
  - **The opener:** `TaskOpener.showTask` keeps `closeAll` and the way back to the Overview, then calls `showInOverview(task, commentId:)`. That replaces the Overview's stack with a fresh entry only when the pane fits, the Overview is the current tab and nothing covers the shell (`ModalRoute.isCurrentOf`); else `showTaskDetail` as before.
  - **Resize, posture and hinge:**
    - `didChangeDependencies` reads `WindowLayout.twoPane` and `isTabletop(MediaQuery.displayFeaturesOf(context))`. When the window stops having room, the current tab's top entry becomes a pending sheet and the other stacks are cleared. The sheet opens after a frame once the shell is in front, through `showTaskDetail`, without a comment. If the room comes back first, with the same tab and location, the entry returns to the pane; a newer task from the opener drops it.
    - `paneSplitFor(windowWidth:, contentWidth:, textDirection:, displayFeatures:)` and `isTabletop` (`app/layout/pane_split.dart`) are pure functions. The split returns the list's width, the gap, the pane's width and whether a divider shows: by default 40 % of the content within 360–480 dp and a 1 dp divider; on a vertical fold or hinge inside the content that has a width or is half opened, the split on it, without a divider, when both sides keep 320 dp. A flat fold of no width, a cutout and a feature outside the content are ignored. Tabletop is a horizontal fold or hinge in half-opened posture.
  - **Selection:** `SelectedTaskHighlight` (`features/tasks/presentation/selected_task_highlight.dart`) is always in the tree and paints an `Ink` decoration (`SelectedTaskDecoration`, which takes no padding) on the nearest `Material`, under the ink of the row or card: the fill with a bar on the start edge for `TaskRow`, the fill with an outline inside the 12 dp corners for `BoardCard`. `TimelineRow` paints its band at 60 % and outlines its bar. Each adds `selected: true` to its existing semantics only while selected, so every other row keeps no selected state at all. The scope's selected id follows a queued create's server id.
  - **Strings:** `taskDetailPaneBack` ("Back to {title}", "Zurück zu „{title}“") and `taskDetailPaneMore` ("More for {title}", "Mehr zu „{title}“"), visible tooltips with descriptions. No label for the pane and no announcement. **No new dependency.**
  - **The sweep** (`app/test/a11y/`):
    - `SweepScenario` takes `surfaces` (by default the phone and the tablet in both orientations) and `tabletBrightnesses` (by default dark).
    - Nine pane scenarios (`scenarios_pane.dart`, run by `pane_sweep_test.dart`) are route scenarios whose variant starts with `pane`, opened by a real tap: the Overview with an overdue task, Upcoming, a project's List view with a related task opened inside the pane (the back arrow), the Table, Kanban and Gantt views, Favourites, a guest's Overview and the Overview with the offline banner. They run on the tablet in landscape only (Android 1280 × 800, iPad 1194 × 834), in light and dark, English and German with the guidelines and the width checks, and dark at 200 % text: 108 tests. Two of them also run on an unfolded foldable (841 × 701, the pane at its 360 dp, Android, dark, 200 %): 4 tests.
    - The scroll stops go through the pane's list first, then the list's. The main scroll view is never the pane's, and the band of a list beside the pane ends at the divider. Each pane scenario has its class in `readable_widths.dart`, and the completeness test checks that it names a swept route and runs on the tablet in landscape only.
  - **The parked session hardening:** none of the six auth files of the package reserved as D59 changed; of its file list, only the ARB and generated localisation files and the docs changed here, which will conflict when the package is applied.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **`secondaryContainer` as the selected tint:** it fails in both high-contrast schemes (above). **`ListTile`'s primary text for the selected row:** it collides with the primary tone of "due today".
  - **Pages inside a tab without predictive back only on two-pane windows:** it would keep the three Android 14+ bugs on phones.
  - **Closing the pane with the lock,** as the roadmap row planned: the gate keeps sheets, pages and unsaved edits, and the pane is kept the same way.
  - **Keeping up to 10 previous details alive offstage** for back inside the pane, instead of the scroll offset alone.
  - **Dropping the task on a resize while something covers the shell,** or showing its sheet over that page.
  - **A snackbar inset on the shell's Scaffold only,** which would need the parent theme set again below it, so that sheets do not take the inset.
  - **The iPad's pane in portrait from 834 pt,** as the spec had it: 834 is below the 840 dp of two panes.
  - **A label for the pane or an announcement when it opens:** the sheet has neither, and a label would be read before every task.
- **Consequences:**
  - On an Android tablet or an iPad in landscape, a 13" iPad in portrait and an unfolded foldable, a task from a tab's list shows beside the list, its row marked, and related tasks open in the same place. Phones in either orientation, tablets in portrait below 840 dp, windows below 480 dp high and the 800 × 600 test surface keep the sheet as before. On Android 14 and later with gesture navigation, a back swipe on a page inside a tab no longer shows the page below, at every size; pages over the shell keep the preview, and the three double pops are gone.
  - Known gaps:
    - Not checked on a device: back swipes with gesture navigation, among them the order by swipe, the phone-size swipe with the sheet over a project page (the double-pop fix) and pages inside a tab without the preview (widget tests drive the swipes); the selected look in dark (the emulator ran in light); a foldable (widget tests with fake folds and hinges only); the iPad (with the iOS device test at the end of the project); and TalkBack and VoiceOver with the pane (the manual pass, [Q19](open-questions.md#q19-device-setup-for-the-manual-talkback-pass), [checklist](accessibility-checklist.md)). *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): on the iPad Pro 13-inch simulator (iOS 26.5) the pane opened in portrait at 1032 pt and in landscape, with the selected row and with Kanban, Table and Gantt beside it, and a task link opened in it; VoiceOver with the pane is still to come.*
    - An 11" iPad in portrait (834 pt) and an Android tablet in portrait (800 dp) keep the sheet; only the 13" iPad gets the pane in portrait.
    - Back inside the pane restores the scroll offset only; the comments are collapsed and the details read again. The pane's tasks change without an animation.
    - Right to left is covered only by the tests of the split function and the highlight; the shell itself was not seen in a right-to-left language, which the app does not have yet.
    - With a page open in a tab that is not shown, the app tells Android that it handles back even on the Overview without a pane, as it did before this step; a comment in `task_detail_pane_back_test.dart` notes it, and no test asserts it.
    - Uncoloured label pills blend into the selected tint in the light brand scheme; their text keeps 7.28:1 on their own fill.
  - Not built: the pane on pages over the shell (search, the inbox, a label's tasks); a transition between the pane's tasks; Esc for the pane (36d); the projects list–detail (36g).
  - Open in the review besides decisions 1 to 12: the main session's choices, first the predictive back change, the one change on phones, and the lock keeping the pane; the selected look in light, dark and high contrast; the choices of the spec check and the review.
  - Tests: `test/app/layout/pane_split_test.dart` (7: the 40 % split within 360–480 dp, a half-opened vertical fold, a hinge with a width, a side under 320 dp, a flat fold, a cutout and a fold outside the content, right to left, tabletop); in `test/features/home/`, `task_detail_pane_test.dart` (15: the core, from opening and closing to the tab switch, the account switch, the location rule, a fold and the back reports), `task_detail_pane_sites_test.dart` (31: every tab's call site in the pane at 1280 × 800 and 1194 × 834, the 13" iPad in both orientations, search, a label's tasks and the inbox as sheets, and a row tap opening a sheet at Android 360 × 740, 740 × 360, 952 × 427, 800 × 600 and 800 × 1280 and iOS 375 × 667, 844 × 390, 834 × 1194 and 744 × 1133), `task_detail_pane_stack_test.dart` (17: back at the earlier offset, the 11th push, deletes in one and in other tabs, the root navigator's pages unchanged, the header below a status bar and in reach at the list's end, the editors and a label's tasks over the pane, every place the pane closes, the breadcrumbs), `task_detail_pane_back_test.dart` (9: a swipe with a hidden tab's page, the order by swipe and by button, the edit page and a sheet over a project page, a tab's menu first, and the two phone bugs as regressions), `task_detail_pane_layout_test.dart` (21: the pane's width, divider, list, FAB and banner at the tablet sizes, the iOS tab bar, the board and the timeline ending at the divider, the 840 dp band at 1366 × 1024, offsets kept, focus into the pane and back to the row, the semantics regions and order, no announcements, Tab order, the snackbar across list and pane), `task_detail_pane_opener_test.dart` (7: a link, a widget's task, a notification at its comment and a share's "Open" in the Overview's pane, from another tab too; the sheet at 800 × 1280 and under a page; the task on top opening anew with its draft) and `task_detail_pane_resize_test.dart` (10: Android and iPad rotations with a reply and a draft typed just before, resizes that keep the pane, the keyboard, folds and hinges); `test/features/tasks/task_selection_test.dart` (19: the selected row in every list, the card and the timeline row, moving with the pane's task, none on phones, on pages over the shell or after a resize, a queued create, and the highlight's painting); one test each in `app_lock_gate_test.dart` (the pane with two tasks and a draft under the lock), `project_board_test.dart` (a card opening a sheet on a phone) and the contrast rows of `app_theme_test.dart` (the selected row's text and the indicator in all four schemes); the helpers `test/helpers/back_gestures.dart` and `task_detail_panes.dart`; the pane scenarios of the sweep. No existing check was relaxed and no test deleted. Two existing tests gained checks: the route coverage test of `sweep_completeness_test.dart` leaves the pane scenarios out of the routes' coverage and checks that each names a swept route, and "keeps text readable" in `app_theme_test.dart` has a row for the selected row's text. The sweep harness (`sweep()` over a scenario's surfaces and brightnesses, the pane's list scrolled first, the bands ending at the divider) and `readableBand` (an optional `right`) were extended for the pane scenarios. In total 8,031 app tests (213 contract, Docker and release-only tests skipped) pass in about 10.5 minutes, 2,641 of them in `test/a11y` with the 112 pane sweep tests; 43 Kotlin tests pass; analyze and format are clean. No package changed.
  - Checked on 2026-10-28 on the Pixel_9_Pro_XL emulator with display overrides for 1280 × 800 dp, 800 × 1280 dp and a phone size, signed in as a link-share guest with write access, with three-button navigation and the light theme. At 1280 × 800 a task opened in a 480 dp pane beside the list, with its row selected (the tint and the primary bar at its start). A related task opened inside the pane with "Back to …". The back button went to the task before in the pane, then closed the pane, then left the project page. A comment draft typed in the pane was still there after rotating to 800 × 1280, where the pane turned into a sheet with the same task; rotating back kept the sheet. At the phone size the sheet opened. The app did not crash. Nothing was run on iOS.

## D71: Complete English and German: British English as the base, a glossary, a wording pass over every string, more server error codes in the app's own words with the server's detail as the fallback, the device's language followed while the app runs, names sorted with accents folded, an App language row that opens the system's page

*Accepted 2026-10-29, step 36e (Android: one release build and a check on the Pixel_9_Pro_XL emulator with the per-app language; nothing was built for iOS, and the iOS changes are checked with the iOS device test at the end of the project). The owner accepted 36c on 2026-10-29 and asked for 36e next, before 36d, whose strings will follow this step's glossary; it is the fourth sub-step of step 36 to be built ([roadmap](roadmap.md)). The roadmap row the owner put on the roadmap named what the step does; how, decisions 1 to 9 below, was decided without the owner and is open in the review, and so are the main session's choices and the choices of the plan and the review listed after them. Carries out [D21](#d21-languages-english-and-german) for the first release and strikes its line on an `error.*` ARB import tool, with a dated note there: [D67](#d67-agpl-30-or-later-with-an-app-store-permission-contributions-under-the-dco-the-name-and-the-logo-reserved-no-f-droid-the-legal-notices-on-an-about-page-and-the-licence-page-the-source-code-published-before-the-first-store-release) had already ruled out copying the web's texts, and the tool was never built. Amends [D39](#d39-local-notifications-a-scheduler-per-signed-in-account-plans-from-the-local-database-and-diffs-against-a-rolling-window-with-stable-keys-exact-alarms-when-allowed-done-through-the-outbox-from-a-background-isolate-without-a-token-refresh-snooze-local-only-permissions-asked-once-after-the-first-sync) for the app's own isolate: its notification texts are no longer resolved once but follow a language change while the app runs, while the headless isolates still resolve them once, from the device or the noted tag. The widget texts of [D63](#d63-home-screen-widgets-from-json-snapshots-the-app-writes-through-an-in-repo-plugin-glance-widgets-on-android-with-done-through-a-headless-dart-worker-a-widgetkit-extension-on-ios-with-an-app-group-outbox-and-no-token) and the lock prompt of [D64](#d64-an-app-lock-over-the-ui-not-encryption-with-biometrics-or-the-devices-screen-lock-device-privacy-settings-for-the-app-switcher-screenshots-notification-content-and-widget-titles) follow the same way, and D64's excursions cover the new App language row. Keeps the translated default names of [D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner) and the account's language setting of [D54](#d54-account-settings-and-profile-edited-in-the-app-with-writes-that-never-wipe-the-webs-settings-the-avatar-managed-password-and-email-left-to-the-web), which still never changes the app's language. Follows the description rule of [D68](#d68-screen-readers-heading-levels-on-screen-titles-and-section-headers-one-announcer-with-the-apps-own-live-region-on-android-high-contrast-and-reduce-motion-followed-and-a-route-sweep-that-every-route-pushed-page-and-overlay-must-pass-wcag-22-aa-as-the-yardstick-with-written-deviations) for the new messages and for the texts the wording pass changed (below), and runs its sweep with the changed texts and the new row. Keeps [D2](#d2-material_ui-and-cupertino_ui): every change uses material_ui and cupertino_ui.*

- **Context:**
  - Before this step both ARB files had about 2,105 messages, German complete, with parity and placeholders enforced by `app/test/l10n/arb_parity_test.dart`. The English was British (colour, favourite, licence, organise), but nothing recorded it; the German used du and „…“. There was no glossary, and the audits found the same thing worded in several ways: anlegen and erstellen, gesetzt and festgelegt, zeigen and anzeigen, Handy and Gerät, "Mark done" and "Mark as done", curly and straight apostrophes, "…" with and without a space.
  - A spec with decisions 1 to 9; three audits, of the English strings, of the German strings and of the server's error codes; a plan that merged them into the glossary, the wording pass, the error mapping and 14 choices for the main session (M1 to M14); four parts built (the language followed at runtime; sorting, the timeline's weekdays and wrapping errors; the App language row; the error mapping); and a review with two lenses shaped the result. The language lens had 15 findings, 3 medium (the German field errors, now „Gib … ein.“; `overviewEmptyBody`; `signInWaiting`, now „Anmeldung läuft …“) and 12 low, all fixed; the code lens found nothing.
  - Verified facts (spec and plan of 2026-10-29, from the code at 36c's commit and from Vikunja v2.7.1, read for behaviour only):
    - **One language for what the OS shows:** `deviceAppLocalizationsProvider` was overridden with a fixed value at bootstrap and in the three headless entry points. It feeds the notification texts and the two Android channels, the activity notifications, the widget texts and the app lock prompt, so after a language change while the process ran (Android's system or per-app language) these stayed in the old language while the UI changed. Android reports both changes as `didChangeLocales` without recreating the activity, because the manifest handles `locale` in `configChanges`; iOS restarts an app whose language changes. The app shortcut already registered again on a locale change.
    - **Channels:** Android renames a notification channel that is created again under the same id and keeps the user's settings for it.
    - **Per-app language:** Android's per-app language was configured (`android:localeConfig`, `res/xml/locales_config.xml`), but the app had no way to reach the system's page for it. Android 13 (API 33) opens it with `Settings.ACTION_APP_LOCALE_SETTINGS` and the package URI; iOS opens the app's page in Settings (`openSettingsURLString`), where the Language entry is.
    - **Server errors:** 14 messages put the server's English detail into a localized sentence for codes the app did not map, and about 45 codes were mapped in presentation code. Only 12 of the 14 show server English in production: `accountSendNowFailed` gets only the app's own texts, and `newTaskAddFailed`'s message arm is reached only by test fakes, as quick add queues locally. The failures that reach them most often are a 5xx (writes are never retried), a session that ended (the session manager rethrows the stored failure with the server's English refresh message), a 404 with a code the path did not map, a 429 when the server's rate limit is on, a file missing from the server's storage (404 code 4034, which the project duplicate called "The project no longer exists"), out-of-date permissions (403) and a guest link that is gone.
    - **Sorting:** `compareTitles` (`core/text/title_order.dart`) already folded case, accents and ß for projects, but label pills on rows, the label list, a team's members and the sort menu compared lower-cased code units, so "Ärger" came after "Zahlung". The timeline header measured the English `'Wed'` to choose between short and one-letter weekdays.
    - **Wrapping:** the repeat amount field had no `errorMaxLines`, so its error was cut to one line at large text.
    - **Docs out of date:** D21's "`error.*` ARB import tool" line, and CONTRIBUTING's rule that new strings go to `app_en.arb` only, which the parity test contradicted.
- **Decision:**
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-10-29), open in the review:**
    1. **British English is the base** (`app_en.arb`, the template). US English could come later as `app_en_US.arb` overrides, without code. German stays complete on every commit.
    2. **A glossary and style guide** for both languages, [glossary.md](glossary.md), from the strings' majority usage and the platforms' conventions. German: erstellen (not anlegen), Fälligkeitsdatum for the field, „fällig am {date}“ in sentences and „Fällig am“ as the detail's row label, while the spoken due texts leave out „am“, Favoriten, „Nicht festgelegt“, anzeigen, erledigt for a task's state and fertig for finishing a picker, öffnen for the action and offen for the state, du, „…“, a space before "…" where it stands for missing words. English: British spelling, sentence case, the ASCII apostrophe, "…" as one character without a space, the app's terms for its objects (a bucket is a "column"). Server and brand names, the Quick Add syntax words and filter operators stay as they are.
    3. **A wording pass over every string** in both files by the glossary: only what is inconsistent, wrong, unclear or a typo changes; keys and placeholders stay; nothing is rewritten for taste. Every change was listed with its key and old and new values for the review, and tests that found a widget by a changed text changed with it.
    4. **Server errors:** the localized sentence with the server's detail stays as the fallback, and the codes that reach the fallbacks most often get the app's own texts in English and German, written in our own words from the server's behaviour.
    5. **The language followed while the app runs:** what the OS shows follows the device's or the per-app language on a locale change and on resume. The notification channels are renamed and the scheduled notifications planned again in the new language, the widget snapshots are published again, and the activity notifications and the lock prompt follow. The headless isolates keep reading the noted tag, which is updated.
    6. **Names sorted with accents folded:** row label pills, the label list, a team's members and the sort menu use `compareTitles`; the timeline measures its widest localized weekday instead of `'Wed'`.
    7. **An "App language" row** that shows the current app language's name and opens the system's page: Android 13's per-app language page, the app's settings page on iOS; hidden on Android 12 and older. Leaving for it is a `launchAway` excursion, so it does not lock the app.
    8. **Errors that wrap** on fixed-width fields: `errorMaxLines` on the repeat amount and on any other such field the audit found.
    9. **The docs:** D21's line struck with a dated note, CONTRIBUTING's translation rule fixed, the glossary, this entry, the architecture page, the roadmap and the README.
  - **Taken by the main session (2026-10-29), also without the owner and open in the review** (the plan's M-items, each with the plan's default):
    - **The App language row is the first row of the Appearance section** (M4), which now shows when either of its rows applies: on iOS and on Android 13 and later without a system palette, the language row alone. A section of its own would have needed a heading string. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): App language is the last row, after Theme and Use system colours, in a section every signed-in user has; `appearanceLightDarkHint` is gone.*
    - **The English sentence dash is a spaced em dash** (M5), the majority (5 values against 2); the two en dashes changed. Should the owner prefer the British spaced en dash, the five change instead.
    - **An API token's expiry says "Last valid day" and "Until the end of {date}"** (M6), not "Valid through" and "Through {date}": British, and the export texts already say "until {date}". The token still expires at the end of the chosen day; German keeps „bis einschließlich“.
    - **The no-project error points to the Projects tab** (M7): "Create one on the Projects tab first" (German: im Tab „Projekte“) instead of "on the web", right for signed-in users. A read-only guest sees it too and can create no project anywhere, as with the old text; a guest text of its own is left out.
    - **Filter codes** (M8): 4033 (too complex) gets the app's text; 4017 joins 4016, 4019 and 4024, which keep the server's detail, because they reach the server only when its parser disagrees with the app's, and then the detail is the only clue. All five show at the filter field.
    - **Account security keeps its own rate-limit text** ("The server is busy…", M9).
    - **`labelPickerCreateOffline` is deleted** (M3): the label picker words its failures through `labelChangeFailureText`, whose offline text is the same after the wording pass.
    - **Left alone** (M11, taste or no clear flaw): the about 60 short German imperatives (Prüf, Versuch, Zieh), „Keins“ beside „Keines“, „können … nicht geändert werden“ beside „lassen sich … nicht ändern“, "can't" beside "cannot", the filter date tooltips, `taskDueSemantics` („Fällig {when}“) and, like it, the other spoken due texts without „am“, `taskOverdueSemantics` („Überfällig, war fällig {when}“) and `quickAddDueSemantics` („Fällig {date}, {relative}“), which the glossary names as exceptions, the English `appearanceLightDarkHint`, `legalImprint`, `priorityUnset`, `connectTagline`, `shareAddToTask`, and Android's `widget_tasks_description`, which has no room to name Overdue and Favourites.
    - **The order of the work** (M1, M2, M12–M14): the wording pass first, so that the parts tested against the final texts; the German size checks before the final run; the emulator check and the final runs of the spec.
  - **Also taken without the owner, by the plan and the review, open in the review:**
    - **„Fälligkeitsdatum“ for the German field** (`taskDetailDueDate`: the edit page, the new-task sheet, the table columns), while the detail sheet's row keeps „Fällig am“ under a new key, `taskDetailDueDateRow`, because its 96 dp label column is too narrow for the long word.
    - **German counterparts of English fixes** where German had the same flaw: „Spalte löschen?“ and „Anhang löschen?“ end in "?" like the other 24 German confirmation titles; › instead of → in a settings path; Gerät instead of Handy in four texts, as the app runs on tablets; „die Person, die ihn betreut“ instead of „seine Administration“.
    - **Two audit proposals not taken:** `sharePeopleSearchHint` (de) keeps its comma, which is correct; `commentReplyDropped` became „Du antwortest nicht mehr“ instead of „Antworten abgebrochen“, which also reads as a plural noun.
    - **"Mark as done" in every English text** (review), the iOS widget extension's keys included.
    - **A 404 counts for the task duplicate and the assignee search only when it is not a missing route.**
    - **Errors and chips that wrap at large text** (the size checks): the repeat amount's and the reminder amount's errors take up to five lines; the reminder picker's reference chips („Fälligkeitsdatum (nicht festgelegt)“) wrap up to four lines with a checkmark box of a fixed size, because the chip's one-line style faded "(not set)" out at 200 %; the help texts under the project identifier and the column limit take up to ten lines, a cap only.
    - **A token deleted elsewhere already** (404 code 14017) counts as deleted, as a session already gone does (16001); no text is needed.
    - **Descriptions for the texts the wording pass changed too** (review; the spec and the plan asked for them on new messages only), as D68 and the roadmap's Definition of done ask for a description on every changed string: the 152 of the 204 changed keys that had none got one, and 12 more whose description predates this step now name their placeholders and, where the text is only spoken, start with "Screen reader: ".
  - **The base and the glossary:** `app_en.arb` stays the template, in British English; [glossary.md](glossary.md) has the rules for both languages and for the native strings. Every new message and every message the wording pass changed has a description, and four descriptions were corrected ("Favourites list", "Snackbar").
  - **The wording pass:**
    - 89 English and 135 German values of existing keys changed (204 keys, 20 of them in both; the German change to `labelPickerCreateOffline` was dropped when that key was deleted), checked by script for placeholders, ICU braces and plural counts; no key was renamed.
    - Tests that found a widget by an old text follow it: an API token's expiry, a widget's Done label, the no-project error, „Standardspalte“.
    - Native strings: Android's widget texts (`widget_empty` "All done" and „Alles erledigt“, as the iOS widget and the Overview say; `widget_quick_add_description` „Erstelle mit einem Tipp eine Aufgabe“); the iOS widget extension ("Mark task as done", "… mark it as done", „… als erledigt zu markieren“); the iOS camera and photo library texts, which now name the avatar too, because its page uses the same picker.
    - 14 messages were added (11 error texts, `appLanguageTitle`, `appLanguageName`, `taskDetailDueDateRow`) and `labelPickerCreateOffline` removed: 2,118 messages in each file.
  - **Server errors** (`features/common/presentation/action_failure_text.dart`):
    - `actionFailureText` gives the app's own text for four failures any action can meet: a guest link that is gone (`LinkShareGone`, first, as it extends `SessionEnded`), a session that ended (`SessionEnded`, `TokenRejected`, `RefreshRaceLost`), a rate limit (`RateLimited`) and a server error (`ServerFailure`); null for anything else. The failure texts of the project move, board columns, attachments, project changes, saved filters, label changes, teams, account settings, account security and the task duplicate take it just before their arm with the server's message, so their own arms still win (`accountSettingsSignInAgain`, `accountSecuritySignInAgain`, `accountSecurityRateLimited`, `FileTooLarge`).
    - Per path: the task duplicate maps 403 (`taskDuplicateForbidden`), 4034 (`taskDuplicateFileMissing`), 4002 (the task gone) and 3008 (`taskDuplicateArchived`); attachments a download's 403 (`attachmentDownloadForbidden`), 4034 (`attachmentFileMissing`) and 4002; board columns 3014 (the view gone) and 3001 (the project gone); the project move 3016 (under an archived parent, `projectMoveArchived`); project changes a duplicate's 4034 (`projectDuplicateFileMissing`, where it said the project no longer existed), an edit's 3016 with a new parent (`projectParentArchived`) and 3005 (invalid); teams 6001 (invalid); the assignee search a 403 or a 404 that is not a missing route (`assigneePickerNoAccess`) and a 5xx, 429 or ended session (`assigneePickerSearchFailed`); the label picker every failure through `labelChangeFailureText`.
    - Filters: 4033 shows `filterErrorTooComplex`; 4017 joins 4016, 4019 and 4024 with the server's detail; all five count as an invalid view or saved filter (`isInvalidViewFilter`, `isInvalidSavedFilter`).
    - `deleteApiToken` (`vikunja_api`) takes a 404 with code 14017 as done.
    - Left on the fallback, rare or diagnostic: 10007, 10002, 10005, 4020, 4038, 4039, 1018, 14002, 11002, 2004 and an unexpected response with a message. `accountSendNowFailed` and `newTaskAddFailed` are unchanged.
    - A 5xx without a body (a proxy's HTML 502, 503 or 504) and an upload's storage error without a code now show `actionFailureServerError` instead of each action's generic "failed" text.
  - **The language followed while the app runs** (`core/notifications/local_notification_gateway_provider.dart`):
    - `deviceAppLocalizationsProvider` is a kept-alive Notifier. Bootstrap overrides its build with what `loadDeviceAppLocalizations` resolved; the three headless entry points override it with a value, as before. `follow(locales)` resolves the first supported locale, else English, and when that differs from the current language sets it and notes its tag under `notifications.locale` for the headless isolates; an empty list changes nothing.
    - `deviceLanguageFollowerProvider`, which only bootstrap listens to, so only the UI engine has it, registers a `WidgetsBindingObserver` for `didChangeLocales` and an `AppLifecycleListener` that calls `follow` with the platform's locales on every resume, which also catches a change that was not reported.
    - Notifications: the gateway is not rebuilt, because the activity tray keeps its instance. `LocalNotificationGateway.updateTexts` takes the new texts and, once the plugin is initialised, creates both Android channels again under their ids, which renames them and keeps the user's settings; it never throws. On iOS the categories keep their texts, as the app restarts. Each account's scheduler reads the texts when it plans and reconciles with `force` on a change, so every pending notification is scheduled again with the new body, channel and action titles.
    - Each account's widget publisher publishes again on a change (UI engine only); the activity notices are rebuilt with the new texts; the lock prompt reads them when it asks; the app shortcut already followed.
  - **Sorting and measuring:** row label pills (`rowLabels`), the label list (`LocalLabelRepository.watchAll`), a team's members (by display name, then username, then id) and the sort menu (`sortsInMenuOrder` through `orderedByLabel`) use `compareTitles`, with tie-breaks that keep the order stable: "apfel", "Ärger", "Zahlung". `TimelineHeader` measures the widest of the seven short weekday names of the current locale and takes the one-letter names only when that one does not fit.
  - **The App language row** (`core/platform/app_language_settings.dart`, `features/home/presentation/appearance_section.dart`):
    - `AppLanguageSettings` with `available()` and `open()`; `PlatformAppLanguageSettings` over the device channel: on Android `canOpenAppLocaleSettings` and `openAppLocaleSettings`, on iOS always available and `openAppSettings`, unavailable elsewhere. `open` runs as `AppExcursions.launchAway`, which ends at once when the page did not open. `appLanguageSettingsAvailableProvider` is false until the platform has answered and when it failed.
    - Android (`DeviceChannel.kt`, `AppLocaleSettings.kt`): `ACTION_APP_LOCALE_SETTINGS` with the package URI from API 33, available only when the intent resolves, which a `<queries>` entry in the manifest makes visible; an `ActivityNotFoundException` gives false. iOS (`AppDelegate.swift`): `openSettingsURLString`, answered with the result of the open.
    - The row: a `ListTile` with the language icon, "App language" („App-Sprache“), the app's language in its own name as the subtitle ("English", „Deutsch“) and an open-in-new mark; the first row of the Appearance section under "On this device". A failure is logged by its type only. The sweep's Account tab scenarios show it. *Note 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)): the last row of the Appearance section, which no longer has the "On this device" mark: the row says "On this device" under its subtitle.*
  - **No new dependency.**
  - **The parked session hardening:** none of the six auth files of the package reserved as D59 changed; of its file list, the ARB and generated localisation files, `account_tab.dart` (its class comment) and the docs changed here, which will conflict when the package is applied.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **The web's translations or error texts,** ruled out by D67; the server's code was read for behaviour only.
  - **US English as the base,** like the web: the app's English was British already, and US English can still come as overrides.
  - **A generic localized error with the code and no detail:** for a code the app does not know, the detail is the only clue.
  - **The system's per-app language without a row:** few users find it. **An own language picker with a stored choice:** a second source of truth, which the pickers, the widgets and the notifications could disagree with.
  - **Rebuilding the notification gateway for a new language:** the activity tray would keep the old instance.
  - **A section of its own for the App language row** (M4): it would need a heading string.
- **Consequences:**
  - When the device's language or Rurana's own language changes while the app runs, the UI, the notification channels, the scheduled notifications, the widgets, the activity notifications and the lock prompt switch together, without a restart. On Android 13 and later and on iOS the Account tab has an App language row that names the app's language and opens the system's page for it. A server error, an ended session, a rate limit or a guest link that is gone reads in the app's language for every action; the server's English detail shows only for the rare codes left. Names with umlauts and accents sort among their base letters everywhere. The texts follow one glossary in each language.
  - Known gaps:
    - Not checked on a device: the lock prompt in the new language (the AVD has no screen lock; the tests cover it) and everything on iOS. `AppDelegate.swift` (the row's `openAppSettings`) and the widget extension's new keys were not built, as there are no iOS builds until the end of the project.
    - On iOS the notification categories keep the texts they were registered with until the app restarts, which iOS does on a language change.
    - iOS is reported to show the Language entry on an app's page in Settings only once the device has more than one preferred language; with one, the App language row leads to the app's page without a language choice. To be checked with the iOS device test. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): on the iPad simulator the row opened the Apps list in Settings the first time and the app's page with Language on later taps; `openAppSettings` and the widget extension's keys build with Xcode 27.1.*
    - Android 12 and older have no per-app language page and so no row; there the app follows the system language.
    - The account's language setting (D54) still changes only the server's emails and the web, which may surprise someone who sets it in the app.
    - A read-only guest is told to create a project on the Projects tab, which a guest cannot do (M7).
    - The rare codes above still show the server's English detail inside a localized sentence.
    - `compareTitles` folds the Latin letters of the app's languages and their neighbours the same way in every language. It is no collation per language, which a language such as Swedish would need.
  - Not built: US English; an own language picker; the work for community translation (36f).
  - Open in the review besides decisions 1 to 9: the main session's choices, first the row's place in the Appearance section, the English dash, the token expiry's wording and the Projects tab in the no-project error; the choices of the plan and the review; the wording pass as a whole, which the diff of the ARB files shows key by key, and the glossary.
  - Tests: `test/core/notifications/device_language_test.dart` (English to German while the app runs: both channels renamed under their ids, the pending notification cancelled and scheduled again with the German body, channel and action titles, the widget snapshots in German, a German activity notification, the lock prompt's reason, hint and cancel in German, and "de" noted for the headless isolates; a language without strings falls back to English; a resume in the same language changes nothing, and one after an unreported change follows it); in `local_notification_gateway_test.dart`, `updateTexts` (both channels again on Android once initialised, nothing before, a refused channel logged and not thrown, nothing on iOS) and `follow` (the first supported locale set and noted; the same language or no locale changes nothing); `test/core/platform/app_language_settings_test.dart` (available on Android 13 and later where the page resolves, not on 12 and older, always on iOS, never elsewhere; the method asked for on each platform; the row hidden when the channel fails) and `test/features/home/app_language_row_test.dart` (the row on Android 13 and later and on iOS and not below, the section without a palette, the row above the colours switch, „Deutsch“ in German, a page that did not open); an excursion case in `excursion_call_sites_test.dart` (the page lasts until the app is back, on Android and iOS, and a page that did not open ends at once); `test/features/common/action_failure_text_test.dart` and the newly mapped codes, in English and German, in `attachment_messages_test.dart`, `board_columns_test.dart`, `project_management_test.dart`, `projects_screen_moves_test.dart`, `task_duplicate_follow_test.dart`, `task_edit_assignees_test.dart`, `task_edit_labels_test.dart`, `test/features/smart_lists/saved_filter_failure_test.dart` and `test/features/teams/team_failure_test.dart`, where cases that relied on a 500's message now use an unmapped code; "Ärger", "apfel" and "Zahlung" in `task_row_test.dart`, `label_and_user_repositories_test.dart`, `team_page_test.dart` and `test/features/projects/project_view_labels_test.dart`, with the sort menu checked in both languages; `test/features/projects/timeline_header_test.dart` (the short names where the widest fits, in English and German; German's short names kept at a width where English's no longer fit; one letter at the narrowest days); `test/features/tasks/repeat_section_test.dart` and `reminder_picker_text_scale_test.dart` (the errors wrap on a 320 dp phone at 200 % in English and German, and the reference chips fit unclipped); in `vikunja_api`, a token that answers 404 code 14017 is deleted and another 404 is rethrown; a Kotlin test of `AppLocaleSettings` (3). Tests that asserted a changed text use the new one. No check was relaxed and no test deleted. In total 8,143 app tests (213 contract, Docker and release-only tests skipped) pass, 112 more than at 36c's commit, the sweep among them in English and German with the changed texts; 1,721 in `vikunja_api` and 46 in Kotlin pass; analyze and format are clean.
  - Checked on 2026-10-29 on the Pixel_9_Pro_XL emulator, signed in as a link-share guest with write access, with a Tasks widget on the home screen. With the app open, setting the per-app language to German (`adb shell cmd locale set-app-locales`) switched the UI in place, without a restart. A test notification came in German, and the notification channels were renamed to „Aktivitäten“ and „Erinnerungen“. The widget showed „Heute“, „1 Aufgabe“, „Neue Aufgabe“ and „Überfällig, war fällig 20. Okt.“. The App language row in the Appearance section, showing "English" and then „Deutsch“, opened the system's per-app language page, where System default brought the app and the widget back to English. The app did not crash. The lock prompt was not seen in the new language, as the AVD has no screen lock. Nothing was run on iOS.

## D72: First release: Android and iOS together, on Google Play with an APK in every GitLab release, the repository on GitLab, version 1.0.0 after a beta

*Decided by the owner on 2026-10-29, after accepting step 36e. Step 37 is split into 37a–37d on the [roadmap](roadmap.md); 36d, 36f, 36g and step 33 follow after the first release. Builds on [D67](#d67-agpl-30-or-later-with-an-app-store-permission-contributions-under-the-dco-the-name-and-the-logo-reserved-no-f-droid-the-legal-notices-on-an-about-page-and-the-licence-page-the-source-code-published-before-the-first-store-release) (the source code published before the first store release, no F-Droid). Opens [Q20](open-questions.md#q20-signing-keys-and-the-apk-beside-the-play-build)–[Q22](open-questions.md#q22-ci-runners-on-gitlab).*

- **Context:**
  - Android is complete and checked on the emulator through step 36e. iOS was built along the way but, by the owner's rule of 2026-10-19, is tested only once at the end of the project: the simulator, a device, the widget extension, Face ID and the passcode, VoiceOver.
  - CI exists as GitHub Actions workflows (`.github/workflows/`: the checks and tests, the editor, the contract tests, the DCO check, the weekly dependency scan) but has never run: the repository has no remote. The release APK is still signed with the debug key ([threat model](threat-model.md) R28).
  - D67 requires the source code to be published, and the app's source link set, before the first release that is distributed.
- **Decision (the owner's answers of 2026-10-29):**
  - **Android and iOS in the same first release.** The release waits for the iOS phase (37b).
  - **Google Play plus a signed APK in every release of the repository**, for devices without Play. No F-Droid (D67).
  - **The repository on GitLab.** CI moves from GitHub Actions to GitLab CI (37a); the DCO check and the dependency scan move with it.
  - **1.0.0 after a beta:** test builds as `1.0.0-beta.N` (Play's closed test, TestFlight, pre-releases in the repository), then `1.0.0` for everyone.
- **Consequences:**
  - Step 37 has four sub-steps with one commit and one acceptance each: 37a release builds and GitLab CI, 37b the iOS phase, 37c store listings and policies, 37d beta and 1.0.0.
  - Some work only the owner can do, and the sub-steps prepare everything up to it: the GitLab project and its publication; the Google Play developer account and the Play app; the Apple Developer Program membership, the team and a device; the signing keys (Q20); the server for the store reviewers (Q21); the testers; the check of the name and logo with the Vikunja maintainers ([D15](#d15-app-id-devcoderoftimevikunja)); the sign-off of the threat model; the contact for the written offer and the privacy policy. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): the owner answered Q20 (option A: the owner's own app signing key, uploaded with PEPK, signs the APK too) and Q22 (every project of the group `coderoftime` public, so that the group can join GitLab for Open Source); the GitLab project exists, private, and goes public before the first beta, whose testers come through a Google Group and a public TestFlight link.*
  - A new personal Google Play developer account has to run a closed test with at least 12 testers for 14 days before it can publish to production; the beta covers it.
  - The GitHub Actions workflows are replaced by `.gitlab-ci.yml` in 37a; the threat model's supply-chain items (R26) are revisited there.

## D73: GitLab CI from scripts that run the same locally, images and downloads pinned, the version from the release tag, release signing from protected CI variables, the source link from the build, a GitLab release with the APK, its checksums and the desugar_jdk_libs source

*Accepted 2026-10-29, step 37a (Android only: every CI script that needs neither GitLab nor a Mac run locally, the release dry run and one release build; nothing ran on GitLab, where no project exists yet, and nothing was built for iOS). The first sub-step of step 37 as the owner split it in [D72](#d72-first-release-android-and-ios-together-on-google-play-with-an-apk-in-every-gitlab-release-the-repository-on-gitlab-version-100-after-a-beta) ([roadmap](roadmap.md)), whose decisions it carries out for CI and the release builds. How, decisions 1 to 10 below, was decided without the owner and is open in the review, and so are the amendments of the spec check and the main session's choices listed after them. Replaces the GitHub Actions with GitLab jobs: `ci.yaml` (there since step 1, with [D66](#d66-a-threat-model-uncaught-errors-redacted-and-never-printed-in-release-builds-the-apps-data-out-of-backups-and-device-transfer-routes-pushed-by-the-platform-taken-only-as-app-scheme-links-widget-links-and-notification-taps-switching-accounts-only-with-a-per-install-key-exported-components-and-dependencies-checked-in-ci)'s additions), D66's `osv.yaml` and its test `app/test/ci/workflows_test.dart`, and the `dco.yaml` of [D67](#d67-agpl-30-or-later-with-an-app-store-permission-contributions-under-the-dco-the-name-and-the-logo-reserved-no-f-droid-the-legal-notices-on-an-about-page-and-the-licence-page-the-source-code-published-before-the-first-store-release); `.github/` is deleted. Carries out most of what D67 left to step 37 before the first store release: release tags in CI, so the release-only source-link test runs; the source link set by the build (D67's `sourceCodeUrl` has a dated note); the desugar_jdk_libs source of each release, its upstream commit recorded, attached to the release; a place for the written offer's contact, without which the release job refuses to run; the notices checked in the release build. D67's last item, Cronet raised to a build of a current Chrome stable release, is not in this step: it is done as its own commit before the first release tag (below). Amends [D1](#d1-flutter-3475-pub-workspace-with-pure-dart-packages): CI pins Flutter 3.47.7, with a dated note there. Closes the debug-key risk R28 of the [threat model](threat-model.md) in the build, while the keys wait for [Q20](open-questions.md#q20-signing-keys-and-the-apk-beside-the-play-build); [Q22](open-questions.md#q22-ci-runners-on-gitlab) stays open.*

- **Context:**
  - CI existed only as GitHub Actions: `ci.yaml` with the jobs analyze-test, editor, contract, build-android and build-ios, `dco.yaml` on pull requests, `osv.yaml` on pushes, pull requests and weekly, and the composite action `setup-workspace`. None had ever run, as the repository has no remote. The release APK was signed with the debug key (R28), the pubspec's version was `0.1.0+1` with `versionCode = flutter.versionCode`, and `sourceCodeUrl` was a `null` constant.
  - A spec with decisions 1 to 10, a check of the spec by a critic (18 findings, B1–B2, M1–M9 and m1–m7, all confirmed, two of them with a changed remedy), the spec editor's binding amendments with eight choices for the main session (M-1 to M-8), one implementer and the main session's local runs shaped the result.
  - Verified facts (spec check of 2026-10-29, from the registries, the release data and local probes):
    - **Images:** `ghcr.io/cirruslabs/flutter` has no 3.47 tag (its newest release tag is 3.44.0). `ghcr.io/cirruslabs/android-sdk:36`, created 2025-12-24, is Ubuntu 24.04 with OpenJDK 21, platform 36, build-tools 36.0.0, git, curl, jq, unzip, ruby, python3 and xz, without an NDK (Flutter's Android build wants NDK 28.2.13676358).
    - **Flutter:** CI pinned 3.47.5 (D1), the local runs used 3.47.7, and the current stable is 3.47.8. The Linux tarballs are about 1.58 GB, with their SHA-256 in `releases_linux.json`.
    - **Contract tests:** they use `localhost` 68 times, and the compose file published the server on 127.0.0.1 only, which a job does not reach inside a docker-in-docker service.
    - **Signing:** `apksigner verify` checks an APK but refuses an app bundle, which `jarsigner -verify` checks. The release APK of 36e's commit was signed with the v2 scheme only, by `CN=Android Debug`.
    - **Cronet:** no Chrome-milestone `cronet-embedded` exists after 143.7445.0. `cronet-embedded` 500.x is a deprecated empty stub under the Android SDK licence that pulls `cronet-bundled`, whose 500.1.0 carries `libcronet.155.0.8059.16.so`, a version that reached Chrome Android Stable on 2026-09-23 (the stable on 2026-10-29 is 157.0.8103.30). Its licence texts are in `third_party_licenses.txt` and `.json` inside the AAR, although its POM names a "LICENSE", and the POM cycles back to `cronet-embedded` 500.1.0. The other Cronet artifacts at 500.x are stubs of about 800 bytes, except `cronet`, which carries annotation dependencies. *Note 2026-10-29, the Cronet move: `cronet` 500.1.0 carries all of Cronet's Java, the API and its implementation (619 classes, with a shaded protobuf), besides those dependencies; only the other five are stubs.*
    - **desugar_jdk_libs:** the upstream tags stop at 1.1.5, so the notice's "(release 2.1.4)" named a release that does not exist. Commit `50d9c1fb…` (tree `e324fc59…`, 2024-12-18) sets `VERSION_JDK11.txt` to 2.1.4; Maven's release time is 2024-12-20. A depth-1 fetch of that commit from GitHub works.
    - **Runners:** GitLab.com's Free tier has 400 compute minutes a month, and macOS runners only on paid tiers. The project's `gradle.properties` asks Gradle for 8 GB, more than a small shared runner has.
- **Decision:**
  - **Taken by the owner before the step (2026-10-29, D72):** the repository on GitLab, with CI moved there; Google Play plus a signed APK in every GitLab release; `1.0.0` after `1.0.0-beta.N` betas.
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-10-29), open in the review:**
    1. **A thin `.gitlab-ci.yml`, the logic in scripts under `tool/ci/`** that run the same way on the owner's Mac: bash (3.2 on macOS) with `set -euo pipefail`, reading only GitLab's `CI_*` variables and the variables named here. Pipelines for merge requests, the default branch, `v*` tags, schedules and runs by hand; no branch pipeline beside a merge request's; interruptible jobs, cancelled by a newer commit.
    2. **The jobs** port everything the GitHub workflows checked, with the same commands: `checks`, `editor`, `contract`, `android`, `dco`, `osv`, `ios` and `release` (below).
    3. **The version from the tag** (`tool/ci/release_version.py`): `v1.2.3` or `v1.2.3-beta.4` gives the build name (`1.2.3`, `1.2.3-beta.4`) and the build number major·1,000,000 + minor·10,000 + patch·100 + n, with n the beta number (1–98) or 99 for the final release, so that every beta orders below its release and Play's limit of 2,100,000,000 holds. Any other tag fails, and so does a pubspec whose version name differs from the build name: the release commit bumps the pubspec.
    4. **Release signing from secrets, never committed:** one set of CI variables for the Play bundle and an optional one for the APK, so Q20 stays open; a local release build without keys keeps the debug key with a warning. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): Q20 is answered with option A, so the APK set holds the owner's app signing key.*
    5. **No product flavours:** one application id serves Play and the repository's APK. The original plan's flavours are dropped.
    6. **The source link from the build:** `sourceCodeUrl` comes from `--dart-define=SOURCE_CODE_URL`, which only the release job sets.
    7. **The release job on `v*` tags** builds, checks and publishes (below); a dry run does everything but the publication.
    8. **Cronet raised to a stable build, if the old artifact has one.** It has none (Context), so 37a keeps 143.0.7445.0 (M-2 below). *Note 2026-10-29: done in the Cronet move, `cronet-bundled` 500.1.0 with Chromium 155.0.8059.16 ([D67](#d67-agpl-30-or-later-with-an-app-store-permission-contributions-under-the-dco-the-name-and-the-logo-reserved-no-f-droid-the-legal-notices-on-an-about-page-and-the-licence-page-the-source-code-published-before-the-first-store-release), "Embedded Cronet").* *Checked on the emulator 2026-10-29 (Pixel_9_Pro_XL, an AOSP image without Play, so no system package offers HTTP flags there; the release build with Cronet's two log tags switched on): a guest project synced over Cronet, and logcat showed `cn_HttpFlagsLoader: Not loading HTTP flags because they are disabled in the manifest` and `name=App-Packaged-Cronet-Provider, version=155.0.8059.16, enabled=true`, no "Found application exporting HTTP flags", no "Cronet unavailable", no crash. The check on a Play image (threat model 8.2 item 10) stays for the release checks.*
    9. **The written offer's contact as one constant,** empty until the owner fills it; the release job fails while it is empty. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37c ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores)): the constant holds `coderoftime@gmail.com`, the contact of the owner's website, and `android.md` names it.*
    10. **GitHub Actions removed** once ported, and the docs updated.
  - **The spec editor's amendments (binding, 2026-10-29), also open in the review:**
    - One Linux image for every Flutter job, `ghcr.io/cirruslabs/android-sdk:36`, with Flutter downloaded from its tarball and checked against its SHA-256, instead of a Flutter image (none exists for 3.47) or an image of the project's own.
    - Every image and service pinned by digest and every download by version and SHA-256, the downloads in one sourced file, `tool/ci/pins.sh`. Caches under `.cache/` inside the project, because GitLab caches nothing outside it; `.cache/` is gitignored and excluded from the analyzer.
    - The checks and the tests in one job, which saves one Flutter setup per pipeline.
    - The contract job on docker-in-docker with the existing compose file, its published address a variable, and a forwarder to `localhost`.
    - The signing variables protected and scoped to an environment `release`, the `v*` tags protected; one `RURANA_SIGNING_*` set per build, chosen by the release script.
    - The release through GitLab's package registry and Releases API with `curl` and `CI_JOB_TOKEN`.
    - The desugar_jdk_libs source fetched by its commit, checked by its tree and attached to every release; the notice names the commit.
    - The contact constant in `tool/build_android_notices.py`, where the offer is generated.
  - **Taken by the main session (2026-10-29), also without the owner and open in the review** (the amendments' M-items, each with its default, and one departure from the amendments):
    - **Flutter 3.47.7 is pinned** (M-1), the version all of 37a's runs used, so that what the release job builds is what was tested. D1 and the README follow; 3.47.5's checksum stays in a comment of `pins.sh`. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): raised to Flutter 3.47.9 for the Dart security advisory of 3.47.8 ([D1](#d1-flutter-3475-pub-workspace-with-pure-dart-packages)'s note); 3.47.7's checksum stays in a comment of `pins.sh`.*
    - **Cronet stays at 143.0.7445.0 in 37a** (M-2). The move to `cronet-bundled` (Chromium 155.0.8059.16 today) needs its own change to the build and to the notices tool, which would read the licences from the AAR's `third_party_licenses.txt`, and is done as its own commit right after 37a and before the first `v*` tag, because the shipped network stack is about eleven months behind stable. *Note 2026-10-29: done (the Cronet move, decision 8's note).*
    - **Q20's default is option A** (M-3): one app signing key held by the owner and uploaded to Play with PEPK signs the repository's APK (the APK set), and a separate upload key signs the bundles (the upload set), so the two channels update each other; a leaked app signing key cannot be replaced for existing installs. Option B (Play generates the app signing key, a key of its own signs the APK, and the channels do not update each other) uses the same variables. The owner decides before the first tag. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): the owner chose option A. Play now enrols a new app in hybrid signing with keys Google generates; whether a key provided with PEPK gets hybrid signing too is not documented, and the owner checks it when creating the app.*
    - **GitLab's small shared runners with Gradle memory caps** (M-4); GitLab for Open Source applied for once the project is public; if the minutes run out, the owner's Mac as a runner for the heavy jobs, a follow-up not built in 37a (Q22). *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): Q22 is answered: every project of the group `coderoftime` becomes public, so that the group can join GitLab for Open Source; iOS stays on the Mac, by hand.*
    - **The contract job on docker-in-docker** (M-5), not only on a Mac runner.
    - **The contact constant in `build_android_notices.py`** (M-6), not in `legal.dart`; the owner supplies the value.
    - **The About page links `$CI_PROJECT_URL`** (M-7); the release's description links the tree at the tag.
    - **One implementer** in the amendments' order (M-8), the Cronet move left out.
    - **The target branch's current tip judges the sign-offs**, not the merge base's copy of `tool/check_dco.py` that the amendments' `dco.sh` used (`git show "$BASE:tool/check_dco.py"`, with the guarantee "the check that judges an MR comes from the target branch's merge base"). A merge request branched from an old commit would otherwise be judged by that commit's old check; the tip is what spec decision 2 asked for ("judged by the target branch's `tool/check_dco.py`"). A departure from the binding amendments, not one of their M-items.
  - **The pipeline** (`.gitlab-ci.yml`, plain YAML with anchors and `extends` only, because its test parses it with Ruby's YAML):
    - Workflow rules: a merge request event; the default branch; never a branch push while the branch has an open merge request; a tag matching `^v` (`release_version.py` then fails loudly on a malformed one); a schedule; a run by hand (`web`). `auto_cancel: on_new_commit: interruptible`, and every job is interruptible by default.
    - Stages `test` and `release`; every job but `release` starts at once (`needs: []`). The schedule runs `osv` only.
    - `checks` (90 minutes, an estimate for a 2-vCPU runner): `checks.sh`, with the generated code compared before and after `flutter gen-l10n` and `build_runner` (so uncommitted local edits do not fail it), `tool/check_format.sh`, `tool/check_pure_dart_packages.sh`, the Android notices' `--self-test` and `--check`, `desugar_source.py --check-manifest`, `flutter analyze`, the Python tests of `tool/ci/` and shellcheck when it is installed; then `test.sh`: the app, `vikunja_domain`, `vikunja_logic` with `test/time` again in Europe/Berlin, `vikunja_api` without the contract tests and `rurana_widgets`, on every CPU of the runner (on half of them locally); after the job, `outdated.sh` lists outdated dependencies for information only.
    - `editor` (`node:26.10.0-trixie-slim`, the owner's Node; `.nvmrc` stays `26`): `npm audit --package-lock-only --audit-level=high`, then `tool/build_editor.sh --check`.
    - `contract`: below.
    - `android`: the release APK with the debug key, as no signing variable reaches this job; the exported-components check after its self-test; then the widgets' Kotlin JVM tests, after the build, which writes `gradlew`, its jar and `local.properties`.
    - `dco`, merge requests only, with the full history (`GIT_DEPTH: "0"`): below.
    - `osv`, in every pipeline including the schedule and not interruptible: `osv-scanner scan source --config=osv-scanner.toml` over `pubspec.lock`, the editor's `package-lock.json` and the Gradle lockfile; the scanner from PATH, else v2.7.0 downloaded for Linux amd64 or macOS arm64 and checked by its SHA-256.
    - `ios`, on a runner tagged `macos` without an image: `flutter build ios --release --no-codesign`, with `DEVELOPER_DIR` set when `xcode-select` points to the Command Line Tools (expected not to be enough, Known gaps). It runs only while the project variable `RURANA_MACOS_RUNNER` is `true`, only on protected refs (the main branch and `v*` tags; a rule on `CI_COMMIT_REF_PROTECTED`, added by the main session so that a merge request's code never runs on the owner's Mac), never on the schedule, and then counts (`allow_failure: false`). While the Mac is away the variable has to be unset: GitLab cannot tell an absent runner from a busy one, so a pending job holds the pipeline open for up to a day and then fails it. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): `ios.sh` refuses to run unless `xcode-select` points to Xcode (`use_selected_xcode` in `lib.sh`) instead of setting `DEVELOPER_DIR`, and checks the build's licence notices with `tool/ci/ios_notices.py`.*
    - `release`, on tags only, after `checks`, `editor`, `contract`, `android` and `osv` (90 minutes): `environment: release`, `resource_group: release`, not interruptible, and no cache, so Flutter comes from its checked download and the packages from their sources, never from a cache another pipeline wrote. Its artifacts are the bundle and `SHA256SUMS`, for members with the Developer role, for 30 days.
    - Caches: Flutter keyed by `pins.sh`, pub by `pubspec.lock`, Gradle by its lockfile and the wrapper's properties, npm by the editor's lockfile; `PUB_CACHE`, `GRADLE_USER_HOME` and `npm_config_cache` point into `.cache/`. On GitLab's Linux runners `lib.sh` writes `$GRADLE_USER_HOME/gradle.properties` with a 3 GB heap, a 1 GB Kotlin daemon and two workers; it never touches `~/.gradle` on a developer's machine.
  - **Flutter in the scripts** (`tool/ci/lib.sh`, `setup_flutter`): on GitLab's Linux runners the SDK of `pins.sh` from its tarball, checked against its SHA-256 and cached under `.cache/flutter/`; everywhere else, the owner's Mac and a Mac runner included, the `flutter` on PATH, with a warning for another version, which is an error when `CI=true`. Then `flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile`, and with `--codegen` the app's generated code written again.
  - **Pins** (`tool/ci/pins.sh`, sourced, values only): Flutter 3.47.7 (`af7455f5…`); osv-scanner 2.7.0 for Linux amd64 and macOS arm64, checked against GitHub's asset digests and the release's `SHA256SUMS`; the Docker CLI 29.9.2, whose hash was computed from the download on 2026-10-29 because Docker publishes no checksum for its static archives; Compose 5.6.1. Images, in `.gitlab-ci.yml`: `ghcr.io/cirruslabs/android-sdk:36@sha256:f9b3ea9e…` for every Linux job but the editor's, `node:26.10.0-trixie-slim@sha256:930557a2…`, and the service `docker:29.9.2-dind@sha256:9e79b879…`, whose daemon pulls the test server's image `vikunja/vikunja:2.7.0` (pinned by tag only) through Google's registry mirror. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): Flutter 3.47.9 (`25ed8c3c…`) and the test server `vikunja/vikunja:2.7.1`.*
  - **The contract job:** `test_servers/docker-compose.yaml` publishes the 2.7.0 server on `${RURANA_TEST_PUBLISH_ADDRESS:-127.0.0.1}`, and the job sets `0.0.0.0` inside its own docker-in-docker daemon (TLS on port 2376). Only there `contract.sh` installs the pinned Docker CLI and Compose plugin and `socat`, waits up to 60 s for the daemon, and forwards `127.0.0.1:34527` and `[::1]:34527` to the service, so that the tests, the server's public URL and `tool/wait_for_test_servers.sh` keep `localhost`. Locally (colima) it uses `docker compose` or `docker-compose`, installs nothing and leaves the servers running, as `tool/contract_tests.sh` does. A failure prints `compose ps --all` and the servers' logs. It runs the `vikunja_api` contract tests, then the app's in Europe/Berlin, one file at a time (`--concurrency=1`): the servers keep their data in SQLite, which answered the writes of parallel files with "database is locked" (500).
  - **The DCO job:** `dco.sh BASE HEAD` runs the merge request's own `tool/check_dco.py --self-test`, then the target branch's current copy of the script (`CI_MERGE_REQUEST_TARGET_BRANCH_SHA` in a merged results pipeline, else the branch's tip, fetched with its history) over the commits from the merge base (`CI_MERGE_REQUEST_DIFF_BASE_SHA`) to the source branch's head. A merge request can neither change the check that judges it nor pick an older one by branching from an older commit. Changes to `tool/ci/dco.sh` or to `.gitlab-ci.yml` show in the merge request's diff, and the reviewer must notice them. The pipelines of a fork's merge request run in the fork with the fork's configuration unless a maintainer runs them in the parent project. Locally, BASE's copy judges.
  - **Release signing** (`app/android/app/build.gradle.kts`): all four of `RURANA_SIGNING_STORE_FILE`, `RURANA_SIGNING_STORE_PASSWORD`, `RURANA_SIGNING_KEY_ALIAS` and `RURANA_SIGNING_KEY_PASSWORD` when any of them is set; else all four keys of `app/android/key.properties` (`storeFile`, `storePassword`, `keyAlias`, `keyPassword`; a relative `storeFile` is relative to `app/android/`; ignored by git); else the debug key, with a warning when a release APK or bundle is packaged. A partial set fails the configuration and names the missing keys, never a value. There is one `release` signing config and no sniffing of task names: the release script chooses each build's key.
    - The CI variables: `RURANA_UPLOAD_KEYSTORE_BASE64`, `RURANA_UPLOAD_STORE_PASSWORD`, `RURANA_UPLOAD_KEY_ALIAS` and `RURANA_UPLOAD_KEY_PASSWORD` are required; the four `RURANA_APK_*` are optional, all or none. Without them the upload key signs the APK too, with a boxed warning in the job's log and a note in the release's description. All are protected and scoped to the environment `release`, the keystores and passwords "Masked and hidden"; PKCS12 keystores take one password, so the key password equals the store password ([releasing.md](releasing.md)).
    - `release.sh` copies the variables into unexported shell variables and unsets them, so no step but the build a set signs sees them. It decodes a keystore into a `mktemp -d` folder (umask 077) only for that build and removes it by traps; passwords reach `keytool` and Gradle through the environment, never through the command line or the log. Before the long builds it checks that each set opens its keystore and reads its certificate's SHA-256.
  - **The release job** (`tool/ci/release.sh [--tag TAG] [--dry-run]`):
    1. The version from the tag (decision 3).
    2. Guards: `CI_PROJECT_URL` is an https URL; `CI_PROJECT_VISIBILITY` is `public`, and anonymous reads of the repository at the tag, of the releases and of the package registry answer 200, because the source link and the release's files must open for everyone (AGPL section 6); the written offer has a contact (`build_android_notices.py --require-offer-contact`); the upload set is complete and the APK set complete or absent; neither the release nor the package `rurana/<name>` exists yet.
    3. The release-only source-link test with the define, then the notices' self-test and check.
    4. `flutter build appbundle --release` with the upload set, and `flutter build apk --release`, one universal APK, with the APK set or else the upload set; both with `--build-name`, `--build-number` and `--dart-define=SOURCE_CODE_URL=$CI_PROJECT_URL`. Release builds never use `--split-per-abi`, which would change the version codes.
    5. The exported-components check of that build.
    6. Checks: the APK has a v2 signature by exactly one signer, whose certificate's SHA-256 is the APK set's (`apksigner`); the bundle passes `jarsigner -verify`, and its certificate is the upload set's (`keytool -printcert -jarfile`); neither is signed by `CN=Android Debug`; the APK's `versionCode` and `versionName` are the tag's (`aapt2 dump badging`).
    7. Into `build/release/`: `rurana-<name>.apk`, `app-release.aab` and `desugar_jdk_libs-2.1.4-src.tar.gz`, with `SHA256SUMS` for the APK and the source archive; the bundle's hash goes into the log.
    8. Publication: the three files into the generic package `rurana/<name>`, then the release of the tag through the Releases API, its JSON built by Python. Its name is `Rurana <name>`, with " (beta)" for a beta, as GitLab has no pre-release flag; its description gives the APK's SHA-256, the SHA-256 of the APK's signing certificate, the source link to the tree at the tag and the note on the desugar_jdk_libs source; it links the three files. A failure after the first upload leaves a package without a release, which has to be deleted before a retry.
    9. The bundle reaches Play only by hand, from the job's artifacts (37d); there is no Play API upload.
    - `--dry-run` runs all of it locally with two throwaway PKCS12 keys (RSA 2048, valid for one day, their generated password only in the environment), the source URL `https://gitlab.example/rurana` and, by default, the tag `v<the pubspec's version name>`. The visibility and contact guards become warnings; it prints the uploads and the release's JSON instead of sending them and ends with `DRY RUN: NOT RELEASABLE (<reasons>)`.
  - **The source link** (`app/lib/app/legal.dart`): `sourceCodeUrl` is `String.fromEnvironment('SOURCE_CODE_URL')`, null when empty. Local builds and CI's test builds show no link, as before; the About page is unchanged. The release-only test passes with the define, fails without it, and names the define in its reason.
  - **The written offer** (`tool/build_android_notices.py`): `WRITTEN_OFFER_CONTACT = ''`. While it is empty the offer ends as before ("Ask through the contact given where the app's source code is published."); once set it ends "Ask Benedikt Lesch at <contact>." `--require-offer-contact` fails while it is empty. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37c ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores)): `WRITTEN_OFFER_CONTACT = 'coderoftime@gmail.com'`, so the offer ends "Ask Benedikt Lesch at coderoftime@gmail.com." and `--require-offer-contact` passes; the texts are unchanged.*
  - **The desugar_jdk_libs source:** the manifest `tool/android_notices/desugar_jdk_libs.source` holds the version, the repository, the commit, the tree and a note. `tool/ci/desugar_source.py --check-manifest` (in `checks`, offline) fails when its version is not the one `build.gradle.kts` desugars with; `--fetch` (in the release) fetches the commit alone with depth 1, checks its commit and tree ids and its `VERSION_JDK11.txt`, and writes the commit's `git archive`. The archive's hash can change with the git version (git 2.56.0 gave 18,229,919 bytes, `da814226…`), so the commit and the tree are checked and the hash is an output in `SHA256SUMS`. About 18 MB go with every release. The notice now says that the source is at that commit, that upstream has no tag for 2.1.4, and that a copy is attached to every release of the app; `android.md` was written again.
  - **Removed:** `.github/` (three workflows and the composite action) and `app/test/ci/workflows_test.dart`, whose checks moved to `tool/ci/test_gitlab_ci.py`.
  - **Other files:** `.gitignore` also ignores `*.p12`, `*.pfx`, `*.pepk`, `*.keystore.b64` and `.cache/`; `analysis_options.yaml` excludes `.cache/**`, checked with a throwaway file with an error there; the comment of `osv-scanner.toml` names `tool/ci/osv.sh`.
  - **No new dependency** of the app, the packages or the plugin.
  - **The parked session hardening:** none of the six auth files of the package reserved as D59 changed.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Product flavours** for Play and the repository (decision 5): one application id serves both, and Q20 decides the keys.
  - **A Flutter image** (`ghcr.io/cirruslabs/flutter` has no 3.47 tag) and **an image of the project's own**, which needs a job that builds it and puts several GB against the namespace's storage.
  - **The osv-scanner image:** a checked binary needs no second image and runs the same on the Mac.
  - **A Compose `!override` file** for the contract job: interpolation in the existing file works with the standalone `docker-compose` too and needs no Compose 2.24.4.
  - **`release-cli`:** the APIs with `curl` need no other tool.
  - **Judging the sign-offs with the check at `CI_MERGE_REQUEST_DIFF_BASE_SHA`** (the merge base), the closest GitLab variable to the GitHub workflow's base commit, which was the target branch's tip: a merge request branched from an old commit would be judged by the old check.
  - **The `prepare` action for the release environment:** with the default action the environment-scoped variables are certainly exposed, and every release leaves a deployment record.
  - **Only a link to the upstream commit of desugar_jdk_libs:** a copy beside each release stays available whatever happens upstream, and each release is complete on its own.
  - **`--split-per-abi`:** one universal APK keeps one version code per tag.
  - **A Play API upload in this step:** 37d uploads by hand first.
  - The M-items' alternatives: **keeping Flutter 3.47.5** (M-1); **shipping the beta on Cronet 143** and moving before 1.0.0 (M-2); **the contract tests only on a Mac runner** (M-5); **the contact in `legal.dart`** (M-6), while the offer appears only in the generated notices; **the tag's URL in the app** (M-7).
- **Consequences:**
  - On GitLab a merge request runs `checks`, `editor`, `contract`, `android`, `osv` and `dco`; a push to main and a run by hand the first five; a tag such as `v1.0.0` or `v1.0.0-beta.1` the five and `release`; the weekly schedule `osv` only.
