With `RURANA_MACOS_RUNNER=true`, the pipelines on main and on `v*` tags add `ios`. A branch without a merge request and a tag such as `foo` run no pipeline.
  - Every build that is distributed comes from the release job of a protected tag: signed with the keys of protected, environment-scoped variables and checked for them, its version from the tag, its source link set, its notices checked, the offer's contact required and the project public. A local release build without keys still gets the debug key, with a warning. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): this holds for Android; the iOS build of a tag is signed on the owner's Mac and uploaded to App Store Connect by hand (`tool/ci/ios_release.sh`).*
  - The threat model's R26 and R28 are updated for GitLab, R34 records the stale Cronet build, and R35 the code a Mac runner runs on the owner's Mac (the job `ios` only on the protected main branch and `v*` tags, never in a merge request pipeline) ([threat-model.md](threat-model.md)). [releasing.md](releasing.md) is the release checklist.
  - Known gaps:
    - Nothing ran on GitLab, where no project exists. The Linux-only paths were not run: the Flutter tarball, the Docker CLI and Compose for docker-in-docker with the forwarder, the osv-scanner Linux binary and the Gradle memory caps. `ios.sh` was not run, and its `DEVELOPER_DIR` fallback is expected not to be enough: the README says the Dart native-asset build hooks ignore it, so a Mac runner needs `xcode-select` switched to Xcode ([releasing.md](releasing.md)). *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): `ios.sh` ran locally with Xcode 27.1, and the fallback is replaced by the refusal.* shellcheck is not installed, so `checks.sh` skipped it. The job timeouts are estimates.
    - **Compute minutes** (Q22): a full pipeline is estimated at about 100–150 compute minutes on GitLab's small shared runners, so GitLab.com's free 400 minutes a month cover only 3 or 4 pipelines. CI can run regularly only with GitLab for Open Source or with the owner's Mac as a runner for the heavy jobs, which 37a does not set up; the Mac runner of the job `ios` saves no shared-runner minutes. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): the minutes count against the top-level group, so the free 400 are shared by every project of `coderoftime`, and each beta costs two full pipelines, the push to `main` and the tag. Q22 is answered with GitLab for Open Source for the whole group.*
    - **Cronet** stays the pre-stable trunk build 143.0.7445.0 until its own commit before the first tag (R34). *Note 2026-10-29, the Cronet move: closed. Cronet is `cronet-bundled` 500.1.0, Chromium 155.0.8059.16, a Chrome stable release; it is still behind the newest stable, which R34 now records with the cadence for raising it. Decided in the move without the owner and open in its review: the transitional Cronet artifacts excluded from every configuration; `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` declared by the app; cronet_http kept at 1.10.0; `--refresh-cronet` checking the AARs against Google's Maven and the build against Chromium Dash instead of comparing the bucket's jars, with the bucket's `LICENSE` only as a cross-check; the OSV acceptance of protobuf-javalite removed with the library.*
    - The iOS build name cannot carry `-beta.N` (`CFBundleShortVersionString` takes numbers only); 37b maps it. *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): done: `release_version.py` also prints `IOS_BUILD_NAME`, the tag's `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, and `IOS_BUILD_NUMBER`, the Android build number.*
    - Without the `RURANA_APK_*` set the upload key signs the public APK too, and sideloaded installs stay bound to it.
    - GitLab has no pre-release flag: a beta becomes the project's latest release, so links point to a specific release, never to `permalink/latest`.
    - "Keep artifacts from most recent successful jobs", a project setting, keeps the latest bundle beyond its 30 days.
    - The Docker CLI's hash was taken on first use; the CI image dates from 2025-12-24 and has no NDK, so the `android` and `release` jobs are expected to install NDK 28.2.13676358 each time, in about a minute (an estimate, not yet seen on GitLab), from Google's SDK repository without a pin of the project; the contract job's `socat` comes from Ubuntu's archive, unpinned. Anonymous pulls from Docker Hub may run into its limits; the remedy, not built, is a read-only Docker Hub token in masked variables `DOCKER_HUB_USER` and `DOCKER_HUB_TOKEN` and a `docker login` in `tool/ci/contract.sh`.
  - **The owner's to-dos before the first tag** ([releasing.md](releasing.md)): create the GitLab project; decide Q20 and set the protected signing variables (without an APK set the APK falls back to the upload key, with a warning); fill in `WRITTEN_OFFER_CONTACT` (*filled on 2026-11-02, D76: `coderoftime@gmail.com`*); decide Q22; publish the project, as the release job refuses a project that is not public. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): the project exists, private, since 2026-10-29; Q20 and Q22 are answered; [beta.md](beta.md) has the order of the owner's steps.*
  - Not built: the Cronet move (*done 2026-10-29, the Cronet move*); a Play upload through the API; a Mac runner for the heavy jobs; a Docker Hub login; Dependabot or Renovate; Gradle's dependency verification metadata; the iOS release (37b). *Note 2026-10-29, step 37b ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)): built as `tool/ci/ios_release.sh`, which the owner runs by hand on the Mac.*
  - Open in the review besides decisions 1 to 10: the amendments and the main session's choices, first Flutter 3.47.7, the Cronet move before the first tag, Q20's default and the target branch's check judging sign-offs.
  - Tests: `tool/ci/test_gitlab_ci.py` (13: the jobs of each kind of pipeline with and without a Mac runner, from a small evaluator of GitLab's rule expressions with a test of its own; a GitLab job for every GitHub job; the scripts it names exist and are executable, and the files they call exist; the scan covers the three lockfiles; images and services pinned by digest; cache keys and paths; the Linux jobs' caches inside the project; the test jobs starting at once; the DCO and release jobs; interruptible); `tool/ci/test_release_version.py` (7: betas, finals, the order across beta.98, the release, the next patch's beta and 1.99.99 against 2.0.0-beta.1, 21 bad tags, the pubspec's version line, a mismatch, the command); `tool/ci/test_desugar_source.py` (6: the committed manifest against the build, parsing, a mismatched version, bad manifests, a fetch from a local repository into an archive, another tree or version refused); the notices' self-test (the commit in the note, the offer with and without a contact, a manifest of another version, a note without a manifest and a manifest without a commit refused); the release-only test's reasons. 26 Python tests in all. 8,130 app tests pass and 213 are skipped (contract, Docker and release-only tests), 13 fewer than at 36e's commit because the Dart test of the GitHub workflows moved to Python; the packages' tests, the Gradle unit tests, analyze and format pass.
  - Checked locally on 2026-10-29 (macOS, Flutter 3.47.7): `checks.sh`, `test.sh`, `editor.sh`, `osv.sh`, `dco.sh`, `android.sh` with the Gradle unit tests, and one release build; the release job's guards against a local fake of GitLab's API; the release dry run, with the bundle and the APK each signed with its own throwaway key and verified against it, versionName 0.1.0 and versionCode 10,099 from the version scheme, `SHA256SUMS`, the desugar_jdk_libs source fetched by its commit and verified, the source URL in the build, and the end "DRY RUN: NOT RELEASABLE …". The contract job ran with colima against the long-running test servers. The first run had 3 app contract failures, a realtime timeout and two 500s, where the servers' SQLite answered "database is locked" under parallel test files; all three passed alone, so `contract.sh` now runs the app's contract files one at a time. The second full run, with the app's contract files one at a time, passed: 353 contract tests of `vikunja_api` and 198 of the app (about 25 minutes in all, the app's part about 13). Nothing ran on GitLab or on iOS.

## D74: The iOS phase: privacy manifests and the system SQLite on iOS, TestFlight from the owner's Mac, the iPad and iPhone checked in the simulator, photo locations stripped, one-time secrets covered while the screen is captured, the share extension after 1.0

*Accepted 2026-10-29, step 37b (Xcode 27.1; the simulator: iPhone 17 Pro and iPad Pro 13-inch (M5) on iOS 26.5, iPhone 16 on iOS 18.6; the Android test suite and one Android release build; no device, no Apple account and nothing on GitLab). The owner accepted 37a with the Cronet move on 2026-10-29 and asked for 37b, the second sub-step of step 37 as the owner split it in [D72](#d72-first-release-android-and-ios-together-on-google-play-with-an-apk-in-every-gitlab-release-the-repository-on-gitlab-version-100-after-a-beta) ([roadmap](roadmap.md)): the iOS app built and run as a whole for the first time. Decisions 1 to 4 below are the owner's, taken during the step; everything else was decided without the owner and is open in the review. Carries out what [D73](#d73-gitlab-ci-from-scripts-that-run-the-same-locally-images-and-downloads-pinned-the-version-from-the-release-tag-release-signing-from-protected-ci-variables-the-source-link-from-the-build-a-gitlab-release-with-the-apk-its-checksums-and-the-desugar_jdk_libs-source) left to 37b, the iOS version from the tag and the iOS release, which runs by hand on the owner's Mac, and replaces its `DEVELOPER_DIR` fallback with a refusal, with dated notes there. Puts the iOS share extension of [D53](#d53-attachments-uploaded-through-the-outbox-in-their-own-lane-opened-shared-and-saved-through-the-platform-cover-and-delete-online-and-an-own-android-share-target) and [D62](#d62-create-account-password-reset-and-invite-links-through-the-browser-sign-in-page-link-share-guests-as-an-account-kind-with-token-renewal-and-a-reduced-ui) after 1.0; covers the one-time secrets of [D55](#d55-security-and-integration-settings-two-factor-status-sessions-and-tokens-in-the-app-a-new-token-shown-once-export-and-deletion-through-the-web) on iOS while the screen is captured, where [D64](#d64-an-app-lock-over-the-ui-not-encryption-with-biometrics-or-the-devices-screen-lock-device-privacy-settings-for-the-app-switcher-screenshots-notification-content-and-widget-titles) had nothing; closes [D63](#d63-home-screen-widgets-from-json-snapshots-the-app-writes-through-an-in-repo-plugin-glance-widgets-on-android-with-done-through-a-headless-dart-worker-a-widgetkit-extension-on-ios-with-an-app-group-outbox-and-no-token)'s bare number; keeps [D69](#d69-large-screens-one-window-layout-helper-readable-widths-of-840-dp-for-lists-and-640-dp-for-settings-and-forms-with-scrolling-margins-dialogs-at-most-560-dp-and-ios-sheets-at-most-640-dp-only-on-tablet-sized-windows)'s cards on the iPad, whose fallback is not needed, and adds a tap beside them; checks [D66](#d66-a-threat-model-uncaught-errors-redacted-and-never-printed-in-release-builds-the-apps-data-out-of-backups-and-device-transfer-routes-pushed-by-the-platform-taken-only-as-app-scheme-links-widget-links-and-notification-taps-switching-accounts-only-with-a-per-install-key-exported-components-and-dependencies-checked-in-ci)'s backup marks; and loads the system's SQLite on iOS instead of the build [D31](#d31-one-drift-sqlite-file-per-account-shared-across-isolates-deleted-on-sign-out) bundles. Steps 18 and 19 stay in review: the Tasks widget and its Done ran in the simulator, and the device test is still to come. Follows [D68](#d68-screen-readers-heading-levels-on-screen-titles-and-section-headers-one-announcer-with-the-apps-own-live-region-on-android-high-contrast-and-reduce-motion-followed-and-a-route-sweep-that-every-route-pushed-page-and-overlay-must-pass-wcag-22-aa-as-the-yardstick-with-written-deviations) and the [glossary](glossary.md) for the new texts, and keeps [D2](#d2-material_ui-and-cupertino_ui).*

- **Context:**
  - iOS was built along the way but, by the owner's rule ([D72](#d72-first-release-android-and-ios-together-on-google-play-with-an-apk-in-every-gitlab-release-the-repository-on-gitlab-version-100-after-a-beta)), run only now. A sweep of the docs for everything deferred to "the iOS phase" merged 107 items into 79 and sorted them into builds, simulator checks, device checks, work in the owner's Apple account and doc changes. The Swift of 35a to 36e already compiled with Xcode 27.1: a simulator debug build with the widget extension, and an unsigned release archive.
  - Three specs and the main session's checks shaped the result: the App Store prerequisites (one implementer); the three fixes of the main session's iPad pass (one implementer); the owner's four answers and the build items of the sweep (two implementers, one for the app's code and one for the tooling and the extension); then the checks in the simulator on three devices.
  - Verified facts (2026-10-29, from the code, the simulator and Apple's documentation):
    - **App Store Connect** refuses, since 1 May 2024, an app that uses a required-reason API without declaring it, and a build refused in processing still uses up its build number. The SQLite that sqlite3's build hook bundles imports `statfs` and `fstatfs`, disk-space APIs, to choose its locking, which none of Apple's reasons describes, and Flutter has no way yet to give a hook's framework a privacy manifest ([releasing.md](releasing.md#privacy-manifests)). `CFBundleShortVersionString` takes only up to three numbers, so a beta's build name cannot be one.
    - **The iPad** (iPad Pro 13-inch simulator, iOS 26.5): the layouts of 36b and 36c held, but a tap beside a sheet's card did nothing, because the route of `showCupertinoSheet` never closes on a tap on its barrier; after a Cupertino sheet closed, a page without an app bar kept the light status bar text of the sheet's top gap, as Flutter keeps the last style when it finds none (seen on the iPhone too); and the hint under "Hide on the lock screen" said "On iPhone".
    - **Attachments:** a photo picked through Photos was uploaded with a full GPS IFD (15 entries; the picker says "Location Included"), since `requestFullMetadata: false` only spares the library permission. A pick from the library was named after image_picker's temporary copy (`image_picker_<UUID>…jpg`). "Save Image" in the share sheet asked for full photo library access with the picking text, because `NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription` was missing.
    - **Screen protection:** iOS has no documented way to keep a window out of screenshots, recordings or a mirrored display; it only says whether the screen is captured (the scene's capture-state trait from iOS 17, `UIScreen.isCaptured` before). `PlatformSecureWindow.setSecure` did nothing off Android.
    - **The Mac:** the Dart native-asset build hooks ignore `DEVELOPER_DIR`, so D73's fallback in `ios.sh` could not work; only `xcode-select` pointing to Xcode does.
    - **The widget extension** read a bare number where a count had no label from Dart (after a Done tap hid a row), and `+%lld more` was no plural. At AX5 the inbox bell's badge was cut off by the screen's edge.
- **Decision:**
  - **Taken by the owner (2026-10-29, during the step):**
    1. **The iOS share extension ("Share to Rurana") comes after 1.0.** 1.0 on iOS takes files only through the in-app pickers.
    2. **One-time secrets on iOS are covered while the screen is captured** (a recording, AirPlay or a mirrored display). Screenshots stay possible; no undocumented secure-field trick.
    3. **Photo locations are stripped on iOS before upload,** as Android's photo picker redacts them. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37c ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores)): by the owner's decision Android removes them too, from photos and videos of the photo picker, the camera and shares from other apps; D76 says how and which files keep their location.*
    4. **No Time Sensitive notifications.** Reminders keep the interruption level `active`.
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-10-29), open in the review:**
    - **Privacy manifests for both targets** (`Runner/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` and `RuranaWidgets/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy`, in each target's resources through `tool/add_widget_extension.rb`): no tracking, no tracking domains and no collected data, since data leaves the device only for the server the user chose, never for the developer or a third party. Required-reason APIs: file timestamp `C617.1` and user defaults `CA92.1` for the app, file timestamp `C617.1` for the extension; the plugins' own manifests are listed in [releasing.md](releasing.md#privacy-manifests). `tool/ci/required_reason_apis.py` checks every binary of the archive (`nm -u`, and the listed Objective-C selectors in the bytes) against the manifests of the bundle that holds it.
    - **`ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption` false** in the app's `Info.plist`: the app uses only standard encryption (HTTPS, TLS with pinning, PKCE), which is exempt, so App Store Connect asks no encryption question for each build.
    - **The system SQLite on iOS:** the workspace's `pubspec.yaml` has sqlite3's hook load `/usr/lib/libsqlite3.dylib` on iOS (`hooks.user_defines.sqlite3`, by its full path); Android keeps the bundled build. A system library needs no manifest. The app's SQL needs SQLite 3.30 (`NULLS LAST`; upserts 3.24), and iOS 15, the oldest the app supports, ships 3.36; the app uses no `RETURNING`, FTS or JSON functions. In the simulator the app opened its existing databases on iOS 26.5 and created new ones on 18.6.
    - **iOS versions from the tag:** `release_version.py` also prints `IOS_BUILD_NAME`, the tag's `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, for `CFBundleShortVersionString`, and `IOS_BUILD_NUMBER`, the Android build number, for `CFBundleVersion`, so the betas of 1.0.0 are 1.0.0 with growing build numbers.
    - **TestFlight from the owner's Mac** (`tool/ci/ios_release.sh`, run by hand; no GitLab job for iOS): it checks that Xcode is selected, Flutter is the pinned one, HEAD is the tag with a clean tree, the source link opens signed out, the team id and the key variables are set and the 1024 icon is sRGB without an alpha channel; runs the release-only source-link test; builds with `flutter build ipa --release`, the tag's version, `SOURCE_CODE_URL` and export options for automatic signing with the team in `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM`; checks the archive (both bundles at the tag's version, the plist key, the committed manifests, the required-reason APIs, the **licence notices** through `tool/ci/ios_notices.py`); and uploads its one IPA with `xcrun altool --upload-package` and an App Store Connect API key from `RURANA_ASC_KEY_ID`, `RURANA_ASC_ISSUER_ID` and `RURANA_ASC_KEY_FILE`, which leave the environment before Flutter and Xcode run. `--dry-run` builds unsigned and stops before the export and the upload ([releasing.md](releasing.md#ios-testflight-from-the-mac)).
    - **Xcode, or no build:** `tool/ci/ios.sh` and `ios_release.sh` refuse to run unless `xcode-select` points into an Xcode app (`use_selected_xcode` in `tool/ci/lib.sh`, which also unsets `DEVELOPER_DIR` and prints Xcode's version), in place of D73's `DEVELOPER_DIR` fallback. The CI job `ios` now also runs `ios_notices.py` on its build.
    - **The licence notices of the iOS build** (`tool/ci/ios_notices.py`): Flutter skips a package without a licence file silently, and the licence page leaves out an asset it cannot read, so the script fails unless every licence in each package's `NOTICES` or `LICENSE` is in the build's `NOTICES.Z` (a package without one must be the repository's or the Flutter SDK's), the repository's licence is listed under the name the app's own notice joins, the only frameworks are Flutter's, `App.framework` and the native assets of `NativeAssetsManifest.json`, and every legal asset of `app_licenses.dart` is bundled as in the source. It does not see Swift packages fetched by URL; no plugin uses one.
    - **A tap beside the iPad's sheet card closes the sheet** (`ReadableSheetWidth.closesOnTapBeside`, set by `showReadableCupertinoSheet`): the areas beside the card pop the route through `Navigator.maybePop`, so a `PopScope` in the sheet keeps its guard, and they are no stop for a screen reader, like a Cupertino popup's barrier. Bottom popups leave it off, as a tap beside their card reaches their own barrier; on phones nothing is beside the sheet.
    - **A system bar fallback for the whole app** (`systemBarStyleOn` in `app/theme/system_bar_style.dart`, `_SystemBarFallback` in `vikunja_app.dart`): a region around the app gives a page without an app bar or a region of its own a transparent status bar whose text reads on the theme's surface, and Android's navigation bar in the theme's `surfaceContainer`, the colour of the bottom bar, with icons that read on it. Flutter applies the region in every frame, so both bars follow the theme when the system or the account's colour scheme changes while the app runs. Before, the status bar kept the light text of a Cupertino sheet's top gap after the sheet closed, and the navigation bar kept the theme the app started in. Flutter sets the navigation bar from Android 8 on, its colour up to Android 14; below Android 8 it keeps the theme's black. The Android 8.1+ themes (`values-v27`, `values-night-v27`) name a transparent navigation bar colour and the icons for the light or the dark launch: on Android 15 and later, without a colour in the theme, Android forces light icons after the window's light background (`APPEARANCE_FORCE_LIGHT_NAVIGATION_BARS`), in dark too, and Flutter no longer sets the colour that would lift it. App bars and the lock cover still decide the status bar where they are; the lock screen and the privacy cover give the navigation bar their own background and icons too, as Flutter applies the last style again when the app returns and below Android 11 that clears dark icons it does not name. The Overview's own region is gone.
    - **Photo locations** (decision 3; the device channel's `removeLocation` in `AppDelegate.swift`, `core/platform/location_removal.dart`, `plugin_attachment_picker.dart`): on iOS every photo and video from `pickPhotos` and `takePhoto` loses its location before the picker hands it on (decision 3 names photos; videos were added in the step), for attachments, the description editor's Image button and the avatar, which the app encodes as a PNG anyway. It happens in place and off the main thread. An image is written again with its metadata minus the GPS tags, in EXIF and in XMP, without encoding a JPEG or a HEIC again (a PNG, which loses nothing, is encoded again), and read back to check that no location is left. A video goes through a passthrough export with `metadataItemFilter = .forSharing()`, checked the same way. A file without a location stays untouched; a failure leaves the file as it was and is logged by its type only. Files from the Files picker are not touched, as on Android, whose document picker leaves them too.
    - **Names of library picks on iOS** (`iosLibraryFileNames`): a photo in image_picker's temporary copy is named like a camera shot, `IMG_<yyyyMMdd_HHmmss>.<ext>` at the time of the pick, numbered `_1`, `_2` when one pick brings several; a video keeps the name the picker puts after its temporary prefix; any other name stays.
    - **`NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription`** in English and German ("Rurana saves attachments you choose to your photos.", „Rurana speichert Anhänge, die du auswählst, in deinen Fotos.“): without it, "Save Image" asked for full photo library access with the picking text.
    - **The capture cover** (decision 2): on iOS `SecureWindow.setSecure` answers whether the screen is captured and reports each change as `screenCaptureChanged` (the device channel's `ScreenCapture` watches the scene's capture-state trait on iOS 17 and later, `UIScreen.isCaptured` below); `SecureDisplay` tells its listeners `hidesSecrets` and `screenCaptured`. While the screen is captured, the one-time token view shows "Hidden while your screen is being recorded or mirrored." („Ausgeblendet, solange dein Bildschirm aufgenommen oder gespiegelt wird.“) in place of the token, and Copy still works; a revealed `SecretTextField` masks itself, with that text under it. A secret also hides whenever the app leaves `resumed`, and shows again only after a fresh answer on the return, which covers the app switcher's snapshot and a capture that started while the app was away; until iOS has answered, the token keeps its space but is invisible. A channel that fails shows the secret, as a token shown only once must not be lost. Android keeps `FLAG_SECURE` and is unchanged.
    - **The widget extension's counts:** `Localizable.stringsdict` in English and German for `%lld tasks` and `+%lld more`, which the extension speaks where Dart sent no count label (`ListContent.spokenCount`) and shows under the rows; `tool/add_widget_extension.rb` adds them to the target. This closes D63's bare number.
    - **The inbox bell's badge** grows with the text only up to 1.3×, like the tab bar's labels (D68); the button's label speaks the count.
    - **The iOS hint under "Hide on the lock screen"** no longer says "On iPhone", as it shows on the iPad too.
    - **The iPad key menu moves to 36d** with the shortcuts on every platform; 36d's row had left it to this phase. The app has no keyboard shortcuts yet (no `Shortcuts`, `SingleActivator` or `PlatformMenuBar`), so a menu would have nothing to list before 36d.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **`kCGImageMetadataShouldExcludeGPS` alone:** a JPEG's copy lost its TIFF directory and with it the orientation, and HEIC and PNG copies kept their GPS.
  - **An undocumented secure text field over the secrets** to keep them out of screenshots (decision 2).
  - **A native cover over the window** while the screen is captured: the placeholder in the secrets' widgets keeps one path with Android's `SecureDisplay`, and widget tests cover it.
  - **The bundled SQLite with a manifest of its own:** Flutter cannot give a hook's framework one yet, and no disk-space reason fits SQLite.
  - **`DEVELOPER_DIR` when `xcode-select` points to the Command Line Tools** (D73): the native-asset hooks ignore it.
  - **A GitLab job for the iOS release, or the Mac runner on a tag:** it would need the team's signing and the API key in the runner's macOS account, which is meant not to hold them (threat model R35).
  - **A native `SceneDelegate` cover for the app switcher** (D64's fallback): the Dart cover showed in the simulator's app switcher.
  - **Material sheets on the iPad** (D69's fallback): the centred cards looked right; they stay, with the tap beside them.
- **Consequences:**
  - On iOS the app has its App Store prerequisites and a way to TestFlight from the owner's Mac. A photo or video from the photo picker or the camera reaches the server without its location; a one-time token or a revealed connection secret hides while the screen is recorded or mirrored; "Save Image" asks only to add to Photos; on an iPad a tap beside a sheet closes it; the status bar reads on every page after a sheet; VoiceOver hears a widget's count as words in both languages.
  - The [threat model](threat-model.md) records the capture cover (R15, M29), the photo locations (M43) and what the simulator showed (M36, section 8.3); [releasing.md](releasing.md) has the iOS section, the checklist for each TestFlight build.
  - Known gaps, the device test's list (37d, or before the first TestFlight build): no device and no Apple account, so nothing is signed, nothing went to TestFlight, and App Store Connect has not processed a build, which is the real test of the manifests and the required-reason APIs; the capture cover in a real recording or AirPlay session (the simulator does not drive the capture state; widget tests cover it); taking a photo with the camera; background refresh through BGTaskScheduler; the passcode fallback, the lockout and Done asking for the unlock; widget redaction on the lock screen and in StandBy; the VoiceOver pass, with [Q19](open-questions.md#q19-device-setup-for-the-manual-talkback-pass)'s TalkBack pass; the sign-in fallback below iOS 17.4 and iOS 15 to 17 (no older simulator runtime is installed); the signed-in account's online flows on iOS (the test account's session had expired, and the checks did not sign in again), Increase Contrast, Bold Text and Reduce Motion; the wheel popups as cards on the iPad; an iPad mini and 11", Split View and Slide Over; Universal Clipboard; a restored backup; whether a release build needs `DEAD_CODE_STRIPPING=NO` for the lock-screen widgets.
  - Not built: the share extension (decision 1, after 1.0); screenshot blocking on iOS (decision 2); Time Sensitive notifications (decision 4); the extra-large portrait widget size of iOS 27; a native `SceneDelegate` cover (the Dart cover showed in the app switcher); Material sheets on the iPad (the cards stay, D69's reserve is not needed); the app's own TLS through `connectionFactory` (D65's reserve; there is no iOS pinning check yet); the drafts out of the backed-up preferences (D66's open question); an iPad key menu, which moves to 36d with the shortcuts.
  - **The owner's to-dos** ([releasing.md](releasing.md#once-before-the-first-upload)): join the Apple Developer Program; the team id; Xcode signed in, with a registered device; the App IDs `dev.coderoftime.vikunja` and `dev.coderoftime.vikunja.RuranaWidgets` with the App Group; the app record in App Store Connect and its API key; `RURANA_SOURCE_CODE_URL`, for which the project must be public; then the first TestFlight build and the device test. *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): the App IDs are `dev.coderoftime.rurana` and `dev.coderoftime.rurana.RuranaWidgets`, with the App Group `group.dev.coderoftime.rurana`.*
  - Open in the review: every decision taken without the owner, first the system SQLite on iOS, "no collected data" in the manifests, the release by hand from the Mac, the location removal in place and for videos too, the capture cover's behaviour while the app is away and the iPad key menu moved to 36d.
  - **No new dependency.** The parked session hardening: none of the six auth files of the package reserved as D59 changed.
  - Tests: `test/app/system_bar_style_test.dart` (11: the helper's fields on light and dark backgrounds, with and without a navigation bar; the status bar after an iOS sheet closes over a page without an app bar, and on the Overview, each in light and dark; Android's navigation bar in light and dark, after a theme change while the app runs, and with the account's dark scheme on a light system; an app bar still deciding where it is), `android_navigation_bar_theme_test.dart` (2: the Android 8.1+ themes, light and dark) and in `app_lock_gate_test.dart` the navigation bar under the lock screen and the privacy cover; in `large_screen_overlays_test.dart` 10 more (a tap beside the card on either side closes the sheet, one on the card does not, a sheet that guards its input stays and its guard hears of it, the areas are no stop for a screen reader, none at an iPhone's size or at half a 13" iPad, a bottom popup whose barrier does not close it stays open, and the wheel and day-range popups close through their own barrier); in `secure_display_test.dart` 12 more (a window that keeps out of captures never hides a secret; on one that shows in captures, a secret hides until the window says the screen is not captured and while it is, an older report and an older answer count for nothing, nothing hides once the last hold is released, a window that cannot answer shows the secret, the app leaving hides it until an answer after the return, a capture started while away, an answer while away, no question when the app comes back without a secret, no listener told after the display is gone); `test/core/platform/secret_capture_cover_test.dart` (7: the new token invisible until iOS answers, the note while captured with Copy still working, the token at once where the window keeps out of captures, invisible when the app leaves and again only after an answer, the note when the app comes back while captured; a revealed secret field masked with the note while captured, and masked while the app is away); in `device_channel_test.dart` `setSecure` on Android, on iOS with its capture reports and elsewhere, and the location removal's call, replacing the test that iOS did nothing; in `plugin_attachment_picker_test.dart` 9 more (camera shots of one second numbered; a library photo named like a camera shot, several numbered, a video keeping its name, a photo beside a video not numbered; the location removed from every photo and video and from a camera shot before they are handed on, a file it could not be removed from still handed on, Android's names left to the picker, the removal on iOS only); `test/features/inbox/inbox_button_test.dart` (1: the badge grows only to 1.3×); `tool/ci/test_ios_notices.py` (16), `tool/ci/test_required_reason_apis.py` (12) and in `test_release_version.py` 2 more (the iOS pair; build numbers that grow across betas and releases). 8,190 app tests pass and 213 are skipped, 60 more than at 37a's commit; the 56 Python tests of `tool/ci/` pass, and the format check is clean, and so is `flutter analyze`.
  - Checked in the simulator on 2026-10-29:
    - **iPhone 17 Pro, iOS 26.5:** the existing databases opened with the system SQLite; a task link (WORK-3) opened its task; the notification prompt and a test notification's banner; the app lock with Face ID (turned on, "Immediately", the cover in the app switcher, unlocked with a matching face); the Tasks widget with real data from the App Group, and a Done from the widget reached the app (the simulator signs the App Group). At AX5 the text grew and long words broke inside the word (Flutter does not hyphenate, which D68 accepts), and the bell's badge was cut off (fixed, above).
    - **iPad Pro 13-inch (M5), iOS 26.5,** as a link-share guest, the link pasted through "Open a shared link" after iOS asked to allow the paste: the layouts of 36b and 36c in both orientations (the readable widths; the task pane in portrait at 1032 pt and in landscape, with the selected row and with Kanban, Table and Gantt beside it; the sheets as centred cards of 640 pt; the project and task menus as popup menus); keyboard insets (the comment field above the keyboard); dark mode; the Account tab; the App language row, which opened the app's page in Settings (the first time iOS showed the Apps list, on later taps the app's page with Language); `isExcludedFromBackup` on `Library/Application Support/accounts` and on the App Group's `widgets/`; a task link opening in the pane; `widget/configure` showing "This widget can't be set up here"; the Home Screen quick action New task opening the composer (warm start); attachments: Photos without a library permission prompt, the upload, the share sheet as a popover at the attachment's row, and "Save Image" asking only to add to Photos, with the new text (declined there); a photo whose original carries a GPS IFD of 15 entries uploaded without it, its image data unchanged byte for byte, the camera's make and model kept, named `IMG_20261029_164833.jpg`. Before the fixes the uploaded photo kept its GPS IFD and was named `image_picker_<UUID>…jpg`, a tap beside a sheet's card did nothing, and the status bar text stayed white after a sheet.
    - **iPhone 16, iOS 18.6:** the launch, a guest by paste, the question about the unencrypted connection, a new database with the system SQLite, the notification prompt, a project's view.
  - Checked locally (macOS, Xcode 27.1, Flutter 3.47.7): `tool/ci/ios.sh` built the unsigned release, and `ios_notices.py`, which the script runs after the build since, passed on that build; `tool/ci/ios_release.sh --dry-run` passed its checks, the notices of 207 packages among them, and ended with "DRY RUN: NOT RELEASABLE (unsigned build, …)". On Android, one release build (arm64) on the Pixel_9_Pro_XL emulator: the status bar reads on the Overview and the Account tab in light and dark. The navigation bar of 3-button navigation kept the look of the theme the app started in when the system switched between light and dark while the app ran, on every page (older than 37b; until this step the Overview's own region hid it there by forcing a black bar), and on Android 13 the bar was black with white icons that stood half over the app's light content. The navigation bar part of the fallback above, a follow-up commit, was checked with 3-button navigation on an Android 13 (API 33) emulator and on the Pixel_9_Pro_XL (Android 16): light, a switch to dark and back while the app runs, and a cold start in dark. Nothing ran on a device or on GitLab.

## D75: The owner's UI review: no task identifier shown, a coloured folder for every project, saved filters that can be hidden, no remembered view, a new-task sheet with chip rows for the due date and priority and more options in place, one Appearance section on the Account tab with Sync up and Server down

*Accepted 2026-10-30, the owner's UI review (the whole app suite passing, `flutter analyze` and the format check clean; the Android widget's Kotlin compiled and the widget extension's Swift type-checked; a release build checked on the Android emulator as a link-share guest, under Checked below; nothing ran on iOS or on a device). With step 37b in review ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)), the owner went through the app on a phone and took decisions 1 to 11 below, quoted where the review quotes them; how they were built was decided without the owner and is open in the review, and so are the choices of the review of the build listed with them. 37b stays in review ([roadmap](roadmap.md)). Amends, each with a dated note there: [D35](#d35-creates-through-the-outbox-temporary-negative-ids-with-remap-createupdatedelete-coalescing-lost-response-recovery-by-title-within-a-1-h-window) and [D38](#d38-quick-add-the-web-grammar-in-vikunja_logic-with-d19s-fixes-weekday-and-ordinal-dates-at-the-default-time-and-an-unknown-project-kept-in-the-title-a-live-composer-labels-created-online-and-assignees-looked-up-on-submit-the-task-through-the-outbox) (the new-task sheet), (the Appearance section and the Account tab's order). Keeps every functional use of the identifier, the colour scheme on the server ([D45](#d45-app-shell-on-wide-android-screens-the-colour-scheme-and-dynamic-colour-the-webs-display-settings-applied-only-when-set-translated-default-names-the-project-tree-with-favourites-saved-filters-and-archived-projects-smart-lists-a-sticky-offline-banner)) and [D2](#d2-material_ui-and-cupertino_ui); follows [D68](#d68-screen-readers-heading-levels-on-screen-titles-and-section-headers-one-announcer-with-the-apps-own-live-region-on-android-high-contrast-and-reduce-motion-followed-and-a-route-sweep-that-every-route-pushed-page-and-overlay-must-pass-wcag-22-aa-as-the-yardstick-with-written-deviations) and the [glossary](glossary.md) for the new texts.*

- **Context:**
  - On 2026-10-30 the owner reviewed the app as 37b left it, on an Android phone, and raised points in four areas: task identifiers, projects, the new-task sheet, and the Account tab with the Account settings page. Before the build, four code maps traced each point to the code, the decision behind it and the tests that pinned it.
  - Verified facts (2026-10-30, from the code):
    - **Identifiers** (`WORK-3`, or `#12` without a prefix) showed in eleven places and were spoken in a twelfth: the rows of every list (in a project's List and Table views before the title when the longest fitted a fifth of the row, measured per list), the Kanban card, the detail sheet's and pane's header, the Subtasks, Related and Parent rows, the add-relation picker, the share target's "Add to task" picker, task-link pills, the inbox ("Anna commented on WORK-12"), the local reminder and due notifications (with a prefix) and, only spoken, the home-screen widgets' rows; screen readers read them wherever they showed. The app needs them only for deep links, the search by `#12` and `PROJ-12`, the pickers' search, filters and logs; quick add's `+project` matches the project's identifier, not the task's.
    - **Projects** were drawn three ways: a colour dot for a coloured project and a grey folder for the others in the tree, the new-task sheet's project field and the task's project chip; a coloured folder in the pickers and the edit page's project tile; a dot or nothing in the task row's meta line, the Table's project column and the widget setup. Android's widget drew "● " only for a coloured project, iOS's a dot in grey for the others. No decision chose the dot: it came from the mockups through [D56](#d56-design-language-after-the-owners-mockups-a-blue-tonal-scheme-with-an-amber-accent-grouped-cards-one-task-row-for-every-list).
    - **The project screen** reopened the view a project showed last (`last_view:<id>`, [D43](#d43-task-lists-from-the-local-database-like-the-webs-a-view-snapshot-for-positions-and-server-only-filters-the-overviews-saved-filter-downloaded-by-the-sync-upcoming-with-the-web-presets-and-a-pinned-overdue-part)), and the guest's landing ([D62](#d62-create-account-password-reset-and-invite-links-through-the-browser-sign-in-page-link-share-guests-as-an-account-kind-with-token-renewal-and-a-reduced-ui)) wrote the link's view there so that the screen opened it.
    - **The new-task sheet** at 360 × 740 with a 300 dp keyboard: the pinned part (the progress slot, the `* + @ !` toolbar and the buttons, 124 dp) left the due date's header right above the toolbar and its chips, three wrapped rows of 48 dp, out of view. "More options" was disabled until a title was typed, because it created the task and opened the edit page on it. A priority came only from `!n` in the text, so none could be set with Quick Add Magic off. The sheet kept clear of the keyboard only, so with the keyboard hidden its buttons sat behind Android's 3-button navigation bar.
    - **Settings:** light or dark (the account's `color_schema`, on the server) was on the Account settings page, "Use system colours" (the device) in the Account tab's Appearance section, with a hint that pointed to the page; the split followed where each value is stored, and the owner's mockup has one Appearance section. The Account tab's "Server" section held the version, Connection and the server's notices, but also the sync status, the pending changes and the sync problems, which show nowhere else.
  - Four implementers built the areas, projects after identifiers for the shared `ProjectIcon`; the main session integrated them, and a review of the result found ten points (two the same), all fixed except the room for the rows while a token is typed, which is listed under the known gaps.
- **Decision:**
  - **Taken by the owner (2026-10-30):**
    1. **"I don't think we need to display the id of a task anywhere."** No task identifier is shown or spoken: not in the rows of any list (nor before the title in a project's List and Table views), on the Kanban card, in the detail sheet and pane, in the Subtasks, Related and Parent rows, the add-relation picker, the share target's "Add to task" picker, task-link pills (the project prefix and the title), the inbox ("Anna commented on *title*"), the local reminder and due notifications or the widgets' spoken rows. The task's project chip moves up into the header where the identifier was, and the row it stood in goes. The Table loses its "Number" column, from the defaults, the Columns sheet and stored choices, and the two "Number" sorts; the default sort (the task number, highest first) stays, as "Default (newest first)". Every functional use stays: deep links such as `task/WORK-3`, the search by `#12` and `PROJ-12`, the relation and share pickers matching on it, quick add's `+project` by the project's identifier, filters and logs. The search's start text no longer names identifiers ("Tasks by title or description; …"), and the project identifier's help names the web's task links ("Used in task links such as PROJ-12"). The project in a task row's meta line, and the Table's project column, is a small folder tinted with the project's colour, `onSurfaceVariant` without one, for every project: the shared `ProjectIcon`. `ColorDot` stays for a task's own colour.
    2. **"Colored Projects have a dot, others a folder. I think we should do colored folders for all."** `ProjectIcon` in the project tree, its drag feedback, the favourites and the search, the new-task sheet's project field (quick add's accent still marks a project the text decides), the task's project chip, the project, parent and default-project pickers, the edit page's project tile, the widget setup's project choice, the filter builder's project suggestions and the share sheet's project chip. The Android and iOS widgets draw a small tinted folder for every project, grey or secondary without a colour, keeping their rows' layout and labels.
    3. **"add an option to hide saved filters."** A checked "Show saved filters" („Gespeicherte Filter anzeigen“) in the Projects tab's menu next to "Show archived projects", not for guests, saved per account with the tree's other choices, on by default. Off, the tab shows no saved filters, also not among the favourites; the empty state counts them as none; the tree no longer waits for them; "New saved filter" stays in the menu, and a filter created while they are hidden opens its list while the choice stays. Search, the widget setup and the Overview's filter choice are not affected.
    4. **"Why do we remember the last selected page in project. I don't think we need that."** A project opens on the first view of the user's `default_view` kind, else its first view. The view picked stays while the screen is open; reopening the project starts again. Nothing reads or writes `last_view:*` any more. A guest's link still lands on the view it names, by another way.
    5. **The new-task sheet as "chip rows"** (the owner's choice among layouts): the headline, the title, the Quick Add preview, the project, then the due date and the priority as two rows of chips, each one line that scrolls sideways, not wrapping: Today, Tomorrow, Next week, a chip for a picked or parsed date on no shortcut, and "Pick date…"; Low, Medium, High, Urgent and Do now (1 to 5), the chosen one tapped again to unset. *Follow-up of 2026-11-02 (the owner, after trying it): each row's heading stands above its chips, no longer at the start of the row, and the chosen shortcut shows the time the due date gets ("Tomorrow at 9:00 AM"), as the sheet's own date no longer repeats under the title.* Both rows stay in view with the keyboard up on a 360 × 740 phone at 1.0 text, above a pinned part that is just the progress slot, the suggestions while a token is typed and the buttons; a date typed in the title adds its chip under the field, and on that phone the priority row is then a short scroll away.
    6. **"Quick options are confusing, should remove that."** The `* + @ !` toolbar goes. Typed Quick Add Magic keeps working where the account's mode has it (the highlighting, the preview, the suggestions); its help opens from a `?` button at the end of the title field, shown unless the mode is off.
    7. **A priority from the sheet in every mode,** a fallback like the due date (`fallbackPriority`): a line's own `!n` wins, and while every line has one the chips are locked, showing it.
    8. **"More options" expands the sheet** (the owner's choice): always enabled, also before a title, it shows below the priority a Description (multi-line plain text, sent as the description's HTML), Labels (the account's, and new ones, applied after the create as quick add's are), Reminders and Repeat (the edit page's sections and pickers; a relative reminder needs a due date), and reads "Fewer options" („Weniger Optionen“) while they show. Nothing is created before Add, and Add still needs a title. With several pasted lines the fields go with every task, a line's own values adding to them or replacing them as its due date does. Creating the task and then opening the edit page on it goes; the "Added …" snackbar stays.
    9. **The sheet's bottom clears Android's navigation bar and the iOS home indicator** while the keyboard is hidden, and the keyboard while it shows.
    10. **"Why is Theme and dynamic colors separate."** One Appearance section on the Account tab: Theme (Light / System / Dark: the account's `color_schema`, saved to the server as before, online only, disabled offline, while a sign-in is needed and while signing out, with the field's error, "Saved" announced and the failure's feedback), "Use system colours" (the device, Android 12 and later) and App language. Each row says where it is stored ("Saved to your account", "On this device") in place of the section's "On this device" mark, and the hint that pointed to the account settings goes. Guests get no Theme row and follow the system. The section shows for every signed-in user. The Account settings page loses its Appearance section, and its entry's subtitle "colour scheme", in English and German.
    11. **"Keep sync up, server down"** (the owner's choice): the Account tab's order is the banners, the header, the notes, Account settings, Organise, Accounts, **Sync** (a heading of its own over the last sync or its phase, a failure's explanation, the background line, Sync now, Full resync, Pending changes and Sync problems), Home screen, Notifications, Privacy, Appearance, **Server** (the Vikunja version, Connection and the server's notices), Sign out and the version line. The texts that point to "Sync problems on the Account tab" stay true.
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-10-30), open in the review:**
    - **The project chip in the header and its fallback line:** the header (`DetailHeaderLayout`, the app's first own layout widget, a `SlottedMultiChildRenderObjectWidget` for both text directions) puts the back arrow of a pane, the chip with its parent projects (`ProjectBreadcrumb`) and the actions (Edit, Follow, the star, the menu, close) on one line when the chip fits there whole; else the chip goes on a line of its own below them, in line with the title, never cut off. The "Open" or "Done" state and the "Not yet synced" note, which stood beside the chip, are a small line under the title. A task not yet sent shows its chip as before. In the pane, Tab reaches the chip first, then Edit, and a screen reader reads the chip alone after the back arrow.
    - **The task's colour dot before the title,** as in the row, no longer at the start of the header, where beside the chip it would read as the project's colour.
    - **"Close details"** („Details schließen“) for the close button: without the identifier, the German close button and the sheet's drag handle (the system's Dismiss) both read „Schließen“ and failed the sweep's duplicate-label check, and "Close task" read as marking the task done.
    - **The Table's five default columns** (Done, Title, Labels, Assignees, Due date), and the stored values: a column choice naming `index` loads without it (an empty choice is the defaults), and a stored `index:asc` reads as the default, as `index:desc` does. The Sort sheet lists "Default (newest first)" („Standard (neueste zuerst)“), also on the chip, then the other 20 pairs of column and direction.
    - **"Search by title"** („Nach Titel suchen“) on the relation picker's field, which still matches identifiers.
    - **Relation subtitles only for other projects:** the Subtasks, Related and Parent rows and the relation picker name the project only for a task in another project, as before; the share target's "Add to task" picker names it always.
    - **`ProjectIcon` everywhere,** also in the native widgets (Android: a vector drawable of Material's outlined folder, `widget_project.xml`, 12 dp with a 3 dp gap, tinted with the project's colour or `onSurfaceVariant`; iOS: the `folder` symbol in the project's colour or `.secondary`) and in quick add's `+project` suggestions, beyond the review's list, to match the labels' colour dots beside them. Archived projects keep their archive icon in the filter builder, untinted; labels keep their coloured circle in the widget setup; the generic folder icons of the project form's parent tile, the account settings' default project row, the quick-add preview's project chip and the help sheet stay.
    - **`show_saved_filters` per account and its hidden behaviour:** the key sits in the `projects` value of `list_preferences` and reads as shown unless it is stored as `false`. Hidden, the Saved filters section and the favourite saved filters go, and the empty state also drops its own "New saved filter" button, leaving it to the menu; the filters are still watched, so showing them again needs no wait. The item stands between "New saved filter" and "Show archived projects".
    - **The `?view=` route for guest landings and old `last_view` rows:** `AppRoutes.project(id, viewId:)` builds `/home/projects/<id>?view=<viewId>` (the plain path without a view); `ProjectTasksScreen` takes it as its first choice while the project has that view, else the default, and its key is the id with the view. The guest's landing uses it, and so do the tests that opened a view through the remembered one. Old `last_view:*` rows are never read: `forget` still deletes a deleted project's (`LocalListPreferences.lastViewKey` stays for that), and the others stay and do no harm.
    - **The sheet's row headings:** small (a 16 dp icon and the small label style, with little padding) so that both rows with their headings still fit at 360 × 740 with the keyboard up; level-2 headings. The chosen shortcut's time comes from a new string, "{label} at {time}" („{label} um {time}“). While the options are closed the content scrolls from its bottom, so the headline leaves first; open, from its top, and "More options" brings the Description into view. The title field has a one-line hint (with the help button the empty field grew to 80 dp) and reveals the caret without the 20 dp margin that pushed the rows down.
    - **The preview shows only what the text sets:** the sheet's own date and priority are the selected chips of their rows and no longer repeat under the title; a due date that only a typed repeat gives keeps its chip. The review asked for a priority chip that can be removed only when it came from the text; it now shows only then.
    - **Priority chips locked by `!n`:** while every line has its own priority, the chips are disabled and show the first line's.
    - **Labels created on Add:** the sheet's label picker marks a new label ("Errands · new") and creates nothing; Add hands it to quick add, which creates it once together with the typed `*labels` and puts it on every task, and one that cannot be created joins the snackbar's labels not created. The edit page still creates a label at once.
    - **Reminders relative to the first line's due date,** or to the sheet's date while there is no text; a reminder already in the list is not added twice, without a message, which as a snackbar would be hidden behind the sheet.
    - **An invalid repeat:** with the options open, Add is disabled; with them closed, Add and the keyboard's Done open them, bring the amount field and its error into view and announce the error, and add nothing. A repeat picked in the sheet gives the task no due date, as on the edit page; a typed one still dates it today.
    - **The extras of several lines:** a line's own labels and reminders add to the sheet's, duplicates dropped, and its own repeat replaces the sheet's (`QuickAddExtras` on `submit`); after "Fewer options" the fields keep their values and still apply. The description goes through the share sheet's `sharedTextHtml` (escaped paragraphs, line breaks, links).
    - **The help button:** the title field's suffix, with the help sheet's existing tooltip "How Quick Add Magic works"; at 200 % text with a long title it can scroll out of view with the field.
    - **The Theme label, "Saved to your account" and "On this device":** the row is "Theme" („Design“), not "Colour scheme"; the page's control moved as it was (a sliding segmented control on iOS); the review listed Light / System / Dark, and the segments keep the page's order Light, Dark, System, as the owner's mockup has it; "Saved to your account" („In deinem Konto gespeichert“) and "On this device" („Auf diesem Gerät“) are small lines under the rows' titles. While it saves, a spinner named "Saving" shows. The row is one screen-reader group, marked disabled when it cannot change (the offline sweep failed on its faded texts without it). It takes "offline" from the sync, and after a failed save it shows the snackbar without locking the control, unlike the page.
    - **The Sync heading:** "Sync" („Synchronisierung“, the glossary's word).
    - **The shared save logic:** `saveAccountSettings` (`account_settings_save.dart`) sends the change, announces "Saved" and gives the haptic and either the field's refusal or the snackbar, for the Account settings page and the Theme row (`theme_setting.dart`); the page behaves as before.
    - **The theme row keeping its state while scrolled away:** a save that runs or the refusal it brought outlives the row leaving the lazy list (`AutomaticKeepAliveClientMixin` while either is there).
    - **A guest's Appearance section** shows the device's rows only and goes when a guest has neither.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **Keeping the identifier in one place,** such as the detail header or a project's own list: the owner wants it nowhere; it lives on in the web's links and the search, which the app keeps.
  - **A Number column hidden by default:** picking one of its sorts would show it again (D61's rule), and the sorts would name what the app never shows.
  - **The edit page's `PriorityChips` in the sheet:** six chips with Unset, which wrap and cannot be cleared by a second tap.
  - **Writing a picked priority into the text** (`!n` at the caret, as the toolbar's menu did): nothing with Quick Add Magic off, and only the caret's line.
  - **A form of its own behind "More options"** (a page for a task that does not exist yet): nothing in the app edits a task before its create; the expanded sheet reuses the edit page's sections and quick add's submit.
  - **Creating a new label when it is picked,** as the edit page does: a dismissed sheet would leave the label on the server (the review's finding).
  - **A one-shot handoff in memory for the guest's view:** the route parameter needs no state between the listener and the screen and opens a view the same way in tests.
  - **A Theme row that only opens the Account settings page, or the control in both places:** the owner asked why the two were apart.
  - **Light or dark as a setting of the device:** it would change D45's colour scheme, which the account shares with the web.
- **Consequences:**
  - The app shows a task by its title and its project, never by its identifier; a project is a folder in its colour wherever it shows, in the app and on the home screen; the Projects tab can hide saved filters; a project opens on its default view each time; the new-task sheet sets a due date and a priority without the keyboard leaving, and More options adds a description, labels, reminders and a repeat without leaving the sheet; the theme is changed on the Account tab, beside the system colours and the language.
  - The local notifications' bodies change, so the scheduler's diff ([D39](#d39-local-notifications-a-scheduler-per-signed-in-account-plans-from-the-local-database-and-diffs-against-a-rolling-window-with-stable-keys-exact-alarms-when-allowed-done-through-the-outbox-from-a-background-isolate-without-a-token-refresh-snooze-local-only-permissions-asked-once-after-the-first-sync)) replaces each pending notification once after the update.
  - Stored values: a table's column choice with `index` and the sort `index:asc` load as above; `last_view:*` rows stay until their project is deleted; older `projects` values have no `show_saved_filters` and show saved filters.
  - Strings, in English and German: ten new keys (`projectsShowSavedFilters`, `appearanceTheme` with its three segments, `appearanceSavedToAccount`, `appearanceOnThisDevice`, `newTaskFewerOptions`, `accountSyncHeading`, `shortcutAtTime`), fourteen removed (the toolbar's four, `notificationBodyWithIdentifier`, the Number column and its two sorts, `appearanceLightDarkHint`, and the page's Appearance heading with the colour scheme and its three segments), and `taskDetailClose`, `addRelationSearch`, `projectIdentifierHelp`, `searchStartBody`, `accountSettingsEntrySubtitle` and `projectTableSortDefault` reworded; both files have the same keys, and none is unused.
  - Removed code: `IdentifierText`, `task_identifier.dart` (`taskIdentifierIn`), `TaskRow.identifiersFitBeforeTitle`, `ActivityItem.taskIdentifier`, `TableColumn.taskIndex`, `TableSort.isDefault`, `ListPreferences.lastViewOf` and `rememberView`, `MagicToolbar` with `insertMagicText` and `insertMagicPrefix`, the `wrap` of `DateShortcutChips` and the page's `_ColorSchemeChoice`.
  - The accessibility sweep keeps 18 overlay scenarios, "the new-task sheet, more options" in place of "the quick-add menu"; the overlay inventory has 63 calls, 21 swept and 42 exempt.
  - **No new dependency.** The parked session hardening: none of the auth files reserved as D59 changed.
  - Known gaps: **the room while a token is typed on a small phone:** with a token such as `+Wo` in progress, the suggestions strip leaves about 220 dp at 360 × 740 with the keyboard up, less than the field, the project, both rows and a preview chip need, so the priority row scrolls under the strip until the token is done; a very long line has the same limit. **The parts only an account has,** not a guest, were not checked on a device: the Theme row saving to the server, "Show saved filters", the labels created on Add and the sheet's extras reaching the server; widget and service tests cover them. The native widgets' folder was only compiled and type-checked in the build, and nothing ran on iOS (the help button, the sheet above the home indicator, the Cupertino Theme control on the Account tab, the folder in light, dark and tinted rendering).
  - Open in the review: every decision taken without the owner, first the project chip in the header and its fallback line, "Close details", the Table's five default columns, the preview showing only what the text sets, the labels created on Add and the Theme row's labels.
  - Tests: `test/features/common/project_icon_sites_test.dart` (6: the project row, the task's project chip, the new task's project field unless the text names a project, and the project, parent and default-project pickers, one each) and `test/features/home/appearance_section_test.dart` (14: the theme saved at once and followed by the app; where each row is kept, without the section's mark and the hint; the check where the segments have room; the iOS control, the same choice sending nothing; the named spinner while it saves; a save outliving the row scrolled out of view; a refusal at the control and announced; no connection; disabled offline, while a sign-in is needed and while signing out; a guest's section, and none without rows; German). For the identifiers: `ProjectIcon` (the colour, the muted fallback, the size, no label); rows that show and read no identifier, with the project's folder tinted or muted; the header with the chip beside the actions and the state and the pending note under the title; on a 360 dp phone the chip on its own line below the actions and not cut, at 1.0 and 200 %; a task not yet sent keeping its chip, and the sheet following a task to its server id; a stored "number, lowest first" sort as the default (the screen, the decoder and the logic); a stored `index` column dropped; the inbox by the title, and "a task" without one; the relation picker without an identifier and without a subtitle for the same project; the share picker's subtitle the project, without `#`; in the pane, Tab reaching the chip first, then Edit, and the chip read alone after the back arrow. For projects: the saved filters' menu item (hiding and showing them, the favourites too, the choice saved, "New saved filter" kept, the tree not waiting, filters alone giving the empty state without its button, German, no item for guests); the stored value (the round trip, an older value, unreadable ones, `forget` removing an old `last_view` row); a picked view not saved, a reopened project on its first view, a view named in the route and a missing one; the `?view=` route, still a tab's; the guest's links landing on their project and view; the coloured folder in the edit page, the widget setup, the filter builder, the share sheet and the composer. For the sheet: the priority chips set and cleared, with Quick Add Magic off too, locked by a `!3` in the text and choosable until every line has its own; both rows on one line and in view at 360 × 740 with a 300 dp keyboard and a 48 dp status bar, also in the typing cases of the review; the buttons above a 48 dp navigation bar and above the keyboard; More and Fewer options with their expanded state and nothing created before Add; the description brought into view; Add sending the description's HTML, the labels, the reminders and the repeat; an invalid repeat (Add disabled while open; Add and Done with the options closed); a dismissed sheet leaving no label, and Add passing a new one; the help button, and none with Quick Add Magic off; the extras with every pasted line; German. In the service: the fallback priority (a line's own wins, a line of magic only, Quick Add Magic off), the extras merged per line, a monthly repeat from the sheet, new labels created on submit and reported while offline. On the Account tab: its whole order; the Account settings page without a theme. Updated: 66 test files and helpers for the new widgets, texts and route, among them the sweep's scenarios and inventory, the 3.12× entry of the expanded sheet, the smoke test and the large-screen guard, whose Overview rows (without the identifier) and Account settings tile (one subtitle line shorter) were recorded again at 360 × 740, 800 × 600 and 375 × 667; the pane's back-stack test accepts any offset between the end of the local content and the saved one, as the shorter header made the exact one depend on when the details arrive. 8,230 app tests pass and 213 are skipped; `vikunja_logic`'s 3,162 pass and 35 are skipped (`flutter test`); the 56 Python tests of `tool/ci/` pass; `flutter analyze` and the format check (1,582 files) are clean; `./gradlew :app:compileDebugKotlin` and `swiftc -typecheck` over `ios/RuranaWidgets/` pass.
  - Checked on 2026-11-02 on the Pixel_9_Pro_XL emulator (Android 16, three-button navigation) with a release build, signed in as a new link-share guest with write access to a coloured project. All nine points passed and no defect was found:
    1. **No identifier:** a scan of every text, description and hint found none on the Overview, Upcoming, the project's List, Table, Kanban and Gantt views, the Sort and Columns sheets, the search, the detail sheet with all its sections (a subtask's row and its Parent row read the title alone), the add-relation picker and the new-task sheet, closed and open. The Columns sheet has no Number column, the Sort sheet's first entry and its button read "Default (newest first)", the search's start text is the new one, and searching `WORK-2` and `#4` still found their tasks.
    2. **The detail header:** the chip on the line of Edit, the menu and "Close details", "Open" under the title, and "Done" with "Not yet synced" there after a task was marked done offline at 360 × 740 dp. With the short name "Arbeit" the chip still fitted beside the actions at 360 dp with text at 200 % and 300 %, so its own line was not seen on the emulator.
    3. **The coloured folder** in the rows' meta line, the Table's project column, the Projects tab, the search's project result, the new-task sheet's project field and its picker, and the detail's chip. Every project of the share has a colour, so the muted folder was not seen.
    4. **No remembered view:** a project left on Gantt, and once on Kanban, reopened on List.
    5. **The new-task sheet at 360 × 740 dp with the keyboard up:** the title with its `?` button (which opens the help), the project, the due date row and the priority row all above the keyboard, each row one line that scrolls sideways, and no toolbar. More options was enabled before a title and Add was not; More options showed the Description, Labels, Reminders and Repeat and read "Fewer options"; a priority chip was selected and cleared by a second tap.
    6. **Tasks created through the sheet, checked on the server:** one with Tomorrow, High and a description arrived with priority 3, due at 9:00 the next day, and the description `<p>Hello from the check</p>`. For another, High was picked and then `!4` typed: the chips locked with Urgent selected, the preview showed a removable "Priority: Urgent", and the task arrived with priority 4 and without the token in its title.
    7. **The keyboard hidden:** Add and More options 16 dp above the three-button navigation bar, at 360 × 740 dp and on the large phone.
    8. **The Account tab of a guest,** in order: the header, the Guest note, Accounts, Sync (the heading, the last sync, the background line, Sync now and Full resync), Home screen, Notifications, Privacy, Appearance (the system colours and App language, each "On this device", no Theme row), Server (the version and Connection), "Remove guest access" and the version line.
    9. **Dark mode:** the open new-task sheet and the detail sheet in readable dark colours.
  - Seen on the emulator, not changed: while `!n` locks the priority, its selected chip can lie outside the visible part of the row, which does not scroll to it (the preview's chip shows the value); the relation picker names no project for a task in the same project, as decided above. Not checked: the parts only an account has (the Theme row, "Show saved filters", which the guest's menu rightly lacks, and the account's labels), the native widgets' folder, German, and the labels, reminders and repeat applied by Add. The check's tasks were deleted on the server, the link share removed and the emulator's settings restored. Nothing was run on iOS.

## D76: Store listings and policies: the name Rurana alone, the public demo for the reviewers, Data safety answered cautiously without a location, photo locations removed on Android too, Rurana's pages on coderoftime.dev, non-trader in both stores

*Accepted 2026-11-02, step 37c (the Python tests of `tool/ci/`, the listings' check, the whole app suite, the Kotlin tests, `flutter analyze` and the format check; the website's build and checks in the owner's website repository; the location removal and the About page's privacy link on an Android 16 emulator; nothing ran on a simulator or a device, no store account or console was used, nothing went to GitLab, and fastlane was neither installed nor run). The owner accepted 37b, the navigation bar fix and the UI review round ([D75](#d75-the-owners-ui-review-no-task-identifier-shown-a-coloured-folder-for-every-project-saved-filters-that-can-be-hidden-no-remembered-view-a-new-task-sheet-with-chip-rows-for-the-due-date-and-priority-and-more-options-in-place-one-appearance-section-on-the-account-tab-with-sync-up-and-server-down)) on 2026-11-02 and asked for 37c, the third sub-step of step 37 ([D72](#d72-first-release-android-and-ios-together-on-google-play-with-an-apk-in-every-gitlab-release-the-repository-on-gitlab-version-100-after-a-beta), [roadmap](roadmap.md)). Decisions 1 to 8 below are the owner's, taken at the start of the step; the defaults under 9 were stated to the owner, who did not object; decisions 10 and 11 are the owner's, taken on 2026-11-02 after reviewing the first version of the step, which had built a website of its own in this repository and removed photo locations on iOS only; everything else was decided without the owner and is open in the review. Answers [Q21](open-questions.md#q21-a-server-for-the-store-reviewers). Amends, each with a dated note there: [D15](#d15-app-id-devcoderoftimevikunja) (the store name), [D17](#d17-notification-policy), [D39](#d39-local-notifications-a-scheduler-per-signed-in-account-plans-from-the-local-database-and-diffs-against-a-rolling-window-with-stable-keys-exact-alarms-when-allowed-done-through-the-outbox-from-a-background-isolate-without-a-token-refresh-snooze-local-only-permissions-asked-once-after-the-first-sync) and [D40](#d40-background-sync-one-periodic-job-for-every-signed-in-account-with-a-deadline-skipped-while-the-app-is-in-front-the-incremental-run-as-the-session-keep-alive-a-one-off-drain-after-a-background-done-server-notifications-synced-into-a-local-inbox-on-their-own-cadence-new-unread-ones-as-activity-notifications-the-first-run-silent) (no exact-alarm declaration), [D53](#d53-attachments-uploaded-through-the-outbox-in-their-own-lane-opened-shared-and-saved-through-the-platform-cover-and-delete-online-and-an-own-android-share-target) (shared files lose their location on Android), [D55](#d55-security-and-integration-settings-two-factor-status-sessions-and-tokens-in-the-app-a-new-token-shown-once-export-and-deletion-through-the-web) (Delete account on every server), [D73](#d73-gitlab-ci-from-scripts-that-run-the-same-locally-images-and-downloads-pinned-the-version-from-the-release-tag-release-signing-from-protected-ci-variables-the-source-link-from-the-build-a-gitlab-release-with-the-apk-its-checksums-and-the-desugar_jdk_libs-source) (the written offer's contact) and [D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10) (photo locations on Android too). Keeps the app without a backend of its own ([D20](#d20-scope-cuts-and-desktop-adaptations), the threat model's assumption 8), [D65](#d65-connection-settings-per-server-address-in-the-apps-transport-a-certificate-pinned-in-the-tls-handshake-or-a-trusted-ca-certificate-a-client-certificate-access-headers-and-unencrypted-connections-only-with-consent-failing-closed)'s consent to plain `http`, which decides the Data safety answer on encryption, and the privacy manifests of [D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10), which Apple's label repeats; follows [D68](#d68-screen-readers-heading-levels-on-screen-titles-and-section-headers-one-announcer-with-the-apps-own-live-region-on-android-high-contrast-and-reduce-motion-followed-and-a-route-sweep-that-every-route-pushed-page-and-overlay-must-pass-wcag-22-aa-as-the-yardstick-with-written-deviations) and the [glossary](glossary.md) for the new texts.*

- **Context:**
  - The roadmap's 37c: a privacy policy in English and German on GitLab Pages; the Play listing with screenshots and the feature graphic, the Data safety answers, the content rating and the exact-alarm declaration; the App Store listing, the privacy labels and review notes with a server and an account for the reviewers ([Q21](open-questions.md#q21-a-server-for-the-store-reviewers)); the support contact. Rurana has no accounts and no backend of its own: it is a client of servers other people run, a case neither store's help pages describe.
  - Four research reports (Google Play, Apple, German and EU law, and what the repository has) read the stores' help pages, the law and the public demo on 2026-11-02. Three implementers then built, in parallel, the first version: a website of Rurana's own in this repository, the app's part with the release scripts, and the store metadata with its tooling; a review found six medium and six low points, all fixed. After the owner's review of that version (decisions 10 and 11), three more implementers built the location removal on Android, Rurana's pages on the owner's website in its own repository, and the move of this repository to those pages. A review of that work found that a shared photo with a generic type and a WebP's own XMP chunk escaped the removal, both fixed in the code, and that the texts promised more than Android's removal does, so the policy and [store-listing.md](store-listing.md) now name the files that keep their location. The Android removal was then rebuilt to blank the location in place, as Android's MediaProvider does, instead of copying videos through `MediaMuxer` and leaving HEIF, AVIF, raw photos and XMP alone; its review raised eleven points, one of them twice, all fixed in the code but place names and country codes, which are not coordinates and stay outside the removal; it was then checked on an Android 16 emulator.
  - Verified facts (2026-11-02, from the stores' pages, the law, the demo server and the code; [store-listing.md](store-listing.md#sources) lists the sources):
    - **Play's Data safety** counts every transmission off the device as collection, with no exception for a server the user chooses; its closest case, uploads to the user's own cloud drive, needs an app that never accesses the data. Apple counts data as collected only when the developer or the developer's partners can access it.
