    - **Exact alarms:** Play's declaration is for `USE_EXACT_ALARM`, which Play reserves for alarm, timer and calendar apps; `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`, which the app uses and the user grants, needs none. The merged release manifest has no `AD_ID`, `USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT` or `READ_MEDIA_*`; workmanager's `SystemForegroundService` has the type `shortService`, which Play does not ask about.
    - **Account deletion:** an app that lets users create an account must let them delete it, in the app on iOS (Guideline 5.1.1(v), where opening the server's own page is allowed) and through a web address on Play. "Create account" leads to the server's registration ([D62](#d62-create-account-password-reset-and-invite-links-through-the-browser-sign-in-page-link-share-guests-as-an-account-kind-with-token-renewal-and-a-reduced-ui)), so both apply. Until this step the Delete account row was hidden on a server that does not allow deletion ([D55](#d55-security-and-integration-settings-two-factor-status-sessions-and-tokens-in-the-app-a-new-token-shown-once-export-and-deletion-through-the-web)).
    - **What the stores publish:** Play shows a personal account's legal name, its developer email and its country; Apple shows the enrolled name as the seller. Both ask the trader question of the EU's Digital Services Act; their own example of a non-trader is a hobbyist without income, and Play shows a trader's address and phone from 2027-03-01. A personal Play account created after 13 November 2023 needs a closed test with at least 12 testers for 14 days in a row before production (37d).
    - **German law** (not legal advice): § 18 (1) MStV asks every public offering that is not purely personal or family for a name and an address for service; a c/o service with authority to accept service is enough, a P.O. box is not. Whether § 5 DDG, for commercial services, applies to a free app is uncertain. Under the GDPR the owner is a controller only for the owner's own processing (support emails, the stores' statistics and crash reports, testers' addresses, the website's logs); whoever runs a server is the controller for the data on it. GitLab Pages sets no cookies, keeps access logs with the visitor's IP address for seven days and is run from the US under the EU-US Data Privacy Framework.
    - **The public demo** (`https://try.vikunja.io/api/v1/info`): demo mode, account deletion allowed; its message says it is reset at irregular intervals, Vikunja's privacy policy speaks of deleting demo data every 24 hours, and the server shows the shared account `demo` / `demo` itself.
    - **The name:** Play's impersonation and metadata policies, and Apple's Guideline 4.1(c), which needs another developer's approval for a name or an icon that uses theirs, make "Vikunja" in a store name a risk; in a description it says what the app is for.
    - **Announced by Google for 2027:** from April, an app with a sign-in has to restore it on a new device through the Restore Credentials API or loses full publishing capabilities and visibility; from 2027-08-25, an app targeting API 37 needs `ACCESS_LOCAL_NETWORK` and a Play declaration to reach the local network.
    - **The code before decision 10:** photo locations were removed on iOS only ([D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)); on Android, a photo from the camera app, a file picked as a file and a file shared to Rurana went up with their metadata. The sign-out wipe left the one saved description draft (`description_editor.draft`) on the device; a fix of the same day deletes it too (see the risks).
    - **The owner's website** (https://coderoftime.dev, an Astro site in the owner's repository `webpage`, published by GitLab Pages; its README covers the hosting and the legal texts) already lists Rurana as a project and has a bilingual Impressum, bilingual privacy policies per app on one page each, support pages per app with one heading per question, and the contact `coderoftime@gmail.com`; the operator's name, address and phone come from its `src/site.config.ts`, where the address is not final yet.
- **Decision:**
  - **Taken by the owner (2026-11-02, at the start of the step):**
    1. **The name is always just "Rurana",** in both stores and both languages; never "Vikunja" in the name, the subtitle or the keywords. The descriptions may say that it is an unofficial client for Vikunja servers, "not affiliated with or endorsed by the Vikunja project". This replaces D15's `Rurana – client for Vikunja`.
    2. **The reviewers use the public demo** `try.vikunja.io` through the app's "Try the public demo", with the shared account `demo` / `demo`, which is reset at irregular intervals. This answers Q21 otherwise than its recommendation, a server of the owner's.
    3. **Play's Data safety is answered cautiously:** what the app sends to the user's server counts as collected, for app functionality, and not as shared; data encrypted in transit "No", because plain `http` can be allowed (D65).
    4. **A website on the owner's own domain,** on GitLab Pages: the privacy policy, the legal notice (Impressum), support and deleting your account. Decision 11 puts it on coderoftime.dev.
    5. **Non-trader** under the DSA in both stores.
    6. **The Impressum gives a c/o address** of a service with authority to accept service, which the owner sets up.
    7. **A new dedicated public email address,** created by the owner, for Play's developer contact, support, privacy questions, TestFlight feedback and the written source offer. With decision 11 the website's own `coderoftime@gmail.com` serves all of these (below).
    8. **The screenshots:** the owner signs in once per device (the Android emulator, the iPhone and iPad simulators) to a seeded local server; the agent does the rest later in this step.
    9. **Defaults stated to the owner, not objected to:** no exact-alarm declaration (D17 corrected); no foreground-service declaration; target audience 18 and over; the content rating for all ages (4+) with "users interact"; Apple's privacy label "Data Not Collected"; every country and region except mainland China; free, without ads, purchases or donations; the listings and the policy in British English (en-GB) and German (de-DE), the German policy binding; the privacy policy linked on the About page; "Create account" kept on Play, with the deletion steps on the website.
  - **Taken by the owner (2026-11-02, after reviewing the first version):**
    10. **Photo and video locations are removed on Android too,** and **Play's Data safety declares no location.**
    11. **Rurana's website becomes part of coderoftime.dev,** the owner's website. Rurana's own `site/`, its job `pages` and `site/site.json` go away.
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-11-02), open in the review:**
    - **Rurana's pages on coderoftime.dev** (decision 11), at fixed addresses that every part of this repository uses and that must not change once they are in the stores:
      - the privacy policy `https://coderoftime.dev/rurana/privacy/`, one page, English and then German at `#de` (`src/content/legal/rurana-privacy.en.md` and `.de.md` in the website's repository);
      - the legal notice, the website's Impressum `https://coderoftime.dev/impressum/`, German first and English at `#en`, with the operator's name, the c/o address and the phone the owner puts in `src/site.config.ts`, and the email;
      - support `https://coderoftime.dev/rurana/support/` and `https://coderoftime.dev/de/rurana/support/` (`src/content/support/rurana.en.md` and `.de.md`), one heading per question, among them "How do I delete my account?" („Wie lösche ich mein Konto?“), whose anchor makes Play's deletion URL `https://coderoftime.dev/rurana/support/#how-do-i-delete-my-account`;
      - the project page `https://coderoftime.dev/rurana/` and `https://coderoftime.dev/de/rurana/` as the marketing URL;
      - the contact `coderoftime@gmail.com` for Play's developer email, support, App Review, TestFlight feedback and the written source offer, and `2026 Benedikt Lesch` as the App Store's copyright; the app keeps "Benedikt Lesch and the Rurana contributors".

      This repository has no website: no `site/`, no `site.json`, no build of its own and no job `pages`. The first version's `site/`, `tool/build_site.py` with its tests and `tool/ci/test_release_scripts.py` are deleted, and `.gitignore`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `tool/ci/test_gitlab_ci.py`, both release scripts and `release_source_code_test.dart` are as they were before 37c. [store-listing.md](store-listing.md#the-website) lists every address with what uses it.
    - **What the pages say** (not legal advice; written in the website's repository from the first version's texts and checked against this repository): the first person, with "du" in German, as in the app; the German privacy policy is binding, and the English one says that it translates it. The privacy policy (the version of 2026-11-02): no server of the developer's, no analytics, ads, tracking or crash reporting; data goes only to the servers the user adds, to a server named in a link the user opens, or to `try.vikunja.io`, which the Vikunja project runs under its own policy; signing in in the system browser; what stays on the device and exactly what sign-out deletes, the description draft among it, backups and the permissions; the location as decision 10 leaves it, with the files that keep one; the stores' own processing, test versions and the APK on GitLab; support emails and the project's issues, with the email provider covered by the website's own policy; the rights under Art. 15 to 18, 20 and 77 GDPR, and the right to object under Art. 21(1) in a section of its own; the controller by name and address from `src/site.config.ts`. Visiting the website is left to the website's own policy, which now names Rurana among the app policies. Support: eleven questions, from connecting and the public demo to deleting the account (what is deleted and what is kept, a server that does not allow it, the demo's shared account, removing an account only from the device), reporting a bug, never with a password or a token, and a security problem.
    - **The About page** links Rurana's privacy policy and legal notice: a section "Privacy and legal notice" („Datenschutz und Impressum“) right under the header, with "Privacy policy" („Datenschutzerklärung“) and "Legal notice" („Impressum“), each opening its address in the browser through the existing link opener, with the same snack bar when that fails. The addresses are constants of `WebsitePage` in `app/lib/app/legal.dart`, the same in every build: an app in German opens `https://coderoftime.dev/rurana/privacy/#de` and `https://coderoftime.dev/impressum/#de`, every other language `https://coderoftime.dev/rurana/privacy/` and `https://coderoftime.dev/impressum/#en`. They are apart from the server's own legal links on the Account tab. The header also says in every build that Rurana is an unofficial app for Vikunja servers, not affiliated with or endorsed by the Vikunja project (`aboutUnofficial`, the store descriptions' wording). *Note 2026-11-02, step 37d ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)): the section is now "Help, privacy and legal notice" („Hilfe, Datenschutz und Impressum“) and opens with a "Help and bug reports" row („Hilfe und Fehler melden“) to Rurana's support page.*
    - **Photo locations on Android** (decision 10; `LocationRemoval.kt` with the device channel's `removeLocation` in `DeviceChannel.kt`, `core/platform/location_removal.dart`): `PluginAttachmentPicker` hands every photo and video from `pickPhotos` and `takePhoto` to the removal on Android as on iOS, for attachments, the description editor's Image button and the avatar, and `ShareFilesController` hands every copy of a share to it on Android before the copy is ready, whatever type the other app gave (`shareLocationRemoval`). Files picked with the file picker ("as a file") are uploaded unchanged on both platforms. The removal runs on one background thread and always answers. It tells formats apart by their first bytes, as a share's copy has a random name without an extension. It is modelled on how Android's MediaProvider hides the location from apps without `ACCESS_MEDIA_LOCATION`: the location is blanked where it is, byte for byte, and nothing is encoded or muxed again but a JPEG's, PNG's or WebP's Exif, which ExifInterface writes again. A file without a location is never written to; an empty or zeroed location box, a GPS directory that holds only its version, or XMP whose only GPS property is `GPSVersionID`, counts as none. No Android version is checked.
      - **Photos:** a JPEG, PNG or WebP is first written again by ExifInterface (`androidx.exifinterface` 1.4.2, the version image_picker_android already resolves to, now declared directly) without the 32 GPS tags it knows, so the GPS directory goes. It writes a copy beside the file, which takes the file's place once written: ExifInterface writes over the file it reads and holds a whole WebP in memory to do so, and an error there, such as an OutOfMemoryError, would leave the file cut short. Where the copy cannot be written, the file stays as it was. Then, in every photo format, the app's own reader finds what is left by the file's own structure and blanks it in place. The GPS values of each Exif are zeroed (`ExifTiff`: the GPS directory that IFD0 or an IFD after it points to, each entry read by its own type and count, only inside the Exif data and only GPS tags 0 to 31). GPS in XMP is overwritten with spaces of the same length, so the packet stays well-formed (`XmpGps`: a property whose local name starts with "GPS" or names a latitude, longitude or altitude, ignoring case, or ends in "Lat", "Lon" or "Lng", as the target of DJI's laser rangefinder does (`drone-dji:LRFTargetLat`), an element without a prefix being in the default namespace, as an attribute, as an element's text, or as an attribute value inside its element such as `rdf:value`). The reader takes a JPEG's Exif and its standard and extended XMP in APP1; a PNG's `eXIf`, its XMP in `iTXt` or `tEXt`, and the text chunks ImageMagick names after a GPS property (`exif:GPSLatitude` and the like, not the version or the directory's pointer), blanked with spaces, computing the chunk's CRC again; a WebP's `EXIF` and its own `XMP ` chunk, which ExifInterface never reads; the Exif and XMP items of a HEIF or an AVIF through `iinf` and `iloc` (an XMP item by its content type, ignoring case and parameters), in the file or in `idat`; TIFF-based raw photos (DNG, CR2, NEF, ARW, ORF, RW2) and a Fujifilm RAF's JPEG; and XMP in Exif's tag 700 in all of them. ExifInterface's own offsets (`getAttributeRange`) are not used for writing: they are off for a GPS value of an unexpected type and by six bytes in a WebP whose Exif starts with JPEG's identifier, and ExifInterface reads HEIF only from Android 9 and AVIF only from Android 12.
      - **MP4, 3GP and QuickTime videos, CR3 and JPEG XL:** the boxes are walked (`IsoLocation`: 64-bit sizes, a size of 0 to the end, at most 8 levels and 100,000 boxes, past which the file is reported as kept, the media data skipped). Every `©xyz`, `loci`, `gps ` and `gps0` box with a value becomes a `free` box with its payload zeroed, which ISO BMFF and QuickTime allow anywhere. In Apple's `mdta` metadata the values of the `data` boxes of each item whose key names a location (`com.apple.quicktime.location.ISO6709`, `.accuracy`, `.body` and the like) are zeroed, their type and locale kept. GPS in XMP is blanked in `XMP_` boxes, XMP `uuid` boxes and `xml ` boxes in `meta`. A CR3's GPS directory in Canon's `CMT4` and a JPEG XL's `Exif` box are zeroed, and a JPEG XL's `xml ` box is blanked. There is no `MediaExtractor` and no `MediaMuxer`.
      - **Read back:** a photo by ExifInterface (its GPS tags and its XMP tag) and by the reader again; a video by the walk again and by `MediaMetadataRetriever`'s `METADATA_KEY_LOCATION`. A location left throws `LocationKept` with a fixed reason that never names the file: "the GPS tags stayed", "GPS in PNG text stayed", "GPS in XMP stayed", "GPS in compressed XMP", "some metadata could not be read", "an XMP packet is too large to read", "a metadata block is too large to read", "the video kept a location" or "the video has a location outside the boxes looked at" (`LocationRemoval.Reason`).
      - **What else changes:** nothing in a video or in a HEIF, AVIF, raw, CR3 or JPEG XL photo but the bytes of its location, so a QuickTime file stays QuickTime with all its tracks, samples, creation time and other metadata. A JPEG, PNG or WebP that ExifInterface writes again keeps its image data and the tags ExifInterface knows, and gains its default tags (width, height, orientation 0, light source) where they were missing. Tags it does not know (Windows' XP tags, PrintIM and the like) go; the maker note moves, so one whose offsets count from the TIFF header (Canon's, Sony's) no longer points at its data; a JPEG keeps only its last standard XMP packet, and its comment (COM) is also copied into an empty Exif UserComment. The blanked values stay in the file as zeros or spaces, as MediaProvider leaves them: Apple's ImageIO shows a zeroed photo's GPS block with latitude and longitude 0, and AVFoundation lists a cleaned QuickTime video's Apple location item with an empty value.
      - **Where the location stays** (the same on every Android version). Not found, so not reported: place names and country codes (XMP's `photoshop:City` or `Iptc4xmpCore:Location`, IPTC in a JPEG's APP13, Samsung's maker-note and trailer fields); GPS in maker notes; anything after a JPEG's first image (the extra images of a multi-picture file, an Ultra HDR gain map, the video of a motion photo, which is the likeliest gap); PNG's raw profile chunks, XMP in UTF-16 and XMP after markup that is not well-formed; a GPS directory outside IFD0's chain or past its eighth IFD (ExifInterface's check reports it where it reads it); a HEIF Exif item whose TIFF offset misses the header, a HEIF whose `ftyp` names no HEIF brand, and HEIF image sequences, read as photos; CR3 GPS outside `CMT4`; GIF, BMP, JPEG 2000, BigTIFF, WebM, MKV and AVI; a photo embedded in another file, such as a PDF, which is uploaded as it is; and in a video a GPS track (GoPro's `gpmd`, CAMM), a dashcam's GPS data in `mdat` that a `gps ` box points to (only the box is freed), boxes deeper than 8 levels or after a broken size, Apple keys without "location" in their name and `©xyz` outside `udta` and `ilst` (`MediaMetadataRetriever` reports these where it reads them). Reported as kept: compressed XMP with GPS (a PNG's `zTXt` or compressed `iTXt`, a HEIF XMP item with a content encoding; only zlib is read, other encodings go unnoticed), PNG text named after a GPS property that is compressed, a JPEG XL `brob` box holding Exif or XMP, a GPS value over 64 KiB, an XMP packet over 64 MiB, a HEIF Exif or XMP item that `iloc` does not place in the file, a video or HEIF of more than 100,000 boxes, an Apple `keys` box over 1 MiB, and GPS in a RW2's embedded JPEG (through ExifInterface); whatever else could be blanked is blanked first, except where a HEIF's `infe` or `iloc` box over 64 MiB stops the removal before it blanks anything. A GPS value of an unexpected type is zeroed; where ExifInterface misreads it onto other bytes of the file that are not zero, the file is still reported as kept.
      - **Failing open, as on iOS:** a file that keeps its location is still handed on and uploaded, with whatever could be blanked blanked. The platform answers `remove_location_failed` with the reason (or an unexpected error's type); Kotlin logs only "A location stays in a file", without the reason, and the app's log adds the platform's reason, never the path. The privacy policy and [store-listing.md](store-listing.md#app-content) give the likeliest cases as examples.
    - **Delete account on every server:** where the server does not allow deletion (`allowsAccountDeletion`, `Capability.userDeletion`), the row reads "Not allowed by this server" („Von diesem Server nicht erlaubt“) with an info icon, and a tap opens only a dialog (`explainAccountDeletionNotAllowed`): "Ask whoever runs the server" („Frage die Person, die den Server betreut“), "This server does not allow deleting accounts in the app. Whoever runs it can delete your account for you.", OK. "Whoever runs the server", not "administrator", as the glossary asks. Where deletion is allowed the flow is unchanged, and a deletion already scheduled still opens the web page to cancel it.
    - **The written offer's contact** is D73's constant, now filled: `WRITTEN_OFFER_CONTACT = 'coderoftime@gmail.com'` in `tool/build_android_notices.py`, so the offer in `app/assets/legal/android.md` ends "Ask Benedikt Lesch at coderoftime@gmail.com.", which is the file's only change, and `--require-offer-contact` passes.
    - **The listings in fastlane's layout** (`fastlane/metadata/`: supply's for Play, deliver's for the App Store), as files only; everything is copied into the consoles by hand. en-GB is the default listing, de-DE the second. Play: title, short and full description, an empty `changelogs/`; App Store: name, subtitle, description, keywords, promotional text, empty release notes, the URL files with the addresses above for each language (the German privacy URL with `#de`), `copyright.txt`, `primary_category.txt` (`PRODUCTIVITY`) and `review_information/` (the notes, `demo` / `demo` and the email). Every feature named comes from the README and is in the app on that platform; both descriptions say that Rurana is unofficial and not affiliated with or endorsed by the Vikunja project; "Vikunja" stands only in the descriptions and in Play's short description, which is a description too. The review notes (3,944 of 4,000 bytes) lead through the demo, ask the reviewers not to delete the shared account, explain the offline use and carry the App Transport Security justification ([D8](#d8-cleartext-policy)).
    - **`tool/check_store_metadata.py`** (standard library, `-I`) checks both languages complete without stray files, the stores' limits (the keywords and the review notes in bytes, as Apple counts them), no other product or company in the name, subtitle or keywords, no claims or emoji in the short fields, no mention of the other platform, the "not affiliated" line, the keyword format, the category, that each URL file is an https address on coderoftime.dev equal to the address above for its language, the email and the copyright, an empty file as an error, and each screenshot's size and alpha channel from its header; `--strict` also wants each store's minimum set of screenshots. One of its tests checks the committed listing, so the `checks` job keeps it valid.
    - **[store-listing.md](store-listing.md):** every form of the Play Console and App Store Connect with its answer, the reason and the source. Data safety: name, user ids, photos and videos, files, in-app messages and other user content, each collected, not shared, for app functionality; no location (decision 10), as Rurana has no location permission and never reads the device's location, and a location left in an attachment is content the user chose, declared with it (uncertain); "Email address" not declared (uncertain); deletion on request "Yes", with the support page's question as the deletion URL. Content rating: PEGI 3, USK 0, ESRB Everyone with "Users interact" expected, and no shared location; Apple's age rating 4+ with "Messaging and chat"; App Access with the demo's instructions; the screenshot sizes; the website's addresses; the owner's to-dos.
    - **The graphics,** from the existing icons only (`branding/build-store-graphics.sh`, ImageMagick): the Play icon (`branding/png/play-icon-512.png`, the full-bleed iOS icon at 512 × 512, as Play rounds the corners itself) and the feature graphic (`branding/png/feature-graphic-1024x500.png`, the llama and the badge of the iOS dark icon on the icon's gradient, without text, so one serves both languages). App Store Connect takes its icon from the build.
    - **The screenshots' tooling:** `tool/seed_demo_data.py --lang en` seeds an English set with the same projects, labels, tasks, saved filter and board as the German one, which stays the default and sends exactly the requests it sent before; `--reset` deletes only items titled like either set, so the language changes without a new sign-in; the board gets renamed columns and two cards in progress; an answer 304 counts as success, and a 500 is tried again after a second (SQLite's "database is locked" on the test server). `tool/store_screenshots.sh` takes an Android emulator (`emulator-<port>` that reports itself as one) or a booted simulator, never a device: Android's SystemUI demo mode or `xcrun simctl status_bar … override` for a status bar at 9:41 with a full battery and signal and no notifications, on Android a 16:9 window (1080 × 1920 on a phone, 2560 × 1440 on the Pixel Tablet) because Play takes no side longer than twice the other; it asks for one motif after the other and saves each without alpha into the fastlane paths, and a trap restores the device on exit, Ctrl-C or an error. The motifs: on the phone the Overview, a project's list, its Kanban board, the task detail, the New task sheet and Upcoming; on the tablet the Overview with a task in the pane. *Note 2026-11-03: the clock shows the device's time at each shot instead of 9:41, Wi-Fi shows no no-internet mark and the mobile signal is hidden, and the task detail and Upcoming are framed anew ([D77](#d77-beta-preparation-the-app-id-devcoderoftimerurana-the-owners-own-app-signing-key-every-coderoftime-project-public-a-help-row-on-the-about-page-testers-through-a-google-group-and-a-public-testflight-link)).*
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **A review server of the owner's** (Q21's recommendation): the owner chose the public demo. It stays the fallback, with a review account seeded by `tool/seed_demo_data.py` and kept off the app's screens, so that Apple's label stays true.
  - **`Rurana – client for Vikunja` as the store name** (D15): decision 1.
  - **"No data collected" on Play,** as Apple's label says: Google's definition has no "accessible to the developer" part, and under-declaring breaks its rules where over-declaring does not.
  - **The precise location in Data safety,** for the location an attachment's metadata can carry (the first version's answer): decision 10.
  - **A website of Rurana's own in this repository** (the first version: `site/` built by `tool/build_site.py` and published by a job `pages`, with the owner's values in one file, `site/site.json`, which the website, the listings, the release builds' `SITE_URL` and the written offer read, and main red until it was filled in): decision 11.
  - **The About page's addresses from a define,** as the source link is: they are fixed and public, so constants link them in every build and need no guard in the release scripts.
  - **Writing XMP on Android through ExifInterface:** it writes XMP as ASCII and would garble other text in it; GPS in XMP is blanked in place instead.
  - **Copying a video through `MediaExtractor` and `MediaMuxer`** (the first Android version): it failed for some codecs, for B-frames on older Android, for a 3GP below Android 8 and for encrypted files, turned a QuickTime file into an MP4 and dropped its subtitle, timecode and metadata tracks; blanking the boxes in place does none of this.
  - **Zeroing at ExifInterface's `getAttributeRange` offsets:** they are off for a GPS value of an unexpected type, which could zero other data, and by six bytes in a WebP whose Exif starts with JPEG's identifier, and ExifInterface reads HEIF only from Android 9 and AVIF only from Android 12; the app's own reader takes their place.
  - **Delete account hidden where the server does not allow it,** as D55 had it: Apple asks every app that creates accounts to offer deletion, and a row that explains is clearer than one that is missing.
  - **fastlane for the uploads:** not installed; the layout keeps it open for later.
  - **New artwork for the stores:** the graphics come from the icons, like every other variant ([D42](#d42-app-icon-the-illustrated-llama-with-a-check-badge-adaptive-themed-and-ios-18-variants-derived-by-script)).
- **Consequences:**
  - The repository holds the listings, an answer to every form, the graphics and the tooling for the screenshots; Rurana's privacy policy, support pages and project page live in the owner's website repository, beside the Impressum. What is public waits for the owner: the Impressum's c/o address and phone, the website's deploy and the work in the consoles. Nothing in this repository waits for the owner's values any more: there is no job `pages`, the release scripts are as before 37c, and the release job's check of the written offer's contact passes.
  - Every build links the privacy policy and the legal notice from the About page, local and test builds included, and says that Rurana is unofficial. On a server that does not allow deletion, Delete account shows and explains instead of being hidden.
  - On Android a photo or video from the photo picker or the camera, and every file shared to Rurana, loses its location before it is queued, where the removal can; the rest goes up with its location, as on iOS when the removal fails.
  - [releasing.md](releasing.md#the-website-and-the-store-listings) has the owner's to-dos, [store-listing.md](store-listing.md) the forms and the addresses, and the website's README its hosting. The [threat model](threat-model.md) adds the store listings and the pages' addresses (M44), extends the photo locations to Android (M43, R36), and keeps M39 at eight pinned images.
  - **Risks:**
    - **The public demo** is reset at irregular intervals; if it is down, or its account has changed, during a review, the review fails, and Play wants sign-in details that work at any time. A reviewer cannot finish deleting the shared account, as the confirmation goes to its email address, and the notes ask them not to try; whether Apple accepts that for 5.1.1(v) is open. The fallback is Q21's own server.
    - **The pages live in another repository:** a moved page or a reworded deletion question there breaks the stores' addresses and the About page's links, and nothing in this repository notices. The website's README says that these addresses and the question must not change.
    - **The location removal on Android fails open:** a file in one of the cases above goes up with its location, with only a log line, and nothing tells the user; the likeliest is the video of a motion photo. Making the promise unconditional would need the app to warn or refuse (the share sheet could show it per file); that is not built.
    - **Blanked, not removed:** except in a JPEG, PNG or WebP that ExifInterface could write again, the GPS directory and Apple's location items stay with zeros in them, as MediaProvider leaves them, so an app that takes zeros for a position puts the photo at 0° N, 0° E, and AVFoundation lists an empty location for a cleaned QuickTime video; turning Apple's location items into `free` boxes, which the design allowed too, would hide that.
    - **The video check trusts `MediaMetadataRetriever`:** any error it throws counts as no location. In the app it has its context; in a bare `app_process` on Android 16 it crashed without one, which the check would have swallowed.
    - **Google's sign-in restore (announced for April 2027)** asks an app with a sign-in to restore it on a new device. The threat model keeps the tokens on the device they were made on (A1 to A4: Keychain `ThisDeviceOnly`, a Keystore-wrapped key, `allowBackup="false"`; [D66](#d66-a-threat-model-uncaught-errors-redacted-and-never-printed-in-release-builds-the-apps-data-out-of-backups-and-device-transfer-routes-pushed-by-the-platform-taken-only-as-app-scheme-links-widget-links-and-notification-taps-switching-accounts-only-with-a-per-install-key-exported-components-and-dependencies-checked-in-ci)), so meeting it would reverse a decision; it needs the owner after 1.0. Only permanently private, enterprise and strictly regulated apps are exempt.
    - **The local network (from 2027-08-25):** once the app targets API 37 it needs `ACCESS_LOCAL_NETWORK` and a Play declaration for servers on the user's network.
  - Strings, in English and German: ten new keys (`accountDeletionNotAllowedRow`, `accountDeletionNotAllowedTitle`, `accountDeletionNotAllowedBody`, `accountDeletionNotAllowedOk`, `aboutUnofficial`, `aboutWebsiteHeading`, `aboutPrivacyPolicy`, `aboutPrivacyPolicySubtitle`, `aboutLegalNotice`, `aboutLegalNoticeSubtitle`); both files have the same keys. The overlay inventory has 64 calls, 21 swept and 43 exempt: the new dialog is exempt like the deletion dialog beside it and opened at 200 % in the smoke test.
  - **No new dependency:** `androidx.exifinterface:exifinterface` 1.4.2, which image_picker_android already brought, is now declared directly; the Gradle lockfile and the libraries of the Android notices are unchanged. The parked session hardening: none of the six auth files reserved as D59 changed.
  - Known gaps:
    - **Statements for the owner to confirm** (not legal advice): the supervisory authority is not named, as it needs the owner's Bundesland (the policy names the right of Art. 77 in general); support emails are deleted once dealt with and the testers' list when the test ends, without a fixed period; the GitLab Pages facts come from the research, not checked again; what the pages say of Vikunja's behaviour (a session ending after three days unused, the mailed confirmation and the three days before a deletion, what goes with a deleted account, the demo's account) comes from the app and its docs, not from the server; "no location permission" is checked against the source `AndroidManifest.xml` and `Info.plist`, not the merged release manifest; the pages describe the stores' statistics, the testers and the APK on GitLab before any of them exists.
    - **The description draft:** sign-out and removal left the one saved description draft on the device. A fix of the same day has the wipe discard the account's draft (`DescriptionDrafts.discardAll`; another account's stays), and an editor still up after its account signed out keeps no draft when the app goes to the background; the website lists the draft among what is deleted.
    - **The release scripts** do not check that the privacy policy answers at release time, as the iOS script does for the source link; that would add a network dependency.
    - **Store answers marked uncertain** in store-listing.md: no Google page says outright that exact alarms and workmanager's foreground service need no declaration (to be checked under App content after the first upload); the IARC questions and Data safety's labels for account creation are worded from the research, not from the Console; no location and no "Email address" in Data safety are judgement calls, and no Google page says whether a location inside media the user chose counts as location data; the wording of App Store Connect's content-rights question; the trader status is a legal judgement; whether App Store Connect takes the German privacy URL with its `#de`; Android's German name for the "Alarms & reminders" page was not checked, so the German text says „genaue Alarme“.
    - Not checked: the pages themselves in a browser (on the emulator the About page's privacy link opened its address, where the website shows its 404 page until it is deployed); shellcheck on the new shell scripts (not installed); `tool/store_screenshots.sh` on an emulator or a simulator (only against stand-ins for `adb` and `xcrun`); either release script end to end; the location removal on a real device, on Android below 16, with a phone's own HEIC or an iPhone's own MOV (the test files were made on the Mac with ImageIO, `sips`, AVAssetWriter, ImageMagick, Pillow and ffmpeg, and on the emulator), with an action camera's GPS track or a phone's own motion photo, and end to end through the pickers, a share and the Dart side of the channel (the removal itself ran on an Android 16 emulator, called directly as the app's user, below); Android lint, which fails on seven errors that were there before and none in the new or changed files.
  - Not done: no fastlane run and no upload to either store; no console form filled; the screenshots, which follow the owner's sign-in later in this step (decision 8); the Impressum's c/o address and phone, and the website's deploy, which are the owner's; anything for the 2027 requirements.
  - **The owner's to-dos** ([releasing.md](releasing.md#the-website-and-the-store-listings), in this order): the c/o service and a phone number in the website's `src/site.config.ts`; the website deployed to coderoftime.dev and every address checked; the Play Console and App Store Connect forms by [store-listing.md](store-listing.md); the trader declarations; the screenshot session.
  - Open in the review: every decision taken without the owner, first the pages' fixed addresses and the German privacy URL with `#de` in App Store Connect; the website's contact for everything in place of a new address; the pages in the first person; the Android removal's cases that keep the location, failing open with a log line only; the dialog on servers that do not allow deletion; and the About page's unofficial line.
  - Tests: `test_check_store_metadata.py` (18, the committed listing among them: the URL files on coderoftime.dev and equal to each language's addresses, the email, the copyright, empty files), `test_seed_demo_data.py` (14: both sets with the same keys, the German default sending the requests it always sent, the board, what `--reset` deletes, 304 and 500); `build_android_notices.py --self-test` with the contact's cases. In the app: `about_page_test.dart` (4 more: the English and the German links at their exact addresses, a link that fails to open, English addresses for every language but German; the unofficial line), `account_security_sections_test.dart` (3 in place of the hidden row: the dialog with nothing opened, German, a scheduled deletion on a server that does not allow it), and the About page with its links and the new dialog at 200 % in both languages in `accessibility_smoke_about_test.dart` and `accessibility_smoke_test.dart` (the About test scrolls to the privacy row, which the taller header pushes down at 320 × 640). For decision 10: in `plugin_attachment_picker_test.dart` the removal on Android too, for the photo picker's photos and videos and for camera shots before they are handed on, never for files from the file picker on either platform, and attached on Android and iOS only, and, in 2 more, that a file whose removal fails, with a reason or unexpectedly, does not stop the removal from the files after it and is logged by the failure's type alone; in `share_files_test.dart` 3 (every copy through the removal before it is ready, whatever type the other app gives; a copy that keeps its location still ready, with no file in the log; Android only); `LocationRemovalTest.kt` (45, on the real ExifInterface with hand-built files: a JPEG, a PNG and a WebP lose their GPS tags and directory while the tags ExifInterface knows, their image data, a PNG's other chunks and a WebP's image and XMP chunks stay as they were, and what follows a JPEG's first image (another image, a motion photo's video) is kept byte for byte; GPS in XMP is blanked in place in a JPEG's XMP and Exif, whose image data stays, and, with nothing else changing, in a JPEG's extended XMP across its chunks and its XMP after fill bytes, a DJI drone's position, a PNG's uncompressed `iTXt` or `tEXt` with the CRC computed again, a PNG's Exif beside a separate packet, a WebP's `XMP ` chunk and a WebP's Exif with or without Exif's identifier; ImageMagick's PNG text chunks named after a GPS property are blanked with every CRC holding, and a chunk's CRC is computed however large the chunk; where ExifInterface cannot write a JPEG, PNG or WebP or writing the copy fails with an error, and in a TIFF-based photo, an ORF, a RW2 and a RAF's JPEG, the GPS values are zeroed in place in either byte order, one stored with another type than its tag's among them, reported only where ExifInterface misreads it; only GPS values inside the Exif are zeroed, wherever IFD0 or IFD1 points to the GPS directory, with a LONG or a SHORT; a HEIF or AVIF photo's Exif and XMP items wherever `iloc` puts them (versions 0 to 2, 8-byte fields, extent indices, base offsets, two extents, a length of 0, `infe` version 3, the XMP content type in another case and with a parameter), a CR3's GPS directory in Canon's `CMT4` and a JPEG XL's `Exif` and `xml ` boxes are zeroed and blanked in place; GPS in a PNG's compressed XMP, a HEIF item that `iloc` does not place in the file or places with a gap, a HEIF's compressed XMP item, a JPEG XL's compressed box, a video or HEIF of more than 100,000 boxes, an Apple `keys` box over 1 MiB and GPS that only ExifInterface still finds (in a RW2's JpgFromRaw) are reported as kept, the PNG's reason naming no path, and the file is left as it was; a GPS value over 64 KiB is reported, and an XMP packet over 64 MiB and a location only `MediaMetadataRetriever` finds are reported with the rest blanked; an `iloc` whose extents take no bytes, and long runs of fill bytes, of end tags that close nothing and of text without its NULs, are read in time; files without a location, already blanked ones and XMP with only the version among them, are not written to; every GPS tag goes; formats are told apart by their first bytes; a video's `©xyz`, `loci` and `gps ` boxes, Apple's location keys and GPS in XMP become `free` boxes, zeros and spaces and nothing else changes, a `data` box's locale kept, also in ISO metadata, a 64-bit box and a last box sized to the end of the file; the video search finds the location in QuickTime, 3GPP, Apple and XMP boxes, not in media data or empty values, and walks large boxes, boxes to the end and broken sizes safely; GPS values in XMP are found as attributes and as elements, in DJI's names, the laser rangefinder's target among them, in `rdf:value`, in a structure and in the default namespace, after a declaration and past an end tag that closes nothing, and nowhere else, and the version alone is none; the device channel's answer is the reason or an unexpected error's type and nothing more), with a test-only `android.util.Log` so that ExifInterface loads on the JVM. In `app/`, `flutter test` (2026-11-02, the final run) passes 8,255 tests, skips 213 and fails none (8,253 before the second review of the location removal added 2); the 12 that failed after the follow-up pass now pass, the share tests with a recording fake for the share's location removal and `log_hygiene_test.dart` with `DeviceChannel.kt` logging no reason. The 88 Python tests of `tool/ci/` and the 91 Kotlin tests, the 45 of `LocationRemovalTest.kt` among them, pass; `flutter analyze` and the format check (1,582 files) are clean.
  - Checked locally on 2026-11-02: `check_store_metadata.py` exits 0, `--strict` exits 1 with 8 errors, the missing screenshot sets; `build_android_notices.py --check` finds `android.md` current, its only change the request line, and `--require-offer-contact` exits 0; `./gradlew :app:dependencies` resolves ExifInterface 1.4.2 as before; a debug APK builds. The first version's checks also ran: the seed against a throwaway local Vikunja container, in German, again, in English (refused without `--reset`, then with it) and back, and the container was stopped; `store_screenshots.sh` against stand-ins for `adb` and `xcrun`: the commands in order, the restore, the screenshots without alpha, the refusals with exit 2; the graphics: the Play icon 512 × 512, 8-bit RGBA, fully opaque, sRGB; the feature graphic 1024 × 500, 8-bit RGB; two runs give the same bytes. In the website's repository its build, its type check and a check of the built pages pass, the addresses and anchors above among them. On the Android 16 emulator (API 36), on 2026-11-02, `LocationRemoval.removeFrom` from the debug APK ran as the app's user on files in its cache folder: of 13 test files with a location (JPEGs with GPS in Exif and in XMP; HEIC and AVIF files from ImageIO and `sips`, with and without GPS in XMP; PNGs with uncompressed and with compressed XMP; WebPs with and without Exif's identifier; a QuickTime video from AVAssetWriter with `©xyz` and Apple's ISO6709 and accuracy keys; an MP4 written with `MediaMuxer.setLocation`), 12 came out without one, and the PNG with compressed XMP threw `LocationKept` ("GPS in compressed XMP"); the HEIC, AVIF and video files changed only in their location bytes, the pixels and video samples were the same on the device and in ImageIO and AVFoundation on the Mac, and the two files without a location were not written to. With ExifInterface unable to write (a temporary folder it could not use) the JPEG, PNG and WebP were zeroed in place instead, the PNG's CRCs valid. After the removal's second review the check ran again from the new debug APK, through `LocationRemoval.failureOf` as the device channel calls it: those 15 files came out byte for byte as before, with ExifInterface writing and unable to write, and the new test files as the JVM tests expect (a DJI drone's rangefinder target, XMP in the default namespace, little-endian Exif, ImageMagick's PNG text, HEICs with the XMP content type in another case or with a parameter and with a two-extent Exif item, `MediaMuxer`'s 3GPP output with `©xyz` and an ffmpeg 3GP with `loci`, a motion photo whose appended video stayed byte for byte, its location with it, and WebPs of 40 and 150 MB, the larger zeroed in place when ExifInterface ran out of memory, with no copy left behind); compressed PNG text named after a GPS property was reported, files with only a GPS version were not written to, and ImageMagick's PNG with XMP kept the location in its raw profile chunk, as listed above. The About page's privacy link opened `https://coderoftime.dev/rurana/privacy/` in the browser, which shows the website's 404 page until it is deployed. Nothing else ran on an emulator, and nothing on a simulator, a device or GitLab.

## D77: Beta preparation: the app id `dev.coderoftime.rurana`, the owner's own app signing key, every coderoftime project public, a help row on the About page, testers through a Google Group and a public TestFlight link

*In review, 2026-11-02, step 37d, first part (the CI scripts of section 8.1 locally with Flutter 3.47.9: `checks.sh`, `test.sh` with 8,264 app tests passed and 213 skipped and every package green, `editor.sh`, `osv.sh` without findings, `android.sh`, the contract tests against Vikunja 2.7.1 with 355 of `vikunja_api` and 198 of the app, the release dry run of `v1.0.0-beta.1` ending with only its three expected reasons, `ios.sh` and the iOS release dry run with Xcode 27.2, which checked 1.0.0 with build 1000001, the privacy manifests, every required-reason API and the notices; probes of Vikunja 2.4.0, 2.7.0 and 2.7.1 servers; the release APK on an Android 16 emulator without signing in: its version, the schemes and the task link resolving to the new id and the old scheme only to an old development build, both widgets, the About page's help row opening the support page. Nothing ran on GitLab, on a device or in a store console; section 8.2, which needs the owner's sign-ins, waits for its session before the tag). The owner accepted 37c ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores)) on 2026-11-02 and asked for 37d, the last sub-step of step 37 as the owner split it in [D72](#d72-first-release-android-and-ios-together-on-google-play-with-an-apk-in-every-gitlab-release-the-repository-on-gitlab-version-100-after-a-beta) ([roadmap](roadmap.md)). This first part is everything the repository needs before the owner tags `v1.0.0-beta.1`; 37d stays in progress, and its later commits carry the beta's fixes and then 1.0.0. Decisions 1 to 5 below are the owner's, taken on 2026-11-02; the choices listed after them were taken without the owner and are open in the review. Answers [Q20](open-questions.md#q20-signing-keys-and-the-apk-beside-the-play-build) and [Q22](open-questions.md#q22-ci-runners-on-gitlab) and opens [Q23](open-questions.md#q23-french-encryption-declaration-for-the-app-store). Amends, each with a dated note there: [D1](#d1-flutter-3475-pub-workspace-with-pure-dart-packages) and [D73](#d73-gitlab-ci-from-scripts-that-run-the-same-locally-images-and-downloads-pinned-the-version-from-the-release-tag-release-signing-from-protected-ci-variables-the-source-link-from-the-build-a-gitlab-release-with-the-apk-its-checksums-and-the-desugar_jdk_libs-source) (Flutter 3.47.9), [D15](#d15-app-id-devcoderoftimevikunja) and [D23](#d23-browser-sign-in-through-flutter_web_auth_2-client-id-and-redirect-uri) (the app id, the `client_id` and the scheme), [D28](#d28-test-only-the-latest-vikunja-release) (the test servers on Vikunja 2.7.1), [D44](#d44-task-detail-complete-rich-text-from-an-own-parser-of-the-web-editors-schema-details-and-comments-online-on-demand-deep-links-on-the-app-scheme), [D53](#d53-attachments-uploaded-through-the-outbox-in-their-own-lane-opened-shared-and-saved-through-the-platform-cover-and-delete-online-and-an-own-android-share-target) and [D55](#d55-security-and-integration-settings-two-factor-status-sessions-and-tokens-in-the-app-a-new-token-shown-once-export-and-deletion-through-the-web) (what Vikunja 2.7.1 changed for previews, two-factor and API tokens), [D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10) (the App IDs of its to-dos), [D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores) (the About page's section), and [D72](#d72-first-release-android-and-ios-together-on-google-play-with-an-apk-in-every-gitlab-release-the-repository-on-gitlab-version-100-after-a-beta) and D73 where they leave Q20 and Q22 to the owner. The beta's runbook is [beta.md](beta.md).*

- **Context:**
  - What 37d has to do, by the [roadmap](roadmap.md): the repository published on GitLab; the Play app created with its signing; the Apple account set up for TestFlight and the iOS device test; Play's closed test (for a new personal developer account at least 12 testers for 14 days before production) and the TestFlight beta, released as `1.0.0-beta.N`; the threat model signed off by the owner; the name and the logo checked with the Vikunja maintainers ([D15](#d15-app-id-devcoderoftimevikunja)); the beta's fixes; then `v1.0.0`. 37c ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores)) left the Impressum's c/o address and phone number, the work in both consoles and the screenshot session to the owner.
  - A scouting pass on 2026-11-02, four scouts and a critic, read the repository, the stores' and GitLab's help pages and the public demo, and sorted what is open by what it blocks: the first beta tag, the closed test or 1.0.0. The main session then raised the Flutter pin, the test servers' Vikunja and the pubspec's version, and brought the open choices to the owner, who decided 1 to 5 below the same day.
  - Verified facts (2026-11-02, from the pages named and the repository; not legal advice):
    - **GitLab:** the project https://gitlab.com/coderoftime/rurana exists and is private (GitLab's project API answers 404 to an anonymous request); `coderoftime` is a group whose only public projects are fints and fints-ts (https://gitlab.com/api/v4/groups/coderoftime/projects); `origin/main` is 80b19c9 of 2026-10-29, and the local `main` is ahead of it. GitLab for Open Source takes groups, not personal namespaces, and asks that every project in the namespace be public under an OSI-approved licence (one private project for security work is allowed with GitLab's permission); it gives 50,000 compute minutes a month at a cost factor of 0.5 and is renewed every year (https://about.gitlab.com/solutions/open-source/join/, https://docs.gitlab.com/subscriptions/community_programs/). Compute minutes count against the top-level namespace, so the free tier's 400 a month are shared by every project of the group, the website's Pages pipelines included (https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/pipelines/compute_minutes/).
    - **Pipelines per beta:** [releasing.md](releasing.md#cutting-a-release) asks for a green pipeline on `main` before the tag, and the tag's pipeline runs the same jobs again and then `release`; at about 100–150 compute minutes a pipeline ([D73](#d73-gitlab-ci-from-scripts-that-run-the-same-locally-images-and-downloads-pinned-the-version-from-the-release-tag-release-signing-from-protected-ci-variables-the-source-link-from-the-build-a-gitlab-release-with-the-apk-its-checksums-and-the-desugar_jdk_libs-source)), each beta costs about 200–300 compute minutes.
    - **Play App Signing:** a new app is enrolled automatically in "quantum-ready hybrid signing" with keys Google generates (an RSA-4096 and an ML-DSA-65 key, and a classical key for Android 16 and older). Another choice, among them "Provide a copy of your app signing key" through PEPK, is possible only until a release reaches open testing or production; the page does not say whether a key provided that way gets hybrid signing too (https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9842756).
    - **Android developer verification:** since 2026-09-30 certified devices in Brazil, Indonesia, Singapore and Thailand install apps from any source only when they are registered to a verified developer, and Google plans the same for every certified device in 2027. Creating an app in the Play Console registers its package name; an APK signed with a key Play does not know needs that key registered as well (https://developer.android.com/developer-verification/guides).
    - **The app id** `dev.coderoftime.vikunja` would show "vikunja" in Play's address of the app, in the closed test's opt-in link (`https://play.google.com/apps/testing/<app id>`) and in Android's app info, after [D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores) took the word out of the store name. [D15](#d15-app-id-devcoderoftimevikunja) makes the id permanent once signing keys or store listings exist; on 2026-11-02 neither exists.
    - **Flutter:** 3.47.8 (2026-10-23) carries the Dart security advisory GHSA-frr6-h895-5pxp: the BoringSSL of `dart:io` blocks compromised eMudhra intermediate certificates, and `dart:io` on Android 14 and later uses the updatable Conscrypt root store. Rurana reaches servers with a pinned certificate, a trusted CA or a client certificate through `dart:io` ([D65](#d65-connection-settings-per-server-address-in-the-apps-transport-a-certificate-pinned-in-the-tls-handshake-or-a-trusted-ca-certificate-a-client-certificate-access-headers-and-unencrypted-connections-only-with-consent-failing-closed)). 3.47.9 (2026-10-29), the current stable, also fixes a native-assets crash of `flutter test` in pub workspaces (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flutter/flutter/stable/CHANGELOG.md, https://storage.googleapis.com/flutter_infra_release/releases/releases_macos.json). The OSV scan reads only lockfiles, so advisories of the SDK are found by reading its release notes ([threat model](threat-model.md#9-review-cadence)). `flutter upgrade` run inside the repository also upgraded 29 packages in `pubspec.lock`; the committed lockfile was restored, and it resolves unchanged under 3.47.9.
    - **Vikunja 2.7.1 against the app** (the contract tests and probes of the local 2.7.1 server, with 2.4.0 and 2.7.0 started beside it for comparison): v2 ignores `preview_size` and answers the original, while v1's `preview_size` gives a real preview on 2.7.1 and, on 2.4.0 and 2.7.0, the same bytes as v2; v2's new `…/preview?size=` route is refused with a 422 by older servers. Previews are JPEGs (PNGs only with transparency), never wider than the image, and a file without a preview (text, SVG, HEIC, AVIF) is 422 code 4038 instead of the original. Five API token permissions were renamed, the move of a task into a bucket among them, which the app's task preset grants; the old names are refused. Deleting a token that is gone answers 404 code 14017 instead of 403. Turning two-factor on or off now needs the password and a code. A view takes at most 100 manual buckets (412 code 10007). The app's two-factor status reading, sign-out, refresh, team, paging and link handling behave as before; the web's Quick Add now keeps a date range such as `31.12.2026 - 01.01.2027` in the title, where the app's parser still takes the first date.
    - **Vikunja 2.7.1** came out on 2026-10-20 with 41 security fixes (https://github.com/go-vikunja/vikunja/releases); `try.vikunja.io`, which the reviewers use ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores)), reports `v2.7.1-114-gfbbd0a17`, a build of `main` after it (https://try.vikunja.io/api/v1/info). The test servers ran 2.7.0.
    - **Reporting a problem from the app:** the About page linked only the privacy policy and the legal notice (`WebsitePage` in `app/lib/app/legal.dart`), release builds log nothing, and the diagnostics export is postponed ([roadmap](roadmap.md)). Rurana's support pages, with a section on reporting a bug and one on reporting a security problem, are online at https://coderoftime.dev/rurana/support/ and https://coderoftime.dev/de/rurana/support/.
    - **Testers:** Play's closed test takes its testers from email lists, Google Groups (a member joins the group, then opts in) or an organisation; every tester opts in through the test's opt-in link, which works only once the app's status is "Published". A personal account created after 2023-11-13 needs at least 12 testers opted in for 14 days in a row before it can apply for production, and the application asks how the testers were recruited, what they used and how their feedback was collected (https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9845334, https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/14151465). TestFlight takes up to 10,000 external testers by email or through a public link; the first build of a version goes through Beta App Review, and a build expires after 90 days (https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/test-a-beta-version/invite-external-testers/, https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/test-a-beta-version/testflight-overview/).
    - **Encryption:** in the US, an end-user app with only standard, mass-market encryption owes no year-end self-classification report since the rule 86 FR 16482 of 2021-03-29 (https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/15/740.17). Apple's table asks for a French encryption declaration for an industry-standard algorithm "not provided within the Apple operating system" when the app is on the App Store in France (https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/reference/app-information/export-compliance-documentation-for-encryption/); the TLS of `dart:io` is BoringSSL in the Flutter engine ([releasing.md](releasing.md#export-compliance)), and the App Store availability includes France ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores)).
    - **App Store Connect:** the Beta App Review notes are not to include demo account details, which have fields of their own (https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/test-a-beta-version/provide-test-information/); `fastlane/metadata/review_information/notes.txt` named `demo` / `demo`. The Accessibility Nutrition Labels are to become required over time, and a feature may be claimed only when users can complete the app's common tasks with it (https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/manage-app-accessibility/overview-of-accessibility-nutrition-labels).
    - **Xcode** on the Mac is 27.2 (27B52); [D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10) and the roadmap record 27.1, and nothing pins Xcode.
    - **What publishing makes public besides the code:** the tracked `test_servers/seed/e2e-user.env` and `e2e-user-2.env`, the throwaway sign-in users of the local test servers (not read here), and the history as it is, with commit messages under the earlier working name and, in one commit, the absolute path of the local Flutter SDK in [research/flutter-stack.md](research/flutter-stack.md).
- **Decision:**
  - **Taken by the owner (2026-11-02):**
    1. **Every project of the group `coderoftime` becomes public,** so that the whole group can join GitLab for Open Source. The project stays https://gitlab.com/coderoftime/rurana, which exists already, and its history is kept as it is, without a rewrite. iOS builds stay on the owner's Mac, by hand (`tool/ci/ios_release.sh`, [D74](#d74-the-ios-phase-privacy-manifests-and-the-system-sqlite-on-ios-testflight-from-the-owners-mac-the-ipad-and-iphone-checked-in-the-simulator-photo-locations-stripped-one-time-secrets-covered-while-the-screen-is-captured-the-share-extension-after-10)). This answers [Q22](open-questions.md#q22-ci-runners-on-gitlab): the programme is the remedy for the compute minutes that 37a's estimate left open ([D73](#d73-gitlab-ci-from-scripts-that-run-the-same-locally-images-and-downloads-pinned-the-version-from-the-release-tag-release-signing-from-protected-ci-variables-the-source-link-from-the-build-a-gitlab-release-with-the-apk-its-checksums-and-the-desugar_jdk_libs-source)); it needs a group in which every project is public; and the project's address is the source link of every release's About page, so it stays where it is.
    2. **The signing keys follow [Q20](open-questions.md#q20-signing-keys-and-the-apk-beside-the-play-build)'s option A.** The owner creates an app signing key that only the owner holds and uploads it to Play App Signing with PEPK when creating the app ("Provide a copy of your app signing key"); a separate upload key signs the bundles; the release job signs the APK with the app signing key (`RURANA_APK_*`), so Play's builds and the GitLab APK share one signature. Providing the key takes the place of Play's new default, hybrid signing with keys Google generates; whether Play gives an uploaded key hybrid signing too is not documented, so the owner checks it in the console when creating the app. Android's developer verification asks that an APK installed outside Play be signed by a registered key; with option A the APK's key is the app signing key Play already knows.
    3. **The application id becomes `dev.coderoftime.rurana`,** before any store record or key exists, with every id derived from it: the Android `applicationId`, namespace and Kotlin package; the iOS bundle ids `dev.coderoftime.rurana`, `dev.coderoftime.rurana.RunnerTests` and `dev.coderoftime.rurana.RuranaWidgets`; the App Group `group.dev.coderoftime.rurana`; the method channels (`dev.coderoftime.rurana/device` and the others) and the background task identifiers (`dev.coderoftime.rurana.sync` and `.drain`); the OAuth `client_id` `dev.coderoftime.rurana`; and the app's URL scheme `vikunja-dev.coderoftime.rurana`, whose prefix stays because Vikunja accepts only redirect schemes that start with `vikunja-` ([D14](#d14-browser-sign-in-only), [D23](#d23-browser-sign-in-through-flutter_web_auth_2-client-id-and-redirect-uri)). D15 made the id permanent once signing keys or store listings exist, so this was the last time it could change; "vikunja" no longer shows in Play's address, the opt-in link or the app info.
    4. **The About page gets a row „Hilfe und Fehler melden“** (the German text, in the owner's words; "Help and bug reports" in English) that opens Rurana's support page, https://coderoftime.dev/rurana/support/ in English and https://coderoftime.dev/de/rurana/support/ in German, like the rows of the privacy policy and the legal notice (`WebsitePage` in `app/lib/app/legal.dart`). Testers and users find the way to report a bug from inside the app, and Play's production application asks how feedback was collected.
    5. **The beta's testers:** Play's closed test gets its testers through a Google Group that the owner creates; TestFlight's external testers come through a public link; the testers use their own Vikunja server (2.3.0 or newer, [D13](#d13-support-floor-230)) or the public demo `try.vikunja.io`.
  - **Taken without the owner (2026-11-02), open in the review:**
    - **The threat model is signed off before the first beta tag, and again before 1.0.0:** the beta is public. Each `v*` tag publishes a GitLab release with an APK signed by the app signing key, which GitLab shows as the latest release (it has no pre-release flag); TestFlight's link is public; and anyone can join the Google Group. The threat model asks for the full review "before each store release" ([section 9](threat-model.md#9-review-cadence)), so [section 8.1](threat-model.md#81-automated) and [section 8.2](threat-model.md#82-manual-on-the-android-emulator) on the emulator, with the emulator check of R33's fix for MainActivity ([threat model](threat-model.md#7-residual-and-accepted-risks)), run before `v1.0.0-beta.1`, and the owner signs [section 8.4](threat-model.md#84-sign-off); 8.2 needs the owner to sign in two accounts. Section 8.3 waits for the device test; before 1.0.0 the owner signs again.
    - **The review notes no longer carry the demo credentials,** as Apple asks: `demo` / `demo` stay in App Review's sign-in fields (`fastlane/metadata/review_information/demo_user.txt` and `demo_password.txt`).
    - **Security reports go by email first,** to `coderoftime@gmail.com`, with a confidential GitLab issue as the alternative and never a public one ([CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md#issues)), as the website's support page and its `security.txt` say; CONTRIBUTING named only a confidential issue.
    - **The beta's runbook lives in a new [beta.md](beta.md):** what the owner does before the tag and in which order, the Google Group, the opt-in link and the TestFlight link, the 14 days, the feedback, Play's production application, and the draft message to the Vikunja maintainers.
    - **The French encryption declaration becomes [Q23](open-questions.md#q23-french-encryption-declaration-for-the-app-store),** to be settled before the App Store release in France.
    - **App Store Connect's Accessibility Nutrition Labels stay blank** until the device and screen-reader passes ([Q19](open-questions.md#q19-device-setup-for-the-manual-talkback-pass), [accessibility checklist](accessibility-checklist.md#3-final-ios-phase)) confirm what can be claimed.
    - **The About page's wording around the owner's row:** the English label "Help and bug reports"; the section heading "Help, privacy and legal notice" („Hilfe, Datenschutz und Impressum“), formerly "Privacy and legal notice"; the subtitle "Answers to common questions and how to report a bug" („Antworten auf häufige Fragen und wie du einen Fehler meldest“); the row first in the section, above the privacy policy and the legal notice; and the source-code note of builds without a source link, which said that the code "will be published with the first release" and would turn false once the project is public: "This build has no link to its source code. Every release of Rurana links the source code it was built from." („Dieser Build hat keinen Link zu seinem Quellcode. Jede Veröffentlichung von Rurana verlinkt den Quellcode, aus dem sie gebaut wurde.“)
    - **Rurana on Vikunja 2.7.1:** attachment previews go over v1's `preview_size` wherever the server offers v1, whatever the client prefers, because the server version stored at sign-in can predate an upgrade and v1 answers the same on every version; a v2-only connection uses v2's preview route from 2.7.1 on. A file without a preview keeps its icon in the attachment list, and an image in a description or comment falls back to the original, as 2.7.0 sent it. The task preset of the API token form takes `projects.views_buckets_tasks_update` where `/routes` offers it. A board at the server's column limit says so in the app's language (`boardColumnsAtMaximum`). The note under the two-factor row says that newer servers also ask for a code to turn it off. The contract tests check each server version's behaviour, with a new test of the column limit; new fixtures and a capture tool for 2.7.1's preview answers. Not changed: the Quick Add date range, and a v1-only edge case in which a view with more than 50 filter buckets from before 2.7.1 would be refused when the app edits it over v1.
    - **This commit is 37d's first part;** 37d stays in progress for the beta's fixes and then 1.0.0.
    - **Done by the main session before the owner's decisions,** also open in the review: Flutter 3.47.9 / Dart 3.13.7 pinned in `tool/ci/pins.sh` (3.47.7's checksum kept in a comment) and installed locally, for the advisory above; the test servers on `vikunja/vikunja:2.7.1`, still pinned by tag, for its security fixes and to match the demo; `app/pubspec.yaml` at `1.0.0-beta.1+1`, as [releasing.md](releasing.md#cutting-a-release)'s example has it, the build number coming from the tag; the committed `pubspec.lock` kept.
    - **The screenshot session for Play's phone set (2026-11-03),** with the owner's sign-in on a new emulator, a Pixel 9 from a Google Play system image: the sign-in goes through the browser, and an AOSP image has only the WebView Browser Tester, without Custom Tabs. Six motifs in each language at 1080 × 1920. A review of the first set led to: a status bar with full Wi-Fi without the no-internet mark and without the mobile signal, which had been grey and half visible; the 24-hour clock and the device's own time at each shot instead of 9:41, which stood beside "Good afternoon" and due times counted from the real clock ([D76](#d76-store-listings-and-policies-the-name-rurana-alone-the-public-demo-for-the-reviewers-data-safety-answered-cautiously-without-a-location-photo-locations-removed-on-android-too-ruranas-pages-on-coderoftimedev-non-trader-in-both-stores) amended, with a dated note there); the task detail shown from its title, without the checklist, which does not fit beside the title in 16:9; Upcoming scrolled to the end of its list, so that it does not repeat the Overview and the New task button covers no card (a second review found it over a due date when Tomorrow was at the top); the second demo user named Sam in the seed, where the detail showed the username `rurana-e2e-2`. The German New task sheet takes its date from the date row, because Quick Add Magic knows only English words. Not taken: the tablet set, which Play does not require, and the App Store's sets, which the 1.0.0 submission needs and TestFlight does not.
    - **In the docs:** the absolute path of the Flutter SDK in research/flutter-stack.md is `<flutter-sdk>` now (the history keeps it); the README marks the Quechua meaning of "Rurana" as waiting for a Quechua speaker's confirmation; M3 is a "beta candidate", no longer a "private" one; older decisions keep the ids of their time, with dated notes on D15 and D23.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - **A group of Rurana's own** on GitLab, which would have let the rest of `coderoftime` stay private and allowed a new history: the owner keeps the project where it is and makes the group public.
  - **GitLab's free tier without the programme:** 400 minutes a month for the whole group fit about one beta a month.
  - **A new project with a rewritten history:** the owner keeps the history as it is.
  - **Q20's option B,** Google's keys for Play and a key of its own for the APK: the two channels would not update each other, and the APK's key would have to be registered for developer verification. **Play's signed universal APK,** downloaded from the console, as the GitLab APK: one signature without a key to hold, but the release job would no longer build and check the APK. **The APK under an application id of its own:** a second id in the build, which 37a does not have.
  - **Keeping `dev.coderoftime.vikunja`** and asking the Vikunja maintainers about it with the name and the logo.
  - **An email list of testers** in the Play Console: the owner chose a Google Group, which testers join themselves.
  - **The threat model's sign-off only before 1.0.0,** after the beta: the beta's APK, TestFlight link and Google Group are open to anyone, so the first beta is a public release in the threat model's sense.
- **Consequences:**
  - No store install exists, so nothing is migrated. A development build under the old id stays on a device beside the new app until it is uninstalled, with its own accounts, data and widgets; the new app starts with none of them, and links with the old scheme `vikunja-dev.coderoftime.vikunja://` no longer open it. Nothing changes on the server: Vikunja has no client registry, and the `client_id` only has to match between the authorize and the token call ([D23](#d23-browser-sign-in-through-flutter_web_auth_2-client-id-and-redirect-uri)).
  - Once the group is public, the release job's visibility guards can pass, `RURANA_SOURCE_CODE_URL` for the iOS release is `https://gitlab.com/coderoftime/rurana`, the group's duplicate-packages setting applies, and issues and merge requests are open to everyone ([releasing.md](releasing.md#once-before-the-first-release)). Until the programme is granted, the free minutes are shared with the group's other projects and each beta costs two full pipelines, so a fix during the closed test's 14 days can ship about once a month; the iOS build costs no shared minutes.
  - The app signing key stays with the owner and, for the APK, in protected variables of the `release` environment ([threat model](threat-model.md#7-residual-and-accepted-risks) R28); a leaked app signing key cannot be replaced for existing installs. Under option A the APK needs no key of its own registered for developer verification.
  - Every build links Rurana's support page from the About page, in the app's language.
  - The history becomes public as it is: the research file's tree no longer names the SDK's path, its history does.
  - Testers need a Google account for Play or the TestFlight app on iOS. Their own servers cover what the demo cannot, such as notifications over days, widgets and several accounts; the demo is reset at irregular intervals.
  - Tests: the id in about 20 test files of the app, `vikunja_api` and `vikunja_logic` (the client id, the redirect URI and the scheme in their case-insensitive spellings, the App Group, the background task names, the Kotlin sources' path), and the Kotlin JVM tests under `dev.coderoftime.rurana`. `about_page_test.dart`: the help row's English and German addresses beside the privacy policy and the legal notice, every other language on the English page, and the page at 360 × 740. For 2.7.1: `attachments_read_test.dart` (previews over v1 whenever the server offers it, whatever the stored version, v2's preview route only without v1 from 2.7.1 on, code 4038 on both APIs from captured fixtures, an unknown attachment, the original on the download route); `attachment_image_cache_test.dart`, `rich_text_view_test.dart` and `task_detail_online_test.dart` (no retry after 4038, the thumbnail's icon, an inline image's original and an inline SVG); `api_token_permissions_test.dart` (the task preset on 2.7.1's names); `account_security_test.dart` (the gone token's 404 code 14017 from captures); `board_columns_test.dart` (the column limit in English and German). The contract tests check each version's behaviour: the previews, the second delete of a token, the two-factor setup cancelled with the password and a code, and a new test of the 100-column limit on v1 and v2.
  - Open and known gaps ([beta.md](beta.md) has the runbook and the order):
    - **The owner, before `v1.0.0-beta.1`:** every project of `coderoftime` public under an OSI-approved licence, then the application for GitLab for Open Source; `main` pushed, and the project's settings, signing variables and weekly schedule as [releasing.md](releasing.md#once-before-the-first-release) says; the app signing key and the upload key, with the `RURANA_APK_*` and `RURANA_UPLOAD_*` variables; a personal Play developer account, the app `dev.coderoftime.rurana` with "Provide a copy of your app signing key" and a look whether hybrid signing applies to it; the Apple Developer Program, the App IDs `dev.coderoftime.rurana` and `dev.coderoftime.rurana.RuranaWidgets` with the App Group `group.dev.coderoftime.rurana`, and the App Store Connect record; the Google Group and TestFlight's public link; a check that the tracked test-user files hold nothing used anywhere else; what 37c left (the Impressum's c/o address and phone number, the console forms; Play's phone screenshots were taken on 2026-11-03); the wording of the written offer ([D67](#d67-agpl-30-or-later-with-an-app-store-permission-contributions-under-the-dco-the-name-and-the-logo-reserved-no-f-droid-the-legal-notices-on-an-about-page-and-the-licence-page-the-source-code-published-before-the-first-store-release)).
    - **The main session, before `v1.0.0-beta.1`:** section 8.1's checks and both release dry runs with the new id, and the contract tests against 2.7.1 (the status line records what ran); then, in a session in which the owner signs in two accounts on the emulator, section 8.2 and the R33 check, and section 8.1 again on the tree to be tagged.
    - **The owner, before `v1.0.0-beta.1`, after that session:** the sign-off of the threat model in section 8.4, with R17, R19, R21, R33 and R36 among the owner's points.
    - **Before 1.0.0:** the threat model signed off again (8.1, 8.2 for what changed, 8.3); the iOS device test and what to do about iOS 15 to 17.3, which no simulator here runs; the TalkBack and VoiceOver passes ([Q19](open-questions.md#q19-device-setup-for-the-manual-talkback-pass)); the name and the logo checked with the Vikunja maintainers, from the draft in beta.md; [Q23](open-questions.md#q23-french-encryption-declaration-for-the-app-store); 14 days with at least 12 testers, and Play's production application; the acceptance of steps 18 and 19; the formal trademark search ([branding](../branding/README.md)); the first iOS release build with Xcode 27.2.
    - **Not verified:** whether an uploaded app signing key gets hybrid signing, and whether Play's builds and an APK signed with RSA alone update each other on Android 17 and later; whether Play asks a new personal account to verify a real Android phone before a closed test; whether an exemption of the French decree covers the app (Q23); how GitLab for Open Source judges a licence with a section 7 additional permission (`LICENSE` is the unaltered AGPL text, the permission is in `NOTICE.md`).
  - Not done: no tag, no push, no change on GitLab, no store account or console, no message to anyone; the owner does these.
